# Sixfold Tower (Godot prototype)

A first playable test of the new magic system: **bonds** marked on body parts that fill through combat. The design lives in the *Sixfold Tower: Magic Redesign* doc; this prototype tests one question: **does filling bonds through combat feel good?**

## Running it

1. Install [Godot 4.3](https://godotengine.org/download) or newer (the standard version; no .NET needed).
2. In the Godot project manager, choose **Import** and pick this folder's `project.godot`.
3. Press **F5** to play.

Everything visual is drawn in code, so there are no art assets. Sounds and music are recorded CC0 effects in `assets/audio`, and the interface's three fonts (SIL Open Font License) are in `assets/fonts` (see [CREDITS.md](CREDITS.md)).

## Playing on a phone

A web version is built automatically every time `main` changes: **https://raphu88.github.io/sixfold-tower-godot/**. Open it in your phone's browser, turn the phone sideways, and tap the **[ ]** button for fullscreen. It also works in a desktop browser.

On a touchscreen the whole game is drawn bigger so it reads on a small screen (how much is the **Interface size** setting: see Settings below; a phone's Normal is 1.25x what it used to be), and on-screen controls appear as soon as you touch it: a joystick under your left thumb (past a light tilt you walk at full speed; there is no sprint), and buttons on the right for attack (hold to draw a bow), roll, parry, your marks' spells and weapon swap, a **Take** button that appears only while you stand on gear you could swap for, a **Tools** button that appears only while you hold a secret bond (tap for the last tool, hold and slide for the tool ring), an **Item** button at the bottom (it shows your current item and how many you have; tap it to use that item, or hold it to open the **belt wheel** (it opens beside your thumb, over the buttons, so nothing hides it, and the game slows), slide your finger onto an item and let go to make it your current item: choosing never uses it; the small arrow beside it picks the next item), and small **Bag** (inventory), **Climber** and **Menu** buttons at the top right. Aiming is automatic: you face the nearest monster in reach, or the way you're moving.

## Six Towers LITE (for young children)

A much simpler game for young children who can't read yet lives in its own folder, [`lite/`](lite/README.md): a separate small Godot project with its own web link, **https://raphu88.github.io/sixfold-tower-godot/lite/**. It shares nothing with this game but copies of a few sounds.

## Controls

| Action | Keys | Controller (Xbox / PlayStation) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Move | WASD or arrow keys | Left stick |
| Aim | The mouse | Right stick; let go and you aim at the nearest monster, or the way you move |
| Roll | Space | B / Circle |
| Jump (over chasms and burning floor) | Z, or the Jump button on a phone | A / Cross |
| Strike / hold to draw a bow / fire the crossbow or a pistol | Left click or J | RT / R2 |
| Parry | Right click or K | LT / L2 |
| Block, with a shield in your off hand (keep holding parry after the parry's moment) | Hold right click or K | Hold LT / L2 |
| Cast your off-hand wand (from its own pool); hold to power an essence item | U or the middle mouse button, or the Off-hand button on a phone (with a wand, or an enchanted weapon in hand: "Power") | Press the left stick in (LS / L3); hold it to power |
| Switch weapon | 1 Fists, 2 your melee weapon, 3 your ranged weapon, or the mouse wheel | RB / R1 or d-pad right (next), d-pad left (previous) |
| Take the gear you stand on (your old piece goes in your pack, or is dropped if it's full) | Y, or the Take button on a phone | Y / Triangle |
| Cast your marks (hand, legs, heart, brow, throat, back) with a tap; hold to choose a spell on the wheel (choosing never casts) | Q, E, F, C, X, V | Hold LB / L1, then X, A, B, Y, RB, RT (Square, Cross, Circle, Triangle, R1, R2) |
| Use your current item (tap); hold to open the **belt wheel**, point at an item and let go (or click, or press its number 1 to 9) to make it current: choosing never uses it | G, or the Item button on a phone | X / Square (point with the right stick) |
| Next item (tap; nothing is used); hold for the belt wheel | T, or the small arrow button beside Item on a phone | D-pad down |
| A secret bond's tool ring (hold; a tap uses the last tool again or closes its panel) | B, or the Tools button on a phone (only with a secret bond) | Hold LB / L1 + LT / L2 |
| Choose a tower, camp card or heirloom | 1 to 6, or arrows and Enter, click, or tap the card (after the Regard, towers are chosen in the train's engine) | D-pad or left stick, then A / Cross |
| The train between climbs: move along to the next car (Engine, Outfitter's Car, Your Car, Workshop, Dining Car, Library, Settings & Controls Car) | A car on the rail down the left, Q / E, the mouse wheel, or drag the train with the mouse; Up / Down on the rail; on a phone, tap a car on the rail or swipe | LB / RB, or up / down on the rail |
| The train: into a car, and back out to the rail | Right or Enter on the rail; Left (past the first thing in the car) or Esc | A or right; left or B |
| A station (Engine → Stations → the lever): its stops (Training, Tournament, the Noble, Renown), a stop's card, back to the train | A stop on the rail, Q / E between stops; Right or Enter into a stop, Enter on a tile for its card's button; Esc back to the tile, the rail, then the train (or Back to the train at the rail's foot); on a phone, tap | A or right, LB / RB between stops, B back |
| In a station's lesson, drill or bracket: give up | The pause menu (Esc): Give up, back to the station | Start / Options, then A |
| After a fall (after the Regard): back to the train now | Enter, Space, click or R (it goes by itself after 3 seconds); on a phone, tap | A / Cross or View / Share |
| Pause menu, with the rail of pages down the left (your name for the climber screen, Resume, Inventory, Library, Settings & Controls, Dev Tools, Exit to Title; up and down move along it, right goes into a page, left or Esc comes back to it) | Esc or P, or the Menu button on a phone | Start / Options |
| Back one level in a menu | Esc, or a right-click (or the mouse's back button) | B / Circle |
| Climber screen (abilities, marks, essence, record); hold to peek at your abilities, let go to return | Tab (hold it to peek), your name at the top of the menu's rail, or the Climber button on a phone (hold it to peek) | Press View / Share |
| The floor map (rooms you've seen, the stair, the portals; on a floor of rooms). Stepping onto a woken portal opens it to travel | O, the pause menu's Floor map, or the Map button on a phone; Esc or O closes it | Start / Options, then Floor map; B closes it |
| Inventory (gear, items, heirlooms) | I, or the Bag button on a phone (on the train, I goes to your car, where the Loadout and stash are) | D-pad up |
| Your car's Loadout and stash: switch between the two tabs | Q / E, or tap a tab | LB / RB |
| The stash's sort and filters, the store's filters (option rows) | Left / Right on one, Enter for the next, or click or tap its left or right half | D-pad left / right, A for the next |
| Move a pack or stash piece across (pack to stash, stash to pack) | Press it a second time, or its card's button | A on its card's button |
| Restart (hold, so a stray press can't end a climb); after the Regard a restart starts again from the train | Hold R | Hold View / Share |
| Start over (this climber only: forget their marks, choose a new look and start the first climb again; it asks first, in a pop-up) | N, or Start over this climber in Settings & Controls (on the train, the emergency brake in the Settings & Controls Car) | The same button |
| Music on/off | M | Music slider in Settings & Controls |
| Show/hide the controls list on screen (off at first; also in Settings & Controls) | H | Settings & Controls |
| Dismiss the Entity's tip (or tap or click it) | Enter | Press the right stick (RS / R3) |
| The Entity's log: open or close its panel (what happened this climb, and its last tip in full) | L, or click its sphere (tap it on a phone); Esc closes it too | Press the right stick (RS / R3) while no tip is waiting |
| Arrange mode: move and resize the HUD, the phone's buttons, the menus, the rail and the train's furniture yourself, a few big blocks at a time (see Arranging the layout) | F2 (with a menu open: that menu), or Dev tools → Arrange layout / Arrange menus / Arrange the train, or Settings & Controls → Arrange this screen (on the train: the Settings car → Arrange the train) | Dev tools → Arrange layout; then d-pad / LB / RB pick, A goes inside, B backs out, left stick moves, right stick resizes |

**Controllers.** Plug in (or pair) an Xbox, PlayStation or similar controller and just press a button. The prompts on screen (the controls panel, the gear card's "take it", the card screens, "press ... to begin your next climb") switch to controller buttons as soon as you use it, with PlayStation names on a PlayStation pad, and back to keys the moment you touch the keyboard or mouse (or to touch wording on a phone). Holding LB (L1) turns the face buttons, RB and RT into your six marks' casts; hold the cast button to open its spell wheel and point with the right stick; let go to put the spell on the button (it isn't cast). The belt and spell wheels slow the game while they're open (to 25%: `scripts/wheel.gd`); the secret bonds' tool ring stops it. The button layout lives in `scripts/controls.gd` (`PAD` and `PAD_CAST`), read by `scripts/pad.gd`.

**If buttons are scrambled in your browser, use Settings & Controls → Controller setup.** Browsers don't all report a controller the same way. Chrome and Edge (and Firefox with many pads) say "standard layout" and everything just works. Firefox often reports an Xbox pad as raw button numbers instead, which differ by system and driver, so the game's buttons land on the wrong ones (or nowhere). The game copes in three ways (`scripts/pad.gd`): a controller the browser or Godot knows passes straight through; an unknown Xbox-like one gets a guessed layout (`FALLBACK`), and the first time you use an unknown controller the Entity mentions the setup; and **Settings & Controls → Controller setup** (`scripts/pad_setup.gd`) walks you through every control one at a time ("Press the button you want for Jump", "Pull the trigger you want for Attack", "Push the stick you want to move with: up"...), records whatever the controller sends (a button, an axis and its direction, a trigger resting at -1, a d-pad sent as buttons, axes or one "hat" axis) and saves it for that controller. Any button starts it (on a controller whose layout is already known, the d-pad moves between the page's buttons and A presses the focused one); once given, your Jump button skips a step and your Roll button goes back one; keys, mouse and touch work too (Esc leaves without saving). **Reset to default** forgets it. Underneath, **What the controller sends** shows the raw button numbers and axis values as they arrive: if something is still off, that's the thing to report. (Godot 4.3's web export also lost the triggers of "standard" pads; `controls.gd` `WEB_STANDARD_FIX` corrects that.)

## Gear: weapons, armor and worn pieces

Gear is everything you carry and wear. Weapons live in `scripts/weapons.gd`; armor, the worn pieces, drops, wear and heirlooms in `scripts/gear.gd`. Names, text and numbers are plain data there, so they're easy to change. Everything is drawn in code, with its own icon.

**Slots.** Gear lasts one climb. You carry **1 fists** (always), **2 a melee weapon** and **3 a ranged weapon**, and you wear a **chest** piece (armor), a **helm**, **gloves**, **legs**, **boots**, a **cloak**, an **amulet**, **two rings**, a **backpack** (your bag: it sets how many pieces your pack holds) a **charm** (see **Charms** below) and an **off-hand** piece (see **The off-hand** below). Every climb starts with a **sword**, a **shortbow** and nothing else, except where you hold an heirloom (below). The HUD shows only the weapon in your hand (swapping with 1-3, the controller or the phone's Swap button pops up all three weapon slots for a moment); the **inventory** (I) shows everything you wear.

**Gear records.** Each piece is a small record, not just a name: its **base** (which piece it is), its **rarity**, its **traits**, its **rank** (0 to 3, tempered at the bench; 4 with a full vessel), a **rune** (a lasting rune from the bench, or none), for a named piece its **name** (`named`), and, for plain Common pieces, its **wear**. Helpers in `gear.gd` make (`make`, `make_named`, `roll_gear`), convert (`rec`, `code`), describe (`display`, `describe`, `trait_lines`, `stats`), compare (`compare`, `plays_like`) and add up (`total_effects`, `capped_keys`, `protection`) records. Old saves, which kept heirlooms as plain names, are converted automatically when they load.

**Finding gear.** Slain monsters (not beasts) rarely drop a piece (4%), with a rarity by floor (see **Rarities and traits** below), likelier one that suits them: knights favour chain mail, plate, swords, maces, greatswords, iron helms and gauntlets; archers bows, crossbows, leather and leather caps; smiths hammers, maces, armor, gauntlets and hobnail boots; bats dusk cloaks; and so on (`FAVOUR` in `gear.gd`). A drop can be for any slot. Rings and amulets don't appear on the bondless first climb. A Forgotten Cache's urn of essence sometimes holds a piece too (35%), a camp offers a piece of gear as a fourth card beside its three items, and a vault may hold one (Fine at the least); caches, camps and vaults roll one rarity row higher than monsters, and the floor 9 camp's piece is Rare. Gear lies on the floor with its name in its rarity's colour (Fine and better pieces also glint). Walk over it with an empty slot and you take it (a second ring goes on your other hand); otherwise a card names its **slot** and compares it with what you wear there (better numbers in green with an up arrow, worse in red with a down arrow), and **Y** (or **Take** on a phone) swaps: your old piece goes in your **pack** if there's room, else it's dropped where you stand, so you can change your mind. The first time you find a worn piece, the Entity explains the slots.

**The pack.** Besides what you wear you carry a **pack** of spare pieces. How many it holds comes from your **bag**, the piece in your backpack slot (the owner's call: bag sizes rather than a weight stat): **no bag 2**, a **Satchel 4**, a **Pack 6**, a **Great Pack 8** (the Belt Pouch, Herb Pouch and Bandolier 3, the Climber's Pack 6; `bag` in `PIECES`, `BAG` in `gear.gd`), and a Pocket Clasp or Keeper's Purse charm adds a space. **A big bag slows you only when it's nearly full**: at three quarters of its spaces or more (`BAG["heavy_at"]`), a Pack trims your walk and your rolls by 5% and a Great Pack by 10% (`slow`); a Satchel, a pouch, a bandolier and the Climber's Pack never slow you. The pack's heading says so everywhere it shows ("Pack · 5 of 6 · heavy"), a small line under the HUD's banner says "Heavy pack: 5 of 6, you're slower" while it lasts, and the first time it happens the Entity explains. Old saves, whose packs held 4 before bags had sizes, keep every piece: a pack holding more than its bag just takes nothing more until it's under (you can still wear or move pieces out of it; `Train.pack_too_small` refuses only a change that makes the pack smaller). A climber with no bag at all always finds a cheap Satchel on the outfitter's rail. Swapping gear puts your old piece in it while there's room. The inventory's **Pack** view (beside Worn and Heirlooms) shows it: choose a pack piece to **Wear it** (what you wore takes its place) or **Drop it here**, and the gear at your feet has **Into your pack**. Get out alive, by the stair at the top or a recall scroll, and **what you wear and your pack come down to the train** with you (`main.gd bring_pack_home`, `bring_worn_home`): the pack to sell, keep in your stash or wear next time, and the pieces you wore still on you (the **Loadout**'s chosen pieces: take them off there or set off in them again). Fall and both are lost; heirlooms, the stash and banked essence are always safe. Belt items still become crystals when a climb ends.

**Worn pieces** (the Common ones; each does one small thing). *Heavy* pieces make every roll cost 3% more stamina, on top of your chest piece's weight.

### The off-hand

The **off-hand** slot (`scripts/offhand.gd`; the pieces' names and looks are in `gear.gd PIECES`, slot "offhand") holds a second one-handed weapon, a shield or a wand (the owner's call, from the old game). Each fits the combat that's already there:

- **A second weapon** adds an **off-hand strike** to your strike chains, just before the finisher (any one-handed melee weapon, and your fists). Its strike has its own numbers (`USES` "strike"), rarity and wear.
- **A shield** blocks: **keep holding parry** after the parry's moment and blows from in front are cut by its share (`guard`), each costing stamina (`guard_sta`); with too little stamina the guard breaks, the blow lands whole and you stagger. You walk slower while guarding. A parry is still the skilful answer: it takes nothing and gives stamina back.
- **A wand** casts a small essence power with the **off-hand button** (U, the middle mouse button, LS on a controller, the Off-hand button on a phone, which shows only while a wand is in a free hand, with the wand and its uses left on it).

**Enchanted off-hand pieces have their own essence pools** for their powers (as every essence item: see **Items and their essence** below): a few uses (their numbers in `charms.gd CHARGES`, with the charms'), spent as the power fires (the pool first, then the essence you carry) and refilled exactly like a charm's charge: in the tower from the inventory's card, with essence you carry; on the train at the workshop bench's Charms counter, with crystals of its kind. **Two-handed weapons** (greatsword, war hammer, spear) **and bows and crossbows** need both hands: while one is in hand the off-hand is put away (its traits still count). Off-hand pieces drop, sell, wear, take traits and become heirlooms like other gear, and show on the climber, in the tower and on every front figure (`Offhand.tower_hand`, `Offhand.front`).

| Piece | Kind | Rarity | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Parrying Dagger | weapon | Common | a quick stab in your chains |
| Hand Axe | weapon | Common | a hacking chop in your chains |
| Ember Dirk | weapon | Fine (Fire) | its stab sets the foe burning (pool: 6 uses, 3 essence each) |
| Buckler | shield | Common | blocks 45%, 8 stamina a blow |
| Kite Shield | shield | Common (heavy) | blocks 75%, 12 stamina a blow; you walk slowest behind it |
| Tide Ward | shield | Fine (Water) | blocks 55%; a blocked blow sends a wave out (pool: 3 uses, 6 each) |
| Cairn Wand | wand | Fine (Stone) | hurls a stone for 12 that staggers (6 uses, 3 each) |
| Edge Wand | wand | Fine (Blade) | a fan of three shards, 6 each (6 uses, 3 each) |
| Iron Wand | wand | Fine (Iron) | a 20-point barrier for 6 s (3 uses, 6 each) |
| Gale Wand | wand | Fine (Air) | a gust that hurls the foes in front back (6 uses, 3 each) |
| Cinder Wand | wand | Fine (Fire) | a bolt for 8 that sets the foe burning (6 uses, 3 each) |
| Tide Wand | wand | Fine (Water) | a cold bolt for 8 that slows (6 uses, 3 each) |

The Entity explains it the first time you wear one in a climb, in "How does the off-hand work?", and on its buttons.

### Items and their essence (own pools)

**Every essence item carries its own essence pool** (`scripts/item_pool.gd`; the owner's rule: "Items like wands, charms, magical ranged and melee weapons should all have their own essence pool. That pool is used first before the player's, but the player's essence can be filtered into the item to either refill it or power it."). The items: every **charm** with a charge, every **enchanted off-hand piece** (the six wands, the Ember Dirk, the Tide Ward) and every **enchanted weapon** (a Fine or better melee weapon, bow, crossbow or pistol, and enchanted relics). The pool is a few uses of the item's own essence, kept on its record (`charge`; none noted is full, so **old saves' pieces start full**), and shown as a **small flat bar** along the top of its slot: on the HUD's weapon slot, the inventory's paper doll, the Loadout, stash and pack, the bench, and the phone's Off-hand button. Numbers: charms and off-hand pieces as in their tables (`charms.gd CHARGES`); enchanted weapons by kind (`WEAPON`): **melee, bow and crossbow 6 uses of 3 essence each, pistols 12 of 2**.

- **Using it** draws one use from **its own pool first**; only when that's dry does it draw on **the essence you carry** (gathered since the last stair, not yet banked: its own kind first, then any). With neither, its essence effect doesn't fire: a charm stays quiet, a wand won't cast, a weapon still strikes and shoots plainly, and an enchanted pistol fires lead and reloads like any other. What spends a use: a charm's charge firing; a wand's cast; the Ember Dirk's burning stab and the Tide Ward's wave; an enchanted melee weapon's **landed finisher** (its **edge**, `EDGE`: stone staggers, blade cuts again for a third of the blow, iron bites deeper and drives back, air hurls, fire burns, water chills); a bow's **full draw** and **every crossbow bolt** (an essence shot's rider, `weapons.gd ESSENCE_SHOT`: stone staggers, blade splits into shards, iron pierces, air flies faster, fire bursts, water chills); every **essence shot of a pistol** (its chambers still fill themselves, as before). The first time a pool runs dry, the Entity explains.
- **Filtering to refill:** in the tower, the item's card in the **inventory** has **Filter in N essence** (essence you carry, any kind, each kind in its share), **not while a monster is after you** (as the Body tab's moves; at a camp always); on the train, the workshop bench's **Charms** counter refills it with **crystals of its kind** (enchanted weapons show there once their pool isn't full). **Nothing else refills a pool**, and a new climb doesn't either; an heirloom's pool follows the climb's.
- **Filtering to power:** **hold the off-hand button** for half a second (U or the middle mouse button, LS on a controller, the phone's Off-hand button, which shows "Power" while an enchanted weapon is in hand). It takes 6 essence you carry (`POWER`): a wand in a free off hand **casts at once, powered**; otherwise the enchanted weapon in your hand (else your Ember Dirk or Tide Ward) is **primed** (a thin frame round its HUD slot) and its next use hits **1.5 times as hard**, its essence effect too. A powered use pays for itself if the pool and your carried essence are dry. A quick tap still casts a wand.

Refills and power cost essence you would otherwise bank, and no pool grows, so the pace stays slow. The Entity explains it when filtering is first offered, when a pool first runs dry and when you first power an item, in "Items and their essence?", and on the bars and filter buttons.

### Summit Blades

The old game's "sacred swords", renamed (`scripts/summit_blades.gd`). **One blade is hidden in each tower**, on one floor picked for each climber (floors 3 to 10), in a quiet corner of a room far from the start (`plan`, called by `floors.gd`), half sunk in the floor. Without a lead it shows only up close, with a faint glint now and then a little farther off; **with a lead** (`profile.summit_leads[tower]`, which the pocket vaults give) its glint shows from anywhere you can see, a pale beam stands over it, and the climb tells you which floor. Walk over it to take it up: it rides across your back. **Reach the summit with it and it's bonded to you for good**; fall, or leave another way, and it slips back to its hiding place. A bonded blade is never lost and isn't gear (it can't be sold, stashed or swapped): you carry **one at a time, sheathed at your hip**, and its **power** fires by itself (on a landed finisher or a perfect parry, with a rest between). **Awaken** it on the train, in the book's Deeds ribbon (Summit Blades leaf: Carry this blade, Awaken), for crystals of its tower's essence: six steps (12, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72), **one step between climbs**, each making its power stronger and its rest shorter. A Summit Blade is distinct from a beast's **Regarded** piece: that is gear you wield and lose in a fall; a blade is wielded by nobody and kept forever. The names are placeholders and each blade's `history` is the owner's to write.

| Blade | Tower | Fires on | Power (grows each step) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Galebrand | Gryphon (Air) | a finisher | a bolt to up to two foes nearby |
| Tidemourn | Kraken (Water) | a perfect parry | a wave that hurls foes back and slows them |
| Anvilsong | Wyvern (Iron) | a finisher | its echo strikes the foe again and staggers it |
| Duneshard | Manticore (Stone) | a finisher | poisons the foes near the one struck |
| Emberwake | Firebird (Fire) | a perfect parry | heals you a little |
| Thornfang | Chimera (Blade) | a finisher | thorns burst round the foe struck |

### Deeds

**36 deeds** (`scripts/deeds.gd`), six kinds of six (battle; climbing; marks and essence; stations and tournaments; collecting; daring), each worth 1 to 6 renown, **120 for them all**. Each is read from what the climber has done already (the record, the Bestiary, renown's title rules, the Summit Blades, and a few counters of its own: perfect parries, blocks, off-hand strikes, floors climbed without a hit, summits). A watcher looks twice a second; a deed newly done pays its renown (said in the feed and the Entity's log, many at once in one line), shows a small notice over the climber, and the first time, the Entity explains. The book's **Deeds** ribbon (`scripts/deeds_page.gd`) lists every deed with a medal, what it asks, how far along you are and what it pays.

### Charms

**Charms** (`scripts/charms.gd`, and the charm pieces in `gear.gd PIECES`; the owner's design: "charms should be different than consumables; perhaps they are a thing that can be equipped") are worn in the **one charm slot**, on its own row under the paper doll. **A charm is never used up** and never wears. Each has a **passive** that is on while you wear it and off when you take it off, and some also hold a **charge**: an effect that fires by itself when its moment comes. The charge is the charm's own essence pool (see **Items and their essence**): once spent, each firing draws on the essence you carry instead; **nothing refills it on its own**. You refill it by **filtering essence in**: in the tower, from the inventory's card for your charm (**Filter in N essence**: essence you haven't banked at a stair, any kind, taken from each kind in its share; not while a monster is after you), or on the train at the workshop bench's **Charms** counter (anyone: crystals of the charm's own kind). Like Arc Raiders' augments, charms shape your kit: overall passives, auras, and special inventory room. Common charms are ordinary; Fine and Rare ones are enchanted in their essence (their card says so). Charms take no traits; their numbers are in `PIECES`, their charges in `CHARGES`, the auras in `AURA`.

| Charm | Rarity | Essence | Passive | Charge (how many; refill per charge) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lucky Knot | Common | | +12 max stamina | |
| Pocket Clasp | Common | | one more pack space | |
| Belt Hook | Common | | one more kind of item on the belt | |
| Ember Charm | Fine | Fire | spells hit 6% harder; your light reaches 10% farther (it was an amulet) | |
| Feather Token | Fine | Air | you can roll again 20% sooner; rolls carry 5% farther | |
| Iron Brooch | Fine | Iron | +8 max stamina; stamina starts refilling 30% sooner after you act | |
| Ward Stone | Fine | Stone | an aura: the first blow that lands on you in each room hurts half as much | |
| Tide Shell | Fine | Water | an aura: a 12-point shield that grows back once you've gone 6 s without being hit | |
| Wardbreaker Charm | Fine | Blade | strikes hit 4% harder | **Snap the ward** (1; 12): the first time a shell or guard would turn your blow, for 20 s your blows and arrows go through shells and guards (it was a belt item) |
| Mending Bead | Fine | Water | healing items heal 10% more | **Mend** (2; 8): falling below a third of your health heals you 25 over 2 s |
| Keeper's Purse | Rare | Iron | one more pack space, and a **safe pocket**: the first piece in your pack (tagged "Safe") comes home even if you fall, and so does the purse | |
| Ashen Knot | Rare | Fire | poison and burning floor hurt 15% less | **Rally** (1; 30): a blow that would fell you leaves you standing at 30% of your health |
| Gale Charm | Rare | Air | you walk 4% faster | **Gust** (3; 6): a blow that lands on you hurls the foes around you back |
| Bulwark Stone | Rare | Stone | +15 health and 4% damage taken off | |
| Hunter's Token | Common | | bows, crossbows and pistols hit 5% harder | |
| Brass Bellows | Fine | Iron | stamina refills 4% faster | **Second breath** (2; 8): running out of stamina fills half of it back at once |
| Duelist's Ribbon | Rare | Blade | ripostes and backstabs hit 15% harder; the parry window is 5% longer | |
| Mist Pearl | Rare | Water | monsters notice you 10% later | **Veil of mist** (2; 10): a blow that lands on you below half your health wraps you in mist for 4 s (as a Smoke Bomb: monsters inside lose track of you; beasts don't) |

Eighteen charms, by sixes (`tests/parts/six_gear.gd` checks every gear and item list's count). The Duelist's Ribbon and the Mist Pearl are recipes a Rune Artist learns from a noble's contract.

**Where charms come from:** the **workshop bench crafts** them at its Charms counter (`CRAFT`: 10 to 70 crystals of the charm's kind, plus the Rune Artist's premium for a climber without the mark; it goes into your stash), the **outfitter** keeps one in his charm tray every visit (`SHOP`: Common, or Fine 30% of the time; priced like other pieces from `BASE_PRICE`), and the towers hold them **now and then** (`LOOT`): a vault's third card is a charm 20% of the time, a secret room's crystals come with one 30% of the time, and a slain monster leaves one very rarely (0.4%); a found charm's rarity rolls like other finds, one row higher (a Storied roll is Rare). Charms are never a plain gear drop (`drop_weight` 0). They come home like other worn gear (a recall scroll or the summit; a fall loses them, save the Keeper's Purse), can be kept as an **heirloom** (the Charm heirloom slot; a charge it spends on a climb stays spent on the heirloom, `keep_heirloom`), and show on the climber as a small charm at the hip. The Entity explains them the first time you find, wear or craft one, in "How do charms work?" and on every new button. **Old saves:** a packed Wardbreaker Charm consumable gives back the crystals paid for it (`OLD_ITEMS`); an Ember Charm kept or worn as an amulet moves to the charm slot.

**Worn gear shows on the climber, everywhere** (`scripts/gear_look.gd`): in the tower, on the climber screen's figure, the tree's body map, the mirror image and the Loadout's large figure (the creation and Appearance previews stay gear-free, so you can see your hair and clothes). The chest piece by its weight (a quilted gambeson, a leather cuirass with its strap and tassets, a mail hauberk, a steel breastplate with faulds and pauldrons), the helm (a cloth hood in its colour; a cap, with a feather on the Feathered Cap; a helm with cheek guards and a nasal, a candle on the Miner's Helm, a narrow slit on the Watcher's Helm; plate brings a helm and mail a coif when you wear no helm piece), gloves (wraps, leather, studs, gauntlets, bracers), the legs (quilting, leather knee panels, mail, plated greaves), boots (laces, hobnails, tall wading boots), the cloak (a worn cloak's colour replaces your own; a Feather Cape is short), the backpack (a Climber's Pack with its bedroll and straps, a satchel at the hip, a belt pouch, a bandolier), the amulet on its chain and a stone on each ringed hand, the weapon in hand (every kind: sword, greatsword, dagger, axe, mace, war hammer, spear, the Coral Trident's three prongs, the walking cane with its crook, the sword-cane with its silver knob (its blade out for the finisher, the empty cane in the other hand), bows, the crossbow, and the pistols: a flintlock's long barrel and wooden stock, a revolver's cylinder, which swings out while it reloads) and the other weapon slung on your back (a bow and quiver, a cane across the back, a hilt over the shoulder, or a pistol holstered at the hip). **Rules with your appearance:** a helm or cap pushes your hood down, so your hair shows under it (long hair and braids still hang below); a worn hood or mail coif replaces your hood's colour; your tunic, trousers and belt show where armour doesn't cover them. **Rarity** shows as a thin edge in its colour on Fine and better pieces (a fine cloak is trimmed in it); **named pieces** (tower pieces, Storied, Regarded) are drawn in their own colour with a gilt line, and a Regarded piece carries a glowing stone. In the tower, where the climber is small, the gear is kept to what reads: the pack and slung weapon on your back, knee plates, the amulet's glint, a candle or feather, a fine helm's crest, a glint on a ringed hand.

**Protection** from everything you wear adds up (plate 35%, an iron helm 6% and chain leggings 5% make 46%) but never goes past **50%** (`PROTECTION_CAP`).

### Rarities and traits: ordinary, enchanted and relics

Gear comes in six **rarities** (`RARITIES` in `gear.gd`), each with its colour on the floor label, the drop's glint, the inventory slot's edge and the card: **Common** (parchment), **Fine** (green, 1 trait, weapons +5% damage), **Rare** (blue, 2 traits, weapons +10%), **Masterwork** (violet, a maker's finest plain work: 3 traits and no name of its own, weapons +11%; only from floor 10 up (`MASTERWORK_FROM`) and seldom, never on the outfitter's rail, so growth stays slow; the Entity explains it the first time), **Storied** (amber, its own named rule and 1 trait, weapons +12%) and **Regarded** (gold, a beast's own weapon or armour: its own named rule and **4 traits**, weapons +15%; see **Regarded pieces** below). Rings and amulets are Fine by nature, so they carry one trait fewer. Backpacks take no traits. Only plain **Common** pieces wear; anything with a trait, and anything finer, never does.

**Ordinary or enchanted** (the owner's direction: "keep tiers but make each piece exciting; some not enchanted and some enchanted"). A **Common** piece is **ordinary**: not enchanted, no traits, only its own use, and every base piece has one clear use of its own (no two the same; the cards say "ordinary"). **Fine** and **Rare** pieces are **enchanted**: their traits, and their card's first line says "Enchanted: <essence> essence" in that essence's colour (the essence of its first trait). Rings and amulets are enchanted by nature, each in its own essence (`PIECES` "kind"); the Climber's Pack and the Bandolier are Rare by nature but ordinary. **Storied** and **Regarded** pieces are **relics**: a name, their own rule, and a card line saying whether they're enchanted (the Lastbell Revolver isn't). Helpers in `gear.gd`: `enchanted`, `enchant_kind`, `shot_kind`. **Relics' histories:** every named piece has a `history` in `NAMED`, blank for the owner to write (in `gear.gd`, or the Data editor's Words → Gear → Named pieces); a card shows it only once it isn't blank.

**No trait twice.** A piece never carries the same trait twice, whatever made it: a drop, a cache, a camp, a vault, today's piece, a Proving relic, Join or Retrait at the bench. `gear.gd make()` (which every piece passes through, old saves too) replaces a repeat with another trait that fits the slot and isn't on the piece, the same one every time, so a piece in an old save is cleaned when it loads; if no trait is left for the slot, the repeat goes. Every slot but the backpack has at least four traits, so a Regarded piece always has four different ones.

**Rarity by floor** (slain monsters; caches, camps and vaults roll one row higher; `RARITY_ODDS`):

| Floors | Common | Fine | Rare | Masterwork | Storied |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 to 3 | 80% | 20% | 0% | 0% | 0% |
| 4 to 6 | 60% | 32% | 8% | 0% | 0% |
| 7 to 9 | 45% | 38% | 15% | 0% | 2% |
| 10 to 12 | 35% | 40% | 19% | 1% | 5% |
| (one row higher) | 25% | 40% | 24% | 3% | 8% |

Storied pieces are only found from floor 7 up. A **Rare** or better find gets a line in its rarity's colour in the left feed ("RARE: Axe of the Riposte"; see **What the game tells you**), and is kept in the Entity's log.

**Traits** (`TRAITS`): each belongs to an essence (shown in its colour on the card) and rolls only on the slots it suits. Names read like *Fine Leather Gloves of Edges* or *Rare Iron Helm of Vigor* (the first trait names the piece).

| of the Riposte | Blade | ripostes and backstabs +8% | +12% | melee, gloves, ranged |
| of Breath | Iron | +8 max stamina | +12 | legs, chest, cloak |
| of Second Wind | Iron | stamina refills 6% faster | 10% | rings, amulet, chest, ranged |
| of Thorns | Iron | foes that strike you up close take 3 | 5 | chest, gloves |
| of the Wind | Air | walk +4% | +6% | boots, cloak, legs |
| of Rolling | Air | rolls cost 5% less stamina | 8% | boots, legs |
| of the Hunt | Air | bows, crossbows and pistols +8% | +12% | ranged, gloves, helm |
| of Kindling | Fire | spells +6% | +9% | amulet, rings, helm |
| of Embers | Fire | hits have a 10% chance to set the foe burning (2 every half second for 2 s) | 15% | melee, gloves, ranged |
| of the Well | Water | marks fill 6% faster | 9% | amulet, rings, helm, ranged |
| of Calm | Water | parry window +5% | +8% | gloves, helm, rings |
| of Mending | Stone | each kill heals you 2 | 3 | melee, ranged, amulet |
| of Venom | Stone | hits have an 8% chance to poison the foe | 12% | melee, ranged |
| of the Quick Draw | Air | bows draw, and crossbows and pistols reload, 8% faster | 12% | ranged, gloves |

| of Striding | Air | rolls carry 5% farther | 8% | boots, legs, cloak |
| of the Lantern | Fire | your light reaches 10% farther | 15% | helm, amulet, cloak, chest |
| of Finishing | Blade | a chain's finisher hits 8% harder | 12% | melee, gloves, rings, off-hand |
| of Grace | Water | a perfect parry heals you 2 | 3 | gloves, rings, amulet, off-hand |
| of Cleansing | Water | poison wears off 10% sooner and burning floor hurts that much less | 15% | cloak, chest, amulet, boots |
| of Zeal | Fire | each kill gives back 3 stamina | 5 | melee, ranged, rings, helm |

Twenty-four traits, by sixes. Poison-and-fire protection from gear stops at 60% (`CAPS`).

The faint traits (Edges, Wind, Second Wind, Hunt, Kindling, Well, Mending, Quick Draw, Striding) were made noticeable; the caps below keep stacking in check. Ranged weapons can now carry fire, water, iron and blade traits too, so an enchanted pistol can fire every essence's shot.

**Caps** (`CAPS`): whatever you wear adds at most +25% strikes, +40 health, +30 stamina, +12% walk and +20% spells; the inventory says "(capped)" beside a number that has hit its cap. The gear card's comparison counts traits (green up arrows, red down arrows), and for weapons its "plays like" line names the traits it adds or loses ("hits harder, adds Embers").

**Named pieces** (`NAMED`) keep their own names. **Tower pieces** (Rare) are only found in their own tower; **Storied** pieces anywhere from floor 7:

| Piece | Base | Found | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Stormstring | shortbow | Gryphon's tower | a full draw also arcs to one more foe for half damage |
| Coral Trident | spear | Kraken's tower | hits slow foes by 25% for 1.5 s |
| Barnacle Mail | chain mail | Kraken's tower | foes that strike you up close take 5 |
| Cinder Axe | axe | Wyvern's tower | the finisher leaves the floor burning for 2 s (it burns foes, not you) |
| Wyvern Scaleplate | plate | Wyvern's tower | 32% off a blow with chain mail's weight |
| Stinger Dagger | dagger | Manticore's tower | every hit poisons the foe a little more |
| Sunsteel Blade | sword | Firebird's tower | kills heal you 4 |
| Thornmaul | war hammer | Chimera's tower | a staggering finisher throws thorns at foes nearby for 6 |
| Watcher's Helm | helm | Storied | foes you parry take 20% more from you for 3 s; +6 health |
| Quickhand Gloves | gloves | Storied | each kill fills your hand's mark by 5%; strikes +3% |
| Phantom Treads | boots | Storied | a dodge quickens your walk by 20% for 2 s; rolls keep you untouchable a moment longer |
| Emberkin Mantle | cloak | Storied | once a floor, falling below a fifth of your health heals 25 over 2 s |
| Heartstone | amulet | Storied | +16 health; 1 health back every 3 s while no monster has noticed you |
| Spellweave Ring | ring | Storied | every third spell you cast costs nothing |
| Reaver's Greatsword | greatsword | Storied | kills restore 4 health and 4 essence to your hand's mark |
| Bastion Plate | plate | Storied | once a floor, falling below 30% health raises a 30-point barrier for 6 s |
| Hollowmoon Bow | longbow | Storied | a full draw that hits pours 4 essence into your hand's mark |
| Rootbound Greaves | plated greaves | Storied | 5% damage taken off and +8 health; while you stand still, blows hurt 25% less |
| Wayfinder's Pendant | sunstone pendant | Storied | secret walls and hidden traps near you glint; your light reaches 15% farther |
| Duelist's Ring | silver ring | Storied | the parry window is 10% longer; every parry gives back 15 more stamina |
| Ashgrove Cane | walking cane | Storied | a perfect parry with it staggers every foe near you for a moment |
| Quietsteel | sword-cane | Storied | its drawn finisher poisons every foe it cuts |
| Lastbell Revolver | revolver | Storied | not enchanted: lead and a reload, but the last shot in the cylinder hits twice as hard |
| Emberlock | flintlock | Storied | fires fire shot, and a kill with it fills its chamber at once |

**Regarded pieces** (`NAMED` entries with a `beast`): each tower's beast has its own weapon or armour, and leaves it where it falls at the summit: always the first time you slay that beast, and a quarter of the time after that (`REGARDED_DROP`). It glints gold on the floor, with a "REGARDED:" notice, and if you take the stair without picking it up it comes down in your pack anyway. A Regarded piece carries its own rule and **four different traits**, never wears, is rarer than anything Storied (no monster, cache, camp or vault ever rolls one), and sells for the most (`RARITY_PRICE` x14). Its icon throws gold rays and two glints behind its glow.

| Piece | Base | Beast | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Stormtalon | longbow | the Gryphon | a full draw flies faster and hits 20% harder, and arcs on to two more foes for 60% |
| Deepcoil Coat | leather (chest) | the Kraken | 24% off a blow with leather's lightness; foes that strike you up close are gripped and slowed for 2 s; once a floor, falling below half health bursts a cloud of ink that hides you (monsters lose track of you) |
| Wyrmspine Mace | mace | the Wyvern | a heavy blow cracks the floor: the foes near the one you hit take 12 and stagger |
| Spinecaster | crossbow | the Manticore | each bolt looses two barbs beside it (half damage), and every barb poisons |
| Sunplume Mantle | cloak | the Firebird | once a climb, a blow that would fell you bursts you into flame and you rise with 40% of your health; poison and burning floor hurt 25% less |
| Triune Fang | greatsword | the Chimera | its strikes take turns between three heads: the lion's hits 30% harder, the goat's staggers, the serpent's poisons |

**Hunter's Keen Eyes** outline gear on the floor in its rarity's colour, even through the dark.

**Wear.** Only **Common** pieces wear, and pieces with traits never will. Each use takes a little condition off the pieces it uses: a landed swing wears your melee weapon and gloves, a shot your bow or crossbow, a blow that lands on you your chest, helm, legs and cloak, a roll or jump your boots (`WEAR` and `WEAR_BY` in `gear.gd`; a piece used all climb long gets badly worn near the end of it). At **25%** a piece is **badly worn**: its bonus is halved (a chest piece protects half as much; a weapon hits 25% softer), it shows cracks on its icon and its card says so. At **0** it **breaks** and its slot is empty: a broken weapon leaves you your fists until you find another. Each piece's condition shows as a thin bar under it in the inventory. Heirlooms start every climb in full repair.

**Melee weapons** are combo chains; every chain ends in a heavy finisher. Damage is a multiple of the base strike (8), as with the sword.

| Weapon | Class | What it's like | Hits | Stamina per chain | Reach |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fists | light | quick jabs on a cheap 4-hit chain ending in a heavy hook | 4 to 10 | 42 | 30 to 36 |
| Dagger | light | fast, short and cheap; a backstab deals 2x instead of 1.5x | 6 to 11 | 43 | 36 to 40 |
| Sword | medium | the all-rounder: 3 hits, a slow heavy finisher; a finisher that lands gives back 10 stamina | 8 to 14 | 58 | 46 to 56 |
| Axe | medium | slower and harder than the sword, with a wider hacking arc | 10 to 18 | 70 | 48 to 56 |
| Mace | medium | every blow counts as heavy against poise: it rocks monsters out of their wind-ups and cracks shells | 9 to 16 | 70 | 44 to 50 |
| Spear | medium | long, narrow thrusts: strike from beyond a monster's reach, but miss what's beside you | 8 to 13 | 54 | 74 to 86 |
| Greatsword | heavy | two-handed: slow, huge sweeping arcs that catch a crowd; hungry for stamina | 13 to 21 | 94 | 60 to 66 |
| War Hammer | heavy | the slowest and heaviest, 2 hits: every blow rocks, and the finisher staggers (half as long on a beast) | 16 to 24 | 74 | 52 to 56 |
| Walking Cane | light | quick, light blows; the hooked finisher staggers (a riposte's opening), and the parry window is 20% longer while it's in hand | 5 to 9 | 30 | 44 to 48 |
| Sword-Cane | light | two quick cane blows, then the blade comes out of the cane for a long, hard finisher (drawn with the empty cane in the other hand) | 5 to 17 | 38 | 44 to 60 |
| Rapier | light | long, slim, one-handed thrusts; while it's in hand ripostes and backstabs hit 40% harder (`riposte`) | 6 to 12 | 37 | 58 to 66 |
| Flail | medium | a spiked ball on a chain: its blows go through guards and shells (`through`), a knight's guard and a shellback's shell as if they weren't there | 9 to 15 | 66 | 50 to 56 |

**Ranged weapons:**

| Weapon | What it's like |
| --- | --- |
| Shortbow | hold to draw (0.75 s), release to loose: 5 to 20 damage; a full draw pierces. You walk at 45% while drawing. |
| Longbow | a slower draw (1.1 s) for harder, farther arrows (6 to 30 damage), but you barely walk while drawing (28%) |
| Crossbow | press to fire a fast, strong bolt at once (24 damage; it rocks what it hits), then a 1.1 s reload you can't skip. You can walk (a little slower) while it reloads; the slot and the phone's attack button fill back up. |
| Flintlock Pistol | one heavy shot (34 damage, short range): it rocks what it hits, throws it back harder than any bolt and kicks you back; then a long 2.2 s reload |
| Revolver | six quick shots (10 each, 0.2 s apart), then a 2 s reload of all six |
| Sling | drawn like a bow: hold to whirl (0.6 s), release to throw a stone (5 to 17 damage). A stone never pierces, but a full whirl rocks what it hits; light on stamina, and you walk at 70% while it whirls (`stone`) |

Eighteen weapons, by sixes, your fists among them. The rapier, the flail and the sling each get a first-time word from the Entity, and its "How do weapons and rarities work?" topic covers them.

**Pistols** (`kind: "gun"` in `weapons.gd`) go in the ranged slot. An ordinary pistol starts reloading by itself once it's empty; reloading is something you do, so **a roll breaks it off** (it starts again from the beginning after the roll), and so does putting the pistol away. You walk a little slower while reloading (`reload_move`), and the quick-draw traits and Archer's Bracers reload pistols faster. Lead shot that lands fills your hand's mark as arrows and bolts do. The HUD's weapon slot shades while it reloads and shows a pip for each shot (lit while loaded); on a phone the attack button reads "Revolver 4/6" and its ring fills as it reloads. The sounds are the bank's own, pitched: thunder and a crossbow's clack for the flintlock, a quick clack and a ring of steel for the revolver, powder poured and a ramrod's click, a ratchet and a lock's turn for reloading.

**Essence shot** (`ESSENCE_SHOT` in `weapons.gd`). An **enchanted** pistol (Fine or Rare, or an enchanted relic) fires its enchantment's essence instead of lead, and **never stops to reload**: each empty chamber fills itself after a moment (`refill`: 1.8 s for a flintlock, 0.5 s a chamber for a revolver), in hand or holstered, even through a roll. An essence shot doesn't fill your hand's mark (the essence is the pistol's, not yours). What it does follows the essence:

| Essence | Shot |
| --- | --- |
| Stone | rocks and staggers what it hits (half as long on a beast) |
| Blade | splits into three shards in a narrow fan, each half the damage |
| Iron | pierces every foe in its path |
| Air | flies 70% faster and half again as far; its chambers fill 30% sooner |
| Fire | bursts in flame over the foes around (60% of its damage) |
| Water | chills what it hits, slowing it for 2 s |

Weapon classes matter elsewhere too: Blade Forger's **Weapon Memory** throws more, smaller knives with a light weapon, fewer and harder blades with a heavy one, and fast darts with any bow or crossbow. Hunter's spells and Hawk's Eye work with every bow and the crossbow.

**Armor** cuts the damage of attacks that land (after parries, the Warden's barrier and the like); poison and burning floor tick past it. Heavier suits weigh on you: rolls cost more stamina and carry you less far, stamina refills slower, and you walk a little slower. Each suit shows on the climber, from a quilted vest to steel pauldrons and a helm.

| Armor | Damage taken off | Roll cost | Roll reach | Stamina refill | Walk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| None | 0% | normal | normal | normal | normal |
| Padded Armor | 8% | +5% | -2% | -2% | normal |
| Leather Armor | 15% | +12% | -5% | -5% | -2% |
| Chain Mail | 25% | +30% | -12% | -13% | -5% |
| Plate Armor | 35% | +55% | -24% | -25% | -10% |
| Warded Robe | 5% | +2% | -1% | normal | normal (and spells hit 6% harder: ARMOR `fx`) |
| Scale Mail | 30% | +42% | -18% | -19% | -8% (and light blows can't knock you back) |

**Heirlooms.** Survive a climb (beat its beast, or reach the Regard on the first climb and leave with your mark) and, after the victory screen, you choose **one** piece you wore to keep as an **heirloom**: a card for each slot you fill (any piece already kept is left out), and one to keep nothing new. Your **rings** share one card and are kept **together**. With many cards they sit in two rows. Every later climb starts with your heirlooms in their slots, in full repair. Each slot holds one heirloom (Rings holds both rings); keeping another piece for the same slot replaces it. The heirloom slots are **melee**, **ranged**, **chest**, **helm**, **gloves**, **legs**, **boots**, **cloak**, **amulet**, **rings**, **backpack**, **charm** and **off-hand**. There's one free heirloom per survived climb. The **inventory**'s Heirlooms view shows them on the same body layout, and the climber screen's Record tab lists them. Starting a new climber forgets them. Slots and text live in `HEIRLOOM_SLOTS` and `HEIRLOOM_TEXT` in `scripts/gear.gd`; heirlooms are saved with your profile as records.

The dev tools have a **Gear** row: a button for every weapon, armor and worn piece, and one to take off every piece, to try them at once; a **Finds** row drops a Fine, Rare or Storied find (or any named piece) at your feet, and an **Items** row puts any item on your belt.

## Items and camps

**Items** (`scripts/items.gd`, where their names, text and numbers live) are carried on your **belt** through the climb. The belt holds **4 kinds** of item, up to **3 of each** (a Climber's Pack: 4 of each; a Belt Pouch, a Climber's Pack or a Belt Hook charm: a fifth kind; the recall scroll is always one). Walk onto a new kind with a full belt and the **take card** opens (the monsters wait): swap one stack out (it's left where you stand) or leave the new item on the floor; either way it waits until you step off and back on. A camp's or vault's item with a full belt is left at your feet. **G** (or the Item button, X on a controller) uses your **current item** and **T** (or the small arrow, the d-pad down) picks the next. **Hold** G, the Item button, X or the d-pad down to open the **belt wheel** (see **The belt wheel** below): **choosing is never using**. A quick tap never opens it. The HUD shows the current item beside the weapon, with its count; the inventory (I) shows the belt, with an empty cell for each kind it has room for. An item that would do nothing (a draught at full health, a vial with full marks) isn't used up.

| Item | Essence | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Healing Vial | Stone | a staple: heals 40 health over 1.5 seconds |
| Stamina Vial | Iron | a staple: shakes off all **fatigue** (max stamina whole again), fills your stamina, and stamina refills 40% faster for 5 seconds; usable even with the bar full while fatigue is on you |
| Cleansing Tonic | Water | cures every state on you (poison, burning, bleeding, soaked, charged), heals every burned body part, and a little health |
| Essence Vial | Water | a staple: fills every mark with essence |
| Smoke Bomb | Fire | a cloud of smoke around you: monsters inside lose track of you (beasts don't) |
| Fire Flask | Fire | thrown where you aim (it stops short of walls): bursts for 30 (+10% a floor) and leaves the floor burning for 3 s (it burns foes, not you) |
| Edge Oil | Blade | for 20 s strikes hit 25% harder and land like heavy blows on shells and guards (a shellback's shell cracks, a knight is rocked) |
| Warding Oil | Iron | a 25-point barrier for 15 s; traps can't hurt you while it holds, and it puts out burning and keeps it off you |
| Haste Draught | Air | walk 25% faster and rolls cost 30% less for 10 s |
| Stoneskin Draught | Stone | 20% damage taken off (it counts toward the 50% cap) and no knockback for 12 s; stone grounds you: it clears a charge and none holds |
| Second Wind Salts | Iron | stamina full at once; for 8 s it never waits after you act and refills 50% faster |
| Flare Powder | Air | lights the room for 20 s; hidden traps and secret walls nearby glint |
| Antivenom | Stone | cures poison, and no poison takes hold of you for 30 s |
| Heartroot | Water (Life) | heals 20 and stops bleeding, and for 30 s every kill heals you 4 more |
| Throwing Net | Water | thrown where you aim: foes it lands on are tangled for a moment and slowed for 4 s; a flier (a bat, a harrier, a wisp, an ashwing...) is brought down and held 2.5 s (a beast only slowed, half as long) |
| Lodestone Bomb | Iron | thrown where you aim: drags the foes around the burst together, with a jolt of 8 (a beast barely moves) |
| Briar Tonic | Blade | for 25 s, foes that strike you up close take 8, and no bleeding takes hold of you (it stops a bleed, too) |
| Lantern Oil | Fire | your light reaches 60% farther for two minutes |
| Ash of Unmaking | Fire (Unmaking) | every foe within reach loses an eighth of its health (a beast 3%) |
| Gale Feather | Air | a gust hurls the foes around you back (a beast only staggers a step) |
| Scroll of Seeing | Air | for a minute every hidden trap and secret wall on the floor glints, wherever it is |
| Thunder Jar | Air | thrown where you aim: a crack of lightning for 14 (+8% a floor), and the foes it catches are stunned for 0.9 s (a beast only jolted) |
| Springheel Draught | Iron | for 20 s jumps cost no stamina and carry 40% farther, and rolls carry 15% farther |
| Stillwater Draught | Water | for 20 s the parry window is 40% longer, and every perfect parry heals you 4 |
| Recall Scroll | Air and Water | from vaults, and always on the train's store shelf for 60 crystal essence of any kind; one at most: read it to leave the tower safely, keeping everything (see **Sealed wings, vaults and secrets**) |

**Staples and the rest** (the owner: "Still lots of consumables. Probably should get rid of minor to greater potions and just go with regular health, stamina and essence, as well as the other utility items"). Three items are **staples** (`staple` in `items.gd`): the **Healing Vial**, the **Stamina Vial** and the **Essence Vial** (it fills your marks, so a bondless climber never finds one). They're cheap at the store (3, 3 and 4: `SUPPLY_PRICE` in `train.gd`, half their small worth), every climb starts with a Healing and a Stamina Vial (`START`), and they're nearly everything slain monsters leave. **Everything else is rare and meaningful**: oils, nets, smoke, flasks, tonics, salts, flares, scrolls and the rest turn up only a few times in a whole climb, so each is worth keeping for its moment; the outfitter's shelf is where you choose them on purpose, at their full worth (10 to 20). Slain monsters (not beasts) leave an item 9% of the time (`DROP_CHANCE`), almost always a staple (a rarer item's `drop_weight` counts only 0.3 beside a staple's: `DROPS` "rare_weight"); walk over it to pick it up. Camps, caches, vaults and fallen climbers never hold a staple. The two items of the secret essences (Heartroot and Ash of Unmaking) are never dropped by monsters (except Heartroot in the Chimera's tower, where it's handy); camps and vaults offer them.

**Far fewer finds, more essence.** Counted over simulated climbs of every tower (floors 1 to 11, each kill, cache and fallen climber's camp rolled as the game rolls it, `tests/parts/consumables.gd`): a climb used to find about **10 rarer items** outright (3.5 from kills, 4.3 from caches, 2.5 from fallen climbers) plus four camp item cards; now it's about **3.5** (1.1 from kills, 1.3 from caches, 1.1 from fallen climbers) plus one item card at each of the two camps, so a typical climb ends up with roughly 3 to 6 of them. Staples stay common: about 6 to 7 a climb from kills (Healing 3.4, Stamina 2.6, Vials 0.9; it was 5 Healing and Stamina). In their place you find essence: a kill that leaves no item leaves a little more of the tower's essence 8% of the time (`DROPS` "essence_chance", 1.5 plus 0.2 a floor), a cache holds an item only 4% of the time and one without holds a quarter more essence (`room_rewards.gd` `RULES` "item_chance", "no_item"), a fallen climber's pack holds an item one time in five and a small cache of essence otherwise (`DROPS` "fallen_item"), and a camp offers **one** item card beside a crystal of the tower's essence and a smaller crystal of another kind (`CAMP_CHOICES`). Tinctures stay rare finds (`essence_flow.gd`). The numbers are all in the Data editor.

**One size.** Every item comes in one size (the old Minor, standard and Greater sizes were taken out at the owner's request): a stack is simply how many you carry, its icon and name are always the same, and what you find, buy and brew are the same item. An old save loads its sized items as the plain item, keeping the count (packed supplies, the shelf's old "draught@3" keys and the Pedlar's wares; the old size notes are dropped).

**The belt's four kinds stay.** With three staples, a marked climber who carries all of them has one kind left for a rarer find, so a rare item is a choice to make room for (the take card asks which stack to swap out); a Belt Pouch, a Climber's Pack or a Belt Hook charm adds a fifth kind for climbers who want more room. Fewer rarer items made a fifth kind unnecessary, and three kinds would leave no room for them at all.

**States on you.** Monsters' blows that land (never ones you parry or roll through) can leave a state on you, as your spells do on them (`reactions.gd` `CLIMBER`, on): **poisoned** (scorplings, the Manticore, bloomcaps: a little health every half second for 4 s, and it can kill), **burning** (cinders, ashwings, the Wyvern, the Firebird, a forge's full heat: 1.5 health every half second for 3 s, never your last point), **bleeding** (thornhounds, mongrels, briars: as burning, 4 s), **soaked** (lurkers, the Kraken: you walk 20% slower for 5 s) and **charged** (thunderlings, the Gryphon: stamina refills at half speed for 4 s). A new blow refreshes a state but never makes it longer, nothing stuns you, and two of your states meeting (soaked and charged: Conduct) cost 6 health. A line at the top of the screen (under the banner, with the beast's bar) shows each state with its sign (a drop, a flame, a wave, a bolt), its seconds left, a gauge running down, and what cures it. A **Cleansing Tonic** cures them all; **Antivenom** (poison), **Warding Oil** (burning), **Briar Tonic** (bleeding) and **Stoneskin** (charged) each cure one and keep it off while they last; **Heartroot** stops bleeding. The rest at a stair (and a camp) clears everything. Each item's `cures` and `ward` in `items.gd` say what it does; the HUD and the Entity read them. The Bestiary says what each monster leaves on you and what cures it, and the Entity explains each state the first time it lands ("What are states, and how do I cure them?" asks it again).

**What each tower asks for** (`scripts/tower_needs.gd`, words and numbers in data). Each tower's monsters and floors make one or two items clearly useful there:

| Tower | Handy | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gryphon | Throwing Net, Stoneskin Draught | bats and harriers fly, and a net brings one down; the wind and harriers shove you toward the drops and thunderlings charge you, and stone keeps your feet and grounds the charge |
| Kraken | Lantern Oil, Edge Oil | from floor 4 the flooded halls are **dark** (your light reaches 70% as far: `DARK`), and Lantern Oil carries it farther than ever; shellbacks turn light blows, and oiled strikes crack their shells |
| Wyvern | Warding Oil, Edge Oil | cinders and the Wyvern set you burning, and Warding Oil puts it out and keeps it off; knights shrug off light blows, and oiled strikes rock them |
| Manticore | Antivenom, Cleansing Tonic | scorplings and the Manticore poison you |
| Firebird | Throwing Net, Cleansing Tonic | wisps and ashwings fly over the abyss; ashwings burn and bloomcaps poison |
| Chimera | Briar Tonic, Heartroot | packs of thornhounds and mongrels leave you bleeding |

A tower's handy items turn up three times as often there (`DROPS` "handy_weight", in drops, caches and camps), its arrival banner says "Handy here: ...", the first dark floor says so, the outfitter's shelf says where each item is handy (the tower chosen at the engine first), and the Entity explains it ("What's handy in this tower?", under "How do things work here?" and "What is the tower like?"). No item was cut: each now has a use somewhere (Second Wind Salts stay as the one that keeps stamina from ever waiting). Handy items are still rare: three times as likely as another rarer item, within the lower totals above. Every item is also on the train's store shelf and brewed at the bench. Items that work for a while (Antivenom, Heartroot, Briar Tonic, Lantern Oil, the Scroll of Seeing; the Wardbreaker is a charm now, see **Charms**) add to the same numbers your gear does (`buff` in `items.gd`); using another refreshes it.

**The belt wheel: hold to choose, tap to use** (`scripts/wheel.gd`, drawn by `hud.gd`, run by `player.gd`). Choosing an item is never using it.

- **Keys and mouse:** hold **G** (or **T**) and the wheel opens around the climber while the game slows to a quarter speed. Move the mouse toward an item (or roll the mouse wheel, or press its number, **1** to **9**) and let go of G, or click: it becomes your current item. Tap **G** to use it.
- **Controller:** hold **X / Square** (or the d-pad down), point with the **right stick**, let go. Tap X to use.
- **Phone:** hold the **Item** button; the wheel opens beside your thumb. Slide your finger onto an item and lift it to choose; a quick tap of Item uses the current item. The joystick still walks you (slowly) while the wheel is open.
- The wheel shows every kind on your belt with its icon, name and count; the current one wears a **gilt pin**, the pointed one is lit and named under the wheel with what it does, and a line under that says what letting go will do. A pause, a menu, a card screen or a fall closes it, using nothing. With one kind on the belt there is no wheel.
- A mark's button works the same way: hold it for the **spell wheel**, let go to put the pointed spell on the button (it isn't cast), tap to cast. The secret bonds' **tool ring** chooses on release too (see below).
- **Arrange mode** lists it as **Item and spell wheel**: move or resize it and it opens there instead. The Entity explains it the first time your belt holds two kinds (and in its talk: How do things work here? → How does the belt wheel work?). Its speed, the hold time and its words are in the Data editor ("The wheels"). Tests: `tests/parts/wheel.gd`.

**The belt at the end of a climb.** Survive a climb (beat its beast, reach the Regard without falling, or read a recall scroll) and whatever is left on your belt breaks into **essence crystals** at a quarter of its worth (it was half), one crystal per essence (`value` and `BREAK_SHARE` in `items.gd`); the victory screen says what it became. Fall and it's lost.

**Bonds and items:**

- **Ashwalker:** Smoke Bombs last twice as long.
- **Flameblade:** Edge Oil builds heat twice as fast.
- **Flame Contractor:** Fire Flasks burst 50% wider (and a foe they kill leaves Kindled ground, as with your other fire).
- **Hunter:** Keen Eyes outline dropped gear in its rarity's colour; Flare Powder lasts twice as long.

**Camps.** Three camps on every climb, the first climb's too: the stairs of floors 3, 6 and 9 lead first to a **camp** (`CAMP_AFTER` in `items.gd`): the open room with a campfire and no monsters. You rest fully (all health and stamina, fatigue, burns, poison and every state healed), then choose one of **three cards** (`CAMP_CARDS`; keys 1 to 3, the arrows and Enter, the d-pad and A, click, or tap): one item (the tower's handy items likelier), a crystal of the tower's essence, or a piece of gear (Fine or better at the floor 6 camp, Rare or better at the floor 9 one; taking it leaves your old piece by the fire). Then take the stair on to the next floor (a camp isn't a floor: after floor 3's camp comes floor 4). Each camp comes once a climb.

- **Surgeon's Steady Hands:** healing items heal 30% more and work faster.
- **Rune Artist's Runesmith:** after taking an item at a camp, a Rune Artist also chooses a rune for their gear (Sharpness, Warding or Swiftness, as with Inscribe) that lasts the rest of the climb, the beast included, or crafts one more item. Casting Inscribe still changes the rune for one floor.

## The train (between climbs)

After the Regard, every climb ends on **the train**, a steampunk train waiting outside the towers, whether you won, fell or read a recall scroll, and every climb starts from it (the first climb starts with no mark and no train: the train appears once the Regard has marked you). **The train's cars are the menu:** you see the inside of one car at a time, filling the screen, and move along the train from car to car. Everything is drawn in code: wood panelling and buttoned velvet, brass rails and rivets, wall lamps that glow, arched windows on the dusk with the six towers on the horizon (each with a window lit in its essence's colour), and each car's furniture holding its things, which you tap (or focus and press) directly. The words, prices and numbers live in `scripts/train.gd`.

**Moving along: the rail.** On the train the menu's **rail** (`scripts/rail.gd`, see Menus below) is always down the left, and it is the train's only menu, in the owner's order from top to bottom: **who you are** (your name and your six marks; it's a button: click, tap or press it and the climber screen opens beside the rail, and it's the first stop for the keys and the controller), then the seven cars front to back, the **Engine**, the **Outfitter's Car**, **Your Car**, the **Workshop**, the **Dining Car**, the **Library** and the **Settings & Controls Car** (each with its own icon drawn in code: a locomotive, a pair of scales, a bed, an anvil and hammer, a steaming cup, a bound book, a signal lever; and on a computer a line under it), then under a thin rule **Dev Tools** (a page beside the rail, not a car), and at its foot **Exit to Title Station** and, on a computer, **Quit game** (a browser or a phone can't quit a game, so there it's left out: Exit to Title Station is the way out). **Inventory** isn't on the rail any more: it's the Inventory button in Your Car, or **I**. **Start over** isn't either: it's in the Settings & Controls Car, set apart as the emergency brake (see below). The cars fill the rest of the screen, and the train opens on your car. Choose a car on the rail and the train slides there; swipe left or right on a phone (the train follows your finger and settles on the nearest car; a quick flick goes on to the next), drag it or turn the mouse wheel on a computer, or press **Q** / **E** (**LB** / **RB**): the rail's gilt light always follows the car you're in, and moves while you swipe. Choose the climber screen or Dev Tools and it opens beside the same rail (the rail never changes; a dim gilt mark keeps your car); **Close**, **Esc**, **B** or choosing a car returns to the car you were on. **Keys and controller:** the train starts with the focus on the rail; **Up** / **Down** move along it, sliding to each car or showing each page as you pass; **Right**, **Enter** or **A** goes into the car (onto its starting point); **Left** past the first thing in a car, or **Esc** / **B**, comes back to the rail on that car. On the rail Esc and B do nothing more (only the engine's lever leaves the train). On a phone the rail narrows to icons with short names (**Engine**, **Outfitter**, **Your Car**, **Workshop**, **Library**, **Settings**, **Dev**, **Station**), and the cars' rows share the height so everything fits an 800 x 400 screen without scrolling. Your **crystals** (a gem and how much essence you hold of each kind) run along the bottom of the cars on a brass strip. A choice's card (its stats set against yours, the price as essence gems, "needs N more X", and its buttons) is a brass-framed placard in the car, beside the furniture. The Entity's tips appear at the top of each car the first time you're there.

**The train on end (a mock-up in Dev Tools).** Dev Tools has **Train rail: Menu / Train on end** (Menu at first; kept with the settings). Train on end draws the same rail, with the same items, order and behaviour, as the train itself standing on end: the station's sign at the top (your name and marks: still the button for the climber screen), the engine under it with its headlamp, red cowcatcher, boiler, smokestack, dome, driving wheels and cab, then each car below it in its room's colour (lit arched windows, a gilt-lined panel, its icon and name on its side, wheels on a track down the left edge), joined by couplings, and under the last car a platform whose enamel signs are Dev Tools, Exit to Title Station and Quit game (on a phone, side by side). The car you're in is ringed in gilt and its windows glow brighter. It fits a computer's screen well; on a phone (800 x 400) it fits too, but the cars are short and stubby.

- **The engine** (at the front; `scripts/engine_page.gd`): the cab, with copper pipes, gauges and the firebox glowing. Its board holds the **tower map**: the six towers on the loop the train rides, each plaque with the tower, the city of the station near it, and the fare there (a small train and "You're here" on the station you're at, a padlock while travel is locked). The tower by your station is chosen at first; its placard shows what it's like, what goes with you (your pack and packed supplies) and what a fall costs, and its **lever** departs. Choose another tower and the placard shows the ride instead (see **Six stations and travel**). **Step off** (top right) gets off at the station you're at.
- **Your car:** what you'll wear on a walnut **wardrobe** (laid out like the inventory's paper doll; an heirloom has a gold gem, a piece you chose to wear a **Set** tag, a pack piece that will go on a **Pack** tag), your **pack** (a leather pack with its pockets) and your **stash** (the first two rows of the Stash tab's own grid, in its order, and **Open the stash**, which says how many more pieces there are). Every pack and stash piece has a small arrow: green and up if it beats what you'd wear in its slot, red and down if it's worse. Choose a piece for its card: **Wear it**, move it between pack and stash, or sell it. Buttons below open the **climber screen**, the **Loadout** and **Appearance** (how you look); closing any of them brings you back to your car.
- **The Loadout** (`scripts/loadout_page.gd`; the Loadout button in your car, or I's inventory on the train): the climber drawn large in what you'll wear, with a small picture of how you look in the tower, beside a paper doll of every slot; under them your **pack** ("Pack · 2 of 4", and a line saying it goes up the tower), your **belt** (supplies packed for the climb; put one back from its card) and the first row of your **stash** (the pieces better than what you wear first), with **Open the stash**. Choose a slot: its card says where the piece comes from (your heirloom, chosen to wear, or a pack piece that goes on because nothing else is worn there), **Take it off: into your pack / stash**, and **Wear instead:** every piece in your pack and stash that fits, best first, each with its arrow. Choose a pack or stash piece: set against what you'd wear (each number green and up where it's better, red and down where it's worse, and how it plays), **Wear it** (for rings: in place of either ring), **Into the stash / Into your pack** (or press it a second time), **Sell**. **The rules** (`train.gd`: `equip`, `unequip`, `dressed`, `setoff`, `pack_up`): what you choose to wear (`profile.wear`, saved with the profile; a new climber's holds the basic sword and bow) goes on as you depart, over your heirloom in that slot; an heirloom you don't wear waits on the train this climb, still yours (never copied into the pack). A piece you swap out goes back where the new one came from. A pack piece still goes on in a slot nothing else is worn in, or that holds only the basic sword or bow (which then waits in your stash, if it has room). A Climber's Pack you wear adds its room at once, and can't come off (or be swapped) while the pack holds more than it would. The climber on the train already wears the Loadout, so the climber screen's figure and numbers show it. **What goes up the tower:** what you wear, your pack and your belt; the stash never leaves the train.
- **The stash** (`scripts/stash_page.gd`; the Loadout's second tab, Q / E or LB / RB): every stash piece in a grid, **40 spaces** (`CHEST_SIZE`, in the Data editor's numbers; it held 8 before, and old saves load as they were), the empty spaces after them so you see the room left, your vessels, and your pack beside it. **Sort** by newest, rarity, slot, essence or price, and filter by **slot** and by **essence** (option rows: Left / Right, a click or tap on either half; remembered while the game runs). The same card as the Loadout's: wear it, move it across in one press (the card's button, or press the piece again), or sell it.
- **One stash, one look** (`scripts/storage_view.gd`): there is one stash (`profile.chest`) and one pack, and every place that shows them builds them through `StorageView.build()`: the Stash tab, the Loadout, your car, the outfitter's Sell section, the workshop bench's pickers and the inventory's Pack view in the tower. So they share one heading ("Stash · 12 of 40", "Pack · 2 of 4"), one slot size (`cell()`), one grid (`columns()`: up to 8 across, 6 on a phone, fewer where a car has less room), the same empty spaces, rarity edges, wear bars and better/worse arrows, the same card for a chosen piece (`loadout_page.gd piece_card`: wear it, move it, sell it) and the same second press to move a piece across. A place short of room shows the grid's first rows (`rows`) and **Open the stash**; a place that offers only some pieces (selling, a bench counter) passes which. The words are in `StorageView.TEXT`, and everywhere a player reads it the stash is called the stash.
- **The Outfitter's Car** (the store; `scripts/store_page.gd` builds it), kept by **Joel, the outfitter**: a retired climber who buys what climbers bring down and sells it to the next ones up (he/him; his lines are in `OUTFITTER` in `train.gd`). His name is in one place, `scripts/outfitter_name.gd`: every line that names him (the Entity's tips, topics and hover help, his tag over his words, the tour) writes `{outfitter}`, filled in as each table is made, so changing `NAME` there renames him everywhere. It is **one page**: everything he sells and buys this visit shows at once, with **no filter and no pages**, in four sections from top to bottom (see **The Outfitter's Car, section by section** below): **Consumables**, **Weapons & armour** (with **Today's piece** in its glass case), **Charms** and **Sell**. Beside them stands **Joel** over **his upgrade tree** (see **The outfitter's upgrade tree** below), and on the right the brass **placard** shows the card of whatever you choose. Choose anything (click it, tap it, or move onto it with the keys or a controller) and its card shows; the card's buttons buy it or sell it, exactly as before.
- **The workshop car: the bench** (`scripts/bench.gd`, where its recipes, costs and words live). The bench's corner (the workbench, tools, a shelf of bottles and the Rune Artist's slate, whose runes glow only when you carry that mark) over a cabinet of labelled drawers, one for each counter (two columns on a phone; the one in use lit), what the chosen counter can work on in the middle, and the placard with the cost as essence gems and "needs N more X" when you're short. A choice that can't be undone asks first: tap the same button again. It works on your heirlooms and the pieces in your pack and chest.
  - **The Rune Artist** (`bench.gd ARTIST` and `PREMIUM`; drawn in code by `train_view.gd RuneArtist`): someone who works the bench, standing over the middle column in an indigo robe trimmed in rune-light, a leather apron, round spectacles and a raised stylus whose tip glows, with a speech bubble beside them. Their name is a placeholder in data, **"(The Rune Artist)"**, with a greeting (another line for a climber who carries the mark) and a few more lines: press them (click, tap, Enter or A) and they say the next. Hovering them, or holding a finger on them, the Entity says who they are. **Every crafted counter is open to every climber:** with the Rune Artist's mark you craft it yourself, with your own essence, for the materials alone (and brew for 20% less, as before); without it the Rune Artist crafts it for you, for **the same materials plus their premium: half the materials again** (`PREMIUM["share"]` 0.5, rounded up, at least 1 of each kind), or, for working a full vessel into a piece (no crystals of its own), **a fee of 10 crystals of the piece's kind** (`PREMIUM["work"]`).
