Every crafted card shows the whole price and a line saying who crafts: "You craft it with your own essence: 20 Blade." or "(The Rune Artist) crafts it for you: 20 Blade materials + 10 Blade premium."; each counter's hover help says it too. The Entity introduces them the first time you're in the Workshop (its "rune_artist" tip), and "How do things work here?" has "The Workshop and its Rune Artist?".
  - **Brew** (anyone): a consumable packed onto your belt for your next climb, a little cheaper than the store (`BREW`; a Healing Vial is 2 Stone, an Essence Vial 3 Water). Put one back for what you paid.
  - **Break down** (anyone): a piece from your pack or chest, or a packed item, becomes a crystal of its kind at 40% of its price (the store's Sell).
  - **Tinctures** (anyone; its own counter, `bench.gd`'s tincture section): craft a tincture of any kind from 40 crystals of that kind (a Rune Artist pays 20% less), packed for your next climb; **Drink one** opens Crystals and tinctures on it.
  - **Crafted** (by you with the Rune Artist's mark, else by the Rune Artist for the materials + premium; the prices below are the materials): **Temper** raises a piece's rank to +1, +2, +3 for 20 / 45 / 80 of its kind (x1.5 Rare, x2 Storied); each rank makes its traits 25% stronger (of their base) and a weapon hit 4% harder, and +2 / +3 also need 100 / 300 density of that kind. **Retrait** swaps one trait for a random one of the essence you pay (15, x2 on Rare). **Join** turns two Fine pieces for the same slot, a trait on each, into one Rare with both traits (30). **Inscribe** puts a lasting rune on a piece for 25 Iron: Sharpness (+10% strikes), Warding (+8% protection) or Swiftness (+5% walk), half the Inscribe spell's strength; it's drawn on the piece's icon as a rune hashed from its id (strokes on a 3 by 3 grid). **Recall scroll**: bind one for 30 Air and 30 Water (you carry one). **Vessel**: brew a vessel, or work a full one into a piece (+4, or a deep rune; see **The vessel**). A climber with the Rune Artist's mark also brews everything else 20% cheaper.
  - **Charms** (anyone): your charms that hold a charge (the one you'll wear, your heirloom, your pack and stash), each with what refilling it costs: **Refill** puts its spent charges back for crystals of its kind. Under them, the charms the bench can **craft** (`charms.gd CRAFT`; crafted, as above), each with its whole price: **Craft it** makes one into your stash.
  - A tempered piece shows its rank in its name ("+2") and as gold pips on its icon. Only Common pieces wear; anything with a trait never does.
- **The Library**: the Entity's **Archive** as a car. Its walls are shelves of books round the windows, with a ladder on a brass rail and a globe. A walnut **bookcase** holds a bound volume for each of the Archive's subjects (First steps, Essence and marks, Secret essences, Secret bond tools, The Unbound, The tower, Puzzles and traps, Gear and items, The train, Screens; each with how many you've heard) and, once you've found a Maker's fragment or met one, the Makers' **forbidden shelf** bound in black. Choose a volume and it lies open on the **lectern** beside the bookcase, under its green reading lamp: its subjects (those you've met as buttons, the rest **???**), and choosing one shows the Entity's line and the fuller account (on the forbidden shelf: each Maker's fragments and meetings). **Replay the Entity's tips** and **Guide tips** are on the lectern too. The page scrolls inside the lectern when it's long. In a climb, **Library** on the pause rail shows the whole Archive on one page.
- **The Settings & Controls Car**: a signal room, painted teal boards with a frame of tall levers (red, yellow, blue and black handles, some pulled over), a ticking clock and a block instrument between the windows, a brass key-plate and a checked floor. Its brass **signal panel** has two tabs, **Settings** (music, sounds, screen shake, the controls list, Interface size, World zoom, HUD size, Lighting, the Entity's tips and Replay, Controller setup: the same rows as the Settings & Controls page, saved the same way) and **Controls** (every key and button), scrolling inside the panel on a phone. Beside it, apart from everything else, the red **emergency brake** plate holds **Start over this climber**: it asks first ("Start over" and Cancel, the focus on Cancel), and the plate says what it does. (The owner's list for the rail had no Start over, so it lives here, where the other settings are, rather than one press from the cars.)

**Money.** Essence crystals are the only currency, and you hold **one crystal of each kind**, which grows as you add to it (old saves' piles combine as they load). They come from:

- **Condensing at every stair**: the crystals' part of the split (see **Marks, essence and the Regard**). **8%** of the essence you bank (`SPLIT["crystals"]` in `essence_flow.gd`, a fixed share in the Data editor) condenses into crystals: per kind, in proportion, whole essence only (a kind with too little for one whole essence goes to your body and marks in full). Your body and your marks share the rest, and only the rest feeds a secret bond or pools raw in an Unbound's parts. It works from the very first climb, so a new climber has money at the first train. The stair's line says it: "Banked 50: 20 to your body, 26 to your marks, 4 into crystals". What your marks or an Unbound's Nodes can't take at a stair (use already refilled, a Node full) only partly condenses: **a tenth** of it (`OVERFLOW` in `essence_flow.gd`; it was a fifth), the rest going back to the well; a crystal you eat is never lost (what can't go in stays a crystal). (The share was once a setting, **To crystals**: the owner fixed it, and a saved value is ignored. With it the monsters' essence, caches, puzzles, tasks and the like dropped to seven eighths of what they were, so what flows into you is what it was at the old 30% and the pace holds, while crystals come about 40% slower: an early Unbound who seldom cast once turned nearly everything banked into crystals.) **The essence a floor** then matched the pace model only for a light player: measured in real, thorough play (the floor bot clearing floors 1 to 7 of three towers the way the owner plays: the task, every monster in reach, every chest, cache and cracked wall: `tests/essence_measure.gd -- thorough`), a floor gave about 1.6 times what the model counts (about 9 kills a floor, not 6.5; chests, cracked walls, caches, puzzles and the task were half of it). So the monsters' drops were halved (the guardians' kept: `DROPS` in `essence.gd`), the tower's bonus a kill went from 1.3 to 0.75, a kill's extra essence to 1.5 plus 0.2 a floor, chests to about three fifths and cracked walls' caches, good rooms' caches, puzzles, the task's reward and scenario grades to about three quarters; a kill's tincture chance went from 1.2% to 1% (more kills). Monsters spawned as **adds** pay a share (`ADDS` in `essence.gd`): a nest's hatchlings, a beast's called help, a plate room's slimes and a wardstone's waves nothing, the guards a wrong answer or failed puzzle sends nothing, a horde a quarter, the Proving's sentinel and its adds twice a kill (what they paid before). A thorough floor then gave what the model counts (`tests/parts/essence_pace.gd` holds the tables to it). **Still too many crystals** (the owner walked out of a climb with 150 to 300, light play or thorough, with little to spend them on): the share went from **20% to 8%**, and every essence find (the monsters' drops, the guardians' too, the tower's bonus a kill, now 0.65, a kill's extra essence, chests, cracked walls' and good rooms' caches, puzzles, the task, scenario grades, a rival's hoard, and the trader's prices with them) to **87%** of what it was, so what flows into your body and marks (92% of 87%) is what it was at 20% (99% of it, counted through the game's own split: never more) and every pace holds. A thorough floor now gives about 7 on floor 1, 45 to 80 on floors 2 to 5, 95 on floor 7. The big non-stair sources were halved too: vault crystals (`REWARD` in `wings.gd`, now 8 + 2 a floor, and with them the camp's crystal card, the Proving sentinel's crystal, secret rooms' and pockets' crystals and a mirror image's prize), the outfitter's pay a floor, the belt at a climb's end (`BREAK_SHARE`, a quarter of its worth) and a rival's duel and gift. **A whole climb** (`tests/parts/essence_pace.gd` counts every source): a thorough summit leaves about **125 crystals** (64 from the stairs, a camp card 12, a vault 18, the sentinel 10, the pay 12, the belt 7; about 330 before), a thorough climb that falls on floor 8 about 75 (180 before), a light summit about 60 (160 before) and a light fall on 8 about 35 (90 before), marked or Unbound alike. A climber who never casts keeps more (what their marks or Nodes can't take): about 165 to 200 a thorough summit.
- **The outfitter's pay for every climb**, however it ends (a fall too, the summit, a recall scroll, the first climb's Regard): crystals of the tower's kind, **1 for every floor reached** (it was 2) and **3 more for every floor past your best in that tower** before the climb (`CLIMB_PAY`). The fall screen, the victory or recall screen and the Regard say it in gold ("Joel pays for your climb: +24 Water"), and his greeting in the store car mentions it until you depart. A fall on floor 6 pays 6, or 24 if it's a new best; a summit pays 12, or up to 48 the first time.
- Selling and breaking down, vault and secret-room prizes, champions and mirror images, a recall scroll (your unbanked essence) and the belt at the end of a survived climb.

Every price is in one kind of essence (a consumable's own kind; a piece's trait or named kind, else its slot's: chest and helm Stone, gloves and melee Blade, legs, ranged and backpack Iron, boots and cloak Air, amulets Fire, rings Water), shown as a gem and a number, red when you can't pay; the card says why in words ("needs 12 more Fire"). The one exception is the recall scroll, which takes any kind. Prices: a piece's `BASE_PRICE` (8 to 20) times its rarity's `RARITY_PRICE`: **Common x0.5** (half what they first were, 4 to 10), Fine x2, Rare x4, Storied x8, Regarded x14 (so a Fine piece costs 4x its Common, the finds you sell keep their worth, and a beast's own piece sells for the most). **The staples** (Healing Vial 3 Stone, Stamina Vial 3 Iron: `SUPPLY_PRICE`) cost half their worth and come first on the shelf; everything else costs its worth in `items.gd` (10 to 20). Selling gives 40% of the price (at least 1), so a Common sells for 2 to 4. In the **Inventory**, the Essence crystals row shows how much each kind holds; **Eat 10** pours up to 10 of it into you at a time, the natural way (`CRYSTAL["step"]` in `items.gd`). The Entity explains the train, the store, the chest, the bench and enchanting the first time you see each. The stash (`chest` in the save), pack, the Loadout (`wear`), packed supplies, the store's stock and the last climb's pay are saved with your profile (the `train` section; old saves load with an empty stash and nothing chosen to wear).

**What a climb earns** (at the fixed 8%, a thorough player's floors: the pace model's): floors 1 to 5 give about 7, 45, 63, 70 and 80 essence, so a fall on floor 6 banks about 265, condenses about 17 (most of it the tower's kind) and is paid 6 (24 on a new best); a camp's crystal card (floor 3's: 8) and a vault's crystal (floor 5's: 18) bring it to about 30 to 50 crystal essence, enough for a few Common pieces and supplies in the tower's kind. A summit gathers about 1000, condenses about 65 and with the pay, a camp card, a vault, the sentinel and the belt leaves about 125 (plus a tenth of what your marks or Nodes couldn't take); a light player's summit about 60. Slow growth: a tincture (40 crystals) is a real choice.

**Falling, and lost things.** Fall in a tower (after the Regard; a fall on the first climb before floor 6 brings the Regard instead, with a faint mark) and the fall screen shows for a moment (what you lost, and "Back to the train in 3"), then you're back on the train, on your own car, ready for the next climb: on its own after 3 seconds (`FALL_WAIT` in `main.gd`), or at once on a tap, Enter, Space, a click, R or the controller's A or View. Your **pack**, what you wear and your **belt** are lost (the belt's items don't break into crystals on a fall; a **Keeper's Purse** charm keeps itself and the first piece in your pack, which come home to the train), and there's a **35%** chance the **weapon in your hand** goes too (your melee weapon if your fists were out; never the basic sword or bow, which aren't remembered for a cache either). **Heirlooms are never lost**: an heirloom in your hand stays yours.

**The basic sword and bow, a gift** (`scripts/basic_gift.gd`, tested by `tests/parts/basic_gift.gd`). A new climber (and Start over) starts with a plain Sword and Shortbow in any weapon slot their past leaves empty, chosen to wear in the Loadout. They're real pieces: worn, kept in the stash, swapped out in the tower or lost like anything else, and **nothing puts them back by itself** (not a new climb, not an empty slot). When you fall, or a climb ends any way but alive (a restart, the game closed mid-climb), and come back to the train with **no melee weapon**, Joel, the outfitter (the name lives in `scripts/outfitter_name.gd`), gives you the basic sword; with **no ranged weapon**, the basic bow. A copy already in your stash or pack is put on instead of a new one, so they never pile up, and you always come back able to fight. The fall screen says so ("On the train, Joel will have a plain sword ready for you."), and on the train his words show once at the top of your car, with the Entity's first-time tip. **Never for sale:** no rail stocks them (the outfitter's rail, a Dining-car climber's goods, a rival's swap in the tower), and nobody buys them: they can't be sold or broken down at the bench (their card says why). A plain Common sword or shortbow found in the tower is the same piece. Old saves are handed the sword and bow once, as they load, unless they keep one already. The words live in `basic_gift.gd` `TEXT` (the Data editor: "The train and store"). The fall screen says plainly what went ("Lost on the way down: your pack (3 pieces), your belt, your Coral Trident"). The tower remembers the **last 6 pieces** you lost, with the tower and floor where they fell (saved with your profile; old saves load with nothing lost). On a later climb of that tower, each floor of rooms within **2 floors** of where things fell has a **25%** chance of a **lost cache**: your own fallen pack with a broken strap and a blade poking out, under a faint pale light, in an ordinary room (never a puzzle hall, a sealed wing or a trap room, and traps keep clear of it). Walk onto it and up to **3** of your lost pieces come back, into your pack while there's room, else at your feet; found pieces are no longer lost. Belt items never come back. The Entity explains the first fall that loses things and the first lost cache. The numbers and words live in `scripts/lost.gd` (`WEAPON_CHANCE`, `HEIRLOOM_WEAPON`, `KEEP`, `CACHE_CHANCE`, `NEAR`, `CACHE_MAX`, `TEXT`).

## Stations, renown, titles and the Noble

Between climbs you can **step off at a station** instead of climbing. Nothing you carry is at risk there. A station is where you **learn the game** (Training), **fight other climbers** (the Tournament), see **the Noble** (titles and contracts) and read the **Renown Board**. Every name, number and word is a placeholder in data you can change in the Data editor (Words: **Stations, renown and titles** and **The Noble and contracts**; Numbers: the same, and **Contracts**). The six stations are `STATIONS` in `scripts/stations.gd`.

**Six stations and travel** (`scripts/travel.gd`). There are **six stations**, each in a major city near its tower, each with the same stops (Training, the Tournament, the Noble's estate, the Renown Board) and **its own Noble**. Their cities and Nobles (from the lore doc, easy to change in `STATIONS`: `name`, `short`, `noble`, `sub`): Windhollow and Aster Vale (Gryphon), Tidemoor and Maren Ostrow (Kraken), Forgehaven and Brannoc Hale (Wyvern), Dunewatch and Sefa Darrow (Manticore), Cinderreach and Ilse Cindra (Firebird), Thornmere and Corvin Thorne (Chimera). Each looks its own so you can tell them apart: its tower stands large on the horizon, the sky glows in its colour, its canopy, columns and pennants are painted in its colours (`lamp`, `banner`, `trim`, `sky`), and a **crest** under the page's title gives the city, the tower it's near and **Home station** or **Visiting**. **Titles are shared by every Noble**: one granted at any station is yours everywhere.
- **Home and where you are.** Your **home station** is the one near `profile.home` (a tower id, set when a climber is made; the Gryphon's until then), and `profile.station` is the one you're at (empty means home). Both save with the climber. A climb starts from the station you're at, and you come back to it.
- **The loop.** The stations sit on a loop in tower order (Gryphon, Kraken, Wyvern, Manticore, Firebird, Chimera, and round again). Riding the train costs a **fare of 12 crystals of any kind for each stop** (`FARE`), going the shorter way round, so 12, 24 or 36 at most, paid the biggest crystal first. **Titles can cut the fare** while you wear them (`SOCIAL` in `scripts/titles.gd`, its **Train fares** group: the Stairworn and the Trusted a quarter off, the Talked-of and the Paragon half), shown on each title's card at the Noble.
- **The lock.** Travel to other stations is **locked until you earn your first mark** (a faint one counts), or become Unbound. Until then the map shows a padlock on every other station and the placard says why.
- **Riding.** On the engine's map choose another tower: the placard shows the city, how many stops, the fare (red if you can't pay; your title's cut in green) and a **Ride** lever. Pull it: the fare is paid, the train pulls into that station, and its tower is the one to climb.
- **The Entity** explains it: the first time on the engine (the map, then the lock or the fares), under **How do things work here?** there's **Stations and travel?**, and every stop, lever and button on the map has its own words when you hover, move onto or hold it.
- **The Entity's tours** (`scripts/tour.gd`; the steps and words are `TOURS` in `travel.gd`). The first time you're at a station, the Entity shows you round in order: the **gym**, the **tournament grounds**, the **Noble's estate** and the **train**. The first time you board the train, it shows the cars: the **climbers' car** (your wardrobe, pack, stash and climber's book, one by one), the **outfitter**, the **workshop**, the **dining car**, the **settings and controls car**, and last the **engine** with the tower map. Each step goes to its stop or car, the Entity's sphere glides beside what it's showing with a short line, and a bar at the top of the page says more, with **Back**, **Next** (**Done** at the end) and **Skip the tour**. Ask the Entity **Show me around again** to replay either. Each tour is told once per climber (it's also in the Archive).

**Getting there and back.** In the **Engine**, **Step off** (top right of the map) gets off at the station you're at. At the station the rail down the left lists four stops: **Training**, **Tournament**, **The Noble** and **Renown Board**, and at the foot **Back to the train**. Each stop is a list of tiles on the left and a card on the right (on a phone the card goes under the list, its button right under its name). Hover or move onto a tile to see its card; press it (click, Enter, A, a tap) to put the keys on the card's button. **Q / E** (LB / RB) move between stops; Esc or B steps back (card, then rail, then the train). A gauge at the top of every stop shows your renown, rank and how far to the next.

**Training** (the first stop; the station opens on it). Short, hands-on **lessons** that teach the game, each in a small arena with the Entity saying how as it begins: the six of **basic training** (Moving, Rolling, Jumping, Striking, Shooting and Parrying on the glint), then **advanced** and **mark training** (see Training, the Grace and tournaments, below). Each is a minute or less, nothing there can fell you, and each is **remembered** once done. A lesson's card also has **Skip it: I know this**. Finishing (or skipping) them all pays **10 renown** once and readies the Trainee's title. Lessons are offered, never forced: the Entity mentions them in "What should I do next?" until they're done, and finishing one stops the Entity repeating that first-time tip in a climb. Below the lessons are the **medal drills** (`DRILLS`): a goal, a bronze / silver / gold medal, a small fee in crystals of any kind (2 to 6), and renown for each first medal. Twelve drills, by sixes: five open early (Parry the Glints, Five Down, Roll Gauntlet, Post to Post, Through the Pack); two **extra drills** (Glass Sparring, Champion's Pace) open only while you wear a title with a Training benefit; and the five higher ranks between the old ones each open one more (`RANK_OPENS` "drill"): **Bullseye** (Recognised: land six bow shots on the far dummies, against the clock), **Shell Breaker** (Esteemed: fell three shellbacks, whose shells turn blows from the front), **Perfect Timing** (Acclaimed: perfect parries only, a beat after the glint), **Hunted Run** (Lauded: the six posts with a knight and a bat after you) and **The Long Dance** (Legendary: parry three kinds of foe, each with its own rhythm).

**Tournament.** A new climber's first tournament comes first (below); after it the brackets are run by the travelling Tournament Manager (below), only at the station they're at, in that location's arena with its creatures in the first round. **Brackets** of three rounds against other climbers (`BRACKETS`): six, by sixes: the **Novice Bracket** (from the start, fee 5), the **Open Bracket** (Known, fee 10), the **Veterans' Bracket** (Esteemed, fee 12: climbers with many young marks), the **Masters' Bracket** (Famed, fee 15; its last round is your own reflection), the **Grand Bracket** (Lauded, fee 18; your reflection in the middle round) and the **Champions' Bracket** (Celebrated, fee 20). (An old save's brackets won keep their names: `OLD_BRACKETS` in `renown.gd`.) Each round is a climber with marks of their own (the same fighter as the rival climbers in the towers: glints, rolls, spells), tougher each round. **Nobody dies**: beaten, they yield; knocked down, you yield and the bracket ends. Between rounds your health and marks are made whole. **The tournament master** (a placeholder name) greets you and speaks between rounds (`MASTER_LINES`). Each round won past your best earns a little renown; winning the bracket pays renown and crystals (a repeat win, a share), in the two higher brackets a relic the first time, and readies a **title** from the Noble. The bracket's card lists its three rounds, the fee and the prize. In the arena, the pause menu's **Give up, back to the station** ends it at once. Nothing done in the arena is kept but the result.

**The Noble** (`scripts/noble.gd`; each station has its own, with a placeholder name in `STATIONS`). Two things:
- **Titles** (`TITLES` in `scripts/renown.gd`). Each title has a **rank** (its tier) and a **deed** (stop at a station, finish Training, win a bracket, fell a beast, team up with a climber, finish three contracts...). Its card says what it **needs** (each part done or not) and **what it gives**. Once both are done, **Be named ...** grants it and you wear it; **Wear this title** / **Take it off** swap. Wear one at a time: it shows under your name on the rail and in your book.
- **Contracts** (`scripts/contracts.gd`). A few are on offer each visit: **reach** floor X of a named tower (on any climb), **slay** a number of one kind of monster (anywhere), or **retrieve** a thing from a floor of a named tower (walk onto it, then take the stair with it; a fall drops it and it waits on its floor again). Hold **one** at a time (**two** from Respected). A held contract shows as a line at the top of the screen while you climb, and the Entity notes it in its log. Done, it pays at once: renown, crystals of its tower's kind and, now and then, a **charm recipe**. A contract never blocks anything: the thing to retrieve always lies where you can walk (it's checked with the same reach rules as the stair), and no floor waits on it. **Give it up** drops one.

**What titles give** (`SOCIAL` in `scripts/titles.gd`). Only the title you wear counts, so the choice matters. Each title helps with some of four groups, said plainly on its card:
- **Rival climbers**: friendlier trades (they ask less), more offers to team up, a smaller toll when one beats you in a duel, and taking your bets. (A later Dining car can read the same table: `Titles.rival_terms`.)
- **Training**: medal drills cheaper or free, and the extra drills open.
- **Tournament masters**: brackets open two ranks sooner, a cheaper (or free) entry, a bigger prize.
- **The outfitter**: a discount, today's piece a rarity higher, or a Fine piece kept on his rail for you. A worn title also tilts his rail toward pieces that suit it (`TITLE_STOCK` in `shop.gd`). Some titles also **open nodes of his upgrade tree** (see below; the title's card at the Noble says which): those need the title granted, not worn. A worn title's discount and the tree's add up, to 25% off at most (`discount_cap`), so they never double up past that.

**Renown** (`scripts/renown.gd`, kept with each climber) only grows. A climb earns **1 for every floor reached** and **2 for every floor past your best in that tower** (`CLIMB`), **20 for a beast**; tournaments, contracts, Training and deeds with other climbers earn more. **You always see what you earn**: every gain says what it was for, in the feed and the Entity's log ("+6 renown: won a duel with Bryn"), with a small "+6 renown" over you in a climb. Anything can add to it with one hook, `Renown.earn(profile, amount, reason)`. **Ranks** come at thresholds (`RANKS`), twelve by sixes, the steps widening as you rise so each comes a little slower than the last: Unknown 0, Noticed 25, Known 60, Recognised 90, Respected 120, Esteemed 160, Famed 200, Acclaimed 260, Renowned 320, Lauded 400, Celebrated 480, Legendary 600. (The seven older ranks kept their thresholds and everything they open; the five new ones came in between and on top. An old save's last rank moment is moved across once: `OLD_RANKS`.) **A rank opens things to do** (`RANK_OPENS`), not just stock:

| Rank | Opens |
|---|---|
| Unknown | Training; the Novice Bracket; reach and retrieve contracts; the first titles |
| Noticed | more titles; slay contracts; a charm recipe (Mending Bead) |
| Known | the Open Bracket; charm refills 25% cheaper; more titles |
| Recognised | a medal drill (Bullseye); charm refills 35% cheaper |
| Respected | two contracts at once; a charm recipe (Keeper's Purse); more titles |
| Esteemed | the Veterans' Bracket; a medal drill (Shell Breaker) |
| Famed | the Masters' Bracket; charm refills 50% cheaper; a charm recipe (Tide Shell); more titles |
| Acclaimed | a medal drill (Perfect Timing); refills 55% cheaper |
| Renowned | a charm recipe (Bulwark Stone); refills 60% cheaper; more titles |
| Lauded | the Grand Bracket; a medal drill (Hunted Run) |
| Celebrated | the Champions' Bracket; a charm recipe (Gale Charm); the last titles |
| Legendary | a medal drill (The Long Dance); refills 65% cheaper |

**A rank-up is a moment**: in a climb a banner says the new rank and what it opens and the Entity remarks on it; reached at a station or at the end of a climb, the Entity says it at the top of the page (or of your car on the train), once.

**Renown Board.** Your rank, how much more the next one needs and **what it opens**, every rank as a tile (its card lists what it opens), your recent deeds with the renown each earned, and the climbers you've met in the towers (their marks and your history with them).

**Charms took over from the boons.** The old **Boon Shrine** is gone. Its lasting upgrades became charm rewards for your rank: six **charm recipes** a Rune Artist must learn before crafting them at the bench (`LOCKED` in `scripts/charms.gd`; a rank teaches five of them, contracts can teach any), and **cheaper charm refills** at Known, Famed and Renowned. **Old saves that bought boons get every crystal they paid back** (the shrine's old prices are kept in `OLD_BOONS`), once, as the save loads; a deed says so and the Entity says it at the station. (The **Pedlar**, who set up at stations and camps, is gone too: the Noble took his place; an old save's stall is simply dropped.)

The Entity explains it all: first-time words for stations, Training, the tournament, the Noble, what titles give, contracts and rank-ups; **How do titles work?**, **What are contracts?** and **How do tournaments work?** under **How do things work here?**; and a hover / hold explanation for every button on the station's pages.

**The shop changes as you rise** (`scripts/shop.gd`, every number and word in the Data editor under **The shop's variety**). The outfitter's stock follows your renown rank, free: this is the **trunk** of his upgrade tree (below). It is new every visit (the shelf and the rail are rolled again each time you come back to the train):

| Rank | Rail spaces (more with a wider rack) | Kinds on the shelf | Fine / Rare on the rail | Also |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unknown | 8 | 6 | none | plain weapons only |
| Noticed | 9 | 8 | none | canes and the longbow |
| Known | 10 | 10 | Fine 10% | pistols (flintlock), crossbows, sword-canes |
| Recognised | 10 | 10 | Fine 18% | |
| Respected | 11 | 11 | Fine 25% | greatswords and war hammers; a second charm |
| Esteemed | 11 | 11 | Fine 28%, Rare 3% | a named Rare tower piece now and then |
| Famed | 12 | 12 | Fine 30%, Rare 6% | revolvers |
| Acclaimed | 13 | 12 | Fine 32%, Rare 8% | a relic now and then |
| Renowned | 14 | 13 | Fine 35%, Rare 10% | a third charm |
| Lauded | 15 | 13 | Fine 38%, Rare 12% | |
| Celebrated | 16 | 14 | Fine 40%, Rare 14% | more of everything |
| Legendary | 16 | 14 | Fine 42%, Rare 15% | relics and named pieces a little oftener |

The Healing and Stamina Vials, the tonic, the Essence Vial and the recall scroll are always on the shelf; the rest are picked from what your rank has opened. A **title** you wear tilts the rail (see below).

**Titles flavour the stock.** The title you wear (the Renown Board's **Wear**) leans the rail toward pieces that suit it, and says so in a line over the rail: for example **For the Contender: blades and pistols hang first.**, **For Ironclad: armour hangs first.**, **For the Talked-of: trade goods hang first, and he takes a little off.** Some titles also put one supply on the shelf for you, or take a little off everything he sells. The table is `TITLE_STOCK` in `shop.gd`, keyed by the title's id, so a new title (a rival-climber one, say) can be given a line; a title that isn't listed leaves the stock as it is.

**The Outfitter's Car, section by section.**
- **Consumables** (his shelf): supplies packed onto your belt for your next climb (as much as the belt holds, beside the starting vials; put one back before you go and what you paid comes back). The **recall scroll** is always there, never sells out, and is priced in crystals of **any kind** (60, `ANY_PRICE`; a gold gem and "60 of any kind"): buying one takes from your biggest crystals first and the card says which ("paid 35 Water, 20 Fire, 5 Air"); you still carry one at most. After a climb his greeting mentions what he paid you for it.
- **Weapons & armour** (his brass rail): 8 pieces to start, more with rank, new every visit: Common pieces (a Satchel among them while you own no bag), finer ones at higher ranks. **Today's piece** sits in a glass case beside it: one Fine or Rare piece, new after every climb (and the **Special order** and a relic **Offered once**, from his tree). Each piece's arrow says whether it beats what you'd wear (green and up, red and down); its card says whether you can afford it, sets every number against what you'd wear, and buys it three ways: **Buy: into your pack** (it goes up the tower), **Buy: into your stash** (it stays on the train) or **Buy and wear it**.
- **Charms** (his charm tray): one charm to start, more with rank and with his tree's charm tray (see **Charms**).
- **Sell**: your pack and your stash, in the same grids as everywhere (`storage_view.gd`). Choose a piece and press **Sell** on its card: it becomes a crystal of its kind at 40% of its price (`SELL_SHARE`, more with his tree; worn Common pieces fetch less). The button asks first: a small pop-up says what you get, with **Confirm** and **Cancel**.

**When it doesn't all fit** (a phone, a big Interface size, or a big stock at a high rank), the buying sections turn into a **list** instead of pages: one row per thing (its icon, its name, its price and the green or red arrow), in a column that scrolls (drag it with a finger, the mouse wheel, or move down it with the keys). Where everything fits as grids (a computer at a low rank), it shows as grids.

**The outfitter's upgrade tree** (`scripts/outfitter.gd` holds it, `scripts/outfit_tree.gd` draws it; every name, number and word is in the Data editor under **The outfitter's upgrade tree**). It's drawn on a green baize board in the car: five columns, a row for each renown rank, Unknown at the bottom, so it grows upward as you rise. On a computer it stands beside the goods, under Joel, **folded or open**: its **Upgrades** sign (a flat brass sign with an arrow, its name and how many nodes are yours; the arrow points right while folded, down while open) folds it away so Joel is drawn big in the room it leaves (he grows with the room his column has), and opens it again. Click it, tap it, or move onto it with the keys or a controller and press; the Entity explains it when you hover or hold it, and the store's topic and the tree's mention it. The choice is saved with the settings (`store_upgrades` in `menu.gd`, shared by every climber on the device), and it starts **folded**, so on a wide window the tree no longer fills the car's whole height and Joel isn't a small face in a corner. Choosing a node's card (a way in that asks for the tree) opens it too. On a phone (or a big Interface size) tap **Upgrades** under Joel to see the tree in place of the goods, and **Goods** to go back; Joel is drawn there as big as the room under his words allows. Each node is a round medallion: **gilt and lit** once it's yours, **brass-ringed** when it's open to buy (brighter if you can pay), **dark with a small lock** while it's locked; a small **pennant** marks a node that needs a title. Choose a node (hover it, move onto it, or tap it) and its card says what it does, what opens it (each part ticked green or red: the rank, the node below it, the title) and its price, with **Buy it**. When you buy one, he says a line about it.

- **The trunk (Rank)** is your renown rank, free and automatic: each rank he stocks more (the table above). Its card lists what that rank's stock holds.
- **The branches** are bought with **crystals of any kind** (your biggest first, like the recall scroll), and are yours for good. A node opens when you have **its rank** and **the node below it** in its branch; a few also need **a title the Noble has granted** (wearing it isn't needed).

| Branch | Node | Opens at | Also needs | Costs | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prices | Fair prices | Noticed | | 20 | 5% off everything he sells |
| Prices | A scroll kept aside | Known | Fair prices | 30 | the recall scroll costs 40 of any kind, not 60 |
| Prices | A better share | Respected | A scroll kept aside | 45 | he pays half a piece's price when you sell, not 40% |
| Prices | A regular's discount | Famed | A better share | 60 | 10% off everything (in place of 5%) |
| Prices | An old friend's price | Renowned | A regular's discount, the title **Goldhand** | 90 | 15% off everything, and 55% when you sell |
| Stock | A wider rack | Noticed | | 20 | two more pieces on his rail |
| Stock | A charm tray | Known | A wider rack | 30 | one more charm each visit |
| Stock | Better stock | Respected | A charm tray | 45 | rail pieces come Fine at least a quarter of the time; today's piece likelier Rare, now and then Storied |
| Stock | A Rare on the hook | Famed | Better stock | 60 | one Rare piece always hangs on his rail |
| Stock | Special order | Renowned | A Rare on the hook | 90 | choose a piece on the rail or in the case and **Hold it for me**: it waits in the **Special order** slot until you buy it, on any later visit (one at a time) |
| Stock | A relic, offered once | Celebrated | Special order, the title **Beastfeller** | 120 | he brings out a relic (a Storied named piece) for you: it waits in his case (**Offered once**) until you buy it; once only |
| Gifts | A vial for the road | Noticed | | 20 | a free Healing Vial on your belt as you leave for each climb (if there's room) |
| Gifts | Something for coming by | Known | A vial for the road | 30 | each visit after a climb, 4 crystals of the tower you came from |
| Gifts | A gift for each rank | Respected | Something for coming by | 45 | each time you rise a rank, a Fine piece into your stash (15 crystals if it's full) |
| Gifts | A climber's kit | Famed | A gift for each rank, the title **the Helping Hand** | 60 | a Stamina Vial and a Cleansing Tonic on your belt too, each climb |
| Info | Word on the tower | Noticed | | 15 | he says what the tower chosen at the Engine is handy for, and tags those supplies **Handy** on his shelf |
| Info | His pick for you | Known | Word on the tower | 25 | he names the piece on his rail or in his case that beats yours by the most, tagged **Best** |
| Info | Climbers' talk | Respected | His pick for you | 40 | each visit after a climb he tells you a monster's weakness from the tower you came from, and it goes in your Bestiary |
| Info | Where it lies | Famed | Climbers' talk, the title **the Trusted** | 50 | on the floor of a contract's thing to retrieve, a line says which way it lies from where you start |

His gifts and word show in gilt under what he says when you come back from a climb, on the sections they belong to (word on the tower over the Consumables, his pick over the Weapons & armour), and in the tower's line at the top of the screen (where it lies).

**Old saves keep what they had.** Before the tree, five upgrades came free with a rank. A save from then is given the nodes that do the same, free, as it loads: **a wider rack** (Noticed), **a scroll kept aside** (Known), **better stock** (Respected), **a regular's discount** (Famed) and **special order** (Renowned), for the ranks it had reached. Anything else on the tree it buys like anyone.

The Entity explains it: the first time the shelf grows with a rank, the first time a title tilts the rail, and the first time you see the tree; and under **How do things work here?** there's **How does the shop change?** and, from it, **How does his upgrade tree work?**. Every node and button has its own words when you hover, move onto or hold it.

## The cities: what people say, and passing as marked

Every word and number is in `scripts/city_lore.gd` (the Data editor's "The cities: lore in what people say", words and numbers). Kept with the climber in the save (`profile.gd lore`; an old save starts with none).

- **The aura changes how people speak** (`AURA`). Core essence changes who a climber is as it builds: once the aura reaches its first step (`secret.gd STEPS`), Nobles, the Dining car's climbers and rival climbers in the towers greet a climber on the path of **Making** or **Unmaking** with a line for that path and step, **70%** of the time (`AURA_CHANCE`; the rest, their usual words): Making draws people near (rested, then trusted, then sought out as a healer), Unmaking makes them give way (wary, then uneasy, then afraid). Six lines per path for each, one a step. Lines that speak of the climber use their pronouns (`{they}`, `{them}`...).
- **The Noble's persuasion** (`PERSUADE`). Nobles are mostly climbers who stopped short of the summits and were persuaded to keep people in order. As the climber grows strong, their **home** station's Noble quietly tries to talk them into stopping: three steps (1 guardian beaten or the rank of Esteemed; 3 guardians or Renowned; 5 guardians or Celebrated), two lines each, told in order, one now and then in the greeting (always the first time a new step is reached, then **60%**). Never a blocker: nothing else changes. The Entity's first-time word: `noble_persuades`.
- **An Unbound passes as marked** (`PASSING`). In the stations an Unbound is taken for marked. Each **visit** (a station, between two climbs: it rolls once, however often you step off) has a small chance of finding them out: **4%**, plus **1%** for each level of their biggest Node and **2%** for each aura step, at most **30%**. Found out, for that visit: a flat warning at the top of every stop, the Noble greets coldly and **grants no titles or contracts**, the station's **fees cost 25% more** (drills, brackets), and a **rumour** spreads: for 3 climbs rival climbers and the Dining car's climbers mention an Unbound found out in that city (25% of the time). Not found out, rivals and diners now and then take the climber for marked ("Which mark got you this high?"). Never a game-over. The Entity: `unbound_passing` (the first station visit as an Unbound), `found_out`, and "How does it work?" has **Passing as marked in the cities?**; the aura's answer says people speak differently.

`tests/parts/city_lore.gd` tests these, the seal on a faint mark and the Regard's glimpse, with the Entity's words and no banned words.

## Training, the Grace and tournaments

A new climber doesn't go straight up a tower. After the questions' **Begin**, they arrive at their **home station** (the one near `profile.home`), and **the towers stay closed until the Noble grants them the Grace** (`scripts/training.gd`; every word and number is in the Data editor under **Training, the Grace and the Tournament Manager**). Until then the engine's lever is a padlock that says why, and leaving for a tower is refused. Climbers made before (and tests) aren't held back: only the new start's Begin marks a climber as needing it.

- **Basic training** (the gym; `LESSONS` in `stations.gd`): six short lessons covering every way to move, attack and parry: **Moving** (posts), **Rolling**, **Jumping**, **Striking** (blows on the near dummies with your weapon), **Shooting** (shots on the far dummies with your bow; a blade doesn't count) and **Parrying on the glint**. Before the Grace they can't be skipped (the card says so). They can be refreshed any time.
- **The first tournament** (the tournament grounds; `FIRST`): three rounds at the home station: two rounds of low-level monsters (the station's `starter` creatures in `tournaments.gd`), then a **human champion fighter** with no marks (`CHAMPION`, a placeholder name). Nobody dies; knocked down, you yield. **The Grace comes win or lose.** Each round won adds a reward (`REWARDS`): **1** a Healing Vial, **2** + a recall scroll, **3** + one **Fine** (the "uncommon" rarity) weapon or armour piece of the type you fought with most in the tournament, into your stash (choice made: a new piece rather than a bonus on the starting weapon; with no weapon used, Fine armour of the kind worn). **Flawless** (no damage taken all tournament) adds one essence tincture of each kind, from the Noble. Gifts are packed for the next climb (putting one back gives nothing for it); tinctures can be drunk from the book's Essence page.
- **Training's essence** (`RATES`, `USUAL`, `SOAK_SHARE`): basic training, medal drills and the first tournament give **a quarter of the usual essence, into the body only** (never the marks or crystals). Before the first mark, what each body part did in training is kept (`training.soak`, at a quarter) and the first climb starts with it, so training decides which part is used most and where the first mark appears. Advanced and mark training and later tournaments give **half** the usual, flowing the natural way (body and marks).
- **Advanced training** (after the Grace): six lessons (the belt wheel, states and cures, cracked walls, the ward and its task, the perfect parry, fighting a crowd) in **three tiers that open by the titles the Noble has granted** (`TIERS`: any title, then a title of rank Noticed or higher, then Respected or higher). **Mark training** opens with the first mark (or Unbound): six lessons with **dummies**, **summoned creatures** and **human rivals** who spar until they yield. Each try costs a small fee in crystals of **the essence that fits it** (mark training: your strongest mark's kind), and the first time each is done pays a little renown.
- **The travelling Tournament Manager** (`scripts/tournaments.gd`, a placeholder name): after the first, brackets are run by the Manager, who moves round the six stations, a stop after each of the climber's climbs (`ROTATION`). Elsewhere the grounds say where they are and where they go next. **Each location changes the tournament** (`LOCATIONS`): the arena's **layout** (its pillars, `LAYOUTS`) and **hazards** (a few of its tower's traps round the edge), the **creatures** of each bracket's first round, and the **rivals**, whose first mark leans to its tower's essence; the prize crystals are its tower's kind. Entry costs a fee; a win pays renown, crystals, essence (half), the **shared minor reward** (a Healing Vial) and, the first time you win the Open Bracket or higher there, **a prize unique to that location** (its tower's named piece).

**The Entity explains it**: first-time words for the Grace, basic training, the first tournament, its rewards, advanced and mark training and the Tournament Manager; under **How do things work here?** there's **The Noble's Grace and my first tournament?**, **Basic, advanced and mark training?** and **Who is the Tournament Manager?**; "What should I do next?" points a new climber at the lessons, then the first tournament; and every new tile and button explains itself on hover, a move onto it or a held finger. The Noble's estate shows **the Grace** as a tile, with how to earn it. Tests: `tests/parts/training.gd`.

## Essence density (attributes)

Each essence type is an attribute: Air is Agility, Blade Strength, Stone Vitality, Iron Will, Fire Intellect and Water Spirit. Your body's share of the essence you bank at a stair (essence eaten from crystals too, and a tincture drunk into an unmarked part) settles in you as **density**, by type and in the body parts that earned it (split by what each part did that floor), and it's kept between climbs. Each type gives a small passive bonus that grows with diminishing returns up to 12%: Air move speed, Blade strike damage, Stone health, Iron stamina recovery, Fire spell power, Water how fast your marks fill. The **climber screen** (Tab) shows your six body parts with their marks, and its Essence tab your density and bonus for every type and the essence settled in each part.

## The secret bonds: Developer, Designer and Manipulator

Three secret bonds, one from each Maker (`scripts/secret_bonds.gd`, where every word and number lives; `scripts/secret_tools.gd` runs the ring and the depth 1 and 2 tools in play; `scripts/secret_deep.gd` the depth 3 to 5 tools; `scripts/secret_page.gd` is the climber screen's Core tab). They're **dev tools for their theme**: the Developer works on your bonds, the Designer on the tower, the Manipulator on the creatures. They go much farther than any tower bond and share no feature with any other bond, item or essence. **A climber holds only one.** It's the **core mark**, kept in the profile as `core_mark` ("", "developer", "designer" or "manipulator") and `core_depth` (0 to 5); the Makers (`makers.gd`, built alongside) grant it, and the dev tools can too. Each depth unlocks one tool, from looking to rewriting: **inspect, switch, edit, reroll, build**, all five built.

**The tool ring.** Hold **B** (**LB + LT** on a controller, the **Tools** button that appears on a phone while you hold a secret bond) and past a quarter second a ring of your unlocked tools opens and time stops; point at one (the mouse, the right stick, or slide your finger from the button) and let go to **choose** it: choosing never uses it. A tap uses the chosen tool, or closes an open panel. Under each tool its **cost shows as gems**: your path's secret essence and ordinary essence. A tool that aims works on what you aim at when you tap (while the ring is open, what each would work on now is outlined). While a tool works, or its panel is open, a faint **wireframe** of the workings shows in its Maker's colour: the tile grid, trap lanes and door hinges (Designer), monster outlines (Manipulator), your spells' reach (Developer).

**Every tool costs essence, paid like a spell from what you carry** (unbanked): a few units of secret essence from the flow you carry, plus some ordinary essence taken from every kind you carry in proportion. Each unit of secret essence you don't carry (no path, the other path, or none carried) costs **3 essence** instead. The Manipulator's tools cost mostly Making, the Designer's mostly Unmaking, the Developer's mostly ordinary essence plus one unit of your path's. Looking is cheap, rewriting expensive. A tool you can't pay for says what's missing, and nothing is paid for a tool that can't work (no target, a great foe, a locked door). An **Inspect** is paid once and lasts the floor: using it again opens or closes its panel for free.

| Bond | Depth 1 | Depth 2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Developer** (grows from all essence) | **Inspect** (1 flow + 6 essence): every spell's real numbers, with everything that changes them counted (faint, density, gear, flow, Tune), and what each step adds: on the spell ring while you aim, and in a panel | **Tune** (between climbs, on the Core tab; 6 crystal essence a change): each spell you carry takes Power (20% more power), Thrift (25% less cost) or Quick (40% quicker wind-up) |
| **Designer** (grows from Unmaking) | **Inspect** (2 Unmaking + 1): the floor's plan as a panel (rooms and corridors, every room's kind, doors, every trap, hidden traps, secret walls, the stair, every trap's timing grouped), and in the world each trap near you shows its timing and when it next fires, each door what kind of room lies behind it (**never what it holds**: a good room is just "a good room") | **Switch** (4 Unmaking + 2, reach 380): the trap you aim at off (it rests, crossed out) or on again, or an unlocked door open, or shut: a shut door holds for 30 seconds and nobody opens it |
| **Manipulator** (grows from Making) | **Inspect** (1 Making + 1, reach 360): a readout over the monster for the floor: health, damage, speed, the essence it carries, and its **next attack before it starts** ("Next: Lunge", from `ATTACKS`, by distance), then "Now: Lunge 0.3s" while it winds up | **Calm** (3 Making + 2): it wanders and ignores you until struck. **Set against** (5 Making + 3): it and the monster nearest it fight each other with their own attacks (40 seconds at most); what falls leaves its essence for you. Great foes (beasts, shadows, mirror images, champions) won't answer |

**Depths 3 to 5.** A tool with a choice shows it on the camp's **card screen** (number keys, arrows and Enter, a controller's d-pad and A, a click or a tap); the monsters hold still and nothing hurts you while you choose, the last card always **leaves without paying**, and a tool is paid only when a card that does something is taken (a two-step tool at its last step; a card that turns out impossible gives back what it took). While the cards show, what they'd work on is outlined in the world.

| Bond | Depth 3: Edit | Depth 4 | Depth 5 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Developer** | **Edit** (on the train, Core tab; 4 crystal essence a point raised): move points between a spell's own numbers: Power, Reach (reach, range, radius, width), Length (how long its effect lasts), Wind-up and Thrift (cost). A point is 12%; one only rises when another of the same spell falls (they add up to zero), at most 3 either way; lowering and clearing are free. Saved per spell (`core_edit`), and Inspect's numbers count it | **Splice** (4 flow + 12): pick one of your spells, then another of your marks: the spell takes that bond's behaviour for the floor (`SPLICE_FROM`: Stormcaller and Rune Artist stun, Windwalker and Sandshaper throw back, Tidecaller pulls, Hunter and Blade Forger fire a bolt at the next foe, Warden and Covenant expose, Deepcaller and Rekindler heal you, the fire bonds burn, Venomist poisons, Ashwalker blinds, Grafter bursts, Surgeon cripples). It goes to every foe the spell strikes as it's cast, to its shot if it fires one, or (striking nothing at once: a shield, a zone) to the foes around you. One at a time: a new splice replaces it | **Write** (8 flow + 24, once a floor): pick an essence, a bond you don't hold and one of its spells: it's cast at once, at your best bond's step (not forms, toggles, kin-book spells or Phoenix Heart, which is never cast: `WRITE_SKIP`). On the train, Write also **moves marks** between body parts (12 crystal essence a move; a marked part swaps): the bond and its growth go with it |
| **Designer** | **Edit** (6 Unmaking + 3, reach 380): on the trap you aim at, **Re-time** (its room's whole beat runs half again slower and goes quiet for a moment) or **Move** (up to 3 tiles the way you aim, to open floor a trap may take; never a dart's slot); or in front of you, **Raise** (3 tiles of wall across your aim for 20 seconds: solid for bodies, sight and paths) or **Sink** (a wall at most 2 tiles thick opens for 20 seconds, rising again once nobody stands in it) | **Reroll** (8 Unmaking + 4, at the stair, once a floor): the next floor's puzzle from two, then how its rooms lean from two (`LEANS`: kinder rooms, trap halls, lairs, hushed halls). The next floor of rooms is planned with them (`Catalog.forced` and `Themes.lean`, for that floor only); never the Proving or the summit | **Build** (each its own cost, 3 a floor): **a trap** (the tower's own plate, set off only by monsters; 8 + 6), **a wall** across your aim for the floor (10 + 6), **a gate** that lets you through but no monster, which goes around (10 + 8); and once a climb **a rift** (28 Unmaking + 30, the dearest tool; `scripts/rift.gd`): a corridor **two tiles wide torn through the floor** from where you stand to the stair, straight or with one bend (whichever tears less rock, and it steers round the live puzzle room), through rock, walls, cover, pillars, doors, trap rooms (the traps on it are destroyed), sealed wings and their vaults, and secret rooms (running right beside one tears into it too). **Anything locked it passes is opened and what it held is taken, not given:** a vault stands open with nothing on its pedestal (its room's question ends), a locked door's lock and its key or lever are gone and its room's cache and drops with them, a hidden room opens empty (and a Maker's fragment on its wall goes too); a banner says each ("The rift tears through the vault. What it held is gone."). Lock-in rooms it runs through never shut. It's drawn as raw torn rock (rubble, ragged teeth and a torn face where rock stands, a faint fissure glowing in the Designer's colour with a few faint lights so it shows in the dark), and while Build's cards show, the rock it would tear is outlined. Everything that reads the walls follows: the tile grid, the path grid, the walls' collision (cut over the rift; rock it bares gets its own), sight and fog, the lighting's shadows, the floor and walls drawing (torches and door frames on torn rock go) |
| **Manipulator** | **Edit** (6 Making + 3, reach 360): on the monster you aim at, Slower (40%), Weaker (40% less damage), Half its reach (it only starts attacks from half as far) or Frail (40% less health), two at most; or, untouched, **turn it** into a weaker kind of its tower where it stands | **Reroll** (8 Making + 4, reach 560): aim at a room you haven't entered: its sleeping monsters become another pack of the tower's monsters, about as strong, chosen from two (a key goes with them). Once a room | **Build** (14 Making + 8, reach 300): the monster you aim at is rewritten into your own for the floor: it fights for you and, every few seconds, uses an attack taken from another kind of its tower (`SECOND`: a knight that also fires arrows, a slime with a spark ring...). One at a time. Not Covenant: it calls nothing new |

**Kept safe.** The Designer never leaves a trap room uncrossable by timing: every re-time and move is checked with the traps' own "is there a safe moment on every tile" test (`Traps.hard_tiles`) and undone if it would add a tile with none. A wall (raised or built) is refused if it would cut you off from the stair or the puzzle's room. Sink never opens the floor's outer wall, a door, a sealed wing, a vault, a secret room or the puzzle's sealed room, and nothing works on the Proving's floor. The rift alone tears through wings, vaults and secret rooms (taking what they held), but never the floor's outer wall, never on the Proving's floor, and it only ever opens, so it can't shut you in and the stair stays reachable. The Manipulator leaves great foes alone, and key carriers and the monsters bound to a floor's test (a wing's waves, a puzzle's ambush) keep their kind and side.

**The Developer can also change your path** (Making or Unmaking) on the train, from depth 1, on the Core tab (20 crystal essence; it asks first, in a pop-up): all its flow, banked and carried, flows out of your shadow (drawn on the page) and the next great foe offers the choice again, starting from nothing.

**Growing.** What you bank at a stair since taking the mark feeds it (`core_xp`): the Designer from banked Unmaking (60 for depth 2, then 150, 280, 450), the Manipulator from banked Making (the same), the Developer from essence of every kind (400, 900, 1600, 2600) **and**, for each depth, one of: **mastery** (three tower bonds at step 5), **knowledge** (8 of the Makers' traces for depth 2, then 10, 12, 14), **a favour** for a tower beast (not in the game yet) or **a bribe** for the Maker of Marks. Every depth up to 5 is reachable. The Core tab says what the next depth needs.

**The bribe.** On the train, the Developer's Core tab offers the Maker of Marks 120 crystal essence, a Regarded piece of gear from your chest or pack, or a full vessel from your chest (see **The vessel**). It may refuse: nothing is taken, but it remembers. It agrees 60% of the time, 12% less for every bribe offered before (15% at least; `BRIBE`); a full vessel adds 25% (90% at most). An accepted bribe counts for the next depth only. Its memory is kept with the Makers' (`profile.makers["bribe"]`).

**The Core tab.** On the climber screen, a fifth tab appears with a secret bond: its name and depth, every tool (depths still to come shown as such) with what it does and its cost, and its growth. The Developer's also lists every spell's real numbers (on the train; in a climb once Inspect is paid on the floor), the Tune and Edit buttons (on the train), moving marks (depth 5), the bribe and the change of path. The Entity explains the ring and each tool the first time (the Archive's "Secret bond tools").

## Menus and the dev tool

The game opens on a **title screen** that covers the whole game: a tower drawn in code against a night sky, with lit windows, a moon, mist and drifting motes in the six essence colours (`scripts/title.gd`; the name, tagline and footer text are in its `TEXT` list). Its buttons are **Continue** (the climber played last; shown once a climber exists, above New Climber), **New Climber** (a new climber in an empty slot: see A new climber's start below), **Climbers** (the climber picker, naming the current climber: see Climbers and saves below; it appears once there is a climber saved), **Settings**, **Dev tools** and **Start over** (only once you have marks to lose), with a line showing your marks and best floor. The climber screen isn't on the title: once you've chosen a climber it's in the pause menu (and Tab, the Climber button on a phone, or Your car on the train). Arrow keys and Enter, a controller (d-pad or left stick to move, A to press), the mouse, or a tap all work; Esc does nothing there, and the list wraps round. The focused button has a gold outline (the mouse moves it), and the first one is focused when a page opens. Pages opened from the title show over its backdrop and **Back** returns to it. Leaving the title takes you to the tower choice (after the Regard, to the train).

During a climb there are three screens, and each pauses the game while it's open (menus fade in and out quickly):

- **Pause menu:** **Esc** or **P** (**Start** on a controller, **Menu** on a phone). The in-game pages share a **rail** down the left of the screen (`scripts/rail.gd`): at its top who you are (your name, the tower and floor or "On the train", and your six marks as small medallions; it's a button that opens the climber screen, **Tab** shown beside it), then **Resume**, **Inventory**, the **Library** (the Entity's Archive), **Settings & Controls** and **Dev Tools**, and at its foot **Exit to Title** (to play another climber, Exit to Title, then Climbers). The names follow the train's: in a climb the Library and Settings & Controls are single pages beside the rail rather than cars. The page shows to the right of it. The pause page itself sums up the climb (the tower, the floor, the essence you carry unbanked and your marks) with **Restart this climb**, and on a computer **Quit game** (in a browser or on a phone Exit takes you to the title instead). **Settings & Controls** has two tabs, Settings and Controls (click them, or **Q** / **E**, **LB** / **RB**), and under a rule at its foot **Start over this climber** (this climber only; it asks first). **Keys and controller:** the menu opens on Resume; **Up** / **Down** move along the rail and show each page as you pass it; **Right**, **Enter** or **A** goes into the page; **Left** from the page comes back to that page's own item; **Esc** or **B** comes back to the rail, and from the rail closes the menu. A page opened by its own key (Tab, I) closes with Esc at once. **Mouse:** hovering moves the focus (without opening the page), a click opens it, and a **right-click** goes back like Esc. **Touch:** tap a rail item; every page has **Close** at its top right. On a phone the rail narrows to icons with short names. On the train the rail stays on screen the whole time and lists the cars (see The train). The words and icons live in `rail.gd` (`CLIMBER`, `ITEMS`, `TRAIN_CARS`, `TRAIN_PAGES`, `FOOT`, `TRAIN_FOOT`, `FOOT_QUIT`, `TEXT`); the pages' icons are drawn in `ui.gd` `draw_icon`, the cars' and the new ones (book, lever, station lamp) in `rail.gd` `car_icon`.
- **Climber screen:** **Tab** (a short press of **View / Share**, the **Climber** button on a phone, your name at the top of the rail, or the book in Your Car on the train). It's **your book** (`scripts/book.gd`, see **The tree, the library and the book**), in five sections, each a ribbon (turn the pages with the ribbons, **Q** / **E**, **LB** / **RB** on a controller, or a swipe across the page on a phone; the book reopens on the section you used last): **Abilities** (see below), **Tree** (your body map: how you can grow, and the choices waiting), **Marks** (the climber drawn in full, in your chosen look, with **Appearance** under it, and each mark glowing on its body part in its bond's colour and a thin ring of the essence settled there, beside a card for each body part with its mark's medallion, step and growth), **Essence** (density and attribute bonus for each type, the essence settled in each body part, and what you carry unbanked), **Record** (the Entity's record of you: this climb's tower and floor, your counters, each tower's best floor and your heirlooms). Everything the Entity has told you is in the **Library** (see below).
- **Abilities** (`scripts/abilities.gd`): what you can cast right now, and how. It's the climber screen's first tab, so Tab (or the Climber button) shows it, and **holding** Tab or the Climber button a moment opens it straight on Abilities (a peek): let go and you're back in the game, on the tab you had before. From anywhere, `menu.open("abilities")` opens it (the train's Climber page and your car's Climber reach it on the train). A marked climber sees each mark (its bond, body part, essence and step, the button that casts it, "hold for the ring" once it has two spells, and on a computer its Body and Passive in a line) with its spells under it: each a card with its medallion, name, what it does in one plain line, the button to press (the key, "LB + X" on a controller, or "tap" / "hold, slide" on a phone, following what you used last; the chosen spell is "on the button") and its cost; spells not learned yet are dimmed with a lock and "learned at step N". The Unbound have no Abilities tab: "Abilities" (and the peek) opens their **Techniques** tab, which holds the same (their six techniques, what each Node gives, the practice room); their technique cards (name, part, shape and essences, what it does, the button, and "full at 20 · +14 strain") still feed the HUD, the phone's long press and the first-cast toasts. Press a card (Enter, A, a click or a tap) for **more**: the full numbers (a spell's cost, wind-up and the rest and what later steps change, and how its mark fills; a technique's fill, strain, boost, what each essence adds, its shape's numbers and its part's level). On the train the page also has **Try your abilities** (the practice room); for the Unbound, **Change techniques** opens the Techniques tab; for a marked climber, **Every mark's steps** goes to the Marks tab. For the Unbound the Marks tab is called **Body**: a card for each part's cup, and **Move essence** under them; beside it they get a **Techniques** tab, where they change their techniques (see **The Unbound**). Every word is in its `TEXT`.
- **The practice room** (`scripts/practice.gd`, the dummies in `scripts/dummy.gd`): **Try your abilities** on the train drops you into a quiet room of the last tower with three training dummies: straw on a post with a painted target. They take every hit (and burns, slows, staggers and shoves), show the damage, never move or strike, and never fall (their health comes back a moment after the last hit). Your marks and techniques start full and refill a moment after each cast; strain still builds, so the Unbound can feel it, but nothing there can fell you. Nothing done there is kept (the save is left alone). A line at the foot of the screen says how to leave: the pause menu's **Back to the train** (or a restart) goes back to the train, on the Abilities page.
- **A mark's page:** choose a card on the Marks tab (click or tap it, or focus it and press **Enter** or **A**). It shows the mark's emblem, its body part and essence, its step and growth (and the banked essence each step needs, or the essence a faint mark still owes), its **Body** and **Passive** effects, and **every spell**: the step that teaches it, how to cast it, its cost, wind-up and other numbers, what it does and what each later step changes. Spells not learned yet are dimmed with a lock and "learned at step N". The steps are listed at the bottom with what each brings. An unmarked part's page says what soaks into it and, where your next mark is forming, which bond it would be. **Q** / **E** (**LB** / **RB**, or the buttons either side of Back) show the next mark; **Back** (**Esc**, **B**) returns to the cards, and **Tab** closes the screen. **Up** / **down** scroll the page.
- **Inventory:** **I** (**d-pad up**, the **Bag** button on a phone). A **paper doll**: what you wear, laid out like a body (helm, amulet, cloak / melee weapon, chest, ranged weapon / ring, gloves, ring / backpack, legs, boots), with fists, the off-hand (a second weapon, a shield or a wand; its card feeds an enchanted one's pool), gear lying at your feet (marked "new") and a camp's rune beside it, and your belt below: its items with their counts (the quick item gilded) and an empty cell for each kind it still has room for. **Worn / Pack / Heirlooms** at the top switches the doll to your pack's spare pieces or your heirlooms. On the train, I opens your car instead. The weapon in hand is gilded, empty slots show their name, Common pieces show their condition as a thin bar, finer pieces are edged in their rarity's colour, and a piece that is also your heirloom has a small gold mark. Hover over or focus a slot to see its card on the right; press it (**Enter**, **A**, a click or a tap) and, with the keys or a controller, the focus moves onto the card's first button (**B**, **Esc** or **Left** comes back to the slot; the train's cards work the same way): what it does, its traits (each in its essence's colour), its rarity and condition, and its numbers set against the gear at your feet or what every climb starts with (green up arrows where it's better, red down arrows where it's worse), with a "plays like" line for weapons ("Compared with yours: hits harder, slower"); the gear at your feet against what you wear, with **Take it** (or, with two rings on, a button to swap either ring); or an item's text with **Use** (or press the item's slot a second time: the first press shows it, with every input) and **Make it your quick item**.

**Every menu moves the same way** (`scripts/nav.gd`): the mouse moves the focus as it passes over a button; the arrow keys, the d-pad and the left stick are one and the same, a step per press, and holding one repeats at the same steady rate whatever you hold; **Enter**, **A**, a click or a tap presses; **Esc**, **B** or a right-click goes back one level and puts the focus back on what opened the page (in a text box they only leave the box); **Q** / **E** (**LB** / **RB**) switch tabs, the inventory's Worn / Pack / Heirlooms included, and the focus moves onto the new tab; the **mouse wheel** and the **right stick** scroll (the settings' sliders never take the wheel); single lists (the rail, the title, the appearance rows, the card screens) wrap round, while grids and long pages stop at their edges; the confirmation pop-up starts on **Cancel**; and the prompts follow what you used last, even halfway through a page; picking up another device never costs a press: its first button, key, click or tap does its job, and the page's words change once it's let go. On a phone nothing shows a focus outline.

**Scrolling, answers and asking first** (one way everywhere):
- **Scrolling** (`scripts/scroller.gd`): the **mouse wheel** moves the list or page under the pointer a good step a notch (120 px, or a fifth of the view if that's more; Godot's own was an eighth), eased over a few frames. A **finger** drags the page one to one (it only starts after 14 px, so a scroll never presses a button) and a flick carries on, slowing down, when it's let go; a touch while it glides only stops it. **Page Up** / **Page Down** (**LT** / **RT**) jump most of a view, **Home** / **End** go to the top and the bottom, and the **right stick** scrolls faster the longer it's held. Swiping sideways on the train still moves between cars.
- **The page stays where it was**: a press that changes something builds the page again (`menu.gd open`), and before, it started again at the top (on a phone nothing holds the focus, so the old code scrolled back to 0). Now each scroll on a page built again as it was (the same page and tab: `_view_key`) is put back where it stood (`scroller.gd snapshot` / `keep_from`), and the keys' focus stays on the same button; another page or tab starts at its top.
- **Answers** (`menu.gd feedback`, `scripts/press_flash.gd`): buying, selling, choosing, equipping, learning and the bench's work each flash the button pressed (flat, in the UI colours: gilt for coins and crafting, green for a choice, grey for putting back) and play a sound from the sound bank (`buy` and `sell` coins, `glint` for a choice, the bench's own sounds), at the Sounds volume.
- **Asking first** (`scripts/confirm_pop.gd`, `menu.gd ask`): what can't be undone or costs a lot opens one small pop-up saying what will happen, with **Confirm** (named for the action) and **Cancel**. The focus starts on Cancel; Esc, B, a right-click or a tap outside cancels; it fits a phone held sideways. It asks before: choosing a branch on the Tree (and learning a passed side again), sealing and unsealing a Node, breaking a faint mark's seal, Start over (the menus' button, the emergency brake and **N**), selling, the bench's work (temper, retrait, join, break down, inscribe, refill or craft a charm, the vessel's deep work), trading a vessel, deleting a climber, being marked again (the Developer), letting a path go, reading a recall scroll from the inventory, and the Data editor's Put all back.

The same key closes its screen (Tab closes the climber screen, I the inventory), and **Esc** or **B** (Circle) goes back a page, or out of the menu from the pause menu or a screen opened by its key; every page shows its Back or Close button with the keys that do the same. **Settings** has music and sound volume, screen shake, the on-screen controls list (off at first), the **Interface size** (Small, Normal, Large or Larger: how big the menus, the rail, the train's interface, the HUD and the touch buttons are drawn; on a phone Normal is 1.25x the old phone size, Small is the old size, and on a computer Normal is as it always was; on a narrow screen a row that no longer fits wraps onto the next line, a grid loses columns and the page, the rail or the train car scrolls instead of shrinking, see `scripts/reflow.gd`; at Large and Larger the touch buttons keep the size they have at Normal, since a bigger size would leave no room for the game), the **World zoom** (Normal, Closer or Closest: how close the camera is to the climber; a phone also starts a little closer than it used to; the fog, lights and the stair's arrow follow the camera), the **HUD size** (small, normal or large, on top of the Interface size), the **Lighting** quality (Low, Medium or High: see The look below), the Entity's **guide tips** (on or off, and **Replay tips**) and **Controller setup** (see Controls above); they're saved. The Entity presents Settings and the Record: its face and a line sit at the top of both. The **Controls** page lists every key and controller button side by side. On a phone the climber screen and inventory tighten up to fit the screen, and scroll where they still can't.

The menus and HUD share one look, taken from the old web game's screens and worked further: a deep violet-indigo night, gilt edges and lettering, warm parchment ink, thin-bordered cards lit faintly from the top, small gothic labels over titles and sections, gilt corner flourishes and double rules with a diamond, and essence colours for essence things. Every title, heading, label, button, tab and name (on cards, over gear on the floor, the beast's bar, the HUD's big words) is set in the gothic **Grenze Gotisch**, always in mixed case since blackletter capitals run together; the text under them in its plain sibling **Grenze**, which stays readable small on a phone (its SemiBold with even-width figures for key badges and numbers that change in place); and words written in the world or quoted (puzzle rule panels, riddles, the Makers' fragments and words, room rules, the Proving's door words, mottos, the title's tagline) in the fine italic **Cormorant Garamond**. They're in `assets/fonts` (SIL Open Font License; also included in the web build), set up once in `ui.gd` as named roles (`ROLES`: display, eyebrow, body, mono, inscription) that every script asks for. The interface colours, fonts, panel, card, button and slot styles (an `Ornate` style box drawn in code: a gradient, a soft shadow, a lit top edge, a gilt hairline and corner flourishes), text sizes and the drawn pieces (bars, essence gems, mark medallions and emblems, key badges, locks, the rail's icons, dividers) live in `scripts/ui.gd`; the item and gear icons are drawn in `scripts/items.gd`, `scripts/gear.gd` and `scripts/vessel.gd` in one shared style from `scripts/icon_art.gd`: slim, colourful shapes with some air round them, smooth gradient shading lit from the top-left, a light violet ink outline scaled to the icon's size, steel with a cool shine and a soft sheen band and gold (the menus' gilt) with a warm one, glass with the liquid inside and a rim light, a faint cool bounce light on the shadowed edges, a soft contact shadow under each icon, and finer detail (grain, seams of stitches on leather, rivets, fullers, twine round a cork) only where the icon is big enough. Named pieces look like themselves: the Coral Trident's three prongs, the Cinder Axe's glowing edge, the Thornmaul's briar, the Wyvern Scaleplate's scales, the beasts' Regarded pieces their own bone, plume and tail. Gear finer than Common glows softly behind its icon in its rarity's colour, brighter the rarer (Fine green, Rare blue, Storied amber, Regarded gold), in its slot and on the floor alike. Each icon is built once per size and copied after that, so drawing them is cheap. Each mark's emblem follows its essence: Air a three-bladed swirl, Water a drop, Fire a flame, Stone a cut gem, Iron a shield, Blade a four-pointed star. The HUD is kept small so the tower gets the screen, in the same look: the bars sit in gilt frames with a dark bezel, a gloss and finials (health turns green while you're poisoned, and health lost to a burned heart or a wound is hatched), the weapon and item slots are chamfered wells, marks are engraved medallions (a red, cracked rim once burned), banners are soft bands between gilt rules, status lines (a shut door, a challenge's rule) sit in cartouches, and a beast's bar is framed with horns and a lingering chunk for the damage just done. The touch buttons are glassy discs in a bezel with a gilt ring (a mark's button fills with essence and glows when it can cast), and every selection wheel (spells, items, a secret bond's tools) is one drawing in `ui.gd` (`draw_wheel`): the world dimmed, a dark disc in an engraved gilt rim, each option in a medallion with its icon, name and cost as gems, the pointed-at one lifted and glowing in its colour. Always there, top left: slim health (with the numbers on the bar) and stamina bars, the weapon in hand and the quick item (small, with its use key and count), and a small medallion per mark that fills with essence and glows once a spell can be cast (on a phone the mark's button lights up too). The rest fades in only when it matters: the essence you've gathered since the last stair (when you pick some up), your three weapons for a moment when you swap, the floor's title when you arrive, the open stair, and for the first seconds of a floor the keys for the menu, climber screen, inventory and controls (H shows every key). The weapon slots, the armor, every item, your attributes and where your next mark is forming are on the climber screen (Tab) and in the inventory (I).
