On a computer, hovering the mouse over a HUD slot or mark shows what it is and its numbers (a mark or technique: its spell's name, what it does, its essence or fill, how to cast it, and "All your abilities: hold Tab"). **Names in play** (`scripts/ability_hints.gd`): on a computer or with a controller, when a mark or technique fills enough to cast, its spell's name fades in on a small cartouche under its medallion and fades out again; on a phone each cast button carries its spell's whole name (on one line, or two), and a long press on a cast button that opens no ring (a technique, or a mark with one spell) shows a card beside it with the name, what it does and its numbers, and casts nothing (a tap still casts). The first time a climber casts each spell or technique, a one-line toast at the top names it and says what it does ("Ember Bolt: a bolt thrown where you aim; it burns what it hits"), once per spell per climber (kept with the Entity's tips; quiet with tips off). The HUD's always-there part is only redrawn when one of its values changes, and the touch buttons too, which keeps phones fast.

The **dev tools** page is for testing (`scripts/menu.gd`, with the actions in `main.gd`):
- **Edit game data:** the Data editor, at the top (see Editing the game's data below).
- **Marks:** give any bond to any body part with the arrows, and set its step from 1 to 5. Changes are saved, and giving a mark ends the bondless first climb. Fill essence, or clear every mark.
- **Climb:** move to another tower, change floor, or go straight to the tower's beast.
- **Practice a beast:** fight any of the six beasts at once, at full health with full marks.
- **Spawn a monster:** any monster or beast, near you.
- **Gear:** give yourself any weapon or armor (it replaces what's in that slot).
- **Puzzles:** put one kind of puzzle on every floor of rooms (or back to the usual mix), and go to a floor of rooms to try it.
- **Secret essences:** take the Making or Unmaking path at once, bank +25 or +100 flow (to try each step), reset the path, or show the path choice.
- **Secret bonds:** grant the Developer, Designer or Manipulator at any depth from 1 to 5, or clear it; carry +100 essence and +20 flow (to pay for tools); +150 crystal essence (for Tune, Edit, moving marks, a bribe and a change of path).
- **Layout:** **Arrange layout** opens Arrange mode for the HUD and touch buttons, **Arrange menus** for the Dev tools page (F2 with any page open arranges that page; see Arranging the layout below).
- **Cheats:** no dying (a killing blow leaves you at 1 health), heal, clear the room, and show traps (outlines every trap and its reach, hidden ones too).
- **Creator tools: Paint rooms** opens the room painter (see below).

## The room painter (making rooms without code)

**Dev tools → Paint rooms** (`scripts/painter.gd`) paints the climb's hand-made rooms tile by tile, the same small grids as `rooms.gd`, without touching code. It fills the screen:
- **The grid** in the middle: the room, drawn in the game's own tile look (`scripts/painter_tiles.gd`: flagstones, walls with their brick faces, low walls, pillars, chasms, pools, monster spots and the puzzle pieces) in the current tower's colours. Small gilt marks outside its edges show where a corridor can join it. Pinch with two fingers, use the mouse wheel or the Zoom buttons to zoom; two fingers or the middle mouse button move the view.
- **The tools** down the left: Brush, Fill (an area of the same tile), Box (a filled box, dragged corner to corner), Eraser (floor), Mirror left-right and top-bottom (what you paint is copied to the other side), Undo, Redo and Zoom.
- **The palette** down the right: every tile with its look and name (floor, wall, rock, pillar, chasm, water, monster spot, random, and a puzzle room's rail, stone eye, plate, block, gate, lever and reward). Under it **Use in climbs** and **the checks**.
- **The top bar:** Back (to Dev tools), the room's name and state, **Rooms** (every room, yours first, then the built-ins: open, copy, switch on or off, rename or delete yours; built-in rooms can be opened and copied but never changed, and painting one makes your own copy), **Room** (its name, job: start, combat, stair or puzzle, the puzzle and tier for a puzzle room, width and height from 7 x 6 up to 14 x 12, first floor, whether it may flip, which towers may use it, and which tower's look it's shown in), **Save** (Save, Copy rooms and Paste rooms, with a text box), **Try it** and **?** (the Entity's explanation again).

**Controls.** Mouse: click or drag to paint, right-click erases. Touch: tap or drag to paint; tap the buttons. Keys: the arrows move a cursor on the grid, Enter or Space paints (hold it and move to paint along), Delete or Backspace erases, Q and E change the tile, Tab goes to the buttons (and back), Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y undo and redo, Esc leaves. Controller: the d-pad or stick moves the cursor, A paints, B erases, LB and RB change the tile, X the tool, Y goes to the buttons (B comes back), Start leaves. On the buttons the usual menu rules apply (`nav.gd`).

**Checks** (`scripts/room_check.gd`) run as you paint and say what's wrong in plain words, outlined on the grid (tap one to find it): a side with no room for a doorway (a corridor needs two open tiles side by side, two deep), a stair with no open floor at the top middle or that can't be reached, a monster spot, a doorway, where the climber starts or a plate that can't be reached, a **low wall that cuts something off** (the game's own check, `rooms.gd` `cover_kept`, which main.gd also uses on every floor: in play such a wall would be left out), and what a puzzle room needs (eyes that can be hit, plates, blocks that can be pushed, a reward behind its gate). Chasms are only real in the Firebird's tower, so a room can pass in one tower and not in another; it's only offered where it passes. A room that fails is a **draft**: it can be saved and tried, but the climb won't use it.

**Try it** drops you into the room at once, in the tower you're in, beside a small hall where you start, with a monster on each monster spot (none in a start room), and you can't fall. The pause key (Esc, Start, Menu) or the stair brings you back to the painter, and the floor you were on is laid out again (`floors.gd` `try_plan`, `main.gd` `try_room`).

**Use in climbs.** A room switched on that passes its checks joins the floor planner's pool for its job (`rooms.gd` `custom`, `for_role`), alongside the built-ins: it can flip, never appears twice on a floor, and only from its first floor and in its towers.

**Keeping rooms** (`scripts/room_store.gd`), the same pattern as the other creator tools:
- Run from the Godot editor, **Save** writes `res://data/rooms_custom.json`. GitHub Desktop then shows the change: write a summary, **Commit to main**, **Push origin**, and the rooms are in every copy of the game, the web build included.
- Anywhere else (the web build, a phone), Save writes `user://rooms_custom.json`, on that device only. **Copy rooms** puts all your rooms on the clipboard and in the text box, to paste to Claude or into **Paste rooms** on the computer (then Save there). Pasting a room with the id of one you have updates it; the rest are added.
- At startup the project's file is read, then the device's on top. Leaving the painter saves what isn't saved yet.

The file is plain JSON, one room per entry, its grid one row per line:

```json
{"format": 1, "rooms": [
	{"id": "painted_1", "name": "Broken colonnade", "role": "combat", "from": 2, "flip": true,
	 "towers": [], "on": true, "grid": [".............", ".o.o.....o.o.", "..."]}
]}
```

`role` is the job, `from` the first floor, `towers` the tower ids that may use it (empty for all), `on` whether it's used in climbs; puzzle rooms add `"puzzle"` (`"eyes"` or `"plates"`) and `"tier"` (1 or 2). The device's file can also list `"deleted"` project rooms. The Entity explains each part the first time (the Archive's "Creator tools").

## Arranging the layout (Arrange mode)

You can move and resize the HUD, the phone's buttons, the menus, the left rail and the train's furniture yourself, by hand, and keep the result. Arrange mode never shows everything at once: it shows **a few big blocks**, with **a list of them at the side**, and you step into a block when you want its smaller parts. The Entity explains it the first time and leaves three steps in the list until you've done them (the toolbar's **?** shows them again; they're also in the Archive under **Creator tools**).

**The short version (three steps).**
1. Open the screen you want to change and press **F2**. In the **Blocks** list, click the name of the block you want to move. It lights up on the screen, with square handles on its corners.
2. Drag the block where you want it (press the mouse button anywhere on it, move, let go).
3. Click **Save**.

**Arranging a train car, step by step (computer).**
1. Walk to the car you want (click it on the rail at the left, for example **Your Car**).
2. Press **F2**. The car's furniture shows as blocks: the wardrobe, the pack, the stash, the book, each sign, the placard, the **Crystal bar** along the bottom and the left rail.
3. To move a whole piece, drag it. To move something *on* a piece (the stash's heading, its grid of spaces, the Open the stash button), **double-click the piece** (or click the **>** on its row in the list). Now only its parts show.
4. Drag a part where you want it, or pull one of its corner handles to make it bigger or smaller.
5. To move one single space (one stash slot, one supply), double-click the grid (**Stash spaces**, **Supplies**...) to go inside it, then drag the space.
6. **Esc** (or **Back out**) goes back up a level. **‹ Car** and **Car ›** in the toolbar move along the train without leaving Arrange mode.
7. Click **Save**, then **Done**.

**Arranging a train car, tap by tap (phone).** F2 doesn't exist on a phone, so:
1. On the train, tap **Settings** on the rail at the left (the Settings & Controls car).
2. In its signal panel (the **Settings** tab), scroll down to the bottom and tap **Arrange the train**. (Or tap **Dev** on the rail, then **Arrange the train**.)
3. Arrange mode opens on this car. Tap **‹ Car** or **Car ›** at the left of the toolbar until you're in the car you want (the toolbar's line and the list's top say which car).
4. The **Blocks** list stands at one side (over the rail), the toolbar along the bottom. Tap a row in the list (or a block itself) to choose it.
5. To go inside a piece, tap the **>** on its row, or double-tap the piece. Tap **Back out** to come up again.
6. Drag the chosen block with your finger to move it. Pull a gold corner square to size it (on something small, the squares stand just off its corners, so you can still drag its middle).
7. To reach something lower down (a car that scrolls), drag an empty part of the screen up or down.
8. Tap **Save**, then **Done**.
The list and the toolbar move out of the way by themselves: when the block you've chosen is under the toolbar, it jumps to the top; when it's under the list, the list goes to the other side. **More** shows the toolbar's other buttons (Redo, Reset page, Reset all, Grid, Copy layout, the computer/phone switch, Arrange the HUD and ?).

**Opening it.**
- **Without a keyboard (a phone):** **Settings & Controls → Arrange this screen** arranges the page showing; on the train it's in the Settings car and says **Arrange the train**. **Dev tools → Arrange the train** does the same from the Dev page.
- **The HUD and the phone's buttons:** press **F2** during a climb (or **Dev tools → Arrange layout**). The game pauses.
- **A menu:** open the page you want to change (the inventory, the climber's book, Settings, the pause menu, the Library, a station's page...) and press **F2** (or **Dev tools → Arrange menus** for the Dev tools page itself). The page stays showing.
- **A train car:** walk to the car, then press **F2**. To arrange another car, click **‹ Car** or **Car ›** in the toolbar (or click **Done**, move along the train and press **F2** again).
- The toolbar's **Arrange the HUD** / **Arrange the menu** button (when a menu is open) switches between the two.

**What you see.**
- **Blocks.** Each block has one gold outline, a number and its name. At first only the big pieces show:
  - over a menu: the **page** and the **left rail** (two blocks);
  - in a train car: each **piece of furniture** (the wardrobe, the pack, the stash, the book, each sign, the placard...), the **Crystal bar** along the bottom and the left rail;
  - in a climb: the HUD's groups: the **Status corner** (health, weapon, item, marks...), the **Key hints**, the **Top notices** (the top-middle lane: its announcements, and the status lines under them), **Pickups and news** (the left feed), the stair arrow's ring and **the Entity** (its sphere, bubble and panel); on a phone also the **Action buttons** and the **Menu buttons**.
  A dashed, fainter outline is something that isn't on screen right now (an announcement, say), drawn where it will appear. Where a block is too small for its name, only its number shows; the list names every number.
- **The Blocks list** (at the side) names the blocks you're looking at, in order. Pointing at a row lights its block up on the screen; **clicking a row chooses that block**. On a row:
  - a **gold dot** means that block has been moved or resized (a ring: only something inside it has);
  - the **round arrow** puts that block, and everything inside it, back where it was built;
  - the **>** goes inside the block;
  - a **red triangle** means it overlaps the block next to it.
  Under the rows the list says, for the chosen block, how far it has moved and how big it is, in plain numbers: for example `Moved 24 right, 16 down` and `Size 110%`.
- **The toolbar:** **Back out**, **Go inside**, **Undo**, **Redo**, **Reset this**, **Reset page** (over a menu: everything showing), **Reset all**, **Grid**, **List** (hides or shows the list), **Copy layout**, **Save** and **Done**. Its first line says where you are (the breadcrumb) and what's chosen.
- Over a menu, the list and the toolbar stand in a column over the left rail, so the page itself is never covered. When you choose the rail, the column steps over to the right. You can drag either one anywhere by its title bar (the one with the six dots).

**Going inside a block, and back out.**
- To arrange the parts of a block, **go inside it**: double-click the block (or click its row twice, or click the **>** on its row, or choose it and press **Enter** or click **Go inside**).
- Now only that block's own parts show (everything outside it is dimmed), and the list names them. For the inventory page that's its **Title**, its **Left side** and its **Right side**. Go inside the right side and there is the **Item details card**.
- In a train car, go inside a piece of furniture and its own parts show: its heading, its grid of slots, its buttons, its notes; on the placard its heading, its lines and its button; inside the speech, the face and the bubble. Go inside a grid (Stash spaces, Supplies, Tower plaques, Shelves...) for each slot or plaque on its own. Some pieces are drawn as one picture; those have parts of their own made for Arrange mode: the **workshop's wall** (Hanging lamp, Pegboard of tools, Shelf of bottles, Workbench and flask, Rune slate) and the **Crystal bar** (each kind's crystals).
- To go back up, click **Back out** (in the list or the toolbar), press **Esc**, or double-click outside the block you're in.
- The breadcrumb at the top of the list always says where you are, for example `Inventory › Inventory page › Right side: the details`.
- You only ever see one level's outlines at a time.

**Moving and sizing the chosen block.**
- **Move:** drag it by its body (anywhere inside its outline; the little four-way arrow in its middle is a reminder).
- **Bigger or smaller:** drag one of the big square **corner handles** (it keeps its shape), or turn the **mouse wheel** while pointing at it, or press **[** and **]**.
- **Wider or narrower:** a few blocks have long blue **side handles**: the page (pull its side: its rows wrap to fit), the left rail (pull its right edge; the wheel changes its text size) and, inside the rail, its list (pull its bottom edge to spread the pages or cars apart).
- **Nudge:** the **arrow keys** move it 8 pixels (1 with **Shift**).
- **Snapping:** it snaps to an 8-pixel grid, to the screen's edges and centre lines, and to the edges of the blocks beside it; a thin blue line shows what it snapped to. Hold **Shift** to place it freely (on a phone, turn **Grid** off).
- **Overlaps:** if a block ends up on top of its neighbour, both outlines turn red, the shared part is shaded red, and the list says `Overlaps Marks` (or whatever it's lying on).
- **Changed your mind?** **Undo** (Ctrl+Z) takes back the last change and **Redo** (Ctrl+Y) does it again. **Reset this** (or the round arrow on its row) puts the chosen block and its parts back as built; **Reset page** everything on the page showing; **Reset all** everything (the last two ask for a second press).
- A group in the HUD (the Status corner, say) moves and scales all its parts together; go inside it to move one part on its own.
- Some things can't be dragged and Arrange mode doesn't pretend they can: the left rail stays at the left edge (only its width and text size change). Things that fill the screen (the ring the stair arrow travels along, the phone's joystick zone) are in the list but have no outline until you choose them there.
- Nothing can be dragged off the screen: HUD things keep at least a corner on it, the phone's buttons stay wholly on it and can't be made too small to tap, a page stays on the screen, what's on a page stays inside the page, the rail's parts inside the rail and a car's furniture inside its car. Leaving with changes you haven't saved asks whether to save them.

**Keys and buttons.**

| | Keyboard and mouse | Controller |
|---|---|---|
| Choose a block | click it, or its row in the list; **Tab** / **Shift+Tab** for the next / the one before | **d-pad** or **LB / RB** |
| Go inside it | double-click, or **Enter** | **A** |
| Back out | **Esc** (or **Backspace**, or a right click) | **B** |
| Move it | drag it; **arrow keys** nudge | **left stick** |
| Size it | drag a corner; the **wheel** over it; **[** and **]** | **right stick** (up and down) |
| Undo / Redo | **Ctrl+Z** / **Ctrl+Y** | **X** (undo) |
| Reset the chosen block | **Delete** | |
| Save | **Ctrl+S** | **Y** |
| Grid on or off / List on or off | **G** / **L** | **View** (grid) |
| Finish | **F2**, or **Esc** at the top level, or **Done** | **Start** |

On a phone: tap a block (or its row) to choose it, drag it to move it, pull a corner to size it, double-tap (or the row's **>**) to go inside, drag an empty part of the screen to scroll, drag the list's rows to scroll the list, and use the toolbar's buttons for the rest. On a phone's short screen the toolbar is one strip (**More** shows the rest) and, over a menu or the train, the list stands over the rail; both step aside from the block you've chosen. Elsewhere on a small screen the list starts hidden: tap **List** to show it.

**What can be arranged.**
- The HUD: health and stamina, the weapon, the current item, the marks, the **Item and spell wheel** (it opens around the climber, or by the thumb on a phone, until you move it; then it opens where you put it, at the size you gave it), the path gem and vessel, the unbanked essence counter, the key hints, the **announcements** (the top-middle lane's banner), the **status lines** under them (the task, "doors shut", a beast's health), the **feed** (pickups and news, on the left), the ring the stair arrow travels along (on a phone the arrow slides along it to stay clear of the touch buttons), and the Entity (its sphere and bubble). The old floor title, centre banner, beast bar and shut-door plate are gone (they all live in the lane now); an arrangement saved for one of them is dropped when the file is read (`DROPPED` in `layout.gd`).
- The phone: the joystick zone and every button (attack, roll, parry, jump, swap, each mark button, item, next item, take, tools, Bag, Climber, Menu, fullscreen). Where a button is drawn is exactly where it answers a tap.
- Every menu page: the page itself (move, scale, make wider or narrower) and, inside it, its title, its tabs or ribbons, each row, grid, column and paragraph; inside those, their columns and cards (the inventory's item card, the climber screen's cards, the book's pages, grimoire and technique cards, the appearance page's live preview). This works for every page, new ones too: the game finds them as the page is built. A page with tabs (the climber's book, Settings & Controls) is arranged per tab; the page's own box is shared by its tabs. What's scrolled out of sight isn't offered: press Done, scroll the page, and press F2 again.
- The left rail: its width and its text size; inside it, who you are (the block at the top), the list of pages or cars (and its spacing) and the bottom buttons.
- The train, car by car: each piece of furniture and placard (the heirloom wardrobe, the pack and the stash, the book on its cushion, the outfitter, his shelves, rail, glass case, charm tray, upgrade tree and sign, the bench and its drawers, the tower board, the bookcase and lectern, the signal panel, the brake), the Entity's or the outfitter's words, the signs, buttons and notes that stand on their own, and the crystal bar. What's in a piece moves with it; go inside a piece to move its heading, grid, buttons and words, and inside a grid to move one slot. The Outfitter's Sell section, the workshop's other counters and the Settings car's Controls tab each have their own parts as they show.
- Not split into parts (each is one drawn thing, and its pieces only make sense together): the book on its cushion, the outfitter himself, a tower plaque, a slot (its icon, price and edge), the furniture's own woodwork (it follows its slots wherever they're moved), and the views outside the windows.
- Buttons stay buttons: a moved or scaled button is pressed where it's drawn, with the mouse, a finger, the keys or a controller.

**Names.** The names Arrange mode shows are in tables you can edit: the HUD's and the phone's in `scripts/layout.gd` (`ELEMENTS`, `TOUCH` and, for the groups, `GROUPS`), the menus' in `scripts/arrange_ui.gd` (`NAMES`: one line per block, such as `"menu.inventory.row1.column2": "Right side: the details"`; `"*.title"` names every page's title, and `"*.placard.button"` every block whose id ends in `.placard.button`, in every car). A block nobody named is called by what it is and the first words on it (`Row “Music”`, `Heading “Layout”`), so new pages get names by themselves. Changing a name never changes what's saved: saved layouts go by the short ids underneath (see The file, below), which stay the same.

**Two layouts.** The computer and the phone each have their own layout (the phone draws everything bigger, at the Interface size, and adds the touch buttons). On a computer, the toolbar's switch shows the phone's layout the way a phone draws it, so you can arrange the phone's buttons from your desk too. HUD things are pinned to the nearest corner or edge of the screen, so they keep their place on other screen sizes, turned sideways, and at every HUD and Interface size (at Large and Larger the phone's buttons and their arrangement shrink together to fit the shorter view, so an arranged button keeps its place). Menus, the rail and the train are kept as a move from their own built-in place, measured as a share of the screen, so a menu arranged on the computer keeps its proportions on a phone; the phone uses the computer's arrangement of them unless you arrange the phone's own.

**Keeping your layout.**
- **Save** keeps it on the device you're using (in the game's own save folder, `user://layout.cfg`). Only that device sees it.
- **To make it everyone's layout** (the one the web version starts with), save it from your computer, inside Godot:
  1. Open the project in Godot and press **F5** to play.
  2. Press **F2** (in a climb for the HUD, or with a menu open for that menu), arrange things, and press **Save**. Because the game is running from Godot, Save also writes the file `data/layout.json` in the project folder (the toolbar says so).
  3. Open **GitHub Desktop**. On the left you'll see `data/layout.json` listed as changed.
  4. At the bottom left, type a short summary, for example `New HUD layout`.
  5. Click the blue **Commit to main** button.
  6. Click **Push origin** at the top. A few minutes later the web version starts with your layout.
- **From a phone (or the web version):** Save can't reach the project, so tap **Copy layout**. It copies the layout to the clipboard and shows it in a box (on a phone: long-press in the box, **Select all**, **Copy**). Paste it to Claude in the chat, and Claude puts it into `data/layout.json` for you.
- **Reset this** / **Reset page** / **Reset all** go back to the shared layout in `data/layout.json` (run from Godot, where Save writes that file, they go back to the built-in layout).

**The file.** `data/layout.json` (and what Copy layout gives) looks like this; an empty set means "as built", and anything not listed stays exactly as built:

```json
{
	"desktop": {
		"hud.bars": {"anchor": "bl", "scale": 1.25, "x": 16.0, "y": -16.0},
		"menu.inventory.panel": {"anchor": "own", "scale": 0.9, "w": 0.85, "x": -0.03, "y": 0.0},
		"rail.box": {"anchor": "own", "scale": 1.1, "w": 1.2, "x": 0.0, "y": 0.0},
		"train.personal.book": {"anchor": "own", "scale": 1.3, "x": -0.05, "y": -0.04}
	},
	"format": 1,
	"touch": {
		"touch.attack": {"anchor": "br", "scale": 1.1, "x": -12.0, "y": -8.0}
	}
}
```

Each entry names a thing and says where it goes:
- `hud.…` and `touch.…` things: the corner or edge it's pinned to (`tl`, `t`, `tr`, `l`, `c`, `r`, `bl`, `b`, `br`), how far its own matching corner sits from the screen's (x and y; in HUD units for `hud.` things, which the HUD size setting multiplies), and its scale. Their list, names and built-in places are in `scripts/layout.gd`.
- `menu.…`, `rail.…` and `train.…` things have the anchor `own`: x and y move them from their built-in place by that share of the screen's width and height, scale grows them about their middle, and `w` / `h` (where they can be resized) are their width / height as a share of the built-in one. Their names are the page (and its tab), then the element: `menu.inventory.row1.column2.card1` is the first card in the second column of the inventory's first row; `train.store.shelf` the Outfitter's shelves; `train.store.shelf.grid` its supplies and `train.personal.chest.grid.slot3` the third space of the stash in Your Car; `train.workshop.bench.slate` the slate drawn on the workshop wall; `train.crystals.water` the Water crystals on the band along the bottom. Arrange mode shows each thing's friendly name instead (see Names, above); the ids are what's saved, and they don't change. The HUD's groups (the Status corner, the Top notices, the phone's Action buttons and Menu buttons) are never saved themselves: moving a group saves each of its parts.

For code: Arrange mode itself is `scripts/arrange.gd` (the levels: `tree`, `enter`, `back_out`; the list: `list_panel`; the toolbar: `toolbar`); `scripts/arrange_ui.gd` finds the menus', the rail's and the train's elements as each page is built (`tag_page`, called from `menu.gd` `open`), says what sits inside what (`parent_of`) and what each is called (`friendly`), and puts their arrangement on top of where their containers lay them out. A page needs nothing of its own; a row can name its kind with `set_meta("arrange", "title")` (as `menu.gd` `_header`, `_tabs` and `_footer` do). A train car's furniture is gone into by `_tag_inside` (its parts, then a grid's slots: `PART_DEPTH`); something drawn in one `_draw` gets parts through `scripts/drawn_parts.gd` (an empty control laid over each part, and `xform` to draw that part where it was moved), as the workshop's `Bench` and the crystals' `Purse` do. The HUD's groups are `GROUPS` in `scripts/layout.gd`.

## Editing the game's data (no code)

**Dev tools → Edit game data** changes the game's **words, numbers and colours** from inside the game, and keeps the changes for good. No code and no prompts: pick a field, change it, done. The Entity explains the page the first time it opens (and how to keep changes the first time you press Save); the **?** button next to Save explains it again at any time, and its words are kept in the Archive under **Creator tools**.

**Finding things.** The tabs along the top are **Words**, **Numbers**, **Colours**, **Appearance** and **My changes** (Q / E or LB / RB switch tabs). Each tab lists its sections: for Words, the Entity's words, the Makers, items and camps, gear, weapons, bonds and marks, towers and rooms, the train and store, the climb (wings, finds, mirrors, traps), puzzles, the secret essences and bonds, the Unbound, and the title and climbers; for Numbers, store prices, climb pay, mark growth and essence, the climber's core numbers, the marks' spells (every bond's costs, damage, reach and timings, step by step), monsters, items, gear and drops, weapons, the workshop and vessel, wings and rewards, traps, the Makers and the secret essences; for Colours, essences, rarities and items, menus and HUD, the world, tower tints, and monsters and traps; Appearance has the appearance options' names and colours. Or type in the **search box**: it finds any field by its name, its key or what it says ("draught", "price", "stair").

**Changing things.** Each field shows its name, its value and (small, underneath) its key, like `items/ITEMS/draught/heal`.
- **Words**: a text box. Click or tap it (or press its **Edit** button with the keys), type, and press Enter or click elsewhere. On a phone's browser, tapping opens the browser's own text box. Words with blanks such as `%d` or `%s` (where the game puts a number or a name) must keep them, or the change is refused. If you type one of the words kept out of the game ("mana", "crag", "trial", "judgment", "attunement") you get a warning; the change is still kept.
- **Numbers**: a slider (drag it, or Left / Right with the keys or d-pad) and a box to type an exact number in. Numbers stay within sensible limits (usually up to ten times the default), and whole numbers stay whole.
- **Colours**: a swatch and a `#rrggbbaa` box. Press the swatch for sliders: hue, colour, light and opacity.
- **On / off**: a switch.

A changed field turns **gilt**, shows its **default** under it and gets a **Default** button that puts it back. **My changes** lists everything you changed, with Default on each and **Put all back** (it asks first, in a pop-up). Most changes show at once (a new item name, a price in the store the next time you look, an Entity tip the next time it's said); a field's row says when it waits for the next page, the next floor or, for a few things read only as the game starts, a restart.

**Keeping changes for good.** Changes save by themselves a second after you make them (and when you leave the page; **Save** does it at once). Where they go depends on where you play:

- **On your computer, running the game from Godot** (open the project in Godot and press the play button or **F5**): Save writes into the project's own file, `data/overrides.json`. To make the changes part of the game for everyone (the web version on your phone too):
  1. Open **GitHub Desktop**. In the **Changes** tab on the left you'll see `data/overrides.json`.
  2. In the **Summary** box at the bottom left, write a few words about what you changed, for example "Cheaper vials".
  3. Click **Commit to main**.
  4. Click **Push origin** at the top. A few minutes later the web version has your changes.
- **On your phone or in a browser** (the web version): Save keeps the changes **on that device only**, so you can try them. To keep them for good, press **Copy changes**: the changes are copied (on a phone, a box opens: press and hold in it, **Select all**, **Copy**). Paste them into a message to Claude and ask for them to be added to the game. Or, on your computer, run the game from Godot, open Dev tools → Edit game data, press **Paste changes**, paste them into the box, press **Apply**, and then commit and push as above.

**The file.** `data/overrides.json` lists only what's changed, by key, and anything not in it stays as the scripts have it. Colours are written as `#rrggbbaa`:

```json
{
	"format": 1,
	"changes": {
		"entity/TIPS/stair/tip": "A stair. Take it, and what you carry is banked.",
		"items/ITEMS/draught/heal": 45.0,
		"train/SUPPLY_PRICE/draught": 4,
		"ui/GOLD": "#f29a59ff"
	}
}
```

At startup the game reads `data/overrides.json`, then this device's own changes (`user://overrides.json`) on top. A key is the script's file name, the table, then the keys down to the value (`puzzles.lock/TEXT/quiet` for a script in a folder). A value of the wrong kind, a missing key or a number out of bounds is refused or kept in bounds, so a bad file can't break the game. For code: the tables are `static var`s (not `const`) so they can be changed while the game runs; `scripts/data_registry.gd` lists which tables the editor offers (its `SECTIONS`: the friendly name, the tables, which kinds of value) and does the applying and saving; `scripts/data_page.gd` is the page. To offer a new table, turn its `const` into `static var` and add it to a section.

## Climbers and saves

Up to **six climbers** can be kept, each a separate climber with their own everything. **Climbers** on the title screen opens the picker: six slots in two rows of three (on a phone, the same, smaller). Each filled slot is a card with the climber drawn in code in their own look (the climber screen's figure, a soft light on each mark), their name (or "Unnamed climber"), their marks (or **First climb, unmarked**, or **Unbound**), best floor, climbs and when they last played; the climber being played is edged in gold and marked **Playing**. An empty slot shows **New climber**.

- **Choose a card** (click, tap, or the arrows / d-pad and Enter / A; the current climber's card is focused first) to play that climber: on the train after the Regard, or the tower choice for a first climb. **Choose an empty slot** to begin a new climber there: the introduction, the name, the look and the questions (A new climber's start, below), then the bondless first climb.
- **Delete** under a card asks first: a pop-up warns that it can't be undone, and its **Delete forever** forgets that climber for good (Cancel, Esc or B keeps them). With all six full there is no empty slot, and deleting one frees it. Deleting the climber being played makes the most recently played of the others current.
- **Continue** on the title plays the climber played last; **New Climber** under it begins a new climber in the first empty slot (with all six full it opens the picker, which says so). **Start over** (title, pause menu, N twice) starts only the current climber over, in their slot; the Record of that slot keeps "the climbers before you". Switching climbers is only from the title (in a climb: Exit to title, then Climbers). The dev tools act on the current climber.

**What is shared and what is each climber's.** Shared by every climber (the settings file, `user://sixfold.cfg`): the settings (music and sound volume, screen shake, Interface size, World zoom, HUD size, Lighting, the controls list), controller setup, the Entity's tips on or off, and which climber was played last. Each climber's own (their slot's file, `user://climber_1.cfg` to `climber_6.cfg`): renown (its deeds, titles, medals, bouts, challenges, boons and the special order: the `renown` section; old saves load with none), marks and bonds, essence and soak, density, crystals, the train (stash, pack, Loadout, supplies and what they cost, the store's stock, vessels, the outfitter's last pay), heirlooms, look and name, the Record (and the climbers before them), the Makers' interest and fragments, the secret path and flow, the Unbound, the secret bond, lost pieces, Provings, the kin book, the last tower, and what the Entity has told them (the tips said and the Archive), so a second climber hears the tips again. In the browser these files live in the browser's own storage for the page, as before.

**Saving safely.** Each climber is loaded from their slot's file and remembers it (`profile.gd save_path`), so a climber only ever writes their own file; choosing another climber starts the game again with that climber, and the one before never saves again. The Entity's memory is read again for each climber.

**The new start clears old climbers once.** When the new start (below) first runs, every climber made before it is cleared, once (`new_start.gd clear_old_once`): a copy of each slot's file is kept beside it (`climber_N_before_new_start.cfg`), the settings are kept, and the title shows the Entity's short notice explaining why, with **Understood** to put it away. A marker in the settings file (`start` section, `cleared`) makes sure it never happens again.

**Saves from before there were slots** move into slot 1 the first time the game starts (`slots.gd migrate()`): a copy of the old file is kept as it was (`user://sixfold_before_slots.cfg`), the climber's sections are copied unchanged into `climber_1.cfg` and read back, and only then does `sixfold.cfg` keep just the shared settings. It happens once; nothing is lost if it can't finish (it tries again next time).

## A new climber's start

**New Climber** on the title begins a new climber (in the current slot if it's empty, else the first empty one). In order (`scripts/start_pages.gd` builds the pages; every word is in `scripts/new_start.gd`):

1. **The introduction.** A black screen where the Entity speaks six short lines, one idea each (`new_start.gd INTRO`, the owner's approved story text: the world nearly unmade and the six who held it; the towers over their wells, each with its guardian beast; the dead land and the Turning, the train that circles the six cities; why people climb; you, boarded at your home station (the intro comes before the questions, so it can't name the home yet); and the Entity that boarded with you). **Next**, **Back**, and **Skip** once any climber has been made on this device (the settings file's `start` section counts them, `made`).
2. **Name and pronouns.** An optional name (the **Dice** offers one) and pronouns (**he / him**, **she / her**, **they / them**), with a line showing how others will speak of you. Kept with the look (`appearance["pronouns"]`), changed later on the Appearance page's name row.
3. **The look** (the appearance page, below), ending in **Next**.
4. **Three questions** (`new_start.gd QUESTIONS`), one at a time, then a summary of what you'll begin with, **Change answers** and **Begin: to your home station** (basic training and a first tournament earn the Noble's Grace before any climb: Training, the Grace and tournaments, above). No question leans toward taking a mark or refusing it.
   - *What did you do before choosing to become a climber?* **Soldier**: a sword and chain armour. **Hunter**: a shortbow, a dagger and leather armour. **Labourer**: a war hammer and padded armour. **Beggar**: a dagger, no armour. **Scholar**: a sword cane and padded armour. **Sailor**: a flintlock and leather armour (keeping the usual starting sword) (`PAST`; six answers, by the counts-by-six rule). The pieces are kept as **heirlooms**, so every climb begins with them; a new climber also starts with the basic sword and shortbow in any weapon slot the past leaves empty (not heirlooms: lose them, and a fall gives them back, `scripts/basic_gift.gd`).
   - *Where did you grow up?* Near one of the six towers: it's kept as `profile.home` (a tower id), the first climb is there, and its station is the climber's home station.
   - *What made you choose to become a climber?* (`WHY`) **Born into it**, the easiest start: Fine gear (with its traits), 30 crystals of the home tower's kind and the family title. **Hired by someone**: Common gear, 15 crystals, the hired title and a reach contract from the Noble for the home tower already in hand. **Self made**, the hardest: worn gear (down to `WORN_START` until the Regard), no crystals, no title, but renown comes a quarter faster until the first title (the extra is kept in fractions, so it stays slow). The two titles are **placeholders** (`renown.gd TITLES` "family" and "hired", named `[Family title]` and `[Hired title]`, with small benefits in `titles.gd SOCIAL`), given only by the start (rule `origin`), which brings the titles to 18.

The Entity explains each step the first time (tips `slots`, `intro`, `create_name`, `questionnaire`; all kept in the Archive's First steps), answers **How did my start work?** under How do things work here?, and explains every new button when you hover it, move onto it or hold it on a phone (`entity_help.gd` "start:..."). Esc, B or Back steps back all the way to the title. Tests: `tests/parts/new_start.gd`.

**Pronouns everywhere.** Anyone who speaks of the climber (the Entity, rivals, the Noble, the Makers, notices and logs) uses their pronouns: write `{they}`, `{them}`, `{their}`, `{theirs}`, `{themself}` (capitalised `{They}`... at the start of a sentence), `{are}`, `{were}`, `{have}`, `{do}`, `{they're}` and a verb ending `{s}` / `{es}` ("{they} climb{s}": "she climbs", "they climb") in any line, and it's filled when the line shows (`scripts/pronouns.gd`; every tip, Entity bubble, page text, notice, banner and log line goes through it). The pronoun words themselves are data (`pronouns.gd FORMS`). The game mostly speaks to the climber as "you"; the lines that speak of them (Start over's warning, the Exit button's help) use the tokens.

## Appearance: how the climber looks

A **new climber** chooses how they look after their name (A new climber's start, above): after **New Climber** on the title, when an empty slot is chosen on the Climbers page, and straight after **Start over** (from the title, the pause menu or N twice). The page shows a large live preview: the climber as the tower draws it (the same drawing code, scaled up, idling on a patch of floor; **Turn** spins it, or **Q** / **E**, **LB** / **RB**) beside the figure from the climber screen. On the right (later, not for a new climber, who chose them the step before), the **name** (type it, or the **Dice** picks one; on a phone in the browser a tap opens the browser's text box) and the **pronouns**, then six **tabs** of options (the figure leans in on the face for Face and Hair; a dot under a tab means it holds something other than today's look). Every list counts by sixes (the body, pronouns and the hood's three are the exceptions the owner allows; `tests/parts/new_start.gd` checks it). Every colour row has six preset swatches and, at its end, a **colour wheel** (a ring of every hue) for a colour of your own: the wheel takes the rows' place, with a disc to pick the colour (a click, tap or drag; Left / Right turn the hue), **Brightness** and **Strength** bars and **Done**; the colour is kept as `#rrggbb`.
  - **Body:** **body** (masculine, feminine or androgynous: the shoulders, waist and hips, the face's shape, brows, lashes and lips), **build** (slight, lean, wiry, average, athletic, broad, stocky, heavy, tall and lean, short and sturdy, lanky, burly: the chest, shoulders, cloak and legs, in the tower too), **skin** (porcelain, fair, olive, tan, brown, deep).
  - **Face:** **eye shape** (round, almond, narrow, wide-set, hooded, upturned, downturned, deep-set, heavy-lidded, sharp, monolid, close-set), **eye colour** (dark, brown, hazel, green, blue, grey), **features** (clear, freckles, a scar, ink lines, war paint, a beauty spot).
  - **Hair:** **hair** (shaved, short, cropped, long, tied up, braid, curls, ponytail, twin braids, crest, wild mane, bun, side-swept, shaved sides with a long top, locks, afro, bob, pigtails), **hair colour** (black, brown, auburn, copper, blond, white), **facial hair** (none, stubble, moustache, goatee, short, full, braided, mutton chops, long, forked, handlebar moustache, chinstrap), **beard colour** (as the hair, black, brown, copper, blond, grey).
  - **Clothes:** **clothes** (tunic, shirt and vest, jerkin, gambeson, coat, long coat, robe, wrap, tabard, sleeveless tunic, doublet, tunic and kilt (the kilt in the cloak's colour): each drawn its own way, from above too; armour covers the chest), **clothes colour** (6), **trousers** (as the clothes, or 5 colours), **boots** (6; worn boots still cover them), **belt** (leather, black, studded, double or chain belt; red, blue, green, gold or purple sash; rope or pale cord).
  - **Cloak:** **cape or cloak** (long, short, tattered, mantle, half cape over one shoulder, shawl, poncho, wrapped, hooded capelet, cowl, trailing, no cloak: with none there's no hood either), **cloak colour** (6, today's teal among them; the hood follows it), **trim** (none or 5 colours), **texture** (plain, banded hem, striped, embroidered, patched, checked, fur-lined, quilted, scaled, runed, spotted, two-tone; in the trim's colour, or a shade of the cloak), and the **hood** up (snug on the head), pulled low (the eyes in its shadow) or down.
  - **Extras:** **accessory** (none, earrings, an eyepatch, a feather, a scarf, a pendant, a torc, a nose ring, a headband, spectacles, a circlet, a mask; an old save's neckpiece becomes its accessory).

The tabs are one row: **Left** / **Right** on it turn them, a click or tap picks one, and Enter or **A** goes into its rows. Up / down run through the name, the tabs, the tab's rows and the buttons in one loop; **Left** / **Right** (the d-pad or stick) change the focused row, and a click or tap on its arrows or a swatch does too; Enter or **A** on a row goes to **Next** (or **Done**). **Randomise** rolls a whole look (every option; the face, beard, trim, pattern and extras stay plain now and then, `RANDOM_PLAIN`) and keeps the name and pronouns. For a new climber, **Next** goes on to the questions and Esc goes back to the name. The Entity explains the page the first time.

Change it later with **Appearance** under the figure on the climber screen's Marks tab, or **Appearance** in your car on the train (beside Climber and Inventory): the same page, ending in **Done** (or Back), which returns where you came from. Every change is kept at once and shows everywhere the climber is drawn: in the tower (every animation and direction: standing, walking, rolling (the cloak's style, trim and pattern on the ball) and fallen (the legs and boots out from under the spread cloak); with the hood down the hair is on show and the hood lies in folds at the neck; a helm or cap still covers the head, and a feather becomes its plume), the mirror image, on the climber screen, the book's body map and the Climbers picker (the name heads it) and in the preview. The face's small details (freckles, a scar) are a pixel or two in the tower at normal zoom; the figure shows them in full. Looks never change how you play.

The options, their names and colours, the tabs, the builds' sizes, the small things' colours (`ACCENTS`), the defaults and the editor's words are data in `scripts/appearance.gd` (`OPTIONS`, `EXTRA_OPTIONS` (the pronouns), `WHEEL` (the rows with a colour wheel), `ROWS`, `TABS`, `BUILDS`, `BODIES`, `ACCENTS`, `RANDOM_PLAIN`, `DEFAULTS`, `NAMES`, `TEXT`; the names, tabs and colours are in the Data editor's Appearance tab too). The look is saved with the profile (the `look` section) as option ids; a save from before there was a choice, or from before an option existed, loads with today's look for what it lacks (the defaults: trousers as the tunic, brown boots, a long cloak with no trim or pattern, an average build, nothing extra), and unknown ids fall back to it. The drawing reads colours and sizes worked out once per change (`Appearance.look()`), never per frame, in **layers** so worn gear can slot in between: in the tower `draw_legs` (body), `draw_cloak` (clothes), `draw_torso` (body and belt), then the caller's gear (armour, shoulders), `draw_head` (the neck, the head, hair, face and headgear), then the caller's arms and weapon (`player.gd` and the mirror image in `mirror.gd` share them, and `draw_rolled_cloak` / `draw_fallen_body`); on the front figure `draw_front` (the cloak behind, legs, tunic and belt, arms, the mantle, then `draw_front_head`, with `gear_look.gd`'s gear layers between them for a dressed look: see **Worn gear shows on the climber** under Gear), which `menu.gd BodyView._figure` calls for the climber screen, the book, the body map, the picker and the preview. Anything drawn like the climber can call them, or use a `player.gd` node with `set_appearance()` (as the preview's doll does). Tests: `tests/parts/appearance.gd`.

## The Entity: tips, the Archive and the Record

**The Entity** is a small, mysterious presence: a softly glowing orb with two dark eyes and a wry little mouth, drifting sparks and a gentle bob, drawn in code. It watches the climber, as the beast does, but it isn't the beast, and it never explains the story outright. Its name and everything it says live in `scripts/entity.gd`, in plain data, so it's easy to rename or rewrite: `TEXT` (its name and the words around it), `LINES` (short lines for moments and pages) and `TIPS` (each tip's line and a fuller account for the Archive, in the groups of `GROUPS`). In the text, `{move}`, `{attack}`, `{roll}` and the like become the keys, controller buttons or touch buttons for whatever the climber is using (`KEYS`).

**Tips.** The first time the climber meets something new, the Entity says a line or two about it in a bubble at the top right, beside its sphere (on a phone, where there's rarely room for a whole tip between the buttons, it's said in the top-middle lane in the Entity's colours, and a tap on it there dismisses it too). One bubble at a time: the rest wait their turn. A tip never stops the game; it stays 12 to 24 seconds (longer for a longer tip; said in the top lane on a phone, it gives way after half that to a banner waiting there), and a tap or click on it or on the Entity's sphere, **Enter**, **L**, or a press of the right stick dismisses it at once. How long tips and tidbits stay is `TIMING` in `scripts/entity.gd` (also in the Data editor: Numbers → The Entity: how long its words stay in a climb). Bubbles wait while a menu is open or the screen is black between floors. Each tip is said once per climber (the "guide" section of the climber's save file: a second climber hears the tips again, with their own Archive; tips on or off is shared). The tips: the Entity itself and the first steps (at the start of a climb), the glint (a monster's first glint), falling, essence (the first essence gathered), banking at the stair, the Regard, casting, your abilities (where the Abilities page is) and the climber screen (a climb with marks); for an Unbound, their techniques (six on the mark buttons, tap to cast, an early cast costs strain) and where to see and change them, overdraw (a burned mark), a new mark, the open stair, rooms and fog, room names, good rooms, monster dens, restless crypts, doors (the first door opened), locked and sealed doors (for doors to come), chasms (near one) and falling through one, camps, the Proving, the beast, the ward, puzzles (each kind, the first time you walk into its room), traps (the first warning), trap rooms, hidden traps, gas vents, poison, gear on the floor, worn pieces (the first helm, ring or other worn piece found), rarities and traits (the first find better than Common), heirlooms, items, the belt (once it's full), the item ring (once you carry two kinds), a fall that loses things, the first lost cache and the inventory.

**The story's moments** (`entity.gd STORY`, the owner's approved words; said once per climber by `guide.gd story` / `story_now`, even with tips off). At six turns of a climber's story the Entity says a line of its own, the Unbound hearing their own where it has one: its first words the first time on the train ("There you are. Don't look for me in the window..."), arriving on floor 5 of the first climb (before the Regard), the Regard over (marked, or refused: "No mark. Good."), the first tower's guardian beaten, the third (the Makers' plea is coming), and arriving at the summit of the last tower whose guardian still stands. Tests: `tests/parts/story_text.gd`.

**Its sphere lingers.** While you play, the Entity's small sphere sits on the right side under the key hints (on a phone under the Bag, Climber and Menu buttons, slid left until it's clear of every touch button). It's quiet when idle (a slow bob), and a ring spreads from it once when it has something new: a line to say, or a new entry in its log. A tip's bubble opens beside it, to its left. With no room beside it (a phone with many cast buttons, or a long line), it speaks in the top-middle lane instead, on a plate in its own colours: never over a button.

**Tidbits.** When something relevant happens, the Entity says **one short line** beside its sphere that goes by itself after a while (about 8 to 14 seconds, by its length; a tap on it, or opening its panel, sends it sooner): on arriving at a floor (about the tower or one of its own systems), a named room, the floor's task, a puzzle, a scenario, a creature the first time it notices you, a reaction, a synergy, a locked door (how that kind opens), shut doors, a cracked wall nearby, low health, a full belt. They're practical facts, true to the mechanics ("Deep water can't be waded. You stop at its edge, and so do walking monsters."), in `scripts/entity_notes.gd` `TIDBITS` by key (`tower:kraken`, `system:tides`, `creature:ram`, `task:levers`, `scenario:gauntlet`, `theme:den`, `puzzle:lock`, `lock:lever`, `doors`...; a reaction with no line of its own says what `reactions.gd` says it does). **No lore:** `LORE` has an empty list for each tower, yours to write; a line added there is said on arriving in that tower and shown in the panel. A line is said once a climb, a key waits 45 seconds before speaking again and tidbits keep 7 seconds apart (`GAP`, `COOLDOWN`), so it never chatters; with tips turned off it keeps quiet. Code asks with `main.remark("key")`.

**Its panel and the log.** Press **L**, press the **right stick** on a controller (while no first-time tip is waiting to be dismissed with it), or **click or tap the sphere**: the Entity says a quip (`QUIPS`) and its panel opens under it (on a phone, down the right side, over the buttons while it's open). It shows the last tip or tidbit **in full**, the tower's lore line if you've written one, a **Talk** button (see **The Entity on the train, and talking with it** below), and **the log**: the last things that happened this climb, newest first, each a short line with a small sign in its kind's colour and its floor ("F3"). Logged: essence gathered (batched, "+14 Air"), essence banked and crystals condensed at a stair, items, gear, keys and crystals picked up, floors reached and rooms entered by name, a task started or done, a puzzle solved or failed, a scenario and its grade, doors shut or opened, a lock or lever opened, a cracked wall broken, a secret room found, the first of each reaction on a floor, a synergy formed, renown earned, the beast noticing, a fall to a lower floor, a camp's choice, a mark's step. **Never damage.** The log is kept for the whole climb (the last 20 show, as many as fit). The same again, **Esc**, or a tap on the panel closes it; the game doesn't stop while it's open.

**Moments.** The Entity also has a short word, in the same bubble, when you fall, when a beast is slain, at a new mark and on reaching the Proving (the first time it's the tip; after that one of a few lines).

**The Archive** (the train's **Library** car, or **Library** on the pause rail in a climb) keeps everything the Entity has told you, grouped by subject (first steps, essence and marks, the tower, puzzles and traps, gear and items, screens), with a count of how many you've heard. Subjects you haven't met show as **???**. Choose one (click, tap, or the arrows and Enter) to read the Entity's line and a fuller, plain account. With tips turned off in Settings the Entity stays quiet, but the Archive still fills in as you meet things. **Replay tips** (in Settings & Controls and the Archive) makes the Entity say everything again; the Archive keeps what it has.

**The Record** (the climber screen's Record tab) is the Entity's record of you: climbs, best floor, marks, floors climbed, monsters slain, beasts slain, falls, puzzles solved, essence banked, heirlooms kept, falls through chasms, the best floor and beasts slain in each tower, and, once you've started over, the climbers before you. The counters are kept with the climber in `profile.gd` (`record`, `note()`, `note_floor()`, `note_beast()`) and start again with a new climber.

### The Entity on the train, and talking with it

**Its sphere is on every car and station screen**, on the pages you reach from the train (the Loadout, the stash, the climber's book), and on the pause menu's pages in a climb (the inventory, the climber's book, the Library and its Bestiary, Settings). When nothing is happening it rests in a corner where it covers no button and no words.

**Its lines on a page stay until you send them away.** What the Entity says at the top of a car or page (a first-time tip, its line for the page, its answer to something you chose) has no timer: it stays, even when the page is built again, until you **click or tap it**, click or tap the **sphere**, or press **L** (the right stick on a controller). The small words in its corner say how. Sent away, it stays away. (In a climb, its bubbles still go by themselves, after twice as long as before.) The Archive's entries and the talk aren't sent away like that: you asked for those.

**It explains what a button does.** On a computer, rest the mouse on any button (a car on the rail, a piece in your stash, Buy, Sell, a bench drawer, a setting...) or on a piece of the train's furniture (the outfitter's rack, the stash, the bookcase, the Engine's board...): after a moment the sphere glides beside it and says, in a small bubble, what it does or where it goes ("Opens the Outfitter's Car: buy supplies and gear, and sell what you found", "Sells Leather Cap for 2 Stone. Press it twice."), and under that a short comment of its own ("Joel remembers who pays."). It stays for as long as the thing is highlighted, with no timer. It waits a moment first, so sweeping the mouse across the screen doesn't make it fly about, and it goes back to its corner when the mouse leaves. With the keys or a controller, it explains whatever button you move onto, for as long as you stay on it. **On a phone** (no mouse to rest), **press and hold** a button for about half a second: the Entity explains it, and letting go then does *not* press it; the explanation stays until your next tap (a tap on its bubble or the sphere only sends it away). A normal quick tap still presses the button at once. The bubble always stays on the screen, never covers the button you're asking about, and keeps off the rail. If you'd rather it didn't, **Settings → The Entity explains buttons** turns it off (it sits in its corner; next to "Guide tips from the Entity").

**Talking with it.** Click or tap the sphere on the train (or press **L**, or the controller's right stick). A small conversation opens: the Entity says something, and you pick one of a few questions (3 to 5 at a time; more go under "Something else..."). It answers from the game as it is right now, in its own voice: *What should I do next?* (a hint from your state: unbanked essence, a faint mark, something in your stash that beats what you wear, an empty belt, a piece you can afford, a title the Noble would grant, Training lessons you haven't done, a mark close to its next step), *How do things work here?* (short, plain answers on essence and banking, how marks grow (by use), what overdrawing does (burns, paralysis, burnout), how a new mark forms, renown, what comes home after a fall, a recall or a summit, the belt, the warded stair and the floor's task, cracked walls and secret rooms; every number in them is read from the game's own tables, so a change in the Data editor shows at once), *What is this tower like?* (the tower's description, its essence, your best floor; then its beast, and what to watch for there), *What do my marks do?* (each mark, its step and how deep it feels toward the next, in words, and what that step brings), *How did my last climb go?* (on the train: from the climb's own log; in a climb, *How is this climb going?*), *Tell me about the train* (and paying for things, and what goes up the tower with you) and *Goodbye*. Answers lead to follow-up questions. Mouse, keys (arrows and Enter), controller and touch all work; **Esc** (or B) leaves, back where you were. **In a climb**, open its panel (L, the right stick, or a tap on the sphere) and press its **Talk** button (or hold L / the right stick, or press Enter while the panel is open); the game waits while you talk.

**Where its words live (to change them or add your own).** Everything is plain data, and also in **Dev tools → Edit game data → Words → The Entity's words**:
- `scripts/entity_help.gd`, `HELP`: what it says about each button, by a stable name for the button (`rail:store`, `slot:chest`, `sell`, `bench_tab:temper`, `setting:music`...; the train's furniture is `furnish:rack`, `furnish:bookcase`...). `COMMENTS`: its comment under an explanation, a few lines for each kind of thing (`slot`, `buy`, `setting`, `furnish`..., and `any` for the rest); one is picked for each button and stays the same for it. In play, a HUD slot's card gets a comment from `HOVER` in `scripts/entity_notes.gd`. (In play there's no pointing at things in the world, such as gear on the floor or a monster: you learn about those by walking up to them, so they have no hover comment.) Words in curly brackets are filled in by the game: `{piece}`, `{price}`, `{tower}`, `{count}` and so on (the top of the file lists them). A button with no line of its own just says its own words; a test lists any train button left without a line, so none is forgotten.
- `scripts/entity_talk.gd`, `DIALOGUE`: the conversation, as a list of steps. Each step has `ask` (the question you pick to get there), `lines` (what the Entity answers: one is picked at random; `{blanks}` are filled from the game), `choices` (the names of the steps you can go to next; `start` goes back to the first questions) and sometimes `if` (when the question shows: `on_train`, `in_play`, `marked`...). `HINTS` holds the "what next" hints. A new mechanic can add its own "How" topic: a step named `how_...` with its `ask` and `lines`, and its name in the `choices` of the `how` step.
- **Story lines are yours.** The Entity never makes up story. Two questions are waiting for you, *Who are you?* (`who`) and *What are the towers for?* (`why`): each has one line that starts with `[Yours to write]`, and while it does, players never see the question. Write the Entity's answer over that line (in the script, or in the Data editor) and the question appears. To add a question of your own: copy a step in `DIALOGUE`, give it a new name, its `ask` and `lines`, and add that name to the `choices` of the step it should come from (for a new first question, to `start`'s choices). Tests: `tests/parts/entity_train.gd`, and `tests/parts/entity_tips.gd` (how long its words stay, sending its lines away, explaining what's highlighted on every kind of page).

**Hooking up a new system.** Call `main.tip("id")` the first time the climber meets it (one line; it's cheap and safe to call every frame) and add an entry for `"id"` to `TIPS` in `entity.gd`. An id with no entry of its own falls back to the part before its last `_` (a new puzzle kind `"puzzle_mirrors"` gets the general `"puzzle"` tip, `"door_iron"` gets `"door"`). Moments use `GuideScript.say("key", {...})` or `GuideScript.moment("tip", "key")`. The bubble, the queue and what's been said live in `scripts/guide.gd`. Tests keep the Entity quiet unless they set the `sixfold_tips` meta on `Engine`. Hovering a HUD slot on a computer shows its tooltip with the Entity's small face in the corner.

## The Bestiary

**What it is.** A book of every monster in the game, kept in the **Library**: in a climb, open **Library** on the pause rail and choose the **Bestiary** tab (or press **E** / **RB** on the Library page); on the train, go to the **Library** car and choose the **Bestiary** volume on the bookcase. Every monster has a tile, grouped by tower (the ones found in every tower first, each tower's beast at the end of its group). A monster you haven't met yet is a dark silhouette marked **???**, so you can see how many are left. Click or tap a tile (or move to it and press Enter / A) to open its entry. The pictures are the monsters' own drawings, the same ones you see in the towers.

**How entries fill in.** An entry grows as you meet the monster:
- **Seen** (it came on screen near you): its name, its picture and where you first met it (tower and floor).
- **Fought** (you hit it, or it hit you): how it fights and what to watch for, whether it flies or swims, and a note on champions (and on calling it to your side, once it's in a Covenant's kin book).
- **Slain once**: its health and damage, and your tallies (times slain, times it felled you). **Slain 5 times**: what it's weak to. **Slain 15 times**: how it takes states. (A beast needs 1, 2 and 3.)

The first time you meet a monster, a line says **"New in the Bestiary: Lurker"** in the left feed and the Entity's "This climb" log; a new tier says so too, but never more than once for the same monster on a floor. What each climber has met is saved with that climber (Start over begins a new Bestiary).

**Where its words live.** All of it is in `scripts/bestiary.gd`: `BESTIARY` holds each monster's `name`, `desc` (how it fights), `states` (anything special about how it takes states) and **`lore`, left blank for you to write** (it shows once one has been slain); `TEXT` holds the page's words and the notices; `TIERS` holds how many slayings each tier needs. You can change all of these in the game too: Dev tools → Edit game data → Words → **The Bestiary**, and Numbers → **The Bestiary**. The numbers an entry shows (health, damage, weakness, flying or swimming) are read from the monsters' own tables, so they always match the game. The record is `bestiary` in `profile.gd`; the game tells the Bestiary what happens through a handful of one-line calls (`attach`, `watch`, `hurt_by`, `slain`, `fell`), and it speaks only through `notify()` (`main.note_event`). Tests: `tests/parts/bestiary.gd`.

## Towers

Every climb starts by choosing a tower (keys 1 to 6, click, or tap a card). Each tower has its own beast, essence, monster mix and look (`scripts/towers.gd`):

| Tower | Essence | Monsters | Look |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Gryphon's Tower | Air | archers, bats, harriers and thunderlings, over open drops in the wind; the Gryphon on floor 12 | cold, blue stone and pale torches |
| The Kraken's Tower | Water | slimes, lurkers, shellbacks and deepfins, in water that rises and drains; the Kraken on floor 12 | damp, teal stone and green-white torches |
| The Wyvern's Tower | Iron | knights, cinders, smiths and forge golems; the Wyvern on floor 12 (the hardest) | a forge: workshop halls, molten channels, grated corridors, furnaces (see below) |
| The Manticore's Tower | Stone | scorplings, rams and burrowers; the Manticore on floor 12 | sun-baked sandstone: courts in the sun, shaded alleys, sand (see below) |
| The Firebird's Tower | Fire | wisps, bloomcaps and ashwings, and chasms to jump; the Firebird on floor 12 | islands of pale, sunlit rock over the abyss, bridges between them, gold torches |
| The Chimera's Tower | Blade | thornhounds, mongrels and briars; the Chimera on floor 12 | the Thornwild: grown caves of mossy green rock, roots and thorns, pale green torches |

**The Gryphon's tower has a summit.** On floor 12, the summit, the Gryphon itself waits, alone. It rakes with its talons up close (a red wedge, parryable), dives on you from range (it locks onto where you stood when it glints; the landing can't be parried, so roll out of the red circle), and throws a fan of feathers (parryable, and a Crosswind turns them back). It can't be lifted or interrupted, only staggered, and at half health it calls two bats. Beat it, take the stair, and the climb is complete.

**The Kraken's tower has a summit too.** On floor 12 the Kraken waits. It sweeps a tentacle across a wide wedge in front of it (parryable), slams three spots at once with one landing where you stand (can't be parried: roll out of the red circles), and spits a fan of ink (parryable). At half health it calls two lurkers.

**The Wyvern waits atop its tower.** On floor 12 it sweeps its tail across a wedge in front of it (parryable), breathes fire down a narrow cone (can't be parried: roll or step aside) that leaves the floor burning, and sprays a fan of embers (parryable). At half health it calls two cinders.

**The Chimera waits atop its tower.** Three beasts in one: the lion mauls up close (parryable) and breathes fire down a narrow cone (can't be parried: roll or step aside); the goat charges in a straight line (parryable; it's stunned if it charges into a wall); the serpent tail spits three poisoned spikes (parryable). At half health it calls two thornhounds.

**The Chimera's monsters:** the **thornhound** is a fast pack hound that circles, glints and leaps at you with a bite (parryable). The **mongrel** is a patchwork beast that alternates a bite up close (parryable) with a fan of thrown quills (parryable).

**Jumping and chasms.** Everyone can jump (Z): a short hop that costs a little stamina and carries you over chasms and burning floor (it isn't a dodge: attacks still hit you in the air). In the Firebird's tower, floors above the first have **chasms**: fall into one and it doesn't hurt, but you land on the floor below and climb again from there (never below floor 1; your unbanked essence stays with you). Walking monsters can't enter chasms; flying ones (bats, cinders, wisps and the beasts that fly) cross them. Every hole (a chasm, a Gryphon drop, a tower's way down) is a **dark pit** that reads as deep and dangerous: a lit rim of cut stone with a hard dark line just inside it, the shaft's walls falling away into near-black, and far below, only faintly, the floor you'd land on (small dim flagstones under the tower's haze and the depth's darkness). It never looks like a doorway (a gap in a wall, level with the floor). It is drawn once into the floor's feature layer (`scripts/holes.gd`), so it costs nothing per frame; its colours are the `HOLE_*` entries in `scripts/palette.gd` (Data editor: Colours, The world; `HOLE_DEPTH` sets how faint the floor below is; the haze per tower under Tower tints).

**Teetering at the edge.** Walk onto a hole's edge and you don't drop at once: you **teeter** on the lip, leaning out over it with your arms windmilling while pebbles crumble in (`player.gd` `begin_teeter`). Move back away from the hole (or roll away) within `holes.gd` `TEETER["time"]` (0.75 s) and you recover; stand still or keep going and you tip in and fall. A roll, a dash, a blow, the wind or a whirlpool's pull takes you straight in, as before, and so does landing in one from a jump. It reads the same move input as walking, so it works the same with keys, a controller or the touch stick. The Entity explains it the first time (Teetering), and its Chasms help says to step back quickly.

**Ways down.** Four more towers have a hole of their own (`scripts/ways_down.gd`; each tower's floor script lays it at the end of its `shape_plan` and makes the node in `setup`). Each drops you a floor unhurt, exactly like a chasm, is cut into the floor as a dark pit in its tower's dress, and is visible through the lighting and fog like the other holes (its warning and glow are drawn over the darkness only while it's in sight):
- **The Kraken's whirlpools.** A round bronze drain grate in a tide room's floor. While the room's water drains (`kraken_floor.gd` tide), a whirlpool spins up over it: foam spirals turning in, a faint ring showing its reach, and its eye opening into the dark. Come within its reach and it drags you toward the eye (stronger nearer it, always weaker than your walk: walk away and you get out). Reach the eye and you fall. When the water is gone it dies down to a grate again.
- **The Manticore's sinkholes** (up to three a floor). A ragged patch of pale sand with a dimple and cracks running out from it. Stand on it and it **sags**: a pulsing red ring, sand pouring in, a rumble and dust, for `RULES["sag"]` (1.5 s). Then it opens for good into a round, sand-rimmed pit. Step off while it sags and you're safe. A walking monster on it when it opens goes down with it.
- **The Wyvern's slag chutes.** A riveted iron chute in the forge floor, two tiles long, chevrons and two runnels of glowing slag running down it into a pit at its foot. Step on the slope and you slide toward the pit (walk back up hard); step onto the pit's edge from the side and you teeter.
- **The Chimera's root hollows.** A ragged gap ringed with earth and moss, roots arching over it and rootlets hanging into the dark.
Each sits inside an ordinary hall (never the start, the stair, a puzzle, a scenario, a good room, a vault or a trap room) with a ring of plain floor round it, never on what matters (`water_wind_grid.gd` protected) and never cutting anything off: its tiles go into `plan["impassable"]` and `reach.gd` checks the floor with them as rock. Monsters path round them (and walking ones can't step into an open one); a lost cache or a contract's thing is never laid on one; what drops on an open one rolls to its lip. The floor bot keeps off them (the tower's `is_void`, `bot_avoid` and `bot_steer` hooks ask the node) and steps back if it ever teeters. The Entity explains each the first time one is in sight (Whirlpools, Sinkholes, Slag chutes, Root hollows), and "How do things work here?" has "Holes, whirlpools and other ways down?". Numbers and words: `WAYS` (which tower, from which floor, how often, how many), `RULES`, `TEXT`, `LOOK` in the Data editor ("Ways down").

**Mimics** (every tower, from floor 3: `scripts/mimic.gd`). Now and then (`RULES["chance"]` of floors with a chest) one of the floor's chests, always one alone in its room, is a mimic. While it waits it is just a chest (`room_rewards.gd` draws it, shut while the room's guards stand, then gold like any other), with a tell for sharp eyes: every few seconds its lid lifts a hair and a pale tooth shows at the seam. Walk up to open it and it **springs**: the lid flies up, all teeth, red eyes and a lolling tongue, and it hops after you. Up close it rears and **bites** (a red wedge, then the glint: parry it); from a few steps off a red line shows where it will **lunge** jaws first (glints: parry or step aside). After each it sits open. Slain, it spills what it swallowed: `RULES["essence"]` (1.5) times a rich chest's essence, often an item, often gear a rarity row up. It has a Bestiary entry (found in every tower) and an Entity first-time moment.

**Inscription stones** (`scripts/inscriptions.gd`). On some floors (`RULES["chance"]`, from floor 2) a standing stone stands near a hall's north wall, rows of letters cut into its face, glowing faintly so it shows in the dark. Stand beside it and its words show over it in a plate (walk away and they fade); the first reading on a floor goes into the Entity's log. Nothing to press, so it works the same on a phone. The words are the owner's approved story text: `INSCRIPTIONS` holds six for each tower (36, by sixes), a stone showing its tower's by floor (floors 1 and 7 the first, 2 and 8 the second...): stones 1-2 the tower's own well and guardian, 3-5 a chapter of the shared history, 6 that tower's line of the prophecy. The Chimera's stones speak of the sixth climber (the one playing) with pronoun tokens, filled with the climber's own pronouns as they show (Data editor: "Inscription stones").

**Guardian glimpses** (`scripts/glimpses.gd`). Now and then the tower's beast is glimpsed while you climb: pure atmosphere, it never touches play. About one floor in three (`RULES["chance"]`, and never more than `RULES["pity"]` floors without one), never on the bondless first climb or the summit, and never while you fight (no monster after you nearby, no blow taken a moment ago). One of three, in its tower's flavour (`LOOK`): a vast **shadow** sweeping across the open hall you stand in (wings for the Gryphon, the Wyvern and the Firebird, a mass of arms for the Kraken, a maned and tailed body for the Manticore and a three-headed one for the Chimera) with its sound far off; **eyes** opening in a high window in the hall's north wall, following you, blinking and gone; or, after a big moment (the floor's task done, a fight won), a distant **roar** that shakes the floor and sifts dust down. Each is noted in the Entity's log.

**The Firebird waits atop its tower.** On floor 12, in an open hall, it swoops straight through you (parryable), leaving fire in its wake; calls down a firestorm on four spots, one where you stand (can't be parried: get out of the circles, or jump); and throws a fan of embers (parryable). The first time it falls it burns to ashes, can't be hurt for two seconds, and rises again at 40% health. At half health it calls two wisps.

**The Firebird's monsters:** the **wisp** flickers around you, glints and darts straight through where you stood (parryable), leaving a little fire. The **bloomcap** is a rooted fire-fungus: it glints, locks onto where you stand and lobs burning spores that land a moment later (can't be parried: step out of the circle or jump); the ground keeps burning.

**The Manticore waits atop its tower.** On floor 12 it swipes with its claws (a red wedge, parryable), fires a volley of three poisoned tail spikes (parryable), and roars, bursting out around itself (can't be parried: roll through it). At half health it calls two scorplings.

**The Manticore's monsters:** the **scorpling** scuttles up, glints and stings (parryable); a sting that lands **poisons** you: 2 health every half second for a few seconds (the stair's rest cures it). The **ram** lowers its horns, glints and charges in a straight line (parryable). A ram that charges into a wall or pillar is stunned, so letting it miss is an opening.

**The Wyvern's monsters:** the **cinder** is a living ember that flits at range, glints and spits a burning ember (parryable). The **smith** plods up and brings its hammer down in front of it (parryable, but it hits hard), leaving the floor there burning for a few seconds. **Burning floor** costs 4 health every half second you stand in it; rolling through is free.

**The Kraken's monsters:** the **lurker** swims under the water, where only ripples and two eyes show and it can't be hit. It rises near you, glints and lunges (parryable), then stays up for a moment: that's your chance. The **shellback** plods toward you behind a shell that blocks most of a blow from the front (heavy blows crack it), and bashes up close; get behind it.
