**The Gryphon's monsters:** the **bat** circles you, then glints and swoops straight through where you stood (parryable). The **thunderling** walks up, stops and sparks, then bursts into a ring of lightning: it can't be parried, so step out of the ring or roll through it.

**New monsters, every tower and the Gryphon's, Kraken's and Wyvern's** (`scripts/foes/`, one script each, extending `enemy.gd`; `foes/marks.gd` keeps what some leave on the floor). Each has a Bestiary entry, an Entity first-time moment (its tip, named after it) and a word in play (`creature:<kind>`), "How do things work here?" has *Monsters that work together?*, and its numbers and words are in its own `RULES` and `TEXT` (Data editor: "New monsters"). Where and from which floor: `floors.gd` MONSTERS and the towers' `weights`.
- **Husk** (every tower, from floor 4): the empty gear of a climber who fell here (climbers say the husks were Unbound once, drained back into the well until nothing came back). It slashes up close and lunges from further off (both glint: parry), and rolls away from your wind-ups (a bow drawn, a spell gathering, the finisher of your chain). Strike it three times in one rhythm and its blade comes up crosswise (a pale arc): the next blow on that beat, from the front, is parried and it cuts back (glinting). Change your timing ("Off the beat!") or strike from behind.
- **Bellringer** (every tower, from floor 3): a hunched servant with a bell. No blow of its own; it runs from you. Every few seconds it lifts its bell (a gold ring shows the reach) and rings: monsters inside are quickened for a while (`enemy.gd` `haste_t`: they recover from attacks sooner and move faster, gold ticks at their feet; wind-ups and glints keep their timing). Strike it while the bell is up and the ring is lost.
- **Squall** (Gryphon, from floor 3, flies): a whirl of wind that turns most of a blow while whirling. It spins up (streaks down a red lane), then gusts: unparryable, so step out of the lane; caught, you're shoved down it, bent toward a drop if one is near. After a gust it's open while it spins up again.
- **Rookery** (Gryphon, from floor 4): a nest on a ledge, set against the nearest wall. An egg rocks and cracks (a ring marks it) and a bat flies out now and then: two of its bats at most at once, three eggs in all. Break the nest.
- **Stormstone** (Gryphon, from floor 3): a standing stone that charges (runes light up), then a red line picks whoever stands nearest, you or a monster, and lightning arcs there (unparryable; a monster takes double, is stunned and charged). Lure them nearer than you; with nobody in reach it grounds.
- **Inkling** (Kraken, from floor 2): puffs up (a ring on you), sprays a cloud of ink that darkens the floor round you for a few seconds, then nips from inside it; its wedge, eyes and glint show over the ink.
- **Drowned** (Kraken, from floor 3): lies under a puddle (it settles in the nearest water) out of reach until you come near, rises and shambles after you; its two-armed slam glints (parry) and soaks you. Slain, it swells (a ring), then bursts, soaking everyone in the ring: soaked, lightning jumps to you.
- **Driftbell** (Kraken, from floor 4, floats): a jellyfish whose trail crackles where it lies on water (touch it: a sting, and charged). Close up its tendrils light, it glints and sparks all round (unparryable).
- **Stoker** (Wyvern, from floor 3): red circles mark where its coal lands (on you, and beside a fire already burning); the floor there burns, and coal on a fire feeds it. Unparryable: step out or jump. Its shovel swing up close glints (parry). Slay it and its fires die down.
- **Slagworm** (Wyvern, from floor 4): its trail hardens into low slag walls that burn whoever stands on them (jump them or roll through); they cool and crumble, are never solid, and are never laid near the stair or in a narrow way. Its bite glints (parry).
- **Riveter** (Wyvern, from floor 2, flies): aims (a red line, a glint) and fires three rivets. The first two glance off a parry; the third glows hot and glints: parry it and it flies back, knocking the riveter down, open. Tests: `tests/parts/foes_gryphon_kraken_wyvern.gd`.

Every kill also yields 0.75 essence of the tower's own kind (`ESSENCE_BONUS`), so the tower you choose is how you steer which bonds you'll form. The tower's beast is the one who regards you at the top of your first climb.

### The Wyvern's tower: the forge

Its floors (`scripts/towers/wyvern_floor.gd`, named as the tower's `module` in `scripts/floor_styles.gd`) are a forge: long workshop halls in a grid (more ways between rooms than most towers), carved in its own shapes: **workshops** with benches and anvils in rows and a clear aisle down the middle, **forge halls** with machine columns along both walls, and long plain halls (hand-made rooms turn up about a third as often, so the forge's own shapes win). Every corridor is an **iron grate** over the forge's fires.

- **Molten channels** run right across many halls, and **gutters** of molten metal line the walls of others (never by a doorway). Nothing crosses the metal: walk into it and you stop at its edge, and monsters can't path over it, but you see across it and arrows and thrown embers fly over it (some spells' bolts stop at its edge). Every channel has a **bridge**: a chain bridge of planks laid across. From floor 3, now and then a bridge is **hauled up** (its outline dashed on the metal, its leaf standing on the near bank): strike its **crank** on the side you came in by and it comes down for good. The game proves the stair and the task can always be reached with the channels as rock and a raised bridge as a lever's door (`scripts/reach.gd`), and traps never sit on the metal (trap rooms keep their whole floor for their set pieces: no channels there).
- **Heat.** Furnaces stand in some halls, their bellows breathing: a warning glow, then a hot spell (a ring of heat around the furnace). Linger in the heat and it builds up on you (a ring at your feet fills, "Too hot!"); full, it burns: 3 health every half second until you step out and cool, or rather the climber's **Burning** state (monsters' states on you: `reactions.gd` `CLIMBER`; with that switched off, the old 3 health every half second).
- **Water barrels** stand by the fires. Strike one and it bursts: the heat around it is **quenched** for 10 seconds (the furnaces go dark), you're cooled at once, foes in the splash are **soaked**, and a forge golem caught in it is quenched. A barrel fills again after a while.

**Its task: Stoke the Furnaces.** Two or three cold furnaces stand in different halls. Strike each one's bellows until it roars (four strikes; it cools back if you dawdle). A waking furnace throws its heat at whoever stands at its mouth, so don't linger.

**Its creature: the forge golem** (from floor 4; `scripts/towers/forge_golem.gd`). A lump of iron with a glowing belly-grate that heats up as it fights: every moment it's after you, every blow it swings and every blow it takes (the seams in its plates glow from dull red to white; a ring at its feet shows how hot). Hot, it hardens (your blows do half), walks faster, its slam (parryable) leaves the floor burning, and now and then it **flares**: a ring of heat bursts out around it (can't be parried: get out of the ring or roll). Full, it must **vent**: it slumps, steaming, for a few seconds and takes double. Water does the same at once: soak a warm golem (a barrel, a pool, a water spell) and it's **quenched**. It carries iron essence.

**Its scenario: Hold the Forge.** Three furnaces burn in the hall; step onto the mark and the doors shut. The fires die down by themselves, and faster with a foe beside them, and waves of the forge's creatures go for them: strike a furnace's bellows to stoke it (or relight it). After 40 seconds: gold if none went out, silver if one did, bronze otherwise; all three out at once ends it.

Every number and word is in the script's `RULES`, `HEAT`, `BARREL`, `FURNACE`, `FORGE` and `TEXT`, and the golem's `GOLEM` (the Data editor's "Towers: the Wyvern's forge and the Manticore's sands").

### The Manticore's tower: sun and sand

Its floors (`scripts/towers/manticore_floor.gd`) are sun-baked sandstone: wide **courts** open to the sky, ringed by a colonnade; **colonnades** (a long nave between two rows of pillars, with side aisles); and **alleys**, mazes of low walls with narrow ways winding between them (again more ways between rooms, and fewer hand-made rooms).

- **Sun.** A shaft of sunlight lies across every court and nave and drifts slowly back and forth across it (about half a minute each way), and the courts lie in a warm open light. Standing in the sun (a small sun shows at your feet) monsters notice you from farther (1.4 times as far).
- **Shade.** The walks under the colonnades, the aisles and the alleys are deep shade (a shade sign at your feet). There monsters notice you only from under half as far, and one that's after you loses track of you (the **Unseen** state, which Unseen Strike and Ambush use) while you stay in the shade more than a few steps away from it.
- **Shifting sand.** Soft **drifts** of sand in the courts slow you a little; a burrower swims in them. **Sand pits** (from floor 3) pull you slowly toward the sink in their middle: reach it and the sand **swallows** you (it hurts) and spits you out at the edge. **Cracked flagstones** crumble into sand after you cross them. None of it ever walls anything off (Sandshaper's Rooted Stance keeps you out of a pit's pull, and fliers aren't slowed).

**Its task: Wake the Sun Seals.** In two or three rooms a polished stone stands under a skylight, its seal set in the floor a few steps off. Strike the stone to turn it (an eighth of a turn a blow). When the drifting patch of sunlight crosses the stone, it throws the light the way it faces: face it at its seal and the seal drinks the light and wakes.

**Its creature: the burrower** (from floor 3; `scripts/towers/burrower.gd`). A sand-coloured, many-legged digger that swims under the sand, seen only as a moving mound and ripples, and can't be struck there. When you stand on sand near it, it marks the spot under you (a ring) and **erupts** out of it (can't be parried: step or roll off the ring), then stays up a moment: your chance. It can only swim in sand: stand on stone and it must come up to reach you, **exposed** ("Exposed!"): slow, taking half again from every blow, only able to bite (parryable). So lure it onto the stone. It carries stone essence.

**Its scenario: the Sinking Court.** Step onto the mark and the court's floor starts giving way to quicksand behind you, rolling toward the far gate. Only the old stones hold: a winding path of them, and a few more. Quicksand slows you and drags you under: stand in it too long and it swallows you (a hit, and you're thrown onto the nearest stone). Reach the far gate: gold for no hits and a quick crossing, silver for one hit, bronze otherwise; leaving the court ends it.

**Its newer monsters** (each with its own script in `scripts/towers/`, sharing `scripts/towers/brood.gd`):
- **Carrion** (from floor 4; `carrion.gd`): a vulture that circles high, out of reach, only its shadow crossing the floor. It dives only when you're badly hurt (below `CARRION` `hurt_below`, 40% of your health), or when it's been the only foe about for a while, or has waited long enough (so a room never waits on it for ever): a cry, a red ring that follows you, the glint, and it drops onto the spot (parryable: it comes down staggered; or step out of the ring). Then it stands on the floor a moment, open, and climbs back up.
- **Basilisk** (from floor 3; `basilisk.gd`): a lizard that keeps its distance and glares every few seconds: its eyes glow and a pale cone fills toward you, the glint, then the glare. In the cone **facing it**, you're Slowed; **turn away** and it does nothing; a blow breaks the glare off. On a phone the aim stops turning you toward a glaring basilisk (`player.gd _auto_aim`), so walking away turns you away. Up close a modest bite (parryable).
- **Cairn** (from floor 5; `cairn.gd`): a pile of stones that pulls itself together into a stone figure as you pass. Slow, heavy, parryable swings; light blows don't rock it. Beaten down it falls into rubble and rebuilds a few seconds later (twice at most), unless a **heavy blow** (a finisher, a heavy weapon, a blast) fells it or lands on the rubble: then the stones scatter for good.

Every number and word is in the script's `SUN`, `SHADE`, `SAND`, `SEAL`, `SINK` and `TEXT`, and the burrower's `BURROWER`; the newer monsters' in `CARRION`, `BASILISK`, `CAIRN` and their `_TEXT` tables.

### The Kraken's tower: flooded floors

The Kraken's floors are built by their own script, `scripts/towers/kraken_floor.gd` (named as the tower's `module` in `scripts/floor_styles.gd`). Its numbers, words and colours are in the Data editor ("The Kraken's water and the Gryphon's heights": `WATER`, `TEXT`, `LOOK`).

- **Layout.** Rooms are mostly round and oval (`own_shapes` in `floor_styles.gd`; hand-made rooms are rarer here), a little smaller than elsewhere so the corridors between them are longer, and the corridors **wind**: each long enough corridor wanders up to three tiles sideways and back (two tiles wide, three at a bend), drawn as a curving channel of water. Round rooms often have a **deep pool** in the middle with a walk all round it.
- **The water.** Every room and corridor is a basin with a **water level** drawn in code: the water spreads across a room as it rises, ripples move on it, a **level line** shows on the room's north wall with a pale **tide mark** above it where it last stood, and salt crusts the floor where it reached.
  - **Tides:** most rooms and corridors fill and drain on a cycle, never deeper than **shallow**; the cycles lag from room to room, so the tide seems to roll through the floor. Shallow water slows you (unless you wear Wading Boots or have a water bond) and soaks foes that wade in it (soaked foes conduct lightning: see States and reactions).
  - **Deep water** can't be crossed on foot: you and walking monsters stop at its edge, and monsters path round it. **Pools** stay deep. A **loop corridor** (a second way round) may flood deep at high tide; if it rises while you stand in it, the water sweeps you out.
  - **Sunken rooms:** now and then a dead-end room lies under deep water with its chest on the bottom. A **sluice wheel** in the room next to it drains it: stand at the wheel until it turns. The water rushes into the wheel's own room for a while, and the sunken room can be walked (its guards are swimmers: when it drains they're stranded).
  - Deep water is never the only way to the stair (the planner checks every floor: a pool or a flooding corridor may only ever take a second way), except for the task below.
- **The task: Drain the Stairwell** (`sluices` in `objectives.gd`, only in this tower). The passage into the stair room is flooded deep. Turn every sluice wheel on the floor (stand at each until it turns; a blow sets it back) and each one lowers the water a step; with the last, the stairwell drains and the ward lifts.
- **The deepfin** (`scripts/towers/deepfin.gd`, from floor 3). It lives in deep water: only a fin and its wake show as it shadows you along the bank, and nothing can touch it down there. Come near the water and it rises, glints and **leaps out** at you, jaws first (parryable). Then it's stranded on the stone, thrashing: soaked through, so lightning arcs through it, and every blow lands half again as hard, until it drags itself back. Too long out of the water (its pool drained) and it dries out and weakens. With no deep water on a floor it keeps to the shallows as a lurker does. Drops water essence.
- **The scenario: the Filling Room** (`flood` in `scenarios.gd`, only in this tower; likelier than the shared scenarios here). Step onto the mark: the doors shut and the water comes in while lurkers and the tower's monsters come for you. Reach the drain wheel at the far end and turn it (a blow knocks your hands off it for a moment) before the water fills the room. Graded on the hits you take and how high the water got.
- **Dark deep floors** (`DARK` in `scripts/tower_needs.gd`). From floor 4 the flooded halls are dark: the light you carry reaches 70% as far and unlit places are blacker. The floor's banner says so the first time and the Entity explains it; **Lantern Oil** carries your light farther than ever, Flare Powder lights a room. (Lantern Oil and Edge Oil, against the shellbacks' shells, are this tower's handy items: see **What each tower asks for**.)

### The Gryphon's tower: open heights

The Gryphon's floors are built by `scripts/towers/gryphon_floor.gd` (the Data editor's "The Kraken's water and the Gryphon's heights": `VOIDS`, `GUST`, `TEXT`, `LOOK`).

- **Layout.** Big open halls with **ledges and balconies over open drops**: a hall may have a round **well** in the middle with a balcony all round it (sometimes a bridge straight across), a **ledge** where one side falls away, or a **span** of drop right across it with one narrow bridge. Long corridors become **bridges**: the rock beside them falls away, so you cross a plank walkway over the drop. Beyond the halls' walls there's no rock to see, only open air: every hall stands like a balcony.
- **Seeing down.** Every drop is a dark pit, and far down it you faintly see the floor right below (`LOOK_BELOW`: smaller, dim, under the depth's darkness): its rock and halls, with their flagstones, walls and torches, sliding with parallax as you move, tiny shapes walking them and wind drifting across, at the bottom of the drop's cut (the lit rim and shaft walls every hole has: `scripts/holes.gd`). Past the halls' walls, where you can't step, you see the open air and two layers of halls further down, smaller and dimmer the deeper they are.
- **Falling.** Step or get knocked off an edge and you fall to the floor below ("YOU FELL", the same as the Firebird's chasms). Walking monsters keep out of the drops; fliers cross them. Drops never cut anything off: they only ever take tiles away (the planner checks every floor).
- **Wind.** Every few seconds a gust blows across the open spans: streaks of wind show which way a moment before, then it shoves whoever stands near a drop (you and foes) that way. Brace by walking against it. A foe thrown into the air (lifted, or knocked up) is carried over the edge and falls when it comes down; a flier stunned over a drop (a parried bat or harrier) falls too.
- **The task: Turn the Wind Vanes** (`vanes` in `objectives.gd`, only in this tower). No bridge reaches the stair room: its passage is an open gap. Strike each wind vane round (two blows each); when the last turns, a steady wind blows across the gap and you walk over on it.
- **The harrier** (`scripts/towers/harrier.gd`, from floor 3). A raptor that soars high over the halls (its shadow sweeps the floor), folds its wings and dives at you with a glint. Its strike throws you the way it flew: toward the edge, if you stand on one. After the dive it stands on the floor a moment (your opening) before it climbs back up. **Parry the dive on a ledge** and it tumbles over the edge and falls. Drops air essence.
- **The scenario: the Long Bridge** (`bridge` in `scenarios.gd`, only in this tower, in dead-end halls). The hall falls away but for a landing by the door, one long bridge and a perch at the far end. Step onto the mark and cross while gusts blow across the bridge toward the drop and harriers dive out of the open air. Graded on the hits you take and your time; leave the hall and it's over.

Tests: `tests/parts/towers_kraken_gryphon.gd` (layouts, reachability over dozens of floors, the water and the wind, both tasks, both creatures, both scenarios, and the floor bot on both towers). The floor bot sweep can be narrowed to one tower: `godot --headless --fixed-fps 60 -s tests/floor_bot_sweep.gd -- 2 tower=kraken`.

### The Firebird's tower: islands over the abyss

Its floors of rooms are **islands of rock floating over the abyss** (`scripts/towers/firebird_floor.gd`, and the shapes in `scripts/towers/organic.gd`). Rooms are ragged **islands** (some are **atolls**, ringed round a hole of real chasm in the middle: mind your footing, and mind being knocked in), rarely a built room (the stair landing always is: `built_stair` in `floor_styles.gd`). The corridors between them are **bridges** of planks with a rope along each side, drawn over nothing: the rock between rooms is painted as the abyss, with the islands' cliffs falling away under their edges and a warm haze far below. Islands are joined sparsely (fewer loops than other towers). You can **see across the abyss** to the islands beyond (it's rock to walk on but open to the eye), so a floor feels open and high.

Its own system is **embers and rebirth**:
- **Updrafts.** A long bridge may be broken by a **gap too wide to jump** (two or three tiles), with a **vent** of rising air on either side (rising streaks, a chevron toward the far side). Step onto a vent and the updraft carries you across, in an arc, to the bridge beyond. In **Firebird Form** you fly anyway, and an updraft carries you faster.
- **Burning bridges.** Some bridges **burn behind you**: once you've crossed one, it smoulders for a moment and a slice of it falls away into the abyss (a one-tile gap: jump it, or wait), then **regrows from embers** about nine seconds later, so the way back always comes again. It never burns out from under your feet.
- **Ember storms.** On some islands (from floor 3) a storm sweeps across **on a beat**: a haze builds on the windward side ("Ember storm! Get behind rock"), then a front of burning wind crosses the island. **Stand behind rock** (a rock crag, a pillar, the island's edge upwind) and it passes over you ("Sheltered"); caught in the open, it burns you. It burns monsters too, except the fiery ones and those that fly above it. Storm islands get two or three rock crags to shelter behind. Firebird Form's own fire doesn't burn you.

Its own task, **Rekindle the Beacons**: a **hearth** burns in a room near the start; walk up to it to take a flame, then carry it to each cold **beacon** (two to four, in far rooms) to light it. The flame **gutters** as you go (the HUD shows how much is left), and a blow costs it a quarter; **braziers** on the way, and every beacon once lit, renew it. The flame always lasts long enough for the longest leg of the best order, with time to spare. The arrow points at a beacon while your flame will last the way, else at the nearest fire.

Its own creature, the **ashwing** (from floor 3): an ember-bird that circles, glints and dives straight through where you stood (parryable), leaving a trail of fire. The first time it falls it sinks into a heap of **glowing ash** and **rises again** three seconds later at half health, unless you **scatter the ash** (strike it) or **soak it** (water, a soaking spell). It's a flier (it crosses chasms) and drops fire essence.

**Its newer monsters** (each with its own script in `scripts/towers/`, sharing `scripts/towers/brood.gd`; numbers and words in `KINDLER`, `FLAMEMOTH`, `SALAMANDER` and their `_TEXT` tables):
- **Flamemoth** (from floor 3; `flamemoth.gd`): a flying swarm drawn to light and fire (burning floor, yours too; the wall torches; a kindler's torch; you, if you're burning). Near a light it circles and forgets you unless you come close, and burning floor feeds it. **Fire makes it grow** (bigger, tougher, harder-hitting, three steps at most) instead of burning it; **water or wind disperses it** (soaked, thrown up or shoved hard: most of its health gone, back to its smallest). It darts through where you stood after a glint (parryable); its touch burns.
- **Salamander** (from floor 4; `salamander.gd`): swims through burning floor, only a heat shimmer showing, and can't be struck there; near the edge it surfaces, glints and lunges (parryable), then lies on the floor a moment. With no fire near it **kindles a pool** of its own (a red ring round it: step out, it can't be parried). It swims in any fire, yours too. **Put the fire out** (water, or soak it) and it's thrown out, dazed. Ember storms don't burn it or the flamemoths.
- **Kindler** (from floor 5; `kindler.gd`): a hooded torch-bearer. While one lives, fallen monsters leave **heaps of ash** (drawn by the `AshHeaps` node it makes); it walks to one and **channels** a beam of fire into it, and the fallen rises as a weak **ash-risen** copy (ash-grey, 40% health, 60% bite; it leaves nothing at all when it crumbles: no essence, drops or tally, so nothing can be farmed). Strike it while it channels to **interrupt** it; **soak** the ash or **stamp it out** (stand on it a moment); or kill the kindler first. Up close it swipes its torch (parryable, burns).

Its own scenario, **the Burning Bridge**: a room broken by a chasm with a narrow bridge across it. Step onto the mark and the bridge starts **burning away behind you** while the tower's creatures come at you from the far side: cross before the fire overtakes you (it drops you to the floor below). The bridge knits back once it's over.

### The Chimera's tower: the Thornwild

Its floors of rooms are **grown, not built** (`scripts/towers/chimera_floor.gd`, shapes in `scripts/towers/organic.gd`): **caves** of lobes grown into each other, round **dens** with burrows off them, **grottoes** with clumps of root to hide behind. Passages bulge and wander where the roots pushed through, and everything is drawn as **rounded, root-bound rock with no straight wall**: boulders bulging over the floor's edges, rounded corners, roots creeping out over the floor, gnarled stumps for cover. Floors are tangled: more loops than other towers, some of them through thorns.

Its own system is **living thorns**:
- **Thorn walls.** Hedges of thorn across a passage. **Any blow cuts them** (two blows, or one finishing blow; any weapon will do, fists too), **fire burns them** at once (a Fire Flask, burning ground, an exploding spell), and they **grow back** after a while (never onto anyone standing there). Like a cracked wall, but alive. Because any weapon cuts them, one may stand on the way to the stair.
- **Closing thickets.** Some thickets stand open as you pass, then **grow closed behind you**: cut your way back out.
- **Brambles.** Patches of bramble on the floor **slow** whoever walks them, and a foe **knocked into them bleeds**.
- **Spore pods.** Glowing pods in the rooms: strike one, walk into it, burn it or knock a foe into it and it **bursts** into a cloud. **Green** poisons whoever stands in it (you and foes alike: lure them in); **blue** calms the foes in it for six seconds (they wander and won't attack until struck).

Its own task, **the Hungry Grove**: thorns choke the stair, fed by **heart-roots** in rooms across the floor (three, then four). One is **awake** at a time, its heart pulsing red; cut it (three blows) and the next wakes. Strike a sleeping one and its thorns lash you. When the last is cut the thorns **recede from the stair**, and every thorn wall on the floor withers with them.

Its own creature, the **briar** (a thicket lurker, from floor 3): it **hides as a bush**, often beside brambles (now and then two eyes blink in the leaves). Come close (or linger near it) and it **lashes out and grabs you**, dragging you toward it while it squeezes (a **roll breaks free**); then it stands and swipes (parryable) until it can hide again. **Fire flushes it out**: it reels, burning, open to blows. It drops blade essence.

**Its newer monsters** (each with its own script in `scripts/towers/`, sharing `scripts/towers/brood.gd`; numbers and words in `LEAFMANTIS`, `GRAFTLING`, `GRAFTS`, `HYDRA` and their `_TEXT` tables):
- **Leafmantis** (from floor 3; `leafmantis.gd`): nearly invisible while it stands still among leaves (it waits beside brambles when it can), and can't be aimed at. Come near or linger and the leaves **rustle**, it rears and glints, then darts in with a **fast double slash** (parry the first and the second never comes; a roll takes you through both). In the open it stalks and slashes the same way until it can hide again. Fire flushes it out; burning or soaked it can't hide.
- **Graftling** (from floor 4; `graftling.gd`): alone it only claws, but beside another monster it reaches out with tendrils (strike it to break them) and **tears a part away**, grafted on where you can see it, with that monster's attack and telegraph: a hound's legs (a leap), a mongrel's quills (three spikes), an iron arm (a slam in a ring in front of it) or a stinging tail (a poisoned sting). Which part each monster gives is `GRAFTS`.
- **Hydra whelp** (from floor 5; `hydra.gd`): its heads rear back, glint and bite one after another (parry the first and the rest stop). Every quarter of its health lost **cuts off a head**, and two grow back from the stump a few seconds later (five at most), unless the wound is **burned**: burning when the head falls, or set burning soon after, the stump is seared for good.

Its own scenario, **the Closing Thicket**: step onto the mark at the room's far end and thorns grow in from the edges, ring by ring, with hedges between you and the doorway. Cut your way out before the thicket seals the doorway. It withers once it's over, whatever happens: you're never shut in.

**Where it lives.** Each tower's floor script is its `module` in `floor_styles.gd` (`scripts/towers/firebird_floor.gd`, `scripts/towers/chimera_floor.gd`, on the shared hooks of `scripts/towers/tower_floor.gd`: see the content contract below); their room shapes are in `scripts/towers/organic.gd`, the tasks in `scripts/towers/rekindle.gd` and `scripts/towers/heartroots.gd`, the scenarios in `scripts/towers/tower_scenarios.gd`, and the creatures in `scripts/towers/ashwing.gd` and `scripts/towers/briar.gd` (their behaviour and drawing in `scripts/towers/creatures.gd`). Numbers and words are data (`RULES` and `TEXT` in `firebird_floor.gd`, `chimera_floor.gd` and `creatures.gd`, `MOUTH` in `organic.gd`, the tasks in `objectives.gd` `KINDS`, the scenarios in `scenarios.gd` `SCENARIOS` and `TEXT`, the rooms' names in `themes.gd`; all in the Data editor). The floor checks (`scripts/reach.gd`) count thorns, gaps and burnt slices as passable (they're cut, carried over or regrow): thorns never cut the monsters' path grid in two, they only weigh on it. The floor bot cuts thorns in its way (`bot_step`), rides updrafts by walking onto them, and does both tasks (`bot_task`). A burst of fire reaches the tower's floor through the `blast(pos, r)` hook (`main.explode`). Tests: `tests/parts/towers_firebird_chimera.gd`; the bot sweep narrows to one tower with `tower=firebird` or `tower=chimera`.

### Giving a tower its own floors: the content contract

Every tower with floors of its own (today all six: the Gryphon, the Kraken, the Wyvern, the Manticore, the Firebird and the Chimera) works the same way, so a new one plugs in without touching shared code:

1. **Its floor script** is `scripts/towers/<tower>_floor.gd` and `extends "res://scripts/towers/tower_floor.gd"`. That base file lists every hook and does nothing in each one, so a tower overrides only what it needs. Don't declare `var main` (the base has it).
2. **Name it** in the tower's entry in `scripts/floor_styles.gd` as `"module": "res://scripts/towers/<tower>_floor.gd"`. Room shapes of its own go in `SHAPE_MODULES` (shape name → script) and are carved by its `carve(shape, size)`. Other style keys it can set: `shapes`, `own_shapes`, `gallery`, `room_grow`, `prefab`, `loops`, `corridor`.
3. **Planning hooks** (static, called while a floor is planned):
   - `shape_corridors(infos, links, corridors, walk) -> Array` returns the corridors' walkable rects. One straight rect per link comes in. The hook may record `link["tiles"]` and `link["path"]`.
   - `shape_plan(plan, infos, links, floor_num, tower)` lays the tower's ground over the finished plan. Set `plan["ok"] = false` to have the floor planned again. It writes:
     - `plan["tower_floor"] = {"module": <its path>, ...}`. `main.gd` makes the node from this.
     - `plan["impassable"]`: Rect2s nobody walks. `reach.gd` and the traps treat them as rock. Today: deep pools, flooding corridors, open drops, molten metal.
     - `plan["gates"]`: `[{tiles, lock_id, opens: {any: [tiles]} or {all: [tiles]}}]`, ground that's shut until what opens it is reached. `reach.gd` counts each one as a locked door, and the traps keep off it. Today: the Kraken's moat and sunken rooms, the Gryphon's gap, the Wyvern's raised bridges.
   - Shared checks that help: `scripts/towers/water_wind_grid.gd` has `cuts()`, which checks that impassable ground cuts nothing off, with doors open and with locked doors shut.
   - `place_task(kind, spec, infos, cands, dist, monsters, floor_num, cell)` places the pieces of its own task kind. The default spreads them over `cands` the plain way (`objectives.gd place_spread`).
   - `scenario_plan(kind, info, gates, floor_num, cell)` returns its own scenario's marks, or `{}` when the room won't do.
4. **Its own task kind** goes in `objectives.gd KINDS` with `"towers": [<tower>]` and `"module": <its path>`.
5. **Its own scenario** goes in `scenarios.gd SCENARIOS` with `"towers"`, `"module"`, optional `"seal"` (the doors shut while it runs) and `"weight"`. Give it an `scn_<kind>` room in `themes.gd`.
6. **Hooks in play** are called on `main.tower_floor`, the node made fresh each floor:
   - building: `setup(plan)`, `clear()`
   - ground: `blocks_walking(tile)` (also counts in `main.is_solid`), `slow_at(pos)`, `wet(pos)` (shallow water: `main.in_water`), `sight_k()` (how far monsters notice you), `is_void(pos)` (an open drop), `reach_live(solid, size, doors, opens)` (for the safety net)
   - the dev tool: `open_gates()` (Open all doors opens its gates too)
   - swings: `strike(from, dir, reach, arc)` (every melee swing); bursts: `blast(pos, r)` (`main.explode`: bombs, exploding spells)
   - its task: `task_tick(ob, delta, p, hurt)`, `task_strike(ob, from, dir, reach, arc)`; it draws its own pieces
   - its scenario: `scenario_begin(s, host)`, `scenario_tick(s, delta, p, host)` (calls `host._finish(s, grade)`), `scenario_title(s)`, `scenario_status(s)`, `scenario_goal(s)`; it draws it too
7. **Floor bot hooks** (`tests/floor_bot_driver.gd`):
   - `bot_step(bot)`: a step before anything else (a crank)
   - `bot_task(bot)`: its own task's next step
   - `bot_grid(astar, bot)`: costs and walls on the bot's grid
   - `bot_avoid()`: tiles never to step on
   - `bot_steer(pos, d)`: adjusts the stick (bracing against wind)
8. **Its creature** is a script that `extends "res://scripts/enemy.gd"`, listed in `enemy.gd SUBKINDS` and spawned with `EnemyScript.make(kind)`. It overrides what it needs: `setup`, `_act`, `_draw_own`, `_overlay_extra`, `take_hit`, `on_parried`. Give it an entry in `enemy.gd TYPES`, `floors.gd MONSTERS`, `essence.gd DROPS`, `gear.gd FAVOUR`, the tower's `weights`, and sounds in `sound_bank.gd` (`hurt_<kind>`, `kill_<kind>`).
9. **Data editor:** put its tables in `static var`s and register them in `data_registry.gd`.
10. **Drawing and sight:**
    - `paint_layout(room_view, batch) -> bool`: draws the floor's walls, rock, cover and door posts itself (`room.gd`).
    - `holes_open_to_rock() -> bool`: true when the floor's rock is open air (an abyss), so a chasm beside it opens into the air instead of getting a shaft wall (`holes.gd`).
    - A way down of its own (`ways_down.gd`): add the tower to `WAYS`, call `WaysDown.plan_for(plan, infos, floor_num, <tower>, opts)` at the end of `shape_plan`, `ways = WaysDown.make(main, self, plan)` in `setup`, and pass `is_void`, `bot_avoid`, `bot_steer` and `reach_live` on to it.
    - `sight_open() -> PackedByteArray`: rock the eye sees across, one byte per grid tile, read as each floor starts (`fog.gd`).
    - Anything else it draws, it draws on its own node (z under the climber), or on a child it adds to `main.room_view` (as the Kraken's water does, under the pillars).

**Older per-tower hooks map onto these:**

| Older hook | Use instead |
| --- | --- |
| a style's "builder", `after_build(plan, infos, links, floor_num, tower, style)` | the style's `module` and `shape_plan(plan, infos, links, floor_num, tower)` (the style is `floor_styles.gd for_tower(tid)`) |
| a style's "script", `finish_plan`, `script_for` | the same |
| an organic `carve()` | `carve()` with the shape in `SHAPE_MODULES` |
| the "prefab" factor | the style's `prefab` |
| `main.tower_systems` / `tower_call(name)` | the one `main.tower_floor` and its hooks (a tower with several systems runs them inside its own node) |
| `tower_call("strike")` from `player._melee` | `strike()` |
| a `water_slow` factor | `slow_at()` (and `wet()` for shallow water) |
| `main.sight_open` / `fog.see_open` | `sight_open()` |
| how far you're seen | `sight_k()` |
| `room.gd layout_painter.paint()` | `paint_layout()` |
| "towers"/"script" task kinds with custom delegation | "towers"/"module" kinds, `place_task` / `task_tick` / `task_strike` (the kind draws itself) |
| "towers"/"script" scenarios and `handler()` | "towers"/"module" scenarios and the `scenario_*` hooks |
| creature hooks | an `enemy.gd SUBKINDS` script |
| `bot_go` | `bot_steer()` (or `bot_step()` for a step of its own) |
| a custom `bot_step` | `bot_step()` |
| `bot_grid` | `bot_grid()` |
| `no_foot` / `fluid_locks` / `lever_gates` | `impassable` and `gates` |

## Marks, essence and the Regard

- **Six body parts**, one for each of the six towers: hand, legs, heart, brow, throat and back. Each holds one mark. A **backstab** (striking a foe that faces away from you, because it has committed to an attack or you rolled behind it) deals 50% more damage.

- **Your first climb starts with no mark.** You start clumsy, with weapons only. It's a normal climb (camps, the stair's barriers, twelve floors), and floor 6 opens with the Regard (below).
- **Essence:** monsters leave essence when they fall: Air, Water, Iron, Fire or Stone (slimes Water, archers Air, knights Iron, cinders and the Wyvern Fire). It flies into you and is **banked at the next stair**. Fall before the stair and the unbanked essence is lost.
- **Banked essence is split, not copied** (`scripts/essence_flow.gd`, every number in the Data editor's "Essence flow"). At each stair what you bank is divided three ways, and the stair's line says how much went where ("Banked 50: 20 to your body, 26 to your marks, 4 into crystals"):
  - **Your body: about 39%** (30 parts of the 70, of the 92% left after the crystals). It soaks into your body parts, spread by what each part did on that floor (below). Unmarked parts fill toward a new mark; every part settles it as density (your attributes, see **Essence density**).
  - **Your marks: about 53%** (40 parts of the 70). It refills what each mark's spells used on that floor and deepens the mark by that use (below, **Marks grow by use**); of what your marks can't take, a tenth condenses into crystals and the rest goes back to the well (the stair says so).
  - **Crystals: 8%.** It condenses into essence crystals, the train's money (see **Money**): a fixed share (`SPLIT`, no longer a setting; it was 20%, with every essence find at 87% now so what flows into you is what it was); your body and your marks share the rest, 3 to 4. Of what the marks can't take, a tenth condenses too (`OVERFLOW`) and the rest goes back to the well.
  - With no mark to take it (the first climb), the marks' share goes to your body. The Unbound have no marks: everything but the crystals pools raw in their parts, as before.
- **Eating a crystal** (the climber's book: the Essence ribbon, then **Crystals and tinctures**; or the Inventory in a climb): its essence flows into you the natural way, the same split without the crystals' share; what your marks don't take (they take what refills and deepens their use, else a trickle) soaks into your body instead. You can't steer it.
- **Tinctures are the only way to steer essence.** A tincture (Air Tincture, Fire Tincture...) is a vial of 40 essence of one kind. Drink it and **choose** where it goes: one of your **marks** (it grows; its own kind counts fully, any other a little) or an **unmarked body part** (it soaks toward a new mark there, and leans that mark toward the tincture's kind). Where they come from: vault pedestals, secret rooms' hidden caches (`TINCTURE`), and now and then a slain monster that left no item or essence (`DROPS` "tincture_chance" in `items.gd`, never on the first climb); they come in one size (no Minor or Greater: found, bought and crafted ones are the same 40); the **outfitter** stocks one kind each visit (half the time the essence of the tower you last climbed, else another; 60 of its kind); the **Workshop**'s Tinctures counter crafts any kind from 40 crystals of it (a Rune Artist pays less). They go on your belt like any item: use one in a climb and the Crystals and tinctures page opens on it, listing your six body parts; choose one. On the train, drink packed ones from the book, the Loadout (a tincture's card: **Drink it now**) or the Workshop (**Drink one**). A tincture left on your belt comes home packed for your next climb (a found one put back gives nothing).
- **Forcing past full** (`scripts/forcing.gd`, every word and number; `FORCE` and `LADDER` in the Data editor's "Forcing past full"). A full mark (no use waiting to be refilled) or a full Node (no room for the kind, no use waiting) takes nothing from a crystal: a mark lets it soak into your body, a Node leaves it a crystal. **Force it**, a separate button beside **Eat** on Crystals and tinctures (for the marked, one for each full mark; for the Unbound, the Node the crystal flows to, and beside a full Node on a tincture's card), pushes the essence in anyway: the part **stretches** (a mark deepens, a Node's size grows) at **4x the essence its use would take** for the same growth (a mark: what a stair spends to refill and deepen it by use; a Node: its `GROW_COST`), its own kind fully and any other a third. A crystal forced is 10 essence; a tincture forced is its 40, not concentrated. Every forced essence adds **1.2 strain** to that part (12 a crystal), with **no cap a climb**: the strain is the brake. It fades **4 at each stair** and **20 as a climb starts** (gone in a climb or two; the Unbound's Broad Back fades it twice as fast). **The ladder** is the marks' own burn steps: **strained** (above 0: the part refills a quarter slower), **burned** at 40 (a mark is burned, as overdrawing burns it; a Node's casting strain never falls below its forced strain, so its casts erupt sooner), **paralyzed** at 70 (a mark can't be cast; a Node goes dark), and a push that reaches **100**: a marked climber's mark **burns out** (dark for this climb and the next 3, shallower, a slip toward a Maker: the guardians frown on it), an Unbound's Node **cracks** (it loses 8 of its size, never falling back asleep, and keeps 40 strain). In a climb a mark already burned or paralyzed by overdrawing starts the push from that step, and the strain applies at once, mid-fight. **It always asks first** (the shared pop-up: what it would grow, and "This pushes your Hand to Burned..."); Cancel does nothing. The Entity explains it the first time the button shows, "How does it work?" has "Forcing a Node or mark?", and the button has hover and hold help. **The pace holds** (`tests/progression_model.gd` with `force`): a typical climber who force-eats every crystal they get, short of burned, still takes their first mark to step 12 after 22 climbs (summiting every climb, 8 against 9), their main Node to level 12 after 23 (unchanged), and all six Nodes no sooner (80 against 78); `tests/parts/progression.gd`, `unbound_cup.gd`, `essence_pace.gd` and `forcing.gd` hold it.
- **Your body's share soaks in** by what each part does (every number in `scripts/part_soak.gd` `SOAK`; only each part's share matters): the **hand** from weapon strikes and shots that land (+2 a hit), the **legs** from a roll or jump that carries you clear of a blow that was coming (a foe winding up near you, or a shot flying at you, that then misses: +3, a jump +4), a roll through an attack (+3), and a leap over a gap, burning floor, a trap or a low blow (+4), at most once every 0.6 s (`LEGS_GAP`: rolls at nothing count for nothing), the **heart** from hits you take and survive (+3), the **brow** from parries (+4, perfect +6), the **throat** when one swing or spell catches several foes (+4 for each foe past the first), the **back** from backstabs (+3.5) and finishing blows (+1.5). Some follow how you fight, some what you struggle with (the heart fills from hits taken, for a mark that helps you last), so a new mark always helps. **Fading:** on one floor, each further unit of the same thing soaks less: the n-th is worth 1 / (1 + n / h) of the first (h = 6, strikes and shots 3.5: `FADE`), and every stair starts it again (`main.gd soak_worn`), so spamming one thing is slower than a bit of everything (a mark's fill for casting never fades). `tests/parts/part_soak.gd` models a typical climber (strikes and shots by the dozen, some parries and rolls, plenty of hits taken, few jumps) and an archer, a dodger, a tank, a backstabber, a parrier and a cleaver: the typical climber's first mark lands on any of the six parts (none more than about a quarter of the time) and each style fills its own part first.
- **Low blows** (`enemy.gd _low_blow`, `_low_marks`): a knight's leg sweep all round it (from floor 3), a shellback rearing to slam the floor (more often when you stand behind it), the Kraken's tentacle sweep and the Wyvern's tail sweep go along the floor: their red warning carries small upward chevrons, and a jump clears them (filling the legs). The leg sweep and the slam can't be parried (jump, roll through or roll out); the tentacle and tail still can. The Entity explains them the first time (`low_sweep`), and the first leap that fills the legs (`leap`). Unmarked parts glow faintly as they soak, and the climber screen's Marks tab (Tab) shows each unmarked part's soak, the leader ("your next mark is forming"), and which bond it would be right now.
- **Where and which:** your play style decides the body part (the one you used most), and the essence you gathered decides the bond. No bond has a home body part. When two bonds share an essence (say, the three Air bonds), the way you fought this climb breaks the tie: lots of parrying leans Hunter, lots of striking Stormcaller, lots of dodging Windwalker.
- **The Regard** (`scripts/climb_shape.gd`, every word and number in its tables): on your first climb, reaching **floor 6** opens with it. The tower's **guardian** (its beast) takes notice, your most-used body part takes its first **mark** at full strength, and the essence you gathered most decides which bond it is. Dismissing it first asks whether you accept the mark (refusing makes you **Unbound**, see **The Unbound**), then the guardian offers an **exit**: two cards, **Leave with the mark** (the climb ends as a survived one: paid, the belt into crystals, your pack and what you wear come home, an heirloom, then the train) or **Climb on** (the Proving follows on the same floor, then floors 7 to 12; from then on a fall is an ordinary fall). **Fall before floor 6** and you're still regarded, but the mark is **faint**, as strong as the highest floor you reached (`DEATH_MARK`): **50%** on floor 1, **10% more a floor**, full on floor 6. A faint mark hits at its strength and costs that much more (half strength: half power, half again dearer) until it has soaked up its debt of essence (80 for a mark at 75%, scaled up as the strength falls: 160 at half strength; from your marks' share, or a tincture). Break a vault's seal or a lock-in room's doorway with bare fists before the Regard, and there's no question: the guardian **refuses** to mark you and you are Unbound; slip past a stair's barrier with no mark and it refuses you on the spot (see **The climb**).
- **The Regard's glimpse** (`scripts/fracture.gd`, its words and seconds in the Data editor's "The cities"): reached on floor 6 (not fallen, not refused), the Regard opens with a glimpse of **the last fracture**, before the guardian's eye: the world, a shaded sphere lit from the top-left, cracks bleeding the six essences' light, a white flash, then six towers standing round it as it fades, with three short captions (**4.8 seconds**; Space, Enter, a click or a tap skips it after the first 0.4). Then the Regard plays as before, and its words end with the guardian's **charge**: "Guard the towers. Stop those who oppose the marks." "Do not let the world crack again." The Entity says a word once it's over (`regard_glimpse`).
- **The seal on a faint mark** (`scripts/mark_seal.gd`): a first mark taken in a fall before the Regard carries the guardian's **seal** (kept as `"seal"` on the mark's record). While the mark is still faint (its debt not yet paid), the climber screen's **Marks** tab, **on the train only**, shows the seal under the marks: what it is, a warning in the warning colour, and **Break the seal**. It asks first (the confirmation pop-up, with the warning); **Confirm** breaks it: the mark comes away (every mark with it) and you are **Unbound**, through the same path as refusing at the Regard (`unbound.gd become`): the third way in. Once the mark has grown to full strength the seal is gone. An old save's faint marks carry none. The Entity: a first-time moment (`mark_seal`), another on breaking it (`mark_seal_broken`), "How does it work?" has **The seal on a faint mark?**, and the button has hover and hold help (`mark_seal_break`).
- **Marks grow by use** (`scripts/mark_use.gd`, every word and number; `USE` and `BURN` in the Data editor's "Mark growth and essence"). Each mark holds a **pool** of essence with a **depth**: the depth carries it up its steps, to step 12 (`STEP_XP` in `profile.gd`: the depth each step needs, kept under the save's old name "xp"), and a deeper pool also holds a little more in a climb (between its step's and the next step's). A marked climber never sees the pool's numbers: the climber screen shows a bar and words ("Feels deep for step 4", "brimming", "nearly dry"), with hover and hold help on the bar. In a climb the mark's spells **draw on its pool**; every cast notes what it drew, and an overdrawn cast also what it pushed past the pool (pushing counts). **At each stair** the marks' share of what you bank first **refills what each mark used** (that much goes back into its pool for the next floor), then **deepens it by 0.6 x that use**, each 1 depth using up 1 banked essence. Its own kind of essence pays fully; another kind pays a third. Each unit of use asks for both at once, so a stair that can't pay all of it pays the same part of each: **casting more than you bank adds nothing** beyond what the bank can pay (and never grows a mark less than casting a little). A mark that **wasn't used** takes in only a trickle (5% of its own kind). A faint mark pays its debt from the marks' share first, used or not. The more marks your body carries the slower each deepens (`BODY_DRAG`). **Whatever the marks leave condenses into crystals**: essence is never lost. There is **no cap a climb**: banked essence and burns hold the pace. Step 2 needs a depth of 170, step 3 300, step 4 420, step 5 540, step 6 820, then 960, 1100, 1240, 1400, 1560 and 1740 for step 12. **Winning the Proving** (floor 6) deepens one mark by 36 (`PROVING_GROWTH`), the one whose body part you used most that climb, and a tincture of its own kind adds about 20 (a tincture's essence is concentrated: `TINCTURE` "mark_boost" in `essence_flow.gd`). Measured with `tests/progression_measure.gd` (a model of a typical climber over real floors' essence, casting the first mark about four times a floor, `tests/progression_model.gd`): climbing half the time in the mark's tower and reaching the summit every other climb, step 2 comes after about 2 climbs, step 5 after 6, step 6 after 8 and step 12 after about 23 (a summit in its own tower every climb: step 5 after 3, step 6 after 4, step 12 after 9: the owner's pace, the same as an Unbound Node's); a mark that is never cast grows less than half as fast; and a second mark comes after about 8 climbs (6 summiting every time). Play style still decides which body part takes the next mark (below). **Old saves** keep every step: a mark's progress is converted to depth as it loads, the same step and as far through it (`OLD_STEP_XP`, `profile/mark_depth` in the save). Your marks and their depth are saved.
- **Later marks: at most one a climb.** Unmarked body parts fill like batteries from your body's share (`SOAK_RATE` of their portion), the fuller one is the slower it fills, and a body that already carries more marks fills every part slower still. **The next mark forms in one place at a time:** only the fullest unmarked part fills all the way; the others fill up to `FORMING_OTHERS` (80%) of `NEW_MARK_SOAK` (just short of ready) and wait there (never losing what they hold), so the marks after the second stay apart even for a climber who does a bit of everything (`profile.gd settle_soak`). A full part (`MARK_FULL` of `NEW_MARK_SOAK`) takes a new mark, shaped by the essence you've banked since your last one:
  - **at a summit**, when the tower's beast takes notice;
  - **earlier, earned**: break into a **sealed wing** on floor 6 or below (`EARNED` in `essence_flow.gd`), keep its rule and beat it, and **the Maker of Marks** grants the mark there: its line on the screen, the light shifting to its cold blue around you, its sign over you, and the new-mark sound. The Entity hints at this beforehand (the first sealed door you see on an early floor, and when a part is nearly full): breaking in can bring rewards, if you prove yourself to those that watch;
  - **in a fall**: fall with a full part and there's a small chance (12%, `FALL_MARK`) it takes a **faint** mark anyway, like a fallen first climb's.
- **Each bond is a kit:** a **Body** (an upgrade to a basic action), a **Passive**, and **spells learned as it grows** (new spells at steps 1, 2, 4 and 5; step 3 changes the first). **Tap** a mark's key to cast its selected spell; **hold** it to open the wheel of its spells (the game slows), point at one and let go to put it on the button: **choosing never casts**, the next tap does. On a phone, hold the mark's button and slide onto a spell (the wheel opens beside your thumb, over the buttons).
- **The Gryphon's bonds (complete):**
  - **Stormcaller** (Air, hand). Body: Quick Nerves (strikes and rolls recover faster). Passive: Static (rank II at step 3: charged foes take 20% more from everything; rank III at step 5: a charged foe's death makes your next Call Lightning free). Spells: Call Lightning (a bolt from above strikes the foe in your aim for 7, 8 at step 2, 10 at step 4 and 11 at step 5, and charges it for 8 seconds; when it dies, lightning leaps from it to every foe within 170 for 12, up to 18 at step 5; costs 10), Chain Lightning (step 2: leaps between up to 5 foes, leaving each charged; costs 12), Call Lightning transformed at step 3 (Branching Storm or Living Bolt, below), Lightning Rod (a rod planted where you aim for 6 seconds: every second it strikes and charges the 2 foes nearest it, within 150, for 3, 4 at step 5; a Chain Lightning with no foe left in reach leaps to it and on from it, around corners, and one that ends near it strikes it too, either way making it beat at once; when it ends, every charged foe within 170 has its charge drawn out in a burst of 4, 5 at step 5; a new rod bursts the old one; costs 10), Thunderhead (chains leap twice as often for 8 seconds; its Apex: every strike you land meanwhile calls a bolt from above for 8, which charges the foe).
  - **Windwalker** (Air, legs). Body: Wind Dash. Passive: Featherweight (rank II: your strikes keep airborne foes aloft a little longer; rank III: your rolls leave a gust that lifts the light foes near where you started). Spells: Gale Lift, Crosswind (a wind wall that turns arrows back), Gale Lift transformed at step 3 (Skyward or Tailwind), Downdraft (slams airborne foes into the ground), Eye of the Storm (a cyclone that holds foes aloft; its Apex: it drifts after your aim, so you steer it).
  - **Hunter** (Air, brow). Body: Keen Eyes (see farther in the dark and spot hidden traps). Passive: Quarry (the last foe you struck is always marked, with an arrow at the screen edge when it's out of sight; rank II: your Quarry takes 25% more from you; rank III: felling your Quarry marks the nearest foe and gives back a Piercing Shot's essence). Spells: Piercing Shot, Track (reveals foes and where they're heading), Piercing Shot transformed at step 3 (Seeking Shot or Rending Shot), Hawk's Eye (arrows deal double damage for 5 seconds), Rain of Arrows (its Apex: the rain drifts after your Quarry).
- **The Kraken's bonds (complete):**
  - **Tidecaller** (Water, legs). Body: Waterborn (full speed through the Kraken's standing water, which slows everyone else). Passive: Undercurrent (foes you drive into a wall are stunned; rank II: a shoved foe knocked into another stuns them both; rank III: your knockbacks leave foes soaked). Spells: Tidal Surge, Riptide (pull a line of foes to you), Tidal Surge transformed at step 3 (Breaking Wave or Return Tide), Carry (a wave rolls 340 forward and sweeps up the foes in front of you: each takes 10 as it is picked up, 12 at step 5; the wave draws them toward its middle, and carried foes that crash into each other both take 4, 5 at step 5, each pair once and a foe at most three times; a foe dashed against a wall or pillar takes 12 more, 14 at step 5, and Undercurrent's stun; where the wave runs out it lets them go; costs 14), Maelstrom (a whirlpool drags foes to its centre; its Apex: the foes it drags are soaked and drenched, taking double from lightning and frost for 2 seconds after).
  - **Warden** (Water, brow). Body: Heavy Frame (keep holding parry to block). Passive: Steadfast (no knockback or interruption while your barrier holds; rank II: a parry while shielded restores 35% of the barrier; rank III: a barrier broken by blows gives back half of Barrier's cost). Spells: Barrier (a shell that absorbs damage), Absorb (store the hits you take in the barrier), Barrier transformed at step 3 (Bastion or Moving Wall), Challenge (nearby foes must come at you and take 50% more damage), Unbreakable (4 seconds of no damage; its Apex: you may strike at half damage, and everything it kept from you bursts out at the end over 130, at least 10).
  - **Deepcaller** (Water, heart). Body: Deep Lungs (stamina refills faster). Passive: Pressure (badly hurt foes near you slow down; rank II: they also hit 25% softer; rank III: a kill near you heals you 6). Spells: Undertow (drain up to 3 foes and heal), Anchor (tether a foe to you), Undertow transformed at step 3 (Abyssal Draw or Cold Current), Siphon (drain essence to refill your marks), Drink Deep (a drain that grows stronger the longer it holds; its Apex: when its foe dies the drain jumps to the nearest foe within 260, its strength kept).
- **The Wyvern's bonds (complete):**
  - **Blade Forger** (Iron). Body: Long Edge (melee strikes reach farther). Passive: Weapon Memory (conjured blades copy the weapon you hold: more, smaller knives with fists or a dagger; fewer, harder blades with a greatsword or war hammer; fast, harder darts with a bow or crossbow). Spells: Conjured Blades, Recall (blades fly out and come back, cutting again on the way), Conjured Blades transformed at step 3 (Embedded Steel or Weapon Echo, below), Bladeward (blades orbit you for 6 seconds, cutting anything close), Thousand Edges (a storm of blades toward your aim; its Apex leaves edges on the floor).
  - **Flameblade** (Iron: it grows from the Wyvern's essence, like every mark from its own tower's). Body: Hot Blood (strike chains never slow down; even the finisher recovers fast). Passive: Heat (each strike in a row heats your weapon, up to half again as much damage; it fades if you stop striking; shown as an arc around you). Spells: Kindle Blade (your weapon burns for 6 seconds: extra damage and heat builds twice as fast), Flame Lash (a wave of fire), Kindle Blade transformed at step 3 (Forge Heart or Brand, below), Searing Parry (kindles your weapon; while it burns, a parry explodes against the attacker), Overheat (release all your heat in one huge blow; its Apex erupts in a ring at full heat).
  - **Rune Artist** (Iron). Body: Steady Mind (every spell from every bond winds up 30% faster). Passive: Runesmith (at a camp, also inscribe a rune on your gear for the rest of the climb, or craft one more item). Spells: Rune Trap (a rune on the floor that detonates under a foe; up to 3), Inscribe (a rune on your gear for the floor: sharpness, warding or swiftness, changing each cast), Rune Trap transformed at step 3 (Linked Runes or Creeping Runes, below), Ward Circle (foes can't enter it for 5 seconds), Grand Sigil (for 6 seconds it sears the foes inside it for 6 every second, and inside it your spells cost nothing; its Apex moves it with you).
- **The Manticore's bonds (complete):**
  - **Sandshaper** (Stone). Body: Rooted Stance (you can't be knocked back or slowed by water). Passive: Shifting Sands (foes walking up to you slow in their last few steps; never an attack already under way). Spells: Quake (hurts foes around you and leaves rubble that slows them), Sand Wall (a wall across your aim that blocks foes and their shots for 5 seconds; you can walk through your own walls), Quake transformed at step 3 (Shatter or Upheaval, below), Sinkhole (a pit at your aim swallows a small foe, or traps a large one), Fault Line (a long split in the floor foes can't cross for 8 seconds; its Apex buries the foes on it).
  - **Venomist** (Stone). Body: Venom Glands (weapon strikes add a stack of poison). Passive: Toxin Build (poison stacks up to 12, and each stack makes it last longer; green drops show the stacks). Spells: Venom Spit, Toxic Cloud (poisons foes standing in it), Venom Spit transformed at step 3 (Corrosive or Bloom, below), Neurotoxin (your next hit on a poisoned foe paralyzes it for 2 seconds), Virulence (every poisoned foe within 240 takes all its remaining poison at once plus 4 for each stack; the venom then leaps to every foe within 90 of a burst that wasn't poisoned, with half the stacks of the most poisoned foe that burst beside it; a foe that had 6 stacks or more is staggered for a second, half as long for a beast; costs 14; every number is in its spell's steps in `bonds.gd`; its Apex refills the mark).
  - **Ashwalker** (Stone: the Manticore's essence). Body: Smokeborn (your light reaches farther). Passive: Unseen Strike (striking a foe that hasn't noticed you yet, or has lost you, deals triple damage; a "?" shows over foes that have lost you). Spells: Smoke Veil (foes inside the smoke lose track of you), Afterimage (an ash copy of you that nearby foes go after), Smoke Veil transformed at step 3 (Ember Smoke or Shroud Walk, below), Vanish (unseen until you strike or 4 seconds pass), Ashfall (for 10 seconds, foes lose you once you're out of reach; its Apex lets an unseen strike leap on). Beasts never lose track of you.
- **The Firebird's bonds (complete):**
  - **Flame Contractor** (Fire). Body: Casting Stride (you can walk while a spell winds up). Passive: Kindled (foes your fire kills leave burning ground that burns foes, not you; **II** at step 3: your burning ground slowly spreads, 4 px a second up to 1.6 times its size; **III** at step 5: each foe your fire kills gives the mark back 2 essence). Spells: Fireball (explodes on impact), Fire Wall (a line of your own burning ground), at step 3 a Transformation of Fireball (**Sunfire** or **Seeker Flame**, below), Cluster Blast (splits into three explosions), Meteor (its **Apex**: a short wind-up, 0.5 s instead of 1, then a great explosion at your aim that leaves a burning crater for 4 seconds).
  - **Flamewing** (Fire). Body: Phoenix Glide (hold jump to glide). Passive: Fireproof (burning floor doesn't hurt you; **II**: standing in fire, yours or not, heals you 1.5 every half second; **III**: fire that would hurt you, burning floor or a fire trap's blast, feeds the aura's heat and the mark's essence instead). Spells: Heat Aura (burns foes near you while it drains its mark; cast again to turn it off), Wingburst (release the aura's built-up heat as burning wings), at step 3 **Furnace** or **Ember Wings**, Warmth (for 10 seconds your aura also restores your health; it will strengthen creatures and allies once those exist), Firebird Form (for 8 seconds the aura reaches half again as far and Wingburst is free, once every second and a half; its **Apex**: you fly while it lasts, over chasms, burning floor, water, pressure plates and anything a jump clears, and drop back to the ground when it ends).
  - **Rekindler** (Fire). Body: Stubborn Heart (below a third of your health you move and recover faster). Passive: Last Ember (once per floor a killing blow leaves you burning at 1 health for 3 seconds; land a hit to rise again with a fifth of your health; **II**: twice a floor; **III**: surviving it erupts in flame around you, 30 to every foe within 110, and leaves burning ground). Spells: Rekindle (you burst into flame for 5 seconds: you lose 12% of your health, 14% at step 4 and 15% at step 5, while a ring of fire around you burns every foe within 65 for 2.5 every half second, rising to 4.5 within 75 by step 5, and sets each one burning, so it burns on after the ring ends; when the flames go out you regain twice what you lost, whatever you did meanwhile; costs 10), Desperation (for 8 seconds you strike harder and faster: a quarter harder and a tenth faster at full health, up to two and a half times as hard and 40% faster as your health runs out), at step 3 **Bonfire** or **Cauterize**, Pyre Oath (for 10 seconds every foe you slay bursts into flame, 14 to the foes within 80, 18 at step 5, and a killing blow makes you explode instead), Phoenix Heart (never cast: it waits in the mark, and pressing its button only says so. On death you rise in a burst of flame with full health, and for 6 seconds you cannot take damage; once a floor, and only while the mark holds a full charge of essence: rising spends all of it. If Last Ember is ready too, Last Ember goes first, and Phoenix Heart catches you if it burns out; its **Apex**: the burst throws back every foe within 120, hurts each for 24 and sets it burning. `player.gd _phoenix_rise`).
- **The Chimera's bonds (complete):**
  - **Covenant** (Blade). Body: Pack Stride (faster with one of your creatures nearby). Passive: Kinship (monsters of a kind in your kin book are slower to notice you; **II**: your creatures stay a third longer; **III**: a creature of yours that falls heals you for a quarter of its health). Spells: Bind and Call (aimed at a monster below a quarter of its health it binds that kind into your **kin book**, kept between climbs; otherwise it calls the next creature of your retinue, the first three kinds in the book, to fight beside you for 30 seconds; a spirit familiar until you've bound one), Command (aimed at a foe, every creature attacks it; aimed at no one, they guard you), at step 3 **Pack Call** or **Champion Bond**, Exchange (swap places with your nearest creature), Grand Call (every creature in your book at once for 12 seconds; its **Apex**: they arrive empowered, half again as strong, and it no longer drains the whole mark, only its cost of 30). Never more than three creatures out at once (Grand Call aside); creatures are as strong as your mark's step, can be killed, and leave when you take the stair. Monsters fight a creature that's closer than you. The book's Record tab counts the kinds bound ("Kin: N bound").
  - **Grafter** (Blade). Body: Adaptive Flesh (each graft strengthens its body part's basic action: hand strikes +20%, legs rolls cheaper and longer, heart takes 15% less damage, brow parry window +25%, throat stamina +25%, back backstabs hit harder). Passive: Grafting (slain monsters often leave a graft: 75% of the time while you carry none, then 55%, 40% and 30% from three on, `GRAFT_DROP` in `main.gd`, in the Data editor; walk over it to bind it to its body part, one per part, for the rest of the climb; **II**: each graft you carry also gives you 5 more health; **III**: a new graft heals its body part's burn). Spells: Graft Burst (flesh lashes out around you: 11 to every foe within 120, shoved back, 12 at step 2, 14 at step 4, 16 at step 5; each graft you carry adds 4 and 6 to its reach; a foe it slays always leaves a graft, if that part is free), Mimic (your newest graft's monster trick, once; with no graft, the trick of the foe you aim at, within 260), at step 3 **Hungry Flesh** or **Flesh Shell**, Shed (cast off your newest graft in a burst of 20, 22 at step 5, plus 4 a graft, that heals its part's burn; with no graft, you shed a layer of skin: a burst at 80% that heals this mark's own burn; costs 6), Chimera Form (every graft's power doubled for 10 seconds; its **Apex**: every second the next graft you carry uses its power by itself, the same powers as Mimic aimed at the nearest foe within 170, without moving you, at 14 damage).
  - **Surgeon** (Blade). Body: Steady Hands (healing heals more, including the stair's rest and healing items, which also work faster). Passive: Diagnosis (you see every foe's health and a note on what it's weak to; **II**: striking a foe where it's weak, from behind or in the opening after its attack, staggers it for half a second, at most once every 3 seconds a foe; **III**: Mend and Bloodletting heal 20% more for every bleeding foe within 220). Spells: Sever (cripples a foe: it can't run or charge), Mend (heals you and nearby creatures, cures poison), at step 3 **Vivisect** or **Arterial Cut**, Transfer (the foe you aim at takes your wounds: 10, 12 at step 5, plus a fifth of the health you're missing, at most 30 more; you heal half of what it takes, and your burns and poison go to it too), Amputate (a deep cut for 20; a foe it leaves below half its health is crippled and disarmed for the rest of the fight; its **Apex**: below three quarters, not just half).
- **Overdraw:** casting without enough essence still works, but the cast is weaker (70%) and the body part **burns**.
- **Burns stack** (`scripts/mark_use.gd`, `bond.gd` pay). **Burned**: its body part is weaker (hand strikes weaker, legs rolls costlier, heart caps your health lower, brow shortens your parry window, throat slows stamina recovery, back loses the backstab bonus). Overdraw a burned mark again and it **seizes up, paralyzed**: the missing essence tears out of your health, so spamming spells can kill you, and a paralyzed mark **can't be cast** at all (its seal shows a red cross; it still weakens its body part). **Each stair heals one rung**: paralyzed to burned, burned to healed (a camp's rest heals a rung too, a Cleansing Tonic heals a burn, and a new climb starts whole). A paralyzed mark can only be cast by a deliberate **forced cast**: hold its key or its touch button for a second while a red warning fills on screen ("FORCING YOUR HAND MARK", and its touch button reads HOLD and fills); let go before it fills and nothing happens. A forced cast fires the spell but **burns the mark out**: it **goes dark** for the rest of that climb and the next three (no casting, no passive, no body; it takes no banked essence), **loses some depth** (half a step's worth, never more than one step back), is **never erased**, and **counts toward a Maker's visit the same way a slip past a barrier does** (every third slip, across climbs, brings a Maker: if a burnout is the third, the Maker comes at the next stair). The guardians frown on a damaged mark: it was their gift. The Entity explains each rung the first time (`entity.gd` TIPS "overdraw", "mark_paralyzed", "mark_force", "mark_burnout"), and "How do things work here?" has "What if I overdraw a mark?".

## States, reactions and synergies

Marks work together. Spells leave **states** on a foe, two states that meet set off a **reaction**, and marks carried together form **synergies**: any two lend each other a trait, and twelve pairs make a **named synergy**. Every name, line, colour and number is in data, in the Data editor's **States, reactions and synergies** sections (words, numbers and colours): `scripts/reactions.gd` (`STATES`, `REACTIONS`, `RULES`, `CLIMBER`) and `scripts/synergies.gd` (`SYNERGIES`, `TRAITS`, `GRAFT_POWER`, `TEXT`).

**States.** A foe can be **charged**, **airborne**, **soaked**, **burning**, **poisoned**, **bleeding**, **slowed** or **unseen** (it has lost track of you: smoke, a decoy, Vanish, Ashfall, Steam, Mist). They live in one place, the foe's `states` dictionary (`enemy.gd`): state to seconds left. The foe's older fields are that same dictionary under their old names (`charged`, `airborne`, `burn_t`, `venom_t`, `chill_t`, `lost_t`), so everything that already charged, lifted, burned, poisoned, slowed or blinded a foe gives it the state too: Stormcaller's lightning and Static arcs, Gale Lift and the cyclones, venom, of Embers and burning ground, a Coral Trident's chill, Ashwalker's smoke and decoys. New: **soaked** (Tidecaller's waves, Riptide, Carry, the Maelstrom, Flood, Geysers, Tidebreak's waves, Deepcaller's Undertow, Anchor, Leech and Drown, the Unbound's Water and its Chill, Current and Mist, and any foe standing in a pool) and a general **bleeding** (`enemy.gd bleed(dps, seconds)`: Surgeon's Sever and Bloodletting, the Unbound's Blade, Rend and Answer, Dust Devil's shards). Fire burns (Fireball, Cluster, Meteor, Firewall and every burning floor, Fire Lash, Overheat, Wingburst, the Heat Aura, an ignited Smoke Veil, a kindled weapon). A heavy blow on a foe in the air is a **slam** (Quake, Downdraft, a finisher, an explosion), and a Deepcaller's drains and a Lifeline are a **drain**: moments, not states. Each state shows as a small badge over the foe's head, a glyph of its own in its colour (a bolt, an arrow up, a drop, a flame, bubbles, a red drop, a snowflake; it blinks as it runs out), with drips, flames or blood on the body; unseen keeps its "?".

**Reactions.** When a state lands on a foe that holds the other of a pair, the reaction fires once (then waits its `again` seconds on that foe) and its word rises over the foe:

| States that meet | Reaction | What happens |
| --- | --- | --- |
| soaked + charged | Conduct | lightning jumps to every soaked foe within 140; each is struck, stunned 1 s and dries off |
| soaked + burning | Steam | a cloud where it stood; foes inside lose track of you (both states go) |
| burning + poisoned | Fumes | the poison spreads to the foes beside it as burning gas (half its stacks, and burning) |
| airborne + slam | Crater | slammed out of the air: a big hit, and the foes around it are struck and knocked down |
| airborne + charged | Skybolt | a bolt from above strikes it, and it falls |
| bleeding + drained | Hemorrhage | the drain is doubled |
| slowed + burning | Smoulder | the burn lasts twice as long |
| unseen + poisoned | Ambush | your first poison on a foe that has lost track of you: 5 more stacks (smoke on a foe already poisoned is no ambush) |

Reaction damage grows 15% for each step of your strongest mark past the first (`RULES per_step`); a chain of reactions setting each other off through a crowd stops after 4 (`RULES depth`). Monsters give *you* states too (the owner said yes: `CLIMBER "enabled"`, a switch in the Data editor): each monster kind in `CLIMBER "from"` leaves its state on a blow that lands, for `CLIMBER "seconds"`, and a reaction on you costs `CLIMBER "damage"`. See **States on you** under **Items and camps** for what each does and what cures it.

**Synergies.** Marks carried together answer each other, two ways (`scripts/synergies.gd`):

- **Lent traits, for every pair.** Each mark lends one trait to whatever mark it's paired with, about a quarter as strong as the real thing (`TRAITS`, every number in the Data editor): carry any two marks and each one's spells pick up a touch of the other's. That covers all 153 pairs of the 18 marks with 18 entries. A **hit** is a foe the spell strikes as it's cast, or one its shots strike later (`player.gd _release_cast` calls `Synergies.cast`, which listens to `main.on_hit` during the cast and hooks the shots' `on_strike`); a **kill** is such a hit that slays; a **casting** trait comes once a cast. A third mark lends to both others.

| Mark | Lends its partner | What it does (numbers in `TRAITS`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stormcaller | Charge | hits leave foes charged (1 s) |
| Windwalker | Lift | hits shove light foes up and back |
| Hunter | Precision | hits on a weak point (from behind, in its opening, stunned) strike a quarter harder |
| Tidecaller | Soak | hits leave foes soaked (1 s) |
| Warden | Guard | casting raises a thin barrier (8, for 3 s) |
| Deepcaller | Drain | 8% of the damage heals you |
| Blade Forger | Shards | a hit throws a small conjured blade at the nearest other foe (once a cast) |
| Flameblade | Heat | each cast heats your weapon a little (10) |
| Rune Artist | Rune | a cast sometimes (1 in 4) leaves a small rune trap |
| Sandshaper | Rubble | hits slow foes briefly |
| Venomist | Venom | hits add a stack of poison |
| Ashwalker | Smoke | a kill may (1 in 4) hide you: the foes near you lose track of you a moment |
| Flame Contractor | Kindling | kills leave a patch of burning ground |
| Flamewing | Embers | hits set foes burning a little |
| Rekindler | Ember heart | kills heal you 2 |
| Covenant | Kin | your creatures hit a quarter harder for 4 s (with none out, a spirit strikes the foe for 3) |
| Grafter | Graft | a foe slain by a spell is likelier (1 in 4) to leave a graft |
| Surgeon | Bleed | hits open a small bleed |

- **Named synergies, for twelve pairs.** Stronger, hand-made answers, which take the place of their pair's lent traits. One holds while you carry both of its marks, wherever they sit; the first time it forms it is announced (a line in the left feed, "Synergy: Tempest", kept in the Entity's log, and the Entity's tip) and remembered (`profile.gd synergies_formed`; `"lent"` there marks the first time two marks lent each other traits).

Your book's **Marks** section lists the named synergies you hold and every trait your marks lend each other ("Stormcaller's spells take Venom from Venomist: hits add a stack of poison"); the **Library** has a shelf of the named synergies you've formed (the rest as ???). A teammate's marks count while they fight beside you (rivals). The Entity explains it the first time (its **Synergies** tip, and "How do synergies work?" under "How do things work here?").

| Synergy | Marks | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tempest | Stormcaller + Tidecaller | your waves leave foes charged as well as soaked (so they Conduct) |
| Dust Devil | Windwalker + Sandshaper | Quake throws foes up; Gale Lift leaves shards that cut and bleed what walks over them |
| Whirling Steel | Blade Forger + Windwalker | conjured blades lift light foes |
| Forge Runes | Flameblade + Rune Artist | kindled strikes leave small fire runes (they arm after a moment and burn what sets them off) |
| Wildfire | Flame Contractor + Flamewing | burning ground anywhere feeds the aura's heat |
| Undying | Warden + Rekindler | Last Ember also raises a barrier |
| Bloodletter | Deepcaller + Surgeon | draining a bleeding foe heals double |
| Ambusher | Hunter + Ashwalker | unseen shots always strike a weak point |
