Where a form opens is `TREE` in the same file. **New effects** (`unbound_effects.gd`: `static var EFFECTS`, with `"kind"`, `"name"`, `"says"`, a phrase for each class of form, numbers and `"opens"` = essence pooled) run in `static func hit_effect(id, p, e, share, power, run) -> bool` on a struck foe and `static func self_effect(id, p, share, power, run, cls) -> bool` on yourself (`cls` "self" for Within, "shell" for a Shell rising). Remove `"placeholder": true` once a piece is written. **Echoes** go in any file's `static var ECHOES`; what an echo adds in play is `unbound_effects.gd ECHO_ACTS` (by the spell's id).

**Tests** go in their own file under `tests/parts/` (a `run(t)` function; see `tests/parts/tree_foundation.gd`), so they don't collide either. `godot --headless -s tests/smoke_test.gd -- parts` runs only those.

## What the game tells you: the lane, the feed and the Entity

The owner's rule: **nothing stays in the middle of the screen.** The centre belongs to the climber and the fight. Everything the game says during play goes to one of three places (`scripts/notices.gd` keeps them, `scripts/hud.gd` and `scripts/guide.gd` draw them):

- **Top middle: the announcement lane.** One compact banner at a time: a title and one short line (a floor and its task, a room's name, "THE DOORS GRIND SHUT", "THE WARD LIFTS", a scenario, a spell's first cast). Banners **take turns**: a new one waits for the one showing, which gives way after a moment; a question that can't wait ("RESTART?", "LOCKED", the recall scroll's "read it again?") takes its place at once; a room's name never pushes anything aside. Each fades in, holds about three seconds (a floor's task a little longer, words to read by their length) and fades out. Under the banner sit the **status chips**, one line each, stacked: a beast's (or champion's) health, a sealed wing's rule, "The doors are shut · 3 left", a scenario's clock, a station drill's line, the floor's task ("Braziers lit 2 of 4"), "The stair is open". They slide down while a banner shows and back up after, and never overlap it. On a phone the lane centres itself between the health bars and the Bag / Climber / Menu buttons.
- **Left: the feed**, under the health and stamina bars, the slots and the unbanked-essence counter. Short lines, four at most, each gone in a few seconds: what you picked up (items, gear, keys, crystals, a cache's essence), what the stair banked and rested, a mark's step, renown, a synergy formed, a camp's choice. A banner's extra lines land here instead of making the banner taller.
- **Right: the Entity**, under the key hints (on a phone, under the Bag, Climber and Menu buttons, slid clear of every touch button). See **The Entity** above: its sphere, its one-line tidbits and tips, and its panel with the log of the climb.

**Floating words in the world** (damage numbers, "Parried", a reaction's name) stay over the fight, but they're short-lived (under a second) and at most eight show at once: in a big fight the oldest give way (`fx.gd` `TEXT_MAX`).

**Full-screen moments** are as they were: the Regard, the victory or recall summary, the fall screen, camp and vault cards.

**For code.** Callers still write `main.banner = {"title": ..., "lines": [...], "t": seconds}`; main.gd hands it to the lane. Extra fields: `pri` (0 waits its turn, 2 can't wait), `line` (the one line to show; the rest go to the feed), `long` (a task), `note` (words to read: a plate, every line, held by length), `hold` (exactly `t`), `show`, `kinds`, `logged` (see the top of `notices.gd`). `main.banner` reads as the last banner set until the lane is empty. For anything worth a line, call **`main.note_event(kind, text, colour)`**: one event source for the left feed and the Entity's log, so nothing is reported in two wordings. The kinds, their colours and signs, and which show in the feed are `KINDS` in `notices.gd` (the words in `TEXT`); essence is batched (`notices.note_essence`: one log line per burst and kind in a room, "+14 Air"); **damage is never an event**. `main.remark("key")` asks the Entity for a tidbit (below). The lane, the status lines and the feed are layout elements (`hud.lane`, `hud.status`, `hud.feed`), so Arrange mode can move them; they follow the HUD size setting as the corner's bars do. Tests: `tests/parts/hud_lanes.gd`.

## The climb

**How tall a tower is.** Every tower is 12 floors to the summit, where its beast, the tower's **guardian**, waits (`SUMMIT` in `towers.gd`). Floor 6 is **the Proving**, where the tower takes your measure (see **The Proving** below). Your first climb is a climb like any other, but floor 6 opens with **the Regard** before the Proving (see **Marks, essence and the Regard**). Camps after floors 3, 6 and 9 aren't floors. When **all six guardians** have been beaten, an **ending** plays after the last summit's victory screen (`ENDING` in `scripts/climb_shape.gd`: placeholder words for the owner to write), then the same climber plays on.

**The guardians learn from each other's deaths** (`scripts/guardian_lessons.gd`, every rule, number and word; marked and Unbound alike). A guardian doesn't adapt within a fight: it only **watches**. Every blow that lands on it is noted by its damage (an overkill counts only what it took): the **essences** in it, its **form group** (a weapon's swing, a close blow of essence such as the hand's Strike, a **shot**: bolts, volleys, arrows and a mark's flying spells, a **rush**: Dash, Leap, Blink, or a **burst**: the rest), the **Node or mark** it came from, and whether it came from **up close** (within 110 of its edge) or at range; and every roll through its attacks and every parry. **When it dies** it leaves a **lesson** in the profile (`profile.guardians`: tower → lesson; the latest kill of each guardian replaces its own, so six at most, saved with the climber; old saves have none): the shares of its damage by essence, form group and Node or mark (under 15% teaches nothing), the share up close, and the rolls and parries. **Every other guardian applies every lesson so far**, adding up, each capped (`LEARN`): per lesson it **resists** the killer's essences (15% times that essence's share) and blows from the Node or mark they leaned on (8% times its share), **braces** against a favoured weapon's swing or close blow (6%) or rush (8%), **sidesteps shots** now and then (20% times shots' share, at most 40%; never while staggered or recovering from an attack, so an opening always lands), **keeps its distance** between attacks from a killer who fought up close or **presses in** on one who hung back (it still comes within reach to strike), and against a heavy **parrier** (and, less, a roller) its **wind-ups run a little longer** at random (at most 40%; the glint still comes just before the blow). **Never immune:** everything together takes at most **50%** off any blow, and DoTs aren't touched. A guardian never applies its own lesson, the Proving's shadow, a station's arena and a mirror image neither learn nor teach, and the first guardian a climber beats has learned nothing, so a focused build wins it with what it has and finds the next ones harder unless it changes its style: **other essences, forms, Nodes or marks, or range beat the lessons**. A short flat word over it ("Resisted", "Braced", "Sidestepped") shows when a lesson works. **Nobody tells the climber outright:** **rival climbers** met in a tower (trading, teaming up, thanking you: half the time, `rivals.gd RULES "rumour_chance"`), the **Dining car**'s climbers (a rumour of its own, `dining.gd RUMORS "guardians"`) and **the Noble** (in the greeting, `noble.gd RUMOUR_CHANCE`) pass on rumours read from the lessons (`RUMOURS`: "They say the Kraken shrugs off Fire now", "I hear the Wyvern sidesteps bolts and arrows now"). The Entity only says, the first time one falls, that the guardians talk when one of them falls ("guardians_talk"), and has a vague "Do the guardians know each other?" topic: no numbers. The numbers are in the Data editor (Numbers: The guardians), the words too. Tests: `tests/parts/guardian_lessons.gd`.

**Summit Blades can't be fully learned.** A Summit Blade's power (the blades are slivers of Lann, the fifth climber) marks its blows (`blow_ctx` "blade", set in `summit_blades.gd`): they count for only **17%** of their damage in a guardian's lesson (`LEARN "blade"`, about a sixth), and the lessons take only **half** their usual toll off a blade's blow and never sidestep it (`LEARN "blade_keep"`), so the blades keep their edge. A lesson notes the share a blade did, and a rumour hints at it ("Some say Lann's slivers never teach", `RUMOURS "blade"`). Tests: `tests/parts/sworn.gd`.

**Wild towers** (`scripts/wild_towers.gd`, every rule, number and word). When a tower's guardian is beaten at its summit, its **well runs wild**: the tower stays open, but from the next climb on it's a **wild tower**, for good. A tower is wild when the climber's record says its beast has fallen (`profile.record "beasts_by"`), so an **old save's beaten towers are simply wild**. What changes:
- **Storms** (`STORM`): on its floors of rooms (from floor 2 to floor 11; never the Proving, a camp, the summit, the arena or the bondless first climb) a storm gathers 12 seconds after you arrive and then every 30 to 42 seconds (2 seconds sooner for each other wild well, never under 24). **Three rings** light up on the floor, one under you, with a flat warning that fills as it counts down for **2 seconds** and "Wild storm! Leave the rings" over you; then they strike (a lit column of wild essence, a flash, thunder) and leave a scar on the floor. A roll's iframes or stepping out of the ring dodges it, and a hit's own moment of grace means overlapping rings land once. A strike hurts you **6 + 0.75 a floor** (about 14 on floor 11) and every monster in a ring **12 + 1 a floor**: lure them in.
- **Wild essence** (`FEED`, and the marks' own burn ladder in `mark_use.gd` and `bond.gd`): a strike that catches a **marked** climber burns one of their lit marks a rung, **burned, then paralyzed** (the stair heals a rung, as ever); it passes over a paralyzed or dark mark and never burns a mark out by itself (only forcing a paralyzed mark does). An **Unbound** is fed instead: the strike hurts **half as much** and leaves **3** of the tower's essence in what they carry, **6 at most a floor** (60 at most a climb); banked at the stair, it flows into their Nodes like any essence. A climber with neither (the first climb) is only hurt.
- **Empowered monsters** (`POWER`): every monster there, the guardian too, has **20% more health and 10% more damage**, never past 25% (`floors.gd toughness` times `Floors.wild_tough`, set for each floor; plain again on the train, so the Bestiary's numbers stay plain). The guardian's lessons are capped on their own, as ever.
- **Richer finds, a little** (`REWARD`, never past its cap; slow growth): **10%** more essence from a kill, **20%** more in a good room's cache, gear drops 1.5 times as likely and item drops 1.25 times.
- **Stranger floors**: the stone takes a violet tint (`palette.gd WILD_TINT`), motes of wild essence drift in the air, and strikes scar the floor.
- **The world**: each well that runs wild pushes the world nearer its unmaking. The summit's lines say a well has run wild, and when the **third** does, that far outside the towers **the Makers stir** (there's no meeting of the Makers yet to hang it on). **The cities talk** (`RUMOURS`): the Noble of a city by a wild tower always speaks of it in the greeting, other Nobles now and then (`noble.gd WILD_CHANCE`); the Dining car's climbers have a rumour of it (`dining.gd RUMORS "wild"`); climbers met in a wild tower mention it (`rivals.gd RULES "wild_chance"`); and the station's crest adds "its well runs wild". After the ending, every tower stays open, wild ones too.
- **Telling**: on the Engine's tower map a wild tower's plaque has a small **storm sign** (a lit violet orb with a strike across it), the Entity's hover help on it says its well runs wild, and the placard under the map says what that means (for you: it burns marks, or feeds the Unbound). The first time you enter one the Entity explains it ("wild_tower" for a marked climber: careful, it burns marks; "wild_tower_unbound": it feeds you), and asking it **Wild towers?** under "How do things work here?" gives the numbers. The numbers and words are in the Data editor (Numbers and Words: Wild towers; its colours under Colours: The world). Tests: `tests/parts/wild_towers.gd`.

**The stair's barrier** (`scripts/climb_shape.gd`: `BARRIER`, `STRIKE`, `SLIPS`, `CONFRONT`, `TEXT`). On every floor (not a camp, the Proving or the summit) a barrier starts forming across the stair as you arrive, with a ring over it counting down, gold turning red, and its seconds; the floor's banner says it too. **Floors 1 to 6: an iron gate**, its bars coming down for **30 seconds**: until it shuts you can **roll under it** and slip past; walking into it, it holds you back. Shut, it can still be **broken** with weapons, bare fists or spells (12 points: a blow 1, a finisher 2, a spell 2; a swing counts only when it strikes no foe, so fighting near it never breaks it by accident). **Floors 7 to 12: a ward** of the tower's essence, a lattice of light rising for **15 seconds** (you can roll through while it rises); up, only **the essence of the tower's own marks** hurts it (a spell of that essence 3, a weapon carrying it 1 or 2, an item of it 4; anything else it drinks, and says so), 12 points in all. The very first time ever you roll into a closing barrier it throws you back as a warning (the Entity says what slipping past means). **Slipping past or breaking a barrier** skips the floor's task and its reward, and the tower sends a **horde**: **6** of its creatures for a gate, **12** for a ward, awake around the stair; the stair waits until the last falls (a count over it shows how many are left). A horde's monsters pay a quarter of a kill's essence (`ADDS` in `essence.gd`): the tower's answer, not its spoils, then opens. A climber with **no mark** who does it is refused by the guardian on the spot: a "Refused" screen, and they are **Unbound** (the horde comes when it's dismissed). A **marked** climber's slips are counted across every climb: the **third** (and every third after) brings **a Maker** once that horde has fallen, the meeting's presentation used as a confrontation (the Maker of Towers; placeholder words; *keep the rules* and it lets you go, *defy it* and it takes half the essence you carry). The floor bot and the tests never roll into a barrier; tests turn barriers on themselves (`barriers_on`).

**Falling below floor 6.** A way down can drop you back below floor 6 after you've won the Proving. Climbing back, floor 6 is an ordinary floor: you don't face the Proving again on that climb, unless you choose to. Arriving there, two cards: **Pass by**, or **The Lethal Proving** (the Proving's floor again with only its lethal door open: harder, for a greater prize, and dying beyond it ends the climb).

**Floors are planned.** Floor 1 and the beast's floor are single open rooms. Every floor in between is planned on a grid of cells, one room each at most, and the grid grows with height: 4 by 3 cells on floors 2 to 5, 5 by 3 on floors 6 to 9, 5 by 4 on floors 10 and 11 (`PLAN` in `scripts/floors.gd`). Each floor has 6 to 11 rooms:
- **The spine** is the main route, a walk from cell to neighbouring cell. Each of its rooms has a job: the **entrance** (you start here), **guard rooms**, the **gallery** in the middle (it holds the floor's puzzle) and the **stair hall** (the stair stands at the top of it).
- **Branch rooms** (2 or 3) hang off the spine in the cells beside it: side rooms worth a look.
- A cell or two beside the spine is kept for a **sealed wing**, and sometimes one for a **secret room** (see **Sealed wings, vaults and secrets** below).

**Rooms.** A room is either **hand-made**, drawn as a small text grid in `scripts/rooms.gd` (one character per tile: walls, pillars, chasms, pools, monster spots and random tiles), or a shape **carved** in its tower's style: plain, open, a long hall, an L, a cross (corners notched), round (corners cut), pillared, a **ring** around one great block, a true **circle**, a long **gallery** with a row of pillars down each side, **split** on two levels (a ledge of low wall across it with steps through, the upper level lit a little) or strewn with **cover** to hide behind (`SHAPES` in `rooms.gd`; each tower's favourites are its `shapes` in `towers.gd`, and `scripts/floor_styles.gd` adds the newer shapes per tower: the hook for giving each tower its own layouts, corridor style and rules later). Hand-made rooms suit their job (calm start rooms, combat rooms, stair rooms, puzzle rooms), flip at random so they don't repeat exactly, and never appear twice on a floor. A wall that touches a room's edge becomes part of its outline; one standing free inside is a low wall you can hide behind (it blocks movement, sight and shots). The game checks that the stair, every monster, every piece of the floor's task, the chests and the cache can be reached (any low wall that would cut one off is left out). To add a room, paint it in **Dev tools → Paint rooms** (see The room painter), or copy one in `rooms.gd` and draw your own.

**Corridors and doors.** Corridors are two tiles wide and run straight from one room's wall to the facing wall of the next, as near the middle of both walls as the rooms' shapes allow, so they never cut through a room and what's drawn in it stays as drawn. Stone posts frame each doorway. A floor isn't a tree: one to three **loops** join rooms in neighbouring cells that weren't joined (never the start room or the stair room, which each keep their one way in) (`loops` in `floor_styles.gd`), and about half of them are doors **barred from one side**: a heavy wooden bar across them. From the barred side it stays shut ("Barred from the other side"); come round to its far side and the bar lifts: a shortcut back. Many doorways have a **door** (`scripts/doors.gd`): likelier on rooms where danger waits (three in four on dens, crypts, the stair landing, guard posts, strongrooms, scenario rooms and the puzzle room; one in three elsewhere; never on the start room). A closed door is solid: it blocks the way, shots and sight, so a room behind one stays dark until you open it. Walk up to a door and it swings open (a thud and a puff of dust); awake monsters open doors too. A door's look hints at the challenge inside, never the reward: plain planks for an ordinary room, iron bands, claw marks and bones at the threshold for a den, an engraved sigil of the hazard (wind, a wave, a flame, spikes, an ember, thorns or a chasm) for a trap room, a stone door with the puzzle's emblem (an eye or a plate) for the puzzle room, warm light under the door for a good room, and chains and a red glow for danger (a crypt, the stair landing), and a dark stone slab carved with a watching eye for a sealed wing.

**Room themes.** Every room has a **theme**: what it is (`scripts/themes.gd`, where the names, rules, tints and door looks live, all easy to rename). The first time you walk into a themed room its name and one-line rule show across the screen, for example "MONSTER DEN: Bones and claw marks. Something lives here." Each theme also washes the room's floor in its colour (with bones in a den, scorch marks in a furnace). Themes are given by rules, not pure chance: the start room is the **Arrival Hall**, the stair room the **Stair Landing** (a destination, lightly guarded: one guard, two from floor 6, and it never shuts you in) and the gallery takes its puzzle's theme (**Hall of Statues**, **Hall of Mirrors**, **Hall of Stones**, **Ice Hall**, **Essence Lock**, **Hall of Pedestals**, **Hall of Eyes** or **Weighing Room**), or the floor's task's hall (**Hall of Runes**, **Wardstone Hall**), or else a theme like any other room. Some rooms are fixed first: **scenario rooms** (see below), now and then a calm **Still Chamber** and, from floor 3, a **Wandering Trader**. Of the other rooms, the first one after the start is always a kind one (a good room) and the next a **Monster Den**; after that comes a mix, with no bad theme twice a floor, a cap on bad rooms (2 on floor 2, 3 up to floor 4, then 4), each tower's own themes twice as likely, and a theme that doesn't fit its room swapped for one that does.

**Every room pays off.** No room is empty and no dead end is pointless: each has a name and something for you. A good room has its gift; a danger room (a den, a crypt, a pillared hall, a trap room, the Chasm, a **Guard Post**) has a **chest** of spoils, shut fast while the monsters that wait in the room still stand and open once they're down (essence, sometimes an item or gear; richer in a dead end: `scripts/room_rewards.gd`, `RULES`); a room holding a piece of the floor's task has that; a **Still Chamber** (no monster comes there; stand in it and your wounds slowly close), a **Wandering Trader** (three items for unbanked essence; walk on if you'd rather not), a **Strongroom** behind a lock (a vault's cards on a pedestal), a scenario room, the puzzle's vault. The only empty hall left is the deliberate Still Chamber. The floor bot's sweep counts rooms with no payoff and dead ends with nothing, and both stay at zero.
- **Good rooms:** a **Healing Spring** (stand in the pool to heal until it runs dry), a **Forgotten Cache** (an urn of the tower's essence: walk onto it for a burst, more on higher floors; sometimes it holds gear), a **Fallen Climber's Camp** (a cold fire, a bedroll and an item left behind), and each tower's own (the Gryphon's Windswept Aerie, the Kraken's Tidepool, the Wyvern's Cold Forge, the Manticore's Hidden Oasis, the Firebird's Ember Pyre, the Chimera's Thornwild Nest). Two at most a floor, and no traps in them.
- **Bad rooms:** the **Monster Den** (more of the floor's monsters wait there), the **Restless Crypt** (from floor 3: its dead rise when you walk in), the **Pillared Hall** (archers like it), the **trap rooms**, each laying its own set piece of traps (see Traps: the Spike Gallery, Dart Hall, the Furnace and Miasma Hall in any tower, plus each tower's own: the Gryphon's Windhall, the Kraken's Sluice Hall, the Firebird's Emberfall and the Chimera's Bramble Hall; every floor from 2 has one), five towers' own **lairs** (the Gryphon's Harrier Roost and the Chimera's Thornhound Kennel are dens; the Kraken's Wrecked Hold, the Manticore's Sand-Buried Tomb and the Firebird's Ash Crypt are ambushes from floor 3; each usually shuts behind you, `LOCK_IN` in `doors.gd`), and in the Firebird's tower **the Chasm**, a band of chasm right across a room (half the time a narrow plank **bridge** crosses it: walk it, or jump anywhere else). Each keeps a chest of spoils. Where the dice would have left a room quiet, it's a **Guard Post** instead: guards and a strongbox.

Monsters wait in the rooms: most in the den, one or two guarding the stair, the rest along the route and in the branches (each dead end gets one before the rooms on the way through), never in the start room, a good room, a rest room, a trader's room or a scenario room (hand-made rooms' marked spots first). New kinds come in floor by floor: floor 2 has only the simplest (slimes and each tower's small ones), archers and each tower's middle monsters join on floor 3, knights and the heaviest (smiths, rams) on floor 4 (`from` in `MONSTERS`, `floors.gd`). A monster stays put until it sees you (or you come close, or it's hit), then rouses its neighbours and finds its way to you around the walls (a path grid, `AStarGrid2D`). Monsters can't attack what they can't see, and walls stop every shot. Each room has one torch on its north wall.

**Fog.** On floors of rooms you see only what your climber can see: walls block sight (chasms, water and pillars don't), places you've seen stay dimly mapped, and places you haven't are dark. Monsters out of sight are hidden until you see them (`scripts/fog.gd`), and closed doors block sight like walls. The cache's and spring's numbers live in `CACHE` and `SPRING` in `scripts/floors.gd`. In the Firebird's tower the rooms' chasm tiles are real chasms, and every floor has a chasm to jump (a Chasm room, or failing that a long corridor cut by one); elsewhere chasm tiles are walls. In the Kraken's tower the rooms' pools fill with water. An arrow at the edge of the screen points at the floor's task's next piece, then at the open stair.

**The floor's task.** Every floor of rooms (not floor 1, the camps, the Proving or the summit) has **one main task**, and its stair is **warded** (a chain across the steps) until it's done: it won't open, however many monsters fall. On arrival the top-middle lane's banner names it and says how (held a little longer than other banners); a status chip under the lane keeps a short line ("Braziers lit 2 of 4"); an arrow at the screen's edge points at the nearest piece still to do (and a gold chevron bobs over it once it's on screen), then at the stair. Its pieces are spread over several rooms, on the main route and the branches, never in the start room, so the task takes you round the floor. A climb never gets the same kind twice if it can be helped (`scripts/objectives.gd`, where every name, line, count and timing lives; `scripts/objective.gd` runs it):

| Task | What you do |
| --- | --- |
| **Light the Braziers** | Stand by each brazier (2, then 3, then 4 higher up) until it catches; a blow while you light one puts it out. |
| **Walk the Rune Path** | Read the tablet (in another room) to learn the order, then step on the runes in the Hall of Runes in that order (3, then 4). A wrong step wakes two guards and the path starts again. |
| **Raise the Levers** | Strike three levers up, in three rooms. Each falls back after a while (shorter higher up, but always long enough to walk the route), so all must be up at once. |
| **Carry the Sun Orb** | Carry the orb from the room farthest from the stair to the pedestal by the stair. A blow knocks it from your hands. |
| **Find the Keeper** | A Keeper (a crowned champion) and one or two guards wait in a far room. It carries the stair's key: picking the key up is the task. |
| **Wake the Wardstone** | Walk up to the stone in the Wardstone Hall to wake it, then keep the beast's creatures off it while it charges (they head for it); it only charges while none is near. |
| **Gather the Key Shards** | Three shards in three rooms, each with a guard. |
| **Catch the Motes** | Motes (4 to 6, named for the tower: wind, tide, ember, sand, spark, seed) drift away from you inside their rooms: corner them. |
| **Slay the Marked** | Two, then three, of the floor's creatures carry the beast's red sign (a ring under them, the sign over them), each in a different room, a little tougher than their kind. Slay them all. |
| **Close the Rifts** | Two, then three, rifts in the floor, in different rooms. While you're near one, it lets a creature through every few seconds (two alive at most, five in all). Three blows shut it. |
| **Search the Urns** | Four to six urns in different rooms; one holds the stair's key, and it hums (rings ripple from it) as you come near. Break them to search: the key finishes the task at once; an empty urn now and then holds a creature. |
| **Lead the Lost Wisp** | A wisp waits in the room farthest from the stair. Come close and it follows you, slower than you walk; it hides while a creature is near it and waits if you leave it far behind. Bring it near the stair's spot and it flies the rest of the way. |
| **Rekindle the Beacons** (the Firebird's only) | Take a flame from the hearth and carry it to each beacon before it gutters; braziers and lit beacons renew it. |
| **The Hungry Grove** (the Chimera's only) | Cut the heart-roots in the order they wake; the thorns recede from the stair. |
| **Drain the Stairwell** (the Kraken's only) | The way to the stair is flooded: turn every sluice wheel (2, then 3) by standing at it; the last one drains the stairwell. |
| **Turn the Wind Vanes** (the Gryphon's only) | No bridge reaches the stair: strike every wind vane round (2, then 3; two blows each) until the wind blows across the gap. |
| **Stoke the Furnaces** (the Wyvern's only) | Strike each cold furnace's bellows (2, then 3) until it roars; don't linger in its heat. |
| **Wake the Sun Seals** (the Manticore's only) | Strike each polished stone (2, then 3) to turn it so the passing sunlight throws onto its seal. |
| **A puzzle** | Any of the twelve puzzles below can be the task: then the gallery holds it, and solving it lifts the ward. |

When it's done: **THE STAIR IS UNSEALED**, a column of gold light on the stair, a burst of essence, and a **reliquary** beside the stair: walk up to it and take one of three (a relic: a ring or an amulet; a piece of gear; an item). When the floor's puzzle isn't the task, the gallery may still hold one, for its own **vault** (a vault's cards for solving it); it never seals you in. The dev tools' **Puzzles** buttons make that puzzle every floor's task.

**Puzzles.** When the floor's task is a puzzle, it's in the **gallery**. It's all part of the Regard: the beast watches, and tests. A main puzzle never needs a bond, an item or a piece of gear, only what every climber has (strikes, a bow, rolls, jumps, parries, walking), and it can never lock you out. Each kind is **generated** at random and **checked by a solver** before it's used, so it's always solvable, and it gets harder up the climb: **tier 1** on floors 2-3, **tier 2** on 4-8, **tier 3** on 9-11. The rule text never covers the puzzle: it's said in the **top-middle lane** as you first step into the room (on a plate, held long enough to read), and it leaves a small **"?" plaque** in the room. Stand by the plaque (it lights up within a couple of steps) and the rule is said again for as long as you stand there, so the Essence Lock's riddle can always be read again without hiding the lock or a spark you need to pick up. A statue's own line shows the same way while you stand by the statue. Eight kinds so far:

| Puzzle | How it's made and checked | How to solve it | Harder higher up |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Three Statues** ("The Riddling Statues" in the Manticore's tower) | Three statues before three plates, each saying something about where the way lies or about each other; a plaque gives the rules (one always tells the truth, the other two always lie). Statements are drawn at random until **exactly one** plate fits every way the roles could be shared out. | Walk up to a statue to hear it. Stand on the plate behind the true way for a second. | Tier 2: at least one statement about another statue's honesty. Tier 3: two statements each, and one statue **alternates** (one true, one false). |
| **Beam and Mirrors** ("Wind Mirrors" in the Gryphon's tower) | A lamp throws a beam across the room's tiles. The generator walks a path with turns (never crossing itself, only over open floor), puts a mirror at each turn, then scrambles them so the beam misses; a solver tries every way the mirrors can face. Walls stop the beam. | **Strike or shoot** a mirror to turn it. Hold the beam on the sigil. Auto-aim turns to the nearest mirror. | 3, 4, then 5 mirrors; decoy mirrors from tier 2; one **fixed** (iron-bound) mirror at tier 3. |
| **The Crumbling Path** ("Ember Path" in the Wyvern's tower) | A grid of stones in the middle of the room, maybe with holes (pits). A search confirms a route exists that steps on every stone exactly once, starting from the edge. | Step on every stone once. Each lights as you step on it; going back onto a lit stone **hurts** and puts the path out, and so does breaking the path (stepping to a stone not beside the last) or wandering off it. Even a jump's shadow wakes the stones. | 5x3 stones; 5x4 with a hole; 6x5 with two holes. |
| **The Ice Hall** ("Tide Slick" in the Kraken's tower) | The hall's floor is ice with rocks frozen in it, inside a ring of plain floor. Rocks are scattered at random; a search over every slide from every way onto the ice finds how many slides each resting place needs, and the **dais** goes on one needing at least 2 (the most it can, up to 3 more). Layouts where you could get stuck are thrown away: from **every** tile of ice that isn't rock (not only where slides stop, since a monster in your lane, a blink or the end of a flight can leave you anywhere) some line of slides leads off, so no tile is ever boxed in by rocks. | Once on the ice you **slide** along the tile grid until a rock stops you (or off the far side back onto the floor); any push of the stick, keys or touch stick picks the nearest of the four directions. No rolling or jumping there. Come to **rest** on the dais. A blow sends you sliding; a monster in your lane stops you short; a blink in the middle of a slide ends it where you land. Getting it wrong costs nothing: slide off and walk round. | The dais needs at least 2, 3, then 4 slides. |
| **The Essence Lock** ("The Hungry Lock" in the Chimera's tower) | A lock whose riddle names one of the six essences (riddles are data in `lock.gd`). Once you're well inside, foes appear glowing in the answer and in **decoy** essences, each with its essence's sign over it. | Slay a glowing foe and it leaves a **spark**: walk over it to carry it (one at a time) to the lock. The wrong spark brings an ambush, then a new riddle. A **spell from a mark** of the right essence cast beside the lock, an **item** of it used there, or a strike from a **weapon** of it (a named piece or a trait of that essence) answers at once, and never fails. | Tier 1: two decoys; tier 2: three; tier 3: two or three essences **in order**. |
| **The Hall of Pedestals** ("The Remembered Fires" in the Firebird's tower) | A row of pedestals, each carved with a sign. The order is kept in numbered **murals** set in the north walls of **other rooms** of the floor (never behind a locked door). The hall's panel counts the murals you've found. | Strike or shoot the pedestals in the murals' order. The first wrong one brings an ambush; then try again. | 3, then 4 signs; tier 3 adds a pedestal no mural shows. |
| **Hall of Eyes** (a hand-made room) | Stone eyes behind low **rails** (rails stop walking but not sight or arrows). | Hit every eye, with a strike or an arrow over the rail. Auto-aim turns to the nearest unlit eye. | Tier 2: an eye stays lit only a couple of seconds, so light them all together; tier 3: shorter still. |
| **Weighing Room** (a hand-made room) | Pressure plates that need a **weight**: the climber is too light. | Push a **stone block** onto each plate (walk into it for a moment and it slides a tile), or lure a monster onto it. Strike the **lever** to put the blocks back. With more plates than blocks, if no monster that could stand on one is near for 8 seconds while you're in the room, the beast sends one ("Something heavy comes"), so the room always has an answer. | More plates. |
| **The Hall of Lanterns** ("The Ember Lanterns" in the Firebird's tower; `lanterns.gd`) | Iron lanterns in a grid, joined by grooves. The generator starts from every lantern lit and makes some blows a climber would, so an answer always exists; a solver (elimination over the blows) finds it from any state. | **Strike or shoot** a lantern: it and the lanterns joined to it change. Light them all at once. No failure: the same blow again undoes it. | 3x3, then 4x3, then 4x4 (and the room closes behind you). |
| **The Hall of Bells** ("The Drowned Bells" in the Kraken's tower; `bells.gd`) | Bells in a ring round a shell, each with its own colour, size and note, and a song drawn from them. | Listen: the bells ring the song by themselves as you come in, each lighting as it sounds. Then **strike or shoot** them in the same order (lights by the shell count your notes). A wrong bell brings an ambush, then the song again; strike the shell to hear it whenever you like. | 4 bells and 3 notes, 5 and 4, then 6 and 5 with a bell that may come twice (and the room closes). |
| **The Crucible** ("The Wyvern's Crucible"; `crucible.gd`) | A smooth iron floor with lumps of slag, a crucible and a mould. A search over every push (with room to stand behind it) keeps only a mould the crucible can reach, at least 2, 3 or 4 pushes away; the floor bot uses the same search. | **Strike** the crucible and it slides away from you until slag or the floor's rim stops it (over the mould without stopping). Bring it to rest on the mould. Push it where it can never reach the mould and it jams: an ambush, then it's set back. Arrows ring off it; a creature in its way stops it short. | 6x5 with 3 lumps, 7x5 with 4, 7x6 with 6 (and the room closes). |
| **The Hall of Pods** ("The Three Heads" in the Chimera's tower; `pods.gd`) | A three-headed idol (a lion, a goat, a serpent) among six seed pods; one head glows. Pods open a few at a time showing a head's sign, always one with the glowing head's among them. | **Strike or shoot** open pods that show the glowing head's sign (lights at the idol's foot count them). Another sign brings an ambush and the count starts again; a pod that closes first costs nothing. | Burst 4 (two open at once), 5 (three open, and the idol turns another head after each), then 6 with shorter openings (and the room closes). |

Each tower favours two of the twelve (`SIGNATURE` in `puzzles/catalog.gd`): the Gryphon mirrors and eyes, the Kraken the ice and the bells, the Wyvern the crumbling path and the crucible, the Manticore the statues and the plates, the Firebird the pedestals and the lanterns, the Chimera the lock and the pods.

Each tower favours its own (the Gryphon mirrors, the Kraken the ice, the Wyvern the crumbling path, the Manticore the statues, the Firebird the pedestals, the Chimera the lock: half its floors).

**How puzzles meet combat.** Low down a puzzle is **quiet**: it sleeps ("The statues will not speak while blood is spilled") until the monsters in its room are dead. From floor 7 it's **pressure**: it wakes at once, and while you're in its room the beast sends a monster every few seconds (two at a time at most), so you think while you fight. Some puzzle rooms **seal** when you step in (the statues, the crumbling path and the lock from tier 2, the mirrors at tier 3; never the ice or the pedestals, whose way back is through the door): the doorways grind shut until the question is answered. A **wrong answer** (a wrong statue plate, a wrong spark, a pedestal out of order) brings an **ambush** and opens the seal so you can fight or run; once the ambush is dead (or after 40 seconds at most) the puzzle **resets**, the statues with a new riddle. It never stays shut.

**Bonds that break puzzles.** Some bonds give a **shortcut** (never needed; the rule says so when you have one): the Windwalker's **Crosswind** turns every mirror its wall of wind passes; the Hunter's **Keen Eyes** shows which statues lie after a few moments near them; the Flamewing's **Phoenix Glide** passes over the stones unfelt, so you can cross a lit one; the Stormcaller's **Static** makes a woken eye spark to the nearest sleeping one; the Sandshaper's **Rooted Stance** is heavy enough to hold a plate down.

Solving the floor's puzzle lifts the ward ("THE STAIR IS UNSEALED"), opens the stair (and a seal), and gives a burst of the tower's essence (behind a **gate** in the hand-made rooms). Floor 1 and the beast's floor have no ward: there the stair still opens once three quarters of the monsters are down. Each kind lives in its own script in `scripts/puzzles/` (`statues.gd`, `mirrors.gd`, `crumble.gd`, `ice.gd`, `lock.gd`, `memory.gd`, `eyes.gd`, `plates.gd`, each with its names, rule text and shortcuts as data at the top); `catalog.gd` picks how hard and which towers favour which; `scripts/puzzle.gd` is the host that runs one (the reward, the seal, quiet and pressure modes, failure and the ambush, auto-aim and the rule panels).

**Traps.** Traps are the beast's tests, laid **on purpose** and meant to be hard (`scripts/traps.gd`, where the names, timings, damage and placement rules live):
- **Trap rooms.** Every floor from floor 2 has at least one trap room (a room theme, see Room themes), and its traps are a **set piece built out of the room's own shape**: rows across its short side, slots in its two long walls, vents at its two ends. All the traps of a set piece keep **one shared beat**, so the pattern can be learned; higher up the beat quickens, the rows close up, and a second wave runs between the first (from floor 7). From floor 4 **monsters wait among the traps** (one, then two from floor 8), so you fight inside the pattern. The room's one-line rule only hints at how it works.
- **Traps may block the way.** A set piece can run right up to a doorway, and a spike patch spans a corridor on the way to the stair, so you must cross it.
- **Guards.** From floor 3, spikes ring the cache's urn two tiles out, rising one after another around the ring.
- **Corridors** hold one spike patch a floor (two from floor 7), all on one beat, some of its spikes hidden.
- **Hidden plates** (one to three a floor) can be anywhere but on a door itself.

| Room (tower) | The set piece | How to get through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Spike Gallery** (any; the Manticore's favourite) | rows of spikes across the room's short side, each row a beat behind the last: a wave rolling down the room (two interleaved waves from floor 7); some spikes are hidden | follow the wave across, stepping into each row just after it drops; roll or jump |
| **Dart Hall** (any) | slits in both long walls fire darts across the room, one wall then the other, half a beat apart; a dart only hurts where it is in its flight | listen for the click and watch the slit's eye redden; cross a line just after its dart passes; roll (jumping doesn't help) |
| **The Furnace** (any from floor 3; the Wyvern's favourite) | grates at both ends breathe fire toward the middle in turns, one half of the room, then the other; a line across the middle never burns, and **one side is always safe** | cross the half that has just burned, wait on the middle line, then cross the other; roll |
| **Miasma Hall** (any from floor 4; the Manticore's and Chimera's favourite) | cracked vents breathe choking gas one after another down the room; a path of pale **clean stones** winds through where the gas never reaches | keep to the clean stones: **linger in a cloud and it will kill you** |
| **Windhall** (Gryphon) | gust lanes blow across the room toward a long wall lined with spikes, lane after lane; the spikes rise just as a gust lands you on them | cross between the gusts, or roll through one |
| **Sluice Hall** (Kraken) | water sweeps down the hall from one end, the rows taking turns, knocking you along and slowing you; a pillar or two stand in the flood | step into the dry row, or shelter behind a pillar; roll |
| **Emberfall** (Firebird) | embers fall on scorch rings a column at a time, a wave sweeping down the room, and the floor burns after; higher up the rings close up into walls of fire, and from floor 9 more fall between them, half a beat apart | watch the rings warm and keep moving; roll or jump |
| **Bramble Hall** (Chimera) | brambles fill the room, with snares among them (more of them hidden higher up); a snare's snap **wakes the pack**; a trodden path winds clear through | keep to the path; once held, a roll breaks free |
| **The Chasm** (Firebird) | a band of chasm right across the room, its ends left open where a doorway meets it | jump it; fall in and you drop to the floor below (no harm done) |

**Hard to spot.** A trap at rest shows only a faint tell on its tile (a few small holes, a grate, cracks, a scorch ring, a slit in the wall), never its reach, and it's drawn under the darkness, so you only see it where your light or a torch falls. **Hidden** traps show nothing at all until you're right on top of them. The Hunter's **Keen Eyes** shows every trap, outlining the hidden ones in gold; the dev tools' **Show traps** outlines every trap and its reach.

**Short warnings.** Just before it fires a trap gives a short tell, never a flash: a click or a hiss, spikes peeking out of their holes, a grate shimmering, a slit's eye reddening, a wisp of gas, a ring warming. Grates, slits and rings also give a small glint in the last moment (0.25 s); spikes, snares and gas don't. A hidden plate, once stepped on, gives you even less time (0.3 s) to get clear. Only fire and falling embers light the dark. Traps **hurt badly** (about 10 to 28, more higher up). Rolling through a firing trap counts as a dodge (feeding your legs), but only once per trap. Monsters that wander into one are caught too. The Manticore's and Chimera's towers also hide **gas vents**: set one off and a cloud spreads that keeps hurting while you stand in it; it can kill you if you don't get out.

**Never shut for good.** After the traps are laid, a **protection pass** keeps them off the start, the stair, the doors themselves, the puzzle room and the good rooms (only the cache's guard ring goes there), then searches the floor treating as walls only the tiles that are **never** safe (where overlapping beats leave no moment to cross). While the stair, the puzzle room, a healing spring or a camp can't be reached that way, it opens one such tile on the way (cutting a lane short there, or taking a trap away) and searches again. So the way through may be guarded, but a climber who times it, rolls or jumps can always get through. A chasm doesn't count: it's jumped. Monsters never wait on a trap tile, and nothing that matters ever sits in a chasm. The dev tools' **Speed readout** shows, at the bottom right, the frame rate and frame time, draw calls, node count, and how many monsters, traps and lights there are (to check for lag on a phone).

**Cheap to draw.** A trap at rest never changes, so every resting trap (and a set piece's path and brambles) is drawn once into a few squares of the floor; each frame only the traps warning or firing on screen (and hidden ones right beside you) are drawn, all in one batch, 30 times a second.

**The stair.** On floor 1 and the beast's floor the **stair** at the top of the room opens once three quarters of the monsters are down; on floors of rooms it opens when the floor's task is done. Walk onto it to climb, or finish the rest first for their essence (the HUD shows how many are left). Taking the stair is a rest: burns heal, you recover a third of your health and all your stamina. Each floor's monsters are bought from a budget that grows with height, and they get tougher too (`CURVE` and `MONSTERS` in `scripts/floors.gd`, both in the Data editor's Monsters section). A fall ends the climb: after the Regard it sends you back to the train (on the first climb, a fall before floor 6 brings the Regard instead; see **Falling, and lost things**). Your best floor is saved.

## Scenario rooms

Some rooms are short set pieces you play through, and how you do is **noticed** (`scripts/scenarios.gd`, where every number and word lives; their names and rules are the `scn_` rooms in `themes.gd`). Most floors from 2 have one (in a dead end first), and higher floors sometimes two. Each waits on a glowing gilt **mark** in its room: nothing starts until you step onto it, so you can walk past. Six shared kinds, and the towers' own:
- **The Last Camp:** a fallen climber's camp is still burning. Keep the monsters off it for 30 seconds: they go for the fire unless you come close.
- **The Falling Hall:** the roof comes down. Run for the far end, watching the rings where rocks will land.
- **The Champion's Circle** (from floor 3): the doors shut and a champion of the tower fights you, fairly and alone.
- **The Watchful Hall:** lanterns sweep the hall. Cross to the far end without their light falling on you; walls and cover hide you, and each time you're seen a monster comes.
- **The Caged Cub** (from floor 3): a small grey creature whimpers in a cage. Break it open, then lead it to the door while the monsters go for it.
- **The Gauntlet:** the doors shut; beat every wave, and be quick.
- **Hold the Forge** (the Wyvern's tower, from floor 3), **the Sinking Court** (the Manticore's tower), **the Burning Bridge** (the Firebird's) and **the Closing Thicket** (the Chimera's): the towers' own scenarios, described with their towers above. A tower's own scenario turns up only in its tower, three times as often as one of the shared ones (`towers` and `weight` in `SCENARIOS`), and its tower's floor script runs it.

Each ends with a **grade**: gold, silver, bronze or none (a fall, leaving the room, the camp or the cub lost). A grade pays essence and **renown** (`Renown.earn`), feeds the Makers' interest (`Makers.feed(main, "scenario")`), and goes on the climb's tally of what the beast noticed (`main.noticed`, with `main.beast_noticed_count()`, for the summit or the Regard to read later), with a banner: **THE GRYPHON NOTICED**. Their status shows under the task's line at the top of the screen.

## The Proving (floor 6)

Halfway up every tower (on the first climb, after the Regard), floor 6 is a special test that shows what the tower is about. It has its own fixed layout instead of planned rooms (`scripts/proving.gd`, where all its words and numbers live; each tower's picks are `gauntlet`, `sentinel` and `sentinel_adds` in `towers.gd`; "the Proving" is a placeholder name):

1. **The antechamber**, where you arrive, with two doors in its north wall and their words under them. **The Proving** (a gold six-pointed star) opens when you walk up to it. **The Lethal Proving** (the star in red, cracked, red light under it) stays sealed until you have won the Proving in that tower once; then bumping it explains it, and you **lean on it** for a moment to go in. Choosing one bars the other.
2. **The gauntlet**: the tower's own trap room (the Gryphon's Windhall, the Kraken's Sluice Hall, the Wyvern's Furnace, the Manticore's Spike Gallery, the Firebird's Emberfall, the Chimera's Bramble Hall). The way behind you shuts. Like every trap room it can always be crossed by timing: no tile on the way is never safe.
3. **The sentinel** (a door bearing a crown): a champion of the tower's sentinel (thunderling, shellback, smith, ram, wisp, mongrel) with two to four of its adds. The doors shut until they fall; the champion leaves an essence crystal.
4. **The beast's shadow** (a dark door with a pale eye, violet light under it): an echo of the tower's beast, drawn in faded violet with wisps rising off it, with about a third of its summit health; it calls no help and the Firebird's doesn't rise again. Its bar says "Shadow of the ...". The doors shut until it falls; its fall doesn't count as slaying the beast.
5. **The reliquary**, sealed until the shadow falls: a pedestal offers **one of three relics** (rings and amulets, Rare on the first win), never shown before you win. Then the stair.

**Losing isn't death.** In the ordinary Proving, when your health would reach 0 the tower "has taken your measure": you are **cast down to a fresh floor 5**, keeping half your unbanked essence, at half health, with a **lingering wound** (a quarter less max health, shown violet on the health bar with a small "wounded", and on the climber screen) that closes after two stairs, when you reach floor 7. Climb back and face floor 6 again. Only the **Lethal Proving**, chosen knowingly, ends the climb if you die there.

**Harder on later climbs.** Every win is saved per tower (`provings` in the save) and raises that tower's Proving a tier, up to 3: the gauntlet is laid as a higher floor's (faster, denser), the sentinel is tougher with more adds, the shadow stronger, and the relics are tempered, some of them Storied. The Lethal Proving is a tier above that. (Everywhere else difficulty rises only with the floor.) The tiers are `TIERS` in `proving.gd`.

## Locked doors and lock-in rooms

**Locked doors** (from floor 3, most floors have one, some higher floors two; `LOCKS` in `floors.gd`, the words in `LOCK_TEXT` in `doors.gd`). A lock never stands on the way to the stair or to any piece of the floor's task: only a **Strongroom** (a branch room that holds nothing the floor needs, made into a vault: a pedestal with a vault's cards, lightly guarded) or a sealed wing (which holds a vault already) is locked, so what's behind a lock is always worth it. What opens it is always found on the main route, so you can reach it without passing the lock. A locked door keeps its usual look with a lock drawn over it; bump it and it stays shut with a clank, "Locked", and a line saying how it opens. Monsters can't open it either. Keys that are part of the floor's task (the Keeper's key, the shards) aren't door keys: picking them up is the task.
- **A padlock:** its key lies in another room (a small gold glint; walk over it), or **one of the floor's monsters carries it** and drops it when it falls. Walk up to the door with the key and it turns.
- **Iron bars:** a **lever** stands in another room; strike it and the bars lift, with a clank, a nudge of the camera and a banner.
- **A violet ward:** solving the floor's puzzle opens it.

**Lock-in rooms.** Some rooms shut their doorways behind you once you're well inside: monster dens (3 in 4), restless crypts (9 in 10), some guard rooms (3 in 10), the stair landing from floor 6 (3 in 5), and the sealed wings (`LOCK_IN` in `doors.gd`). Iron bars drop with a slam and a banner in the top lane ("THE DOORS GRIND SHUT"), and a status chip under it says the doors are shut and how many foes are left (on a phone too). They open again when the monsters that were in the room are dead. A room with no monsters left never shuts, and two and a half minutes open it whatever happens, so you're never stranded.

**There is always a way out.** `scripts/reach.gd` walks a floor the way the rules allow: a closed door opens as you walk up to it, a locked door only once its key, lever or carrier (or, for a warded door, every place the floor's task asks you to reach) can be reached without passing it, a door barred from one side once its far side is reached, cracked walls and what's behind them count as rock (they're never needed), and doorways that open again by themselves (a lock-in room's bars, a wing's gate, a puzzle's seal) don't count as walls. Every floor plan is checked with it before it's used (`Floors.rooms_floor`): the stair and every piece of the task, every room, each wing's door, and every key, lever and carrier must be reachable from the start, or the floor is planned again (the planner itself never makes such a floor that the tests have found: this is the backstop). A monster carries at most one key. The cover in rooms never walls off a piece of the task, a chest, a key, lever, mural, camp or spring (`Floors.cover_targets`), and traps keep clear of them. And while the climb goes on, every two seconds while a door is locked, the game checks from where you stand (`main.gd` `check_stuck`): if the stair can't be reached and a locked door in your way has no key, lever or carrier you could reach, that lock gives way, with a banner ("A LOCK GIVES WAY"; the words are `relent_title` and `relent` in `LOCK_TEXT` in `doors.gd`). The recall scroll isn't relied on for this (you carry one at most, not always, and not on the first climb).

**Doors that only look dangerous.** The stair landing and a restless crypt have a **chained** door (two chains across it, an iron ring where they cross, red light under it): danger waits there, but the door isn't locked and opens as you walk up. Only a locked door shows a gold padlock.

## Sealed wings, vaults and secrets

Everything here is part of the Regard: the beast watches and tests, using what comes into the tower. Names, lines and numbers live in `scripts/wings.gd` (`TEXT`, `CONDITIONS`, `WAVES`, `REWARD`, `SECRET`) and `scripts/items.gd` (`RECALL`, `CRYSTAL`), so they're easy to change.

**Sealed wings** (from floor 2; one a floor, two from floor 10). A room of 9-11 by 7-9 tiles is carved into each kept cell beside the spine, joined by a corridor to its spine room, behind a **sealed door** (a dark stone slab carved with a watching eye, red light under it). Cut into one of its walls is a 3x3 **vault alcove** behind a one-tile vault door drawn as a **pulsing gold seal with a keyhole**, with warm light leaking out from under it, so you know from the moment you see it that a locked vault waits and that the room's question opens it. The seal's emblem shows the **challenge**, never the reward: a shield (take no hit), an hourglass (be quick), crossed blades (staggered kills), or a puzzle's emblem.
- **Walk in and the way shuts behind you:** the doorway grinds closed (solid to walking, sight and monsters' paths) with a slam and a banner: "The way behind you grinds shut. This room will not let you go until its question is answered."
- **The question**, for now, is a **challenge room**: two or three waves of the tower's own monsters and one rule, shown on screen with the wave and the clock: **Untouched** (take no hit), **Swift** (beat every wave within the time given) or, from floor 4, **Staggered** (every foe must fall while staggered: parry it, then strike).
- **Keep the rule** and beat the waves: the seal breaks, the vault opens, and so does the way out.
- **Break the rule** and either the waves go on and the vault opens with a **lesser prize** at the end, or the tower sends its **champion**: a bigger, tougher version of one of its monsters (three times the health, crowned, a gold aura, and a ground slam telegraphed by a closing ring: roll out of it). Its fall reopens the way, but the vault's seal goes dark for good; the champion leaves an essence crystal where it fell.
- **You're never trapped:** every ending reopens the way out, and a question that drags on for four minutes gives up.
- **For puzzles later:** a puzzle host can claim a wing (its slot is `wing["puzzle_slot"]`): `puzzle.gd` answers `wing_puzzle(wing)` with a puzzle type (and `wing_emblem(type)` with the seal's emblem), gets `start_wing_puzzle(wing)` when the climber walks in, and calls `main.wings.wing_solved(id)` or `main.wings.wing_failed(id)` (a failure sends the champion).

**Vault rewards** are real things to walk up to: a **pedestal** in the alcove with a gold light over it. Walk up and choose one of its cards (like a camp's: keys 1 to 3, arrows and Enter, the d-pad and A, click or tap): two **essence crystals** of different kinds (the tower's own and another: a slight steer toward the next bond), and a piece of **gear** (the rarer of two), a **recall scroll** (only if you don't already carry one, and not on the first climb) or an **item**. A lesser prize is a smaller crystal and an item. The monsters wait while you choose.

**Essence crystals** hold essence outside you and are **kept between climbs** (in your save). Crystals of a kind combine into one. Eat some (the Inventory: I, or the Bag button on a phone, the Essence crystals row, then **Eat 10**; or the book's **Crystals and tinctures**, on the train too) and that essence flows into you the natural way: your body and your marks share it as at a stair, without the crystals' share, and it counts toward which bond your next mark becomes. Or spend it at the train's store or bench (see **The train**), or craft tinctures from it. A vault's third card is sometimes a **tincture**, and a hidden cache sometimes holds one in place of a crystal.

**The recall scroll** (you carry one at most) shows with your items: make it your current item (T, the belt wheel, or the small arrow on a phone) and use it, or pick it in the Inventory and press **Read it**. It asks first; use it again within 3 seconds to read it. Reading it **leaves the tower safely, keeping everything**: your unbanked essence settles into essence crystals (one per kind; none of it flows into you); there's no heirloom choice (the climb wasn't won); your pack comes with you, and you go on to the train. Its words won't come on the bondless first climb.

**Secret rooms are pocket dimensions** (see **Portals, pocket dimensions and the floor map** below). Sometimes a kept cell holds a small room with no way to it on foot: rock all round. Its way in is a hidden spot in a spine room's wall that looks like any other wall (no torch hangs on it, and no trap or shelter pillar stands in front of it). Until it opens, the room is solid rock to sight, fog and paths. Its hints: **dust** drifting down near it now and then when you're close, a **hollow knock** when you strike it, and **Hunter's Keen Eyes** outline it. **Lean into it** (walk into it) for three quarters of a second, or break it with a **finishing blow** or a burst, and it gives way to a niche holding a **portal**; on a phone, push the stick into it. Through the portal is the pocket and its **vault** (three chests; some vaults guarded); a portal inside leads back.

**Cracked walls.** One or two walls a floor show **cracks** (drawn in code in the tower's stone, with chips at their foot). A finishing blow (the last strike of a weapon's combo) breaks one with a crumble of dust and rubble, and so does a burst: a Fire Flask, a Lodestone Bomb, an exploding spell. A lighter blow only knocks hollow ("A finishing blow might break it"), and dust drifts down from one when you're near. Behind it is always something: a **passage** between two rooms that weren't joined (a loop), or an **alcove** with an essence cache (sometimes gear) or a hidden vault's cards; some alcoves sit at the end of a short **corridor that ends at the cracked wall**, the clue. Cracked walls are never needed for the stair or the floor's task; the hidden wall you lean on (above) stays the rarer, subtler secret (`scripts/cracks.gd`, `TEXT` and `RULES`; how many and what they hide per tower: `cracks`, `crack_kinds` and `alcove` in `floor_styles.gd`).

## Portals, pocket dimensions and the floor map

Words and numbers: `scripts/portals.gd` (`TEXT`, `RULES`, `WAKE`, `LOOK`), `scripts/vaults.gd` (`TEXT`, `RULES`), `scripts/floor_map.gd` (`TEXT`), and in `scripts/floors.gd` `PORTALS` (how many a floor) and `SECRET_CONTENTS` (how a pocket vault is guarded).

**Portals always look the same**, in every tower, in pocket dimensions and when a Maker draws you away: a ring of dark stone with six notches round a swirl of light, drawn in code (`portals.gd` `draw_portal`). Asleep, the stone is grey and the swirl barely turns behind a dull film; woken, it turns fast in violet light with sparks circling.

**The floor's portals** join places on the **same floor**, never one floor to another. Every floor of rooms has **3** (6 from floor 10): always one in the entrance and one in the stair hall, the rest spread over the floor (each as far from the others as can be). They stand only in rooms you can walk to from the start through no locked or barred door, so a portal never carries anyone past a lock, and never in the puzzle's gallery, a scenario room, a trap room, a mirror room or a strongroom. `reach.gd` checks every portal can be walked to (a plan that fails is planned again), so travelling never strands you.
- **Asleep, then awake.** They sleep until you **complete a challenge** on the floor (its task, a **sealed wing** where the Regard watches you, the gallery's optional puzzle) or **slay every creature** on it; then **every portal on the floor wakes at once** (a banner, or a line under the challenge's own banner). Stepping onto a sleeping one says so.
- **Travel.** Step onto a woken portal and the **floor map** opens (the game waits): choose another woken portal (click or tap it; the arrows or D-pad, then Enter or A, or the Travel button) and you step out of it. Esc, B or Close and you stay. Once you've arrived, a portal answers only after you step off it. Portals won't answer while a room's doors are shut on you or while the Regard tests you in a sealed wing.

**The floor map** (O, the pause menu's **Floor map**, or the **Map** button on a phone's top bar; only on a floor of rooms): the rooms and corridors you've seen (seen rooms outlined), where you stand, the stair once you've seen it, and the portals (asleep ones once seen; woken ones everywhere, their unseen rooms tinted, since a portal can take you there). Pocket dimensions are never on it. **Hover** a marker or button with the mouse, move onto it with the keys or a controller, or **hold a finger** on it, and the Entity says what it is. Esc, B, O or Close shuts it.

**Pocket dimensions** are the secret rooms (see **Sealed wings, vaults and secrets** above for the hidden spot that opens one). Through the portal, the pocket: a room no corridor reaches, a faint ring of light in its floor, motes drifting up. **The floor waits as it was**: while you're in a pocket, the floor's monsters stand still, and a Keeper or mimic left in a pocket waits there while you're on the floor. The fog shows only the place you're in (the pocket, or the floor, never the pocket seen from the floor). The portal inside takes you back to where you came in.

**A pocket's vault** is richer than a chest, and still modest (slow growth: the draw is the gear's quality, not piles of essence). **Three chests:** one always holds a piece of **gear a rarity above** what the floor usually gives (Fine at the least, its traits rolled), dropped beside it; one holds a **choice of essence crystals** (three kinds, each three quarters of a vault crystal; now and then a tincture in place of one); the third holds a **smaller crystal** of the tower's essence (about a third of a vault crystal), or from floor 3 may be a **mimic** (one vault in four). Rarely (one in twenty, never on the first climb) a vault holds a **lead to the tower's Summit Blade**, given when the last chest is opened and kept in the profile (`profile.summit_leads[tower]` if the profile has it, otherwise its record's `summit_leads:<tower>`; `vaults.gd` `lead_count`). **Some vaults are guarded** (half of them): the chests stay chained until the guard is dealt with.
- **A Tithe Stone** (a lock): a pale slab with an open hand asks for unbanked essence (18 + 4 a floor), saying only what it asks. Lean on it from below to pay; it sinks and the chains fall.
- **Runes** (a short puzzle): three runes in the floor light in an order as you arrive. Step on them in that order; a wrong one shows the order again.
- **A Keeper**: a crowned champion of the tower's monsters rises as you arrive. Beat it and the chains fall.

Every new piece has the Entity's first-time word and a fuller answer in the Archive (`portal`, `portal_awake`, `pocket`, `pocket_vault`, `vault_guard`, `floor_map`, `maker_portal`), a "How does it work?" topic (**Portals, pockets and the floor map?**), and help on the map's markers and buttons and on the pause menu's Floor map button.

## The Makers and their fragments

Three godlike beings, siblings of the Entity, watch every climb from outside the towers: **the Maker of Marks** (made the bonds; cold blue, an eye), **the Maker of Towers** (made the towers; amber, a trowel) and **the Maker of Creatures** (made the monsters; rose, a paw). The names are placeholders. **Their words are written** (the owner approved the first draft): what each Maker says, its six fragments (18 in all: the owner's approved story text, the sixth of each speaking of the sixth climber in the climber's pronouns) and the Entity's line about it, all in `scripts/makers.gd` (`MAKERS`), each in its own voice (Marks precise and cold, Towers proud and slow, Creatures warm and fierce). Any line can still be redacted: a string of `▇` shows as blacked-out word-shaped bars. Names, colours, signs, every number and the answers live there too.

- **Fragments** (their traces) are rare: at most one on a floor, on about one floor in eight (`FRAGMENT`), never on the first climb, and only on the north wall of a **secret room** or a sealed wing's **vault**, never on the way to the stair. A fragment is a pale inscription with its Maker's sign, faintly lit in its colour. **Walk up to it** to read it: the room's light shifts in the Maker's colour, its words float up for a moment, a banner counts how many you've found, and the Entity goes quiet for a while.
- **The forbidden shelf**: once you've found a Maker's first fragment (or met it), the **Archive** (the Library) gains its shelf (on the train, the forbidden volume on the Library's bookcase): its sign, how many fragments, each one (where you found it, and its words), and how your meetings went. Before anything is found, nothing shows. Once you've met one, the Entity speaks of each sibling there.
- **How they watch**: each Maker keeps an interest score across every climb, fed by what it cares about (`WEIGHTS`): the Maker of Marks by spells cast (more for spells not cast yet this climb) and bond steps grown; the Maker of Towers by traps rolled through, secret walls leaned open, locked doors opened, vaults opened and puzzles solved; the Maker of Creatures by kills, champions and beasts; all three by marks taken, wounds, Making or Unmaking banked (once those exist) and fragments read (most for a fragment's own Maker).
- **Glimpses**: as a Maker's interest grows (past about a third of what it needs), now and then after a stair: a second, far too long shadow beside you, the light bending for a moment, or the Entity falling silent.
- **The meeting**: a Maker shows itself only once you've found **at least six fragments** in all, and then only when its interest crosses its threshold (`REVEAL`). It comes in the quiet just after a stair: the game pauses and a vast presence in its colour fills the screen, an eclipse with lit slits for eyes, too big to fit, a small climber beneath it. It speaks, then you choose an answer on cards (left and right and Enter or A, the number keys, a click or a tap). The answers are "To understand it.", "To take more of it.", "To change it." and "Nothing. Only to climb."; which ones each Maker takes well is in its `good` list. A **good answer grants its secret bond** (the Developer, the Designer or the Manipulator) as a **core mark** (saved as the profile's `core_mark` and `core_depth`); a **bad answer turns it away**: its interest drops, it needs more next time, and it remembers. Once you have a Making or Unmaking path, there's also a card to **plead for a change of path**: it may refuse; if it agrees, the path is reset. Carrying a full vessel adds **Offer the vessel**: the same plea, likelier granted, and the Maker keeps the vessel (see **The vessel**).
- **The confrontation**: a marked climber who keeps slipping past the stairs' barriers (the third slip, counted across every climb, and every third after) is met by **the Maker of Towers** once that floor's horde has fallen: the meeting's scene as a confrontation, with its own placeholder words and two answers (`CONFRONT` in `scripts/climb_shape.gd`): keep the rules and it lets you go, defy it and it takes half the essence you carry. It never grants a secret bond, and no other Maker interrupts it.
- **A Maker's portal**: every meeting (and the confrontation) happens in a pocket dimension of the Maker's own. As it begins, a portal (the same look as every portal) opens where you stand and draws you in; the meeting plays over a void of drifting stones, you on a floating stone with the portal behind you; when it ends you step back out of a portal that closes behind you (`maker_view.gd` draws it, `portals.gd` `maker_return`).
- **The core mark**: a fine pattern over all six body parts at once, in its Maker's colour, beneath your tower marks: threads (Marks), stepped courses like masonry (Towers) or curving veins (Creatures). It shows on the climber in play and on the climber screen, which also names it. Each depth makes it fuller; its tools are described under "The secret bonds" above. **One secret bond per climber**: the other two can still show themselves, but the Maker whose core mark you carry may **interrupt** (half the time): it arrives mid-scene and cuts the meeting short, speaks over its sibling, or stays and listens, and no second bond is granted.
- **Hooks for the secret essences**: when a Maker grants its bond it gives secret essence (Creatures: Making, Towers: Unmaking, Marks: both) through `main.gain_secret(kind, amount)` (or `main.rise_secret`) if the game has it, and a granted plea calls `main.reset_path()` if it exists; otherwise nothing happens.
- **The Sworn** (`scripts/sworn.gd`): taking a Maker's secret bond makes the climber **one of its Sworn** (read from the core mark, so old saves with one are Sworn too). The meeting says so ("You are one of the Sworn of ..."), the Entity reacts ("The Sworn"), and the climber screen shows a flat button under the core mark ("Sworn of the Maker of Towers": hover/hold help, and pressing it asks the Entity "Who are the Sworn?") with the errand under it. **Errands**: now and then (**about 1 in 3** climbs, at the end of a climb, one at a time; `SENDS`) the Maker sends its Sworn to a floor (4 to 9) of another tower; reaching it in any climb pays **6 crystals** of that tower's essence (slow growth). Kept in the profile's Makers state (`makers["sworn"]`: the errand, errands done, times rescued). The Sworn are also the ones who save the Unbound from the drain (the Unbound section).
- **Dev tools** (the Makers section): add a fragment, add interest to a Maker, meet a Maker now (once the menu closes), a glimpse now, place a fragment on this floor, and clear everything.

## Mirror images

Part of the Regard too: the beast tests you with your own likeness. Every word and number is in `scripts/mirror.gd` (`ROOM`, `STATS`, `SWING`, `SHOT`, `ROLL`, `GUARD`, `SPELL`, `SHAPES`, `BOND_SHAPE`, `PRIZE`, `LOOK`, `TEXT`).

- **Mirror rooms.** From floor 4, about one floor in six (`ROOM`) turns one of its branch rooms (off the way to the stair) into a **Hall of Glass** (chosen before the other rooms get their themes, so the floor still has its good room, den and trap room): silvered mirrors stand along its walls and polished panes lie in its floor. Never on the bondless first climb, the Proving's floor (6) or the summit. Its doorway always has a door, bearing an oval of silvered glass with a hooded figure in it and cold light under it (it hints at the fight, never the prize). No ordinary monsters wait inside.
- **Walk in** and a mirror image of you steps out of the glass in the middle of the room, and the doors grind shut (a lock-in room, `LOCK_IN` in `doors.gd`) until it falls (the usual failsafe opens them after 150 seconds).
- **It is you as you walked in:** drawn like the climber (the same hood or helm, armor, gloves and boots, its weapon in the *other* hand) but in silvered glass, a shimmer sweeping across it, a faint cool double a pixel aside and a pale rim and glints at its feet so it reads in the dark. It carries the **weapon in your hand** (its swing chain, up to three links, with your weapon's reach and arcs; or your bow or crossbow), wears **your armor** (your blows on it are cut by your own protection) and bears **your marks**. Up to two of your bonds (the highest steps) give it a simpler spell named after the bond's first spell, by `BOND_SHAPE`: a **volley** of three bolts (Hunter, Blade Forger, Venomist (poisons), Covenant), a **blast** circle where you stand that locks half a second before it bursts (Stormcaller, Deepcaller, Rune Artist, Sandshaper, Flame Contractor), a **cone** that throws you back (Windwalker, Tidecaller, Flameblade, Grafter, Surgeon) or a **nova** ring around it (Warden, Ashwalker, Flamewing, Rekindler). Blasts, cones and novas can't be parried: roll out.
- **Fair and readable:** every attack has its wind-up and telegraph (its weapon wound back and a red cone; a red aim line that thickens as it locks; the spell's red circle, cone, ring or fan with an inner edge in the bond's colour) and glints a quarter second before it lands, then recovers, open (the gold ring). It sometimes **rolls** clear of a blow from the front (at most every 3 seconds), and after an attack it may raise its **guard** (a silver arc in front): a light blow from the front is turned aside, throwing you back and opening you for a moment while it answers with a swing (still telegraphed). A finisher, a blow from behind, a riposte while it's staggered or a lifted foe get through.
- **Health:** 140 at floor 1, growing with the floor like other monsters (184 on floor 5): a few monsters' worth, below a champion or a beast. Its swings deal your weapon's damage times 1.3; its arrows 75% of a full draw, slower than yours.
- **Its prize:** it is a **great foe**: its secret essence (mostly Unmaking, some Making, `secret.gd`) rises through the usual path, so the first one opens the path choice if you haven't chosen. It also leaves an **essence crystal** of the tower's kind worth half a vault's (like a champion's), and a 1 in 4 chance of a piece of gear a rarity row higher than the floor's. It can't be bound by Covenant.
- The Entity explains it the first time you meet one. The **dev tools** have a *Mirror images* row: spawn one near you, or make the next floor hold a mirror room.

## Rival climbers

You aren't the only one climbing, and other climbers are a regular part of a climb (the owner: "rival climbers weren't encountered, so I think more often would give the towers more life and variety"). From floor 2 each floor rolls a **0.45** chance (`RULES` "chance"; about 4 floor plans in 10 end up holding one, since a floor needs a calm room free for them), up to **two a climb** (`per_climb`; it was 0.25 and one), never on the bondless first climb, the Proving or the summit. A climber who has **never met one** (the first climb after the Regard, or an older save that never did) is **sure to meet one by floor 3** (`sure_by`; if floor 3 has no room for them, the next floor that does). A second rival in the same climb is **someone else**, and wants **something else**: a meeting the climb hasn't had yet (a grudge or a debt still picks its own). Over simulated climbs nearly every climb meets two. Their room is calm: no monsters, no traps, no piece of the floor's task, so they never block the way. Every name, line and number is data in `scripts/rivals.gd` (`RULES`, `MEETINGS`, `BOND_STATE`, `WEAPONS`, `ARMORS`, `NAMES`, `LINES`, `TEXT`; the Data editor's **Rival climbers** sections), easy to rename or retune. Rarely, when they have no rumour of the guardians to pass on, one says something in passing: their own Entity ("Mine went quiet just before you showed up."; `LINES` "entity", `RULES` "entity_chance") or a line of the children's counting rhyme (`LINES` "rhyme", the words in `dining.gd RHYME`; `RULES` "rhyme_chance").

**Who they are.** Each rival is a climber like you: a name from `NAMES`, their own look and gear (drawn exactly as your climber is), a melee weapon, and **one to three marks** of their own (a second from floor 4, a third from floor 8, at steps that grow with the floor). Their marks glow on their body, and the exchange lists them. No backstory: a name and their marks, nothing more.

**Meeting them.** Walk up to them and a short exchange opens on the card screen (the same one camps and vaults use): their name, a line of what they say, their marks, and the cards. **Walk on** is always there, and you can come back. Each one wants one thing (picked by the weights in `MEETINGS`):

1. **Trade.** Two or three swaps: a piece of their gear, an item or crystals, each for something you carry worth about the same (the outfitter's own prices). A card you can't pay says so. Take one, then step away and come back for another.
