2. **Duel for the floor.** The room's doors shut and they fight you as a climber does: telegraphed swings with the glint before each, rolls, a guard, and a spell for each of up to two marks (red circles, cones and rings to roll out of; a spell that lands leaves its mark's state on you). They **yield** when badly hurt: you get crystals, renown, sometimes a piece of their gear, and **the stair's ward lifts** (a bigger prize if it already has). **You can't die in a duel:** a blow that would fell you knocks you down instead, with a fifth of your health left, and they take a toll (a quarter of your unbanked essence, or a piece from your pack).
3. **Team up.** They follow you and fight the floor's monsters **until you take the stair**: they swing at the nearest foe and cast their marks' spells. While they're with you, you keep **half** the essence you gather and finds drop half as often. The good part: **their marks react with yours.** Each of their spells leaves its mark's state on the foes it hits (charged, soaked, burning, poisoned...), so even if you carry a single mark you see the reactions: your lightning on a foe they soaked sets off **Conduct**, with *"with <their name>"* under the reaction's word (and in the feed and the Entity's log). Their marks lend yours their traits, and a **named synergy** one of whose marks is theirs holds, while they're beside you (see **Synergies**). Monsters strike them too; badly hurt, they fall back. Reaching the stair together earns renown. Their health shows on a chip at the top of the screen.
4. **A wounded climber** (from floor 3, the old game's "greed test"): they're slumped beside a hoard. **Help them** with a Healing Vial (or a quarter of your health if you carry none): renown, and they owe you; meet them again and they bring a gift (crystals and an item) and offer to climb with you. **Take the hoard**: essence and a finer piece of gear, but you lose a little renown (never a rank), the Makers take note, and if you meet them again they want a duel.

**Remembered.** Everyone you meet is kept with your climber (the save's `rivals` section; an old save simply has none) and listed on the **Renown Board** at a station, under **Climbers met**: their name and marks, and on their card your history with them (times met, duels won and lost, teamed up, helped, robbed, traded, reactions together), where you last met, and whether they owe you or hold a grudge. A rival you've met can come back on a later climb (`return_chance`), **every mark a step stronger**, sometimes with a new one.

**Renown and titles.** A duel won, a climber helped and a stair reached together earn renown. Three titles come from them (names in `renown.gd TITLES`): **the Duelist** (win 3 duels), **Shieldmate** (team up once) and **the Helping Hand** (help a wounded climber).

**The Entity explains it.** The first time you meet one it says what they're about, and again the first time each kind of meeting comes up, and the first time a reaction is shared. Ask it **"Who are the other climbers?"** under *How do things work here?*. Rivals are not monsters, so they stay out of the Bestiary. To try them without waiting for one, the **dev tools** have a *Rival climbers* row: a climber beside you who wants to trade, duel, team up or lies wounded, or one on the next floor of rooms.

How it fits together, for whoever changes it next: `floors.gd` gives them a calm room (theme `rival` in `themes.gd`) and `plan["rivals"]`; `rivals.gd` runs the exchange, the four meetings and the record; their body in a fight is `rival.gd` (it extends the mirror image, `mirror.gd`, and draws a climber of its own; as an ally it fights monsters); `main.gd` asks `rivals.share()` and `rivals.drop_mult()` while you're teamed up, and `rivals.spare()` before a fall; `reactions.gd` notes which climber gave a state (`giver`, `partner`, `SHARED`) and `synergies.gd` counts the ally's marks (`shared`, `lent`). Tests: `tests/parts/rivals.gd`.

## The Dining car and the Arena car

Other climbers ride the train too. The **Dining Car** (between the Workshop and the Library on the rail, with a steaming cup for its icon) is where you meet them between climbs, so the climbers you run into in the towers become people you see again. It's drawn in code like the other cars: wine-red flocked walls, mahogany panelling, a sideboard with decanters and a bell, a slate of tonight's fare, and booths where the climbers sit behind laid tables with little lamps, each drawn in their own look and gear (exactly as they look in a tower), their marks as small gems on a brass name plate.

**Who sits there.** Every time you're on the train (a "visit": from coming back until you leave for the next climb) **three to five** climbers sit at the tables: mostly climbers you've **met in the towers** (they remember everything you did together), plus a **new face** or two, made the same way as the climbers in the towers. Talk to a new face and they join the climbers you've met, listed on the Renown Board, and they may turn up on a later climb. Climbers you meet in a tower sit in the Dining car on later visits. Their record (met, duels won and lost, traded, helped, robbed...) is one record, shared by the towers and the car.

**Talking.** Tap (or click, or choose with the keys or controller) a climber, and a talk opens on the placard beside the tables: their name, their marks, your history with them, and what they say first (it follows your history: a duel, a climb together, a hand up, a hoard taken; or their marks, for a new face). Then pick a line, as with the Entity:

- **"Heard anything useful?"** A **rumor**. It's always **true**: the game reads it from its own tables as they say it. A tower's handy item and why, its beast and what to watch for, a **monster's weakness** (written into its Bestiary entry at once, marked "a climber told you"), the floor from which a sealed wing can ask a certain rule, a dark tower and what helps, another climber's mark, or the best odds on offer tonight; or, as lore rather than use, a line of the counting rhyme the children chant in the cities (`RHYME`, six lines). Each climber tells a different one. Now and then (`RULES` "entity_chance", one time in ten) a climber you know mentions their own Entity in passing (`LINES` "entity": "Yours tell you about the Makers too?"); nobody knows if there is one Entity or many.
- **"Fancy a wager on my next climb?"** Two **wagers** on your next climb, in the tower chosen on the Engine's board: reach a floor, fell the beast, reach a floor without drinking a Healing Vial, or slay so many of one monster. Harder ones pay more (the odds show beside each). Choose one, then a stake: 10, 20 or 40 crystals of any kind (your biggest first). You can have **two** open. They show as a small line at the top of the screen in the climb, and the Entity's log notes them. When the climb ends (a fall, a recall, the summit), a win pays the **stake times the odds** in the tower's essence, a loss keeps your stake, and a wager on a tower you didn't climb is called off and your stake comes back. The Dining car says how they went.
- **"Got anything to trade?"** Their goods for your crystals, at the outfitter's own prices: a tincture of their mark's essence, a charm of it (or a piece they carry), and an offer for one piece of your stash (they pay 60% of its price; the outfitter pays 40%). A tincture goes on your belt for the next climb, a piece into your stash.
- **"I'll fight you for it."** A **duel**. Choose the stake: 20 crystals, a piece of gear (your stash piece nearest in worth against one of theirs), or 6 renown. Then **To the Arena car**.
- **"Goodbye."** Ends the talk.

**How they treat you.** A climber you **robbed** (took their hoard) is **cold**: no rumors, no wagers, no trades, though they'll still fight you. One you **helped** up or **climbed with** is **warm**: a small gift of crystals each visit, better odds and a little off their goods. The title you wear can warm the others too: see "Titles" below.

**The Arena car.** It's coupled behind the Dining car, but it's **never on the rail**: the only way in is a duel. It's a small arena inside a train car, drawn in code: floorboards, a ring of sawdust roped off on brass posts, benches of onlookers along both sides, and lamps down the middle. The climber fights you as climbers do in the towers (their weapon, rolls, a guard and their marks' spells). **Nobody dies**: they yield when badly hurt, and a blow that would fell you makes *you* yield instead, with a little health left. After two minutes it's called a draw and nothing changes hands. Win: you take their stake, a little renown, and it counts toward **the Duelist** title. Lose: they take yours (a piece you lose is theirs now, and they carry it). The pause menu's **Yield, back to the Dining car** gives up (a loss). Either way you're back in the Dining car afterwards, and they remember.

**Titles.** The title you wear changes how climbers treat you, read in one place: `perks()` in `scripts/dining.gd` asks `rival_terms` in `scripts/titles.gd` (the Noble's titles). A "friendly" title makes plain climbers warm (better odds, a little off) and "trade kinder" takes its share off their goods; a title that **takes bets** opens full wagers with everyone (both offers, every stake, a little better odds; without one, a climber you haven't helped offers one wager at the smallest stake); a title that **halves the toll** halves a lost duel's crystal or renown stake (a staked piece still changes hands). A title without rival terms changes nothing.

**The Entity explains it.** The first visit to the Dining car, the first talk, the first rumor, the first wager and the first duel each get a line from the Entity, and the Arena car the first time you step in. Ask it **"What's the Dining car?"**, **"How do bets work?"** and **"How do duels work?"** under *How do things work here?*. Hover (mouse), move onto (keys) or hold (touch) any button in the car and it says what the button does.

**Where it lives, for whoever changes it next.** Every word and number is data in `scripts/dining.gd` (`RULES`, `LINES`, `RUMORS`, `BETS`, `TEXT`; the Data editor's **The Dining car** sections), easy to change; names and lines are placeholders. The car's things are built by `scripts/dining_page.gd` (the tables, the talk; a climber at a table is its `Diner`), the room is drawn in `scripts/train_view.gd` (`_dining`, and the `tables` furniture), the Arena car by `scripts/arena_car.gd`, and the duel runs in `scripts/arena.gd` (type `duel`, the station arena's own machinery). The car's state is kept with the climber (the save's `dining` section; an old save has none). `main.gd` tells it when a climb starts (`on_depart`), a foe falls (`on_kill`) and the climb ends (`settle`, from `pay_climb`); `player.gd` when an item is used. Tests: `tests/parts/dining_car.gd`.

## Glows, sparks and the Effects setting

An **enchanted** weapon (a Fine or Rare piece, or a relic of one essence) shows what it is made of. It glows along its blade in its essence's colour, carries a **small moving light** (it helps a little on a dark floor, nothing like your own light), and gives off specks by essence: **fire** sends embers rising, **water** drips, **air** drifts wisps, **iron** throws sparks when it hits, **stone** throws grit, and **blade** leaves a thin shimmer on a swing. An ordinary (Common) weapon stays plain. An enchanted bow, crossbow or essence-shot pistol gives its arrows and shots a coloured trail and a little light. Rival climbers and mirror images wear the same look if their weapon is enchanted. The Entity says so the first time you hold one ("Enchanted: it shows its essence, and lights a little of the dark") and under **How do things work here? -> What are the glows and sparks?**.

**Spells** flash a short light where you cast, throw sparks that suit the mark's essence and the spell's shape (a cone along your aim, a ring all round, up, or pulled inward for Deepcaller and the Unbound's Unmaking), and carry a small light with their bolts, orbs and fields while they fly or last. Chain Lightning crackles along its arcs; fire spells shed embers and smoke; Sandshaper's spells raise dust. Foes (and you) under a state give off matching specks (burning embers, bleeding drips, poison bubbles, soaked drops, charged crackle), a reaction bursts in its own colour, and every blow, critical blow and kill sparks.

**Settings -> Effects** is **Low**, **Medium** (the default) or **High**. Low keeps every light and the important flashes (a crit, a reaction, a cast) and drops nearly all specks, which is kindest to a phone. Medium is the usual look, and High has more, with a higher cap. The Entity explains the buttons when you point at (or hold) them.

How it fits together, for whoever changes it next: **one** node, `scripts/particles.gd`, owns a pool of particles and draws them all itself (a soft dot, a streak, a tumbling shard or grit; normal or additive), like `lighting.gd` does for light: no node per particle, no `GPUParticles2D`. It sits above the darkness and under the foes' telegraphs and the spells' own drawing, so a speck never hides a warning. `Particles.burst(main, pos, "preset", count_scale, dir, colour)` makes a puff; `Particles.emitter(main, node_or_callable, "preset")` makes a stream and returns a handle (`stop()`). Every preset (colour, count, speed, life, size, gravity, drag, spread, blend, shape, rate) is data in `PRESETS` and in the Data editor (Numbers and Colours -> "Effects"), and so are the levels (`EFFECTS`: how many, the cap, how many small lights), the weapon's light (`WEAPON_LIGHT`) and a spell's (`SPELL_LIGHT`). `ESSENCE_FX` gives each essence a look, `BOND_FX` and `SPELL_FX` tune each mark and its spells, so all eighteen marks have one without hand-coding each (each mark's first spell and its Apex are tuned). A few small hooks call it: `player.gd` (the weapon's place, a blow), `bond.gd` and `unbound_run.gd` (a cast), `projectile.gd` and `spell_fx.gd` (shots, orbs, fields), `reactions.gd` (a reaction) and `main.gd` (a floor clears it, a kill, a lightning arc). It pauses with the game, is capped (a busy screen makes fewer per source), and nothing is made out of sight or in fog. Tests: `tests/parts/particles.gd`.

## Motion and life

Everything here is for the eye: **no blow, roll, parry window or telegraph changes its timing** (a swing's hit lands in the same call as before, a roll is untouchable for exactly as long, a foe's glint shows exactly when it did).

- **The climber moves.** Each step lifts the body a little; a swing throws sparks along its path and shows a bright leading edge (a finisher draws wider, heavier outer arcs); a roll kicks up dust, stretches you mid-way and puffs as it ends; a landing puffs; a parry throws glints; a hit makes the body shiver; a drink mends with a green glow and rising sparkles; a fall folds the body as it drops.
- **Foes move.** They breathe when they stand, pull back as they wind up (the glint is unchanged), flash white when struck, and when one falls it comes apart into motes that rise and drift toward you (the essence is awarded exactly as before). Each beast arrives with a ring and a flare, flares again at half health, and its fall gives a short beat of slow time (about 0.8 s of real time at about a third speed; automated test runs skip it).
- **Each tower has its own life**, behind the action: drips and bubbles (Kraken), wind streaks and feathers (Gryphon), ash and heat (Wyvern), blowing sand (Manticore), embers (Firebird), leaves and spores (Chimera). Torches throw the odd spark; a warded stair pulses and bursts open in gold when the ward lifts; doors kick up dust, cracked walls come down in chips and dust, chests pop open in a fountain and picked-up items leave a small arc of sparks.
- **Feedback.** Damage numbers pop and arc away; a critical blow's is big and gold. The belt and spell wheel bloom open, new lines in the left feed slide in, and a mark that grows a step flares (a ring and rays) on the Marks tab's body.
- **Settings.** **Screen shake** (on by default) turns every shake off; **Effects** (Low, Medium, High) scales the rest: Low has no ambient streams and only a few key flashes, High has more. The Entity explains the buttons when you point at (or hold) them.

Where it lives: `scripts/anim.gd` holds every amount, duration and strength (`DATA`, in the Data editor as "Motion"), each tower's ambient streams (`AMBIENT`) and the few calls the rest of the game makes (a swing, a roll, a landing, a parry, a drink, a foe's end, a beast's arrival, the ward lifting, a door, a wall, a chest, a pickup). The specks go through `particles.gd` (new presets: `swing_*`, `roll_dust`, `dissolve*`, `flourish`, `pop`, `chips`, `torch_spark`, `amb_*`), so the Effects level and the cap apply. The drawing hooks are in `player.gd` (`_draw`: bob, stretch, shiver, fold), `enemy.gd` (`_draw`: breath, wind-up pull, white flash), `fx.gd` (number arcs), `hud.gd` (wheel bloom), `notices.gd` (feed slide) and `menu.gd` (`BodyView` flourish). Tests: `tests/parts/animation.gd`.

## What's in this build

- **The base climber.** Started very clumsy; now a bit tougher: 140 health, quicker on its feet, faster stamina recovery, cheaper rolls, a slightly wider parry window, and a longer grace period after being hit so a crowd can't chain-hit you.
- **Eighteen bonds**, three for each of the six towers, all written (each a Body, a Passive and its spells, with every fork on its tree: see the towers' bonds above and Marks' trees below).
- **The Unbound**, the other path: techniques built on the climber screen's Techniques tab, a tree per body part with its new forms, every effect and a capstone at level 12 (six Nodes that wake from banked essence and grow by use, to size 120; a young technique at level 3, the full one at 6), core essence and the aura (six steps) for both paths.
- **Overdraw.** Cast without enough essence and the spell still fires, but the body part burns: a burned hand weakens your strikes, burned legs make rolls cost more and travel less. Overdraw it again and it's paralyzed; each stair heals a rung; a forced cast burns it out for climbs.
- **Marks grow by use.** Cast a mark's spells and each stair refills what you used and deepens its pool; a mark you never cast barely grows.
- **Marks and motes on the body.** Each bond shows as a sigil that grows lines with its step, circled by motes as its essence fills.
- **Gear.** Ten melee weapons (canes among them), two bows, a crossbow and two pistols, four suits of armor, and twenty-seven Common worn pieces plus Fine and Rare rings, amulets, packs and a bandolier for the helm, gloves, legs, boots, cloak, amulet, ring and backpack slots; Fine, Rare and Storied finds with twenty essence traits (never one twice on a piece), twenty-four named pieces (eight only in their own tower), six Regarded pieces left by the beasts, caps on what gear adds, wear on Common pieces and a 50% protection cap (see **Gear: weapons, armor and worn pieces** above). *Bows*: hold to draw (you walk slowly while drawing and stamina stops recovering), release to loose. Damage and speed grow with the draw, and a full draw (a soft tick and ring tell you) pierces through foes. A quick tap fires a weak shot; rolling or getting hit drops the arrow. Weapons are plain data in `scripts/weapons.gd`, armor and worn pieces in `scripts/gear.gd`.
- **Solid bodies.** The climber and monsters can't share space; overlapping bodies are pushed apart, and heavier ones (the knight) give way less. Foes lifted by Gale Lift pass over everyone.
- **Timing over mashing.** Swings cost real stamina (a sword chain is 58), and stamina waits almost a second after you attack before it refills, so mashing leaves you winded. **Fatigue** (the owner: "when you run out and continue to perform actions that use stamina, the bar loses max capacity. A stamina vial should restore the lost capacity"): a roll, jump, strike or shot with too little stamina still happens, and what stamina can't pay comes off your max stamina instead (`player.gd` `spend_sta`), never below 40% of it (`combat.gd` CLIMBER "fatigue_floor", in the Data editor); past that a roll or jump is "too winded" as before (a strike with a little stamina left still goes, as it always did). Stamina refills only up to the shrunken bar, whose lost end the HUD shows dark and hatched (its hover help says how much). A **Stamina Vial**, a camp's rest and every new climb restore it; the stair's rest fills stamina but leaves fatigue. The Entity explains it the first time (tip `fatigue`, and "Stamina, and why my bar shrinks?" under How do things work here?). Monsters have **poise**: once they commit to an attack, light hits don't stop it (read the glint, then parry or roll); only heavy hits (finishers) rock them, and light hits make a monster flinch at most once every 1.2 seconds, so you can't stun-lock it. After every attack a monster is **open** for most of its recovery: a gold ring pulses around it and your hits deal 60% more ("Opening!"). Beasts have less health than before, longer openings after their attacks, and their fire burns out faster.
- **Readable fights.** Every monster attack follows one rhythm: a wind-up, then a **glint** (a white star and a "ting") exactly 0.25 s before the blow lands, then a recovery where the monster is open. Parry on the glint and it always works; parry a beat after the glint for a **perfect parry** (longer stagger, more stamina back, bigger flash). The parry window is about a third of a second, turns you toward the attacker (or an incoming arrow) so aiming can't make it miss, and can cancel the tail of a swing. Only two monsters attack at a time; the rest circle and wait their turn. Slimes and archers flinch when hit (so aggressive play works on them); knights keep winding up through your hits, so parry, riposte or roll against them. The knight slashes up close and lunges from further out (from floor 3 it also sweeps low all round it: a low blow, jump it); the slime creeps up and pounces (touching it is harmless).
- **Combat feel.** Strikes chain into a 3-hit combo that costs stamina, with the third swing a heavy finisher; each swing lunges a little and locks your aim. Presses made up to 0.2 s early are buffered. Every hit gets a short hit-pause, sparks, knockback and a squash, and heavier hits get more of each. Hitting a staggered foe is a **riposte** for 2.5x damage. A clean **parry** flashes, rings, refunds some stamina and **reflects arrows** back at monsters. Rolls leave afterimages for exactly as long as you're invulnerable, and a landed dodge shows a ring. Getting hurt knocks you back, flashes the screen edge red and interrupts your swing or cast.
- **The look.** A dark tower: flagstone floor with moss and cracks, a brick north wall, flickering torches, drifting dust and embers. The darkness is **one layer** over the world (`scripts/lighting.gd`, like the web version's): a single screen-sized shader that darkens everything in the tower's own fog colour and cuts soft holes of light around the climber, the torches, the stair, the cache, lit puzzle eyes, burning floor and short flashes, and adds a vignette. It costs one draw call however many lights there are (up to 48 at once), and it reads the line-of-sight fog in the same pass. The open stair, the cache, lit eyes and the ward also get a soft coloured glow so the eye goes to them.

  **Shadows, spotlights and glow** (all in `lighting.gd`, built for the browser's WebGL2 and a phone, no 2D light nodes):
  - **Soft shadows.** Light stops at walls, closed doors, low cover and pillars, and fades softly around corners and doorways instead of shining through rock. Each light's view of the walls is worked out once on the CPU, on a grid of half tiles around it (ring by ring outward, each cell taking light from the cells nearer the light, so a shadow starts crisp at a wall's edge and widens into a soft penumbra), and kept as a 48x48 square in one small atlas texture that the darkness shader reads with smooth filtering. Torches, the stair and flashes never need it again; a moving light gets a new one a few times a second; a door opening redoes only the squares around it. A frame spends at most a millisecond or two on new shadows (`QUALITY` `cast_ms`). On High the climber's own light casts shadows too, worked out twenty times a second as you move, so pillars sweep their shadows round you (walls need none: the fog already hides what's behind them). A wall's brick face catches the light up to its top.
  - **Spotlights.** A light can shine in a soft-edged cone (`add_spot(pos, dir, cone_deg, reach, colour, ...)`, or `set_spot` on an existing light): the light under a lit door (a good room's warm glow, a chained or sealed door's red, the shade's violet) fans out into the corridor (`doors.gd` `UNDER_LIGHT`), and a vault's seal spills its gold out in front of it.
  - **Volume and the finishing pass.** Every code-drawn character, chest and prop is shaded the same way by one shading toolkit (`scripts/shade.gd`): one small texture built in code holds how each material takes the light (a ball, a flat face, a cylinder, wood, metal, cloth, a figure's overall light, a soft shadow), and the drawing batches (`draw_util.gd` `Batch`, the monsters' `Pen`) carry UVs into it, so balls read as spheres, limbs and blades as cylinders with a sheen, polygons as lit faces with a highlight on their lit edges, and everything standing on the floor gets a soft contact shadow, at no extra draw call. The floor bakes in the rest (each flagstone tilted to the light, soft shade at the foot of walls, cover and pillars). Over it all, `scripts/finish.gd` grades the screen in one pass (a little contrast and colour, each tower's own tint: `TOWER_GRADE` in `palette.gd`) and on High adds bloom on the brightest things. The treasure chest (`scripts/chest_art.gd`) and the climber are the hand-finished showcase.
  - **Glow.** Bright things get a soft additive halo just above the darkness: torch flames, the open stair, the ward, the cache, the campfire, burning floor, spells' flashes, essence motes flying to the climber, and any registered light with enough glow (`BLOOM`). Halos of things out of sight fade with the fog. All of them are one more draw call.
  - **Settings → Lighting:** **Low** is the plain darkness (no shadows, no glow, flat shapes and no finishing pass: the lightest for an old phone), **Medium** has shadows (worked out a little more slowly) and glow, **High** adds softer shadow edges and quicker updates for moving lights. A computer starts on High; a browser or a touchscreen (the phone build) starts on Medium. It's saved with the other settings.
  - Every number is in `QUALITY`, `SHADOW_*`, `SPOT_*` and `BLOOM` at the top of `scripts/lighting.gd`. Each tower's darkness colour and stone hue live in `scripts/palette.gd` (`TOWER_FOG`, `TOWER_TINT`). Characters are outlined and shaded: a hooded climber with a visible weapon, a wobbling slime whose eyes follow you, a hooded archer with bow and quiver, and an armoured knight with shield and plume who raises his sword to wind up. Telegraphs, swing trails, health bars and words in the world (damage numbers, gear names) are drawn above the darkness so they always read. The camera follows the climber, zoomed in. Spells, parries, crits and burns flash light (`main.light_flash`); slain monsters burst into debris.
- **Sound.** Recorded CC0 effects, layered by `tools/build_audio.py` (sword hits are a chop plus a punch body plus a low thump, parries are a metal clang with a ringing tail). Every weapon sounds like itself: a dagger's quick swish and slice, a spear's thrust, the dull whoosh and bone-deep thud of a mace or war hammer, and its own sound when you switch to it. Bows are real bow recordings: the stave creaks while you draw (lower on the longbow), the nock clicks at full draw, and the release is a string snap and a whoosh that grows louder and sharper the longer you held it; arrows land with a wooden thunk (into walls too). The crossbow fires with a hard clack, ratchets as it reloads and clicks when the bolt drops in. Hits sound different on each monster (armour clanks, slime squelches), the archer's bow creaks before it fires and the knight's armour rattles as he winds up, beasts' blows land with a deep slam, chain mail and plate jingle as you walk, trap plates click before they fire, gas vents hiss (`hiss`), and there are pick-up, armor and heirloom sounds, soft page-turn sounds in the menus, and a gong when the room is clear. Levels are set by kind (footsteps and menus quiet, hits in the middle, slams and parries loudest) so nothing jumps out, and in a busy fight one sound never plays more than four times at once. Looping music and tower ambience play underneath; M toggles them. The knight's wind-up and the archer's aim lock each play a warning tone. Every monster has a voice: a warning as it winds up (the bat's squeak, the thornhound's snarl, the scorpling's chitter, the ram's snort, the cinder's flare, the smith's grunt...), a cry when struck (spaced out so a flurry of blows doesn't make a choir) and its own death. Each beast roars as you reach its floor and before its biggest blow, and has its own warning, cry and death (the Gryphon's screech, the Kraken's deep wet roar, the Wyvern's reptile call, the Manticore's lion roar, the Firebird's scream, the Chimera's lion, goat and serpent at once). The train rumbles and its wheels clack over the rail joints, the whistle blows as you depart, the cars' doors slide as you walk through and the outfitter's bell rings; crystals clink when you buy and sell, the workshop bubbles as you brew and rings with hammer on anvil as you craft. Items sound like themselves when used (a drink, a puff of smoke, a vial, oil, a ward, a flask that shatters, a flare that catches). Puzzles have their own sounds (mirrors creak as they turn, ice scrapes and bumps, statues murmur as they speak, the lock's key turns, eyes open, plates slide, levers clank, pedestals light, a chime when solved and a groan of stone on a failure), as do the Makers (meetings, glimpses, fragments, a grant), the secret essences, Making's mending and Unmaking, the vessel filling, the Regard, a summit won, the Proving's shadow, the rift and the Entity's tips (a soft chime). The bond spells and the Unbound's forms and effects sound like their family: lightning and thunder, wind, water and waves, frost, fire and fire blasts, stone and sand, iron, blades, poison, life, runes and Unmaking. Each tower has its own ambience under the music (wind in the Gryphon's halls, dripping water in the Kraken's, the forge's boiler and fire in the Wyvern's, a dry wind and trickling sand in the Manticore's, a high wind and embers in the Firebird's, a low wind, birds and growls in the Thornwild), and so does the train; one music track plays under all of them.
- **Three telegraphed monsters.** A slime that hops at you, an archer that locks its aim before firing (a red line), and a knight that winds up a lunge (a filling red bar). Parry the knight's lunge to stagger it.

## What to look for when testing

- Does the base climber feel clumsy but fair?
- Does rolling through attacks to fill the legs feel rewarding or tedious?
- Is overdrawing tempting in a tight spot, and does the burn feel like a real cost?
- Can you read every enemy attack before it lands?
- Do Fine and Rare finds feel worth hunting, without the HUD filling up? Are the trait lines and "(capped)" clear on a phone?
- Is the belt wheel (hold the Item button, slide, let go to choose; tap to use) quick enough mid-fight, and is 25% speed while it's open right (`scripts/wheel.gd` RULES, or the Data editor's Numbers, "The wheels")?

| `scripts/room_rewards.gd` | Chests of spoils (and the mimic waiting as one), strongrooms' pedestals, rest rooms and wandering traders |
| `scripts/rooms.gd` | The hand-made rooms, drawn as text grids, the carved shapes, and how they turn into walls, pillars, chasms and pools; the painter's rooms in play (`custom`) and the low-wall check (`cover_kept`) |
| `scripts/painter.gd` | The room painter (Dev tools → Paint rooms): the grid, tools, palette, checks, room list, settings, Save / Copy / Paste and Try it |
| `scripts/painter_tiles.gd` | The painter's tiles: their names and notes, and how each is drawn in a tower's colours |
| `scripts/room_check.gd` | The checks a painted room must pass (doorways, the stair, reachability, low walls, puzzles) |
| `scripts/room_store.gd` | The painter's rooms: `data/rooms_custom.json` (from the editor) and `user://rooms_custom.json`, Copy and Paste |
| `scripts/themes.gd` | Room themes: names, one-line rules, tints, door looks, and the rules that hand them out |
| `scripts/doors.gd` | Doors: solid until you walk up, styled to hint at the room behind; locked doors with their keys and levers; lock-in rooms |
| `scripts/reach.gd` | Can the climber get out? Walks a floor plan, or the floor being played, the way doors, locks and keys allow (the planner's last check and the game's safety net) |
| `scripts/unbound.gd` | The Unbound: their profile state, their six Nodes (asleep and soaking, waking at 30, what they hold and their size, growth by use (no cap a climb), sealing and unsealing Nodes and their bonus, attributes from sizes, old saves made into Nodes, the primary and three-kinds rules, moving and feeding essence and its price), natural parts and routing, levels (12), minor abilities, gifts, signatures, core-led parts and core techniques, stages, becoming Unbound and going back |
| `scripts/technique.gd` | The Unbound's techniques: fill, boost, strain, recipes, plain-words descriptions, casting and the variations on a cast (shapes and kinds come from the library) |
| `scripts/bestiary.gd` | The Bestiary: every monster's entry in data (name, how it fights, blank lore), the tiers, the per-climber record's calls, the page and the monsters' pictures |
| `scripts/library.gd` | The shared library: forms, effects, variations and echoes in data, the content contract, and loading the content files |
| `scripts/spells/` | The content files: each tower's forks and their spells (`gryphon.gd` ... `chimera.gd`), the Unbound's new forms and tree layout (`unbound_forms.gd`) and effects (`unbound_effects.gd`) |
| `scripts/spell_fx.gd` | What spells leave in the world for a while: timed casts, fields, orbs and warning rings |
| `scripts/tree.gd` | The tree's rules: marks' forks, the Unbound's trees, choosing, learning a passed side, effects opening, old saves |
| `scripts/tree_view.gd` | The book's Tree section: the body map, the part's tree of buds, the chosen bud's card, choosing and learning |
| `scripts/book.gd` | The book: its leather and pages, the ribbons, turning a page, and the closed book in Your Car |
| `scripts/grimoire_card.gd` | The grimoire card: one card for every ability, its small drawing and glyphs |
| `scripts/technique_bond.gd` | A carried technique standing in a mark's place on the climber, so the mark buttons, HUD and touch buttons cast it |
| `scripts/unbound_run.gd` | The Unbound in a climb: filling and boosting techniques, strain and eruptions, bolts and bleeds, punching through barriers (and the first climb's broken rules), the refusal and condensing cards, the veins |
| `scripts/technique_page.gd` | The Unbound's part cards on the climber screen, the Developer's way back and the dev tools' section |
| `scripts/technique_tab.gd` | The Unbound's Techniques tab on the climber screen: the six slots, the drawn globe and strain dial, form, effects, variation, name, boost and routing (every press saved at once; only on the train) |
| `scripts/body_move.gd` | The Unbound's moving essence on the climber screen's Body tab: From / Essence / To / Move, Drain into crystals, the reason a move can't happen, and no moving while hunted |
| `scripts/node_seal.gd` | Sealing and unsealing a Node on the Body tab (on the train, the confirmation pop-up with the warning), its words, and the sealed Node's drawing on the figure |

| `scripts/abilities.gd` | The Abilities page (the climber screen's first tab): its entries for marked climbers and the Unbound, the cards and their "more", the button words for each input, and the short words used in play (button names, toasts, the long-press card) |
| `scripts/ability_hints.gd` | Names in play: ready labels under the HUD's medallions, first-cast toasts, the phone's long-press card, the practice room's line |

| `scripts/stations.gd` | Stations between towers: the stations and their stops, Training's lessons (`LESSONS`) and medal drills, the tournament's brackets and its master's lines, fees, prizes and what a result pays (`apply_result`) |
| `scripts/training.gd` | Training: the required start and the Noble's Grace, the first tournament (`FIRST`, its champion and `REWARDS`), training's essence (`RATES`: a quarter into the body, a half later), advanced and mark training (`ADVANCED`, `MARK`, `TIERS`), and the Entity's hint and facts |
| `scripts/tournaments.gd` | The travelling Tournament Manager: where they are (`ROTATION`), each location's layout, hazards, creatures, rivals and prize (`LOCATIONS`, `LAYOUTS`), and the shared and unique rewards |
| `scripts/travel.gd` | Six stations and travel: the loop, the fare (`FARE`) and titles' cuts, the lock until the first mark, riding the train, the home and current station, and the Entity's tours (`TOURS`) |

| `scripts/tour.gd` | The Entity's tours of the station and the train: each step's stop or car, what it points at, the bar with Back, Next and Skip |
| `scripts/station_page.gd` | A station's page beside the rail: each stop's tiles and card (lessons and drills, brackets, the Noble's titles and contracts, the ranks, deeds and climbers met), what's said at the top (a rank reached, the Entity's first word), Begin / Skip / Enter / Be named / Wear / Take / Give it up |
| `scripts/station_view.gd` | The station drawn behind its page (the platform at dusk and each stop's things on its walls), the stops' icons (also on the rail), the tiles, the renown gauge and a station's plaque on the engine's board |

| `scripts/renown.gd` | Renown: `Renown.earn` (every gain said, through `Renown.heard`), the ranks and what each opens (`RANK_OPENS`), a rank-up's moment, what a climb earns, deeds, titles (a rank and a deed each; granted by the Noble, worn), old saves' boons refunded |
| `scripts/titles.gd` | Titles' social benefits (`SOCIAL`): rival climbers (`rival_terms`, the Dining car's hook), Training, tournament masters, the outfitter |

| `scripts/city_lore.gd` | The cities: how people speak by the aura's path and step, the home Noble's persuasion, an Unbound passing as marked (found out, the rumour) |
| `scripts/mark_seal.gd` | The seal on a faint first mark: breaking it on the Marks tab (on the train, through the confirmation pop-up) makes the climber Unbound |

| `scripts/contracts.gd` | The Noble's contracts: kinds (reach, slay, retrieve), offers, taking and giving up, tracking in play (the line at the top, the log), the thing to retrieve on its floor, paying when done |
| `scripts/shop.gd` | Shop variety: the outfitter's stock by renown rank (`RANK_STOCK`, `SHELF_RANK`, `WEAPON_RANK`: the upgrade tree's trunk), the worn title's tilt (`TITLE_STOCK`) and its outfitter benefits |
| `scripts/outfitter.gd` | The outfitter's upgrade tree: its nodes (`NODES`: branch, rank, cost, what each needs, its words), what they do (`FX`), what a node needs and buying it, its gifts and word each visit, and old saves' upgrades kept as nodes (`OLD_UPGRADES`) |
| `scripts/outfit_tree.gd` | The upgrade tree drawn in code: the trunk, the branches' lines and a medallion button for each node (bought, open or locked) |

| `scripts/dummy.gd` | A training dummy: takes hits and shows them, never attacks or falls, drawn in code |

| `scripts/wings.gd` | Sealed wings (the seal, the shut doorway, the challenge waves, the champion), vault pedestals and their cards, secret rooms' hidden spots (dust, knock, lean or a finishing blow: a portal opens) |

| `scripts/floor_map.gd` | The floor map: seen rooms and corridors, you, the stair, the portals; choosing a portal to travel to; the Entity's help on its markers and buttons |

| `scripts/fog.gd` | Line-of-sight fog on floors of rooms (worked out up to 12 times a second; drawn by `lighting.gd`) |

| `scripts/towers.gd` | The towers: height (12 floors, the Proving on 6), beast, essence, monster mix, room shapes, look |
| `scripts/lost.gd` | Falling: what a fall takes, the lost pieces remembered, and the lost cache that gives them back (its numbers, words and drawing) |

| `scripts/dining_page.gd` | The Dining car's things: the tables of climbers (`Diner`, each drawn behind a laid table) and the talk on its placard |
| `scripts/arena_car.gd` | The Arena car, reached only through a duel: floorboards, a roped sawdust ring, benches of onlookers and lamps, laid over the room while the duel lasts |

| `scripts/menu.gd` | Title screen, pause menu, climber screen, inventory, settings, controls list and dev tools |

| `scripts/train.gd` | The train between climbs: its cars, the store's prices, stock and words, the outfitter's lines, buying (with change), selling, the chest and departing (the outfitter's upgrades are his tree: `outfitter.gd`) |
| `scripts/train_page.gd` | The train's cars as the menu: what's in each car (the engine's board and lever, the Outfitter's Car, your car, the workshop's bench, the Library's bookcase and lectern, the Settings & Controls Car's signal panel and emergency brake), their cards, moving along (swipes, drags, the wheel, keys; the rail's light follows), into a car and back to the rail, and the crystal strip |
| `scripts/train_view.gd` | The train drawn in code from the inside: the cars side by side, each in layers (walls, panelling and floor; the view outside its square-framed arched windows, with the stars, the moon, the six towers, hills, clouds and mist sliding past as you walk along; frames, curtains and each car's fittings: the cab's pipes, gauges, backhead and firebox, the store's shelves, the workshop's tools and bottles, the Library's book-lined walls, ladder and globe, the signal room's levers, clock, block instrument and key-plate; lamplight and firelight; swinging lamps, moving needles, steam and dust in the light), their furniture (wardrobe, pack, chest, shelves, rail, glass case, destination board, drawers, placards, the Library's bookcase, volumes and lectern, the signal panel and the emergency brake), the outfitter, the lever; and the workshop's corner. It fills the screen right of the menu's rail. Almost all of it is drawn once; only small moving things are redrawn |
| `scripts/train_art.gd` | The train's own colours (the old game's dusk-violet night, gilt and lamplight) and drawing helpers: gradients, soft light, wood grain, raised panels, rivets, brass bars, pipes, bottles |
| `scripts/bench.gd` | The workshop car's bench: brewing, tempering, retraiting, joining, breaking down, inscribing, the recall scroll, the vessel's counter; the Rune Artist who crafts for climbers without the mark (name, lines, premium); its costs and words |
| `scripts/vessel.gd` | The vessel: capturing the secret essence that isn't your path, coming home or being lost, brewing, the vault's chance, the store's trade, the bench's uses, its icon and the capture's curl, all its numbers and words |
| `scripts/vessel_page.gd` | The vessel on the train: in your chest (packing it), at the store (trading), at the bench's Vessel counter, and the dev tools' row |
| `scripts/ui.gd` | The interface's colours, fonts, panel, card, button and slot styles, ornaments and text sizes (menus and HUD) |
| `scripts/entity.gd` | The Entity: its name, lines, tips and Archive text (data), and its face, drawn in code |
| `scripts/guide.gd` | The Entity in play: the tip registry and queue, its sphere on the right, the bubble (tips and one-line tidbits), dismissing it, the panel with the climb's log, and what's been said |
| `scripts/entity_notes.gd` | The Entity's tidbits (true facts about towers, creatures, tasks, scenarios, rooms, puzzles, locks), each tower's empty lore list, its quips and the panel's words (data) |
| `scripts/entity_helper.gd` | The Entity in the menus: its sphere on every car, page and station stop and the pause menu's pages, resting clear of every button; it glides beside the button (or furniture) you hover, move onto or hold (on a phone), explains it and comments, for as long as it's highlighted; its lines on a page stay until a click, a tap or L sends them away; a click or tap on it (with no line waiting) talks |
| `scripts/entity_help.gd` | What the Entity says about each train button, by a stable name worked out from the button's tags, with the blanks filled from the game (data) |
| `scripts/entity_talk.gd` | Talking with the Entity: the questions and answers (`DIALOGUE`, a small tree), its "what next" hints, the facts that fill its answers, and the owner's story slots (data); menu.gd's "talk" page shows it |
| `scripts/notices.gd` | What the game tells you in play: the top-middle lane (banners taking turns, status chips), the left feed and the event log the Entity shows; `main.note_event` feeds both |
| `scripts/title.gd` | The title screen's drawing: sky, tower, motes, the name and tagline |
| `scripts/controls.gd` | Input actions, set up in code; the controller layout and the controls list |
| `scripts/pad.gd` | Game controller: puts each controller into the standard layout (straight through, a guess, or your setup), turns its buttons into actions, LB for casts, notices which device was used last |
| `scripts/pad_setup.gd` | Settings → Controller setup: records a controller's layout one control at a time, with a live readout of what it sends |
| `scripts/touch.gd` | On-screen touch controls |
| `scripts/layout.gd` | The layout registry: every arrangeable thing (HUD and touch buttons), its built-in place, the groups Arrange mode shows them in, the arrangement over it (the menus' too), loading and saving (see Arranging the layout) |
| `scripts/arrange_ui.gd` | Arrange mode for the menus, the left rail and the train's furniture: finds what can be arranged as each page is built, what sits inside what and what each is called (the `NAMES` table), and puts the arrangement on top of the containers' layout |
| `scripts/drawn_parts.gd` | Arrange mode's parts for something drawn in one go (the workshop's wall, the crystals along the bottom of the train): an empty control over each part, and the move each part is drawn through |
| `scripts/arrange.gd` | Arrange mode: the blocks a level at a time (Go inside, Back out), the list at the side, the handles, dragging, scaling, snapping, overlap warnings, Undo and Redo, the toolbar, Copy layout and the Entity's card and three steps |
| `data/layout.json` | The shared layout everyone starts with (written by Save when the game runs from the Godot editor) |
| `export_presets.cfg` | Web export settings |
| `.github/workflows/web.yml` | Builds and publishes the web version |
| `scripts/audio.gd` | Plays sound effects (random variants) and music; keeps sounds that come from many places at once (fire's crackle) to one every so often (`GAP_MS`) |
| `scripts/sound_bank.gd` | Generated list of sound files |
| `scripts/room.gd` | The room's look: floor, walls, torches, dust |
| `scripts/holes.gd` | Holes you fall through (chasms, the Gryphon's drops, the ways down): dark pits with a lit rim, shaft walls falling into near-black and the floor below faint at the bottom; the teeter's numbers (`TEETER`) |
| `scripts/ways_down.gd` | Each tower's way down: the Kraken's whirlpools, the Manticore's sinkholes, the Wyvern's slag chutes, the Chimera's root hollows (planned by each tower's floor script, run and drawn here) |
| `scripts/mimic.gd` | The mimic: a chest that bites back (planned into a floor's chests, sprung by `room_rewards.gd`) |
| `scripts/foes/` | New monsters, one script each: the husk and the bellringer (every tower), the squall, rookery and stormstone (Gryphon), the inkling, drowned and driftbell (Kraken), the stoker, slagworm and riveter (Wyvern); `rivet.gd` the riveter's shot, `marks.gd` what they leave on the floor (slag, a charged trail, ink) |
| `scripts/inscriptions.gd` | Inscription stones: where one stands, and each tower's six inscriptions (the owner's story text) |
| `scripts/offhand.gd` | The off-hand: a second weapon's strike, a shield's block, a wand's power, their pools, how they're drawn |
| `scripts/item_pool.gd` | Every essence item's own pool (charms, off-hand pieces, enchanted weapons): drawing a use (its pool first, then the essence you carry), filtering essence in to refill or power it, an enchanted melee weapon's edge, bows' and crossbows' essence shots, the small flat bars |
| `scripts/summit_blades.gd` | Summit Blades: hidden on a floor, found, carried, bonded at the summit, awakened; their powers |
| `scripts/deeds.gd` | The 36 deeds, how each is read, and the watcher that pays their renown |
| `scripts/deeds_page.gd` | The book's Deeds ribbon: the deeds, and the Summit Blades leaf |
| `scripts/glimpses.gd` | Guardian glimpses: the tower's beast seen now and then (a shadow, eyes in a high window, a distant roar) |
| `scripts/climb_shape.gd` | The climb's shape: the first climb's Regard on floor 6 and the guardian's exit, the death mark's scale, the Lethal Proving offered after a fall below floor 6, the stair's gate and ward, hordes, slipping past (Unbound, or a Maker after three slips), and the ending |
| `scripts/guardian_lessons.gd` | The guardians learn from each other's deaths: what a guardian watches in a fight, the lesson a slain one leaves in the profile, what every other guardian does with the lessons (resist, brace, sidestep, distance, timing; never past the cap), and the rumours |
| `scripts/wild_towers.gd` | Wild towers: a beaten guardian's tower runs wild (from the record, old saves too): its storms (warned rings, then strikes), wild essence burning marks on their ladder or feeding the Unbound, empowered monsters, richer finds (capped), the look, the Makers stirring at the third, what the cities say, and the Entity's facts |
| `scripts/palette.gd` | The world's colours (the interface's are in `ui.gd`) |
| `scripts/draw_util.gd` | Helpers for drawing outlined, shaded shapes |
| `scripts/shade.gd` | The shading toolkit: one texture of materials (ball, face, tube, wood, metal, cloth, figure, soft shadow) that gives every batch-drawn shape volume; how to use it is at the top of the file |
| `scripts/finish.gd` | The screen's finishing pass: grade (contrast, colour, each tower's tint) on Medium and High, bloom on High |
| `scripts/chest_art.gd` | The treasure chest, drawn one way for room chests, vault chests and a waiting mimic |
| `assets/audio/` | Built sound effects and music |
| `tools/fetch_audio_sources.sh` | Downloads the CC0 source sound packs |
| `tools/build_audio.py` | Builds `assets/audio` from the source packs |
| `scripts/weapons.gd` | Weapon definitions: every melee weapon, cane, bow, crossbow and pistol, as data, and enchanted pistols' essence shot (`ESSENCE_SHOT`) |
| `scripts/charms.gd` | Charms: their charges (`CHARGES`), auras, crafting costs, the outfitter's stock, loot chances, refilling with crystals or unbanked essence, the safe pocket, old saves' Wardbreaker consumables, and their words |
| `scripts/gear.gd` | Gear records, rarities and their odds by floor, traits, caps, named pieces (Storied and tower pieces), armor and the worn pieces (the charms and bags among them; `BAG`, `pack_capacity`, `bag_slow`), slots and the paper doll, drops, wear, protection, heirloom slots (and old-save conversion), comparing and gear icons |
| `scripts/data_registry.gd` | The Data editor's engine: which tables can be edited (sections, kinds of value, bounds), applying changes by key, refusing bad values, the two changes files, Copy and Paste |
| `scripts/data_page.gd` | Dev tools → Edit game data: the Data editor's page (tabs, sections, search, a row per field, My changes, Save / Copy / Paste, the Entity's help) |
| `data/overrides.json` | Changes made in the Data editor (empty until you save some from the Godot editor) |
| `tests/smoke_test.gd` | Headless test that drives every action |
| `tests/reach_sweep.gd` | A heavier sweep of the floor planner: thousands of floors checked for a way out |
| `tests/floor_bot_driver.gd` | The floor bot: plays a floor through the same inputs a controller gives (walks by path, opens doors by walking, fights, does the task, solves the puzzle, takes the stair) |
| `tests/essence_measure.gd` | A measure, not a check: the floor bot plays floors of one tower and it prints the essence gathered and each body part's soak per floor, for tuning the split and the soak rate |
| `tests/floor_bot_sweep.gd` | The floor bot's heavy sweep: many floors per tower and depth, with the completion rate, time per floor, failures and what the floors hold |
| `tests/layout_audit_driver.gd` | The layout audit: opens the screens and flags text on text, cut short, cut off, off screen or under the rail |
| `tests/layout_sweep.gd` | The layout audit's full sweep: every screen at every Interface size, on a computer and three phones |
| `scripts/test_run.gd` | Gives each test run its own folder for the files it saves (`user://test_runs/<number>/`), so two test runs at once never share a file |

## Testing

`tests/smoke_test.gd` loads the room and drives the climber through every action (combo, riposte, parry, arrow reflect, roll, both casts, overdraw, getting hurt, death, clearing the room), and checks gear and items too (rarity odds by floor, traits and caps, the belt and its take card, each consumable, the item ring, the belt breaking into crystals, the pack and the train: opening on your car, moving along it by keys, a swipe, the wheel and the arrows, buying with change, a price you can't pay, selling, the chest's save, choosing a tower in the engine and departing, a fall after the Regard returning to the train; the bench: brewing, tempering only for a Rune Artist, retrait, join, break down, inscribe, the recall scroll and their save; climber slots: two climbers saved and loaded apart, switching swapping marks, crystals, train, look and the Entity's tips, settings shared, the slot played last remembered, the title's Climbers button, the picker by keys and by a tap, an empty slot's new climber, Delete twice removing only that climber, no seventh, and an old single save moving into slot 1 whole; the vessel: passing through without one, capturing up to what it holds with the path's essence untouched, lost on a fall, home on survival or recall without breaking, packed for a climb, brewed only by a Rune Artist, a vault's card, the store's trade, the bench's +4 and deep rune, the plea and the bribe, and its save; the Data editor: changes by key, bad values refused, the two files layered, Copy and Paste, putting one or all back, search, a price in the store, an Entity tip, the page by keys, controller and touch, fitting a phone), then reports the results:

```bash
godot --headless -s tests/smoke_test.gd
```

Each run keeps the files it saves (test climbers, the Data editor's and the room painter's test files, the layout) in a folder of its own (`scripts/test_run.gd`), removed when it ends, so several test runs can go at once without tripping each other up.

It ends with every test file in `tests/parts/` (each a `run(t)` function, so several people can add tests without touching the same file): the menus' feel (`menu_feel.gd`: a page built again after a press keeps its scroll, on the Library page and the Outfitter's list, by a tap and by keys; buying, selling and choosing play their sound and flash; the confirmation pop-up: Cancel, Esc and a tap outside do nothing, Confirm does it, the focus on Cancel, fitting an 800x400 phone; the wheel's bigger step, a finger's 1:1 drag that presses nothing, a flick, Page Down), charms (`charms.gd`: a passive on while worn and off when removed, a charge that spends and refills only by feeding it essence, the special slots and the safe pocket on a fall, the bench crafting one (the Rune Artist's premium for a climber without the mark), the outfitter selling them, vault, secret-room and monster finds, old saves with the Wardbreaker consumable or the Ember Charm amulet, the Entity's words, no banned words) and bags (`bags.gd`: the pack follows the bag, slowed only when a big bag is nearly full, the heading and HUD cue, over-full old saves lose nothing, a first Satchel on the rail); the spell rework (`spell_rework.gd`: Lightning Rod's beat, its chain link and its burst; Virulence's burst, spread and stagger; Carry striking, gathering, crashing foes together and into a wall; Graft Burst with no grafts and grafts coming sooner; Mimic and Shed with no graft; Volley adding up and bending; Vault staying on the ground); a mark's high steps (`mark_ascent.gd`: all 18 marks' rank IV, forks, Apex+, Transformation II and Zenith written and aimed at their own spells; the tree showing and choosing them for good; upgrades changing numbers only once chosen; rank IV raising the passive; each kind of effect working in a room; a step-12 mark's essence and creatures; the Entity's words) and the pace of a mark (`progression.gd`: steps 6 and 12 at the owner's targets); the tree's foundation (`tree_foundation.gd`: the library playing as before, choosing for good, the Unbound learning a passed side and its cost, placeholders that can't be chosen, old saves, the book from every way in, turning its pages by keys, controller and swipe, choosing on the Tree through the confirmation pop-up, Ball Lightning and Lingering in play, every variation on a technique, Volley and Spark, the Data editor seeing the library, and the book fitting a phone at every Interface size); the Unbound's effects and the Techniques tab (`techniques_tab.gd`: every effect on a hit, a move, a shell and on yourself at each strength, a dozen of them doing their thing in play, the echoes found, costing strain and recorded, the tab shown only to the Unbound beside Body, building a technique there with a form, a variation and an effect, each saved and carried at once, the strain dial, naming, boost and routing, an echo recorded, a part not yet half full, saved and loaded, nothing changing away from the train, the essence bench and the Techniques page gone, the Entity's words, the keys, a controller and a tap, a marked climber kept out, and the tab fitting 844x390 at every Interface size), the Rune Artist (`rune_artist_npc.gd`: every crafted work open to a climber without the mark for the materials plus the premium, a Rune Artist paying the materials alone, the vessel work's fee, the Rune Artist drawn in the Workshop car with their greeting, lines, hover help and a card saying who crafts, the counters' help, a phone, the Entity's first-time tip and topic, no banned words), body move (`body_move.gd`: the Unbound's moving essence on the climber screen's Body tab working in a climb, the default target a part that can take it and never a drain, To skipping parts that can't, Drain into crystals its own button, the reason and the result right under the control, a card choosing the part, no moving while a monster is after you, a phone keeping its place and fitting 844x390, the Techniques tab only pointing there, and the Entity's words); and secret rooms (`secrets.gd`: pocket dimensions carved in every tower with no way in on foot, behind a plain wall tile with no torch on it and no trap in front, shut to sight, fog, paths and bodies until leaned open by keys, a controller or the touch stick, then a portal in the wall leads in and back; each vault's guard laid out; a Maker's fragment on the north wall; and a rift beside one tearing into it), portals (`portals.gd`: where the floor's portals stand on planned floors, reachable through no lock; asleep until the task, a sealed wing or a cleared floor wakes them; travel by the floor map with keys, a click, a tap or a held finger asking the Entity; the map's look, help and buttons, the pause menu's Floor map and the phone's Map button; a pocket dimension's portal in and back, the floor waiting and the fog showing only where you are; the vault's chests, a mimic and a lead to the Summit Blade; the Tithe Stone, runes and Keeper; a Maker's portal; the Entity's words), and the Loadout and stash (`loadout.gd`: wearing from the pack and the stash, taking off, an heirloom waiting on the train, rings, a Climber's Pack's room, what goes on as you depart, moving between pack and stash and the stash's room, sorting and filtering, old saves, buying into the pack, stash or straight onto you and selling from either, comparing with what you wear, every worn piece and weapon drawn on the front figure and in the tower, the screens by their buttons, and the Loadout, stash and store fitting at every Interface size), and stations (`stations.gd`: stepping off at the station from the engine and back by the rail; every Training lesson run in the arena, remembered, one skipped, and the set's renown paid once; a medal drill's fee and medal, a fall losing it; a tournament bracket won round by round against climbers with marks, lost by being knocked down (never killed), and given up; the Noble granting a title when renown allows and showing what it gives; titles' benefits applying (the outfitter's discount and better pieces, rival climbers' terms, Training's fee and extra drills, tournament masters' earlier brackets and bigger prizes); charm recipes and cheaper refills from rank; contracts taken, tracked at the top of the screen and in the log, and done (a thing retrieved, a floor reached, monsters slain), a fall dropping a thing carried, and the thing to retrieve always in reach without blocking the stair; every renown gain saying what for, the next rank's preview and rank-ups said once; old saves with boons refunded once; the Boon Shrine and the Pedlar gone; no banned words; and every stop fitting a phone at Normal and Larger). Travel (`travel.gd`: six stations on a loop in tower order, each with its own Noble and colours; the loop distance the shorter way round; fares of 12 a stop and titles' cuts; travel locked until the first mark or the Unbound path; riding from the engine's map and stepping off; the home and current station saved; the Entity's tours in order, skipped, started once by themselves and replayed from its talk; its topic and help on every new button; the map at a phone's size). To run only those: Also the new weapons and consumables (`weapons_items.gd`: the canes' parry and stagger and the sword-cane's drawn finisher; the flintlock and revolver firing, reloading, a roll or putting one away breaking the reload off; every essence shot, and enchanted pistols filling their own chambers without filling the hand; the new relics' rules; every Common piece ordinary but useful and no two alike; Fine and Rare finds enchanted and saying so; relics' blank histories shown only once written; the staples' prices and drops; the Stamina Vial; how the new weapons are drawn; and a save keeping them). Fatigue (`fatigue.gd`: acting at zero stamina shrinks max stamina by the shortfall, down to the 40% floor and then too winded; stamina refilling only to the shrunken bar; the stair leaving it and a Stamina Vial, a camp's rest and a new climb restoring it; the HUD's hover help and the Entity's tip and answer; the Healing and Stamina Vials' names). Consumables (`consumables.gd`: staples common and every other item rare over a seeded sample of whole climbs (about 3 to 5 found a climb), with essence instead; each state from its monster on the HUD and cured by its item, the wards, and states being fair; each tower's handy items doing their job, a net bringing a bat down, Edge Oil cracking a shell, Lantern Oil on a dark floor; no item with a size anywhere (every Healing Vial heals the same, a full stack leaves another on the floor); old saves loading sized items as plain ones; the Entity's words; no banned words). Counts by sixes (`six_counts.gd`: every list the player collects or chooses from on the floors-and-progression side (puzzles, floor tasks, room themes, ranks, drills and lessons, brackets, titles, camp cards, synergies, deeds, inscriptions) divides by six or is three; the four newest puzzles fit every tower's halls at every tier and their own solvers answer them; each tower's two favourites; the four newest floor tasks played to done; the ranks' widening steps and an old save moved onto the twelve-rank ladder; the six brackets climbing). The story text (`story_text.gd`: the intro's six screens with no placeholder left, the 36 inscription stones and 18 Makers' fragments written, the sixth climber's in the climber's pronouns, the Entity's six story moments marked and Unbound and said once, the counting rhyme in the Dining car and from rivals, rivals mentioning their Entity, and no banned words).

```bash
godot --headless -s tests/smoke_test.gd -- parts
```

The Outfitter's Car has its own part (`outfitter.gd`): everything shown at once with no filter or pages, the four sections, the list when space is short (every row tapped and bought at a phone's size, with `store_taps.gd`), the tree's nodes opening by rank and title, being bought and doing what they say (prices, rare stock, gifts, word), old saves keeping their upgrades, the Entity's words, and no banned words.

Or only the part files whose names hold a word: `godot --headless -s tests/smoke_test.gd -- parts stations`.

Add `part=` and a word to run only the parts files whose names hold it (`-- parts part=reachability`). The reachability part (`tests/parts/reachability.gd`) sweeps a few hundred floor plans and sixty floors as the game builds them; a heavier sweep, which also counts dead ends left with nothing in them, is its own script:

```bash
godot --headless -s tests/reach_sweep.gd -- 200        # floor plans: 200 per tower and floor
godot --headless -s tests/reach_sweep.gd -- live 40    # floors as the game builds them
```

The floor bot (`tests/parts/floor_bot.gd` plays four floors in the parts run, at twice the speed; its heavier sweep is its own script) plays real floors the way a player would, through the same inputs a controller gives: it walks by path, opens doors by walking up to them, fights with normal attacks, does the floor's task (strikes the levers, carries the orb, walks the runes, solves the puzzle with what its generator knows: the true plate, the mirrors' turns, the path over the stones, the slides on the ice, the pushes for a block) and walks up the stair. The parts run plays it with the stairs' barriers on (`climb_shape.gd`): it never rolls, so it waits out each gate or ward for its task and never slips past one. `tests/parts/climb_shape.gd` tests the climb's shape itself: the first climb, the Regard on floor 6 and its exit, death marks, the Lethal Proving after a fall, gates, wards, hordes, slips, the Maker, camps and the ending. On the ice it plays one slide at a time with the puzzle's own solver: it clears the monsters about first, steps on from a way on it picked (lined up on the tile's middle, standing still), waits until each slide has fully stopped, and plans the next from wherever it ended up, so a blow or a monster in the lane costs it nothing; walking round the ice it never cuts a corner onto it. A monster that stays out on the ice (keeping a quiet hall asleep) it goes after, sliding over to rest beside it (round to its back if its front turns blows). `tests/parts/ice_hall.gd` checks the slide graph over a few hundred generated halls, the real controls in a real hall, and the bot on a few Ice Halls. It's given a sturdy climber (1500 health, and the dev tool's No dying: it counts the close calls instead) so the sweep tests the floors rather than the fighting. It reports how many floors it finished, the time per floor, every failure and why, and how many rooms have no payoff and dead ends hold nothing:

```bash
godot --headless --fixed-fps 60 -s tests/floor_bot_sweep.gd -- 2      # 2 floors per tower and depth (108 floors)
godot --headless --fixed-fps 60 -s tests/floor_bot_sweep.gd -- 3 kind=runes tower=kraken
godot --headless --fixed-fps 60 -s tests/floor_bot_sweep.gd -- 2 kind=ice     # only Ice Halls, every tower
```

**The layout audit** (`tests/layout_audit_driver.gd`) opens the game's screens and looks at every piece of text on them: Labels, RichTextLabels, Buttons with words, and text drawn in code. It flags text **on top of other text**, text **cut short** by its own control (ending in "..." or squeezed a word to a line), text **cut off** by something it sits in that doesn't scroll, text partly **off the screen**, text **under the rail** or a touch button, and buttons on top of each other. Text drawn in code is seen through `ui.gd draw_text` / `draw_text_lines` (use them instead of `draw_string` in menus: they also draw a line smaller, no smaller than `FIT_FLOOR`, before any of it is cut), which note where they drew while the audit watches; a control whose words may be squeezed uses `UI.keep_text(control)` (smaller, then wrapped, never cut) rather than `clip_text`. Two things stacked on purpose are marked with the meta `layout_stack`. The parts run has its light form (`tests/parts/layout_audit.gd`: a representative set of screens at the Large Interface size, on a computer and a 915x412 phone, and a page made wrong on purpose to prove the audit still sees each kind of trouble). The full sweep is its own script: every menu page and tab, every train car with the store's and the bench's views and their cards, every station stop, the Loadout and stash, at all four Interface sizes on 1280x720 and three phones (915x412, 800x400, 740x360, touch on), as a marked climber and again as an Unbound one (about 1,500 screens, four minutes). It prints each problem and a count per screen and size, and lists the HUD's and the card screens' boxes separately (those are drawn through the HUD's own layout, so they are reported, not counted):

```bash
godot --headless -s tests/layout_sweep.gd                       # everything
godot --headless -s tests/layout_sweep.gd -- ui=large size=915x412 only=stash,loadout
xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" godot --rendering-driver opengl3 -s tests/layout_sweep.gd -- ui=large size=915x412 shots=/tmp/shots   # and a picture of each screen
```

The bot also turns every Wyvern crank it can reach while its bridge is up (its floor script's `bot_step`), keeps clear of furnaces' heat and sand pits when it can (`bot_grid`), and does the towers' own tasks (`bot_task`: strikes the bellows; turns each sun stone to its seal and waits for the sun). A tower's own task kind is only forced in its own tower. `tests/parts/towers_wyvern_manticore.gd` covers the Wyvern's and the Manticore's floors: their shapes, their systems, their tasks, creatures and scenarios, reachability over thirty floors of each, and the bot doing their tasks.

## Rebuilding the sounds

The finished sounds are committed, so you only need this to change them. Each game sound is defined in `SOUNDS` at the top of `tools/build_audio.py` (which source clips to layer, how loud, and an optional low-pass/high-pass to tame harsh highs or rumble). Needs Python 3 with numpy, ffmpeg, and to fetch the sources `7z` or `pip install py7zr` (one pack comes as .7z). The ambience loops for each tower and the train are defined in `AMBIENCE` just below (long beds, and one-shots scattered at random: drips, distant cries, an anvil). The effects are imported with QOA compression (the build tool writes that into each new sound's `.import` file), which keeps the web build small. The smoke test checks that every sound the code plays is in the bank, that every monster has its hurt cry and death and every beast its roar and warning, and that every tower has its ambience.

```bash
tools/fetch_audio_sources.sh
```

```bash
python3 tools/build_audio.py
```

To rebuild only some of them (and leave every other file as it is), name them: `python3 tools/build_audio.py .audio_sources --only=music,burn` (a sound from `SOUNDS`, `music` or `ambience` from `MUSIC`, or an ambience loop by its file name, such as `ambience_kraken`). `MUSIC` also sets each track's **tempo**: the music plays at `0.85`, 85% of its recorded speed at the same pitch (a time-stretch, so it's longer, not lower); change the number and rebuild `music` to try another speed.

**Fire's crackle** (`burn`) is played from many places at once (a Flameblade's swings at full heat, standing in fire, burning ground, every fire spell), so it's a soft crackle of a real fire in five takes a little apart in pitch, and `scripts/audio.gd` plays it at most once a quarter second (`GAP_MS`). It used to be a magic whoosh with a ringing shimmer over a metal slam's clank, which, many times a second, sounded like a slot machine. Tests: `tests/parts/sound_fixes.gd`.
